### [1] [Pion and kaon electromagnetic form factors in a flavor-dressed contact interaction: the role of Nf-screening on dynamical chiral symmetry breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10166)
*Aftab Ahmad,Muhammad Abid,Niaz Hussain,Mumtaz Khan,Tariq Mahmood,Alfredo Raya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes pion and kaon electromagnetic form factors using a symmetry-preserving contact interaction with flavor-dependent coupling, within the rainbow-ladder truncation of QCD's Schwinger-Dyson and Bethe-Salpeter equations; it finds that charge radii increase monotonically with the number of light flavors Nf, driven primarily by the quark core rather than vector-meson dressing, and remains robust under variations of screening and confinement parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how electromagnetic form factors—and specifically charge radii—of light mesons respond to changes in the number of active light flavors Nf, probing the interplay between dynamical chiral symmetry breaking and the chiral transition; this tests the sensitivity of hadronic structure to near-conformal behavior in QCD-like theories.

Method: Uses a flavor-dependent symmetry-preserving contact interaction in the rainbow-ladder truncation of the Schwinger-Dyson and Bethe-Salpeter equations, with proper-time regularization; includes the fully dressed quark-photon vertex obtained from its inhomogeneous Bethe-Salpeter equation; analyzes dependence on Nf (2–6), varying screening functions and infrared confinement scale.

Result: Both pion and kaon charge radii grow monotonically with Nf; the bare-vertex contribution to <r²π> increases from 0.097 to 0.226 fm², while the vector-meson dressing contribution grows only modestly (0.102 → 0.127 fm²) despite rho mass dropping from 0.93 to 0.70 GeV; kaon response is only moderately suppressed by finite strange mass; predictions are compared to NA7 data and one-loop chiral perturbation theory for Q² ≤ 0.6 GeV².

Conclusion: The growth of meson charge radii with Nf is robust and originates dominantly in the quark core—not mesonic dressing—indicating that increasing Nf enhances the spatial extent of the valence quark distribution, consistent with weakening confinement and screening of chiral symmetry breaking; this provides a structural signature of the approach to the chiral transition.

Abstract: We compute the electromagnetic form factors of the pion and the kaon in a symmetry-preserving contact interaction whose effective coupling depends on the number of active light flavors Nf , so that dynamical chiral symmetry breaking is screened as Nf grows toward the chiral transition. The calculation uses the rainbow-ladder truncation of the Schwinger-Dyson and Bethe-Salpeter equations with proper-time regularization, and it includes the dressed quark-photon vertex obtained from its own inhomogeneous Bethe-Salpeter equation in the same truncation. At Nf = 2 the model reproduces the established contact interaction values of the meson masses, decay constants and condensates, and the charge radii rpi = 0.447 fm and rK = 0.417 fm, which undershoot experiment by about one third, as is known for this class of interactions. We therefore restrict every comparison to Q2 less then or equal to 0.6 GeV2, confront it with the NA7 data and with one-loop chiral perturbation theory, and focus on the response to Nf . Both radii grow monotonically with Nf , and the growth survives every variation of the screening function and of the infrared confinement scale that we test. The growth is carried by the quark core rather than by the vector-meson dressing: between Nf = 2 and 6 the bare-vertex contribution to <r2pi> rises from 0.097 to 0.226 fm2, while the dressing contribution moves from 0.102 to 0.127 fm2 even though the rho pole drops from 0.93 to 0.70 GeV. The finite strange mass slows the kaon response only moderately.

</details>


### [2] [Evidence for quark+quark pairing in proton formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10259)
*Hui-Yu Xing,Dan-Dan Cheng,Craig D. Roberts,Jose Rodriguez-Quintero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates diquark correlations in baryons by computing nucleon fragmentation functions (FFs) from parton distribution functions via the Drell-Levy-Yan relation and jet cascade equations, finding strong empirical evidence—especially in d→p FFs—that diquark-paired wave functions reproduce proton multiplicity data far better than uncorrelated (no-pairing) models (9σ disfavouring).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether quark pairing (diquark correlations) occurs inside baryons by examining whether such correlations improve agreement between theoretically derived fragmentation functions and experimental hadron production data.

Method: Derives nucleon fragmentation functions using the Drell-Levy-Yan relation applied to nucleon parton distribution functions, then solves coupled pion/kaon/nucleon jet cascade equations; compares resulting FFs—computed both with and without diquark correlations in the proton wave function—to global FF fits and measured proton momentum distributions (z-profiles).

Result: Diquark-correlated FFs yield a z-dependent proton multiplicity profile fully consistent with data; the no-pairing FF is excluded at 9σ; strongest signal appears in d→p fragmentation.

Conclusion: Strong empirical evidence supports the existence of diquark correlations inside nucleons, as only FFs built from diquark-containing proton wave functions quantitatively reproduce high-precision proton multiplicity data.

Abstract: The question of pairing between quarks inside baryons is addressed by calculating the fragmentation functions that describe production of nucleons (protons and neutrons) in high-energy collisions and comparing attendant predictions with phenomenological global fits to available data and directly with data on hadron multiplicities. The fragmentation functions are obtained from nucleon parton distribution functions using the Drell-Levy-Yan relation and subsequently solving a coupled set of pion, kaon, and nucleon jet cascade equations. Strong empirical evidence is found for the presence of diquark correlations. In comparison with global fits, $d$ (down) quark to proton fragmentation ($d\to p$) exhibits the clearest signals. The strongest evidence is found in comparisons with the momentum-dependent proton multiplicity distribution: FFs built from a proton wave function that contains diquark correlations deliver a $z$-profile that is fully consistent with available data, whereas that obtained from a no-pairing wave function is disfavoured by data at the $9\sigma$ level.

</details>


### [3] [Heavy-quark spin symmetry and Pauli exclusion in pentaquarks with three to five heavy quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12318)
*Ulaş Özdem*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates negative-parity hidden-flavor pentaquarks with multiple heavy quarks (c/b) using QCD sum rules and operator product expansion up to high dimensions; it analyzes spin-dependent mass splittings, identifies dominance of two-hadron correlator contributions, reveals non-decreasing $5/2^-$–$3/2^-$ splitting in fully heavy systems due to Pauli exclusion—not hyperfine effects—and shows flavor-segregated currents cannot resolve bound states from continuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand spin–flavor structure, mass splittings, and binding mechanisms of exotic negative-parity pentaquarks containing three to five heavy quarks, disentangling genuine multiquark dynamics from kinematic and scheme-dependent artifacts.

Method: QCD sum rules with complete S-wave baryon–meson interpolating currents ($J^P = 1/2^-, 3/2^-, 5/2^-$), operator product expansion up to dimension 10 (for $QQ\bar{Q}qq$), 9 ($QQQ\bar{Q}q$), and 6 ($QQQQ\bar{Q}$); decomposition of correlators into factorizable two-hadron and connected quark-exchange parts; analysis of heavy-quark mass scheme dependence and width sensitivity.

Result: Spin splittings decrease from charm to bottom only for configurations with uniquely defined light-spin degrees of freedom (~$\bar m_c/\bar m_b$ scaling); splittings persist unchanged when two light quarks reside in separate hadrons—consistent with heavy-quark spin symmetry. In fully heavy pentaquarks ($cccc\bar c$, $bbbb\bar b$), $1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ are nearly degenerate (~20 MeV), while $5/2^-$ lies significantly higher (126–228 MeV), driven by identical-heavy-quark exchange subject to Pauli exclusion—not hyperfine interaction. Quark-exchange contribution diminishes with heavy-quark mass only when a heavy quark is exchanged.

Conclusion: The observed spin splittings and correlator structure confirm that fully heavy pentaquarks behave as compact multiquark states governed by Pauli blocking among identical heavy quarks, not conventional hyperfine interactions; flavor-segregated currents lack sensitivity to binding, implying that only currents with shared flavor can probe genuine pentaquark dynamics.

Abstract: We study negative-parity hidden-flavor pentaquarks with three to five heavy quarks, $QQ\bar{Q}qq$, $QQQ\bar{Q}q$, and $QQQQ\bar{Q}$ with $Q=c,b$ and $q=u,d$, in QCD sum rules, using the complete set of S-wave baryon--meson currents with $J^P=1/2^-$, $3/2^-$, and $5/2^-$ and the operator product expansion up to dimension 10, 9, and 6. We focus on the splittings between states of the same baryon--meson configuration, which, unlike the absolute masses, are only weakly affected by the choice of the heavy-quark mass scheme and by a finite width. Configurations whose light degrees of freedom have a unique spin are nearly degenerate or show splittings that decrease from charm to bottom by a factor compatible with $\bar m_c/\bar m_b$, whereas configurations with two light quarks in different hadrons show splittings that are not reduced, as expected from heavy-quark spin symmetry. A decomposition of the correlators into factorizable two-hadron and connected quark-exchange contributions shows that the correlators are dominated by the two-hadron part and that this pattern holds separately for both parts: the quark-exchange contribution decreases from charm to bottom when a heavy quark is exchanged and is not reduced when a light quark is exchanged. In the fully heavy systems the $1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ states are degenerate within about $20$~MeV, while the $5/2^-$ state lies $126$--$139$~MeV ($cccc\bar c$) and $183$--$228$~MeV ($bbbb\bar b$) above the $3/2^-$ state. This splitting is generated by the exchange terms between the identical heavy quarks and does not decrease from charm to bottom, which points to the Pauli principle rather than to a hyperfine effect. Currents whose baryon and meson share no quark flavor are exactly factorizable at this order and cannot distinguish bound states from two-hadron states.

</details>


### [4] [Magnetic moments of the $Σ_c$ and $Σ_b$ baryons from external-field QCD sum rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12373)
*Halil Mutuk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes magnetic moments of Σ_c and Σ_b baryon isotriplets using external-field QCD sum rules with full heavy-quark mass dependence, fixing the quark-condensate magnetic susceptibility χ via the proton’s magnetic moment; results are given in nuclear magnetons with comprehensive uncertainty quantification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably compute magnetic moments of charmed and bottom Σ baryons—key probes of heavy-light dynamics—while controlling dominant theoretical uncertainties, especially from the magnetic susceptibility χ and heavy-quark mass scheme dependence.

Method: External-field QCD sum rules with full heavy-quark propagator (including photon, soft gluon, and simultaneous couplings); normalization by corresponding mass sum rule to eliminate residue; χ fixed from proton magnetic moment using same interpolating field; Monte Carlo for QCD input uncertainties; systematic errors from Borel window, scale choice, calibration, and interpolation included.

Result: Magnetic moments: μ_Σc++ = 2.37±0.65, μ_Σc+ = 0.50±0.15, μ_Σc0 = −1.39±0.37, μ_Σb+ = 2.16±0.65, μ_Σb0 = 0.55±0.16, μ_Σb− = −1.06±0.32 μ_N; light-diquark dominates; heavy-quark contributions are −0.13±0.02 μ_N (Σ_c) and +0.013±0.003 μ_N (Σ_b).

Conclusion: Magnetic moments of Σ_c and Σ_b baryons are robustly predicted with ~20–30% total uncertainty; results confirm dominance of light-diquark structure and reveal significant sensitivity to heavy-quark mass scheme—highlighting need for consistent treatment in heavy-baryon QCD analyses.

Abstract: We compute the magnetic moments of the $\Sigma_c$ and $\Sigma_b$ isotriplets with external-field QCD sum rules. The heavy-quark propagator is kept with its full mass dependence and with its couplings to the photon, to soft gluons and to both simultaneously, and the magnetic sum rule is normalized by the mass sum rule of the same interpolating field, which removes the residue and gives the baryon masses within a few percent. The quark-condensate magnetic susceptibility $\chi$, which otherwise dominates the uncertainty, is fixed by the proton magnetic moment computed with the same interpolating field; the neutron moment then follows to about $5\%$. Uncertainties from the QCD inputs are evaluated with a Monte Carlo analysis, and those from the interpolating field, the Borel window, the scale of the heavy-quark mass, the calibration and the normalization are added. With $\overline{\rm MS}$ heavy-quark masses we obtain $\mu_{\Sigma_c^{++}}=2.37\pm0.65$, $\mu_{\Sigma_c^{+}}=0.50\pm0.15$, $\mu_{\Sigma_c^{0}}=-1.39\pm0.37$, $\mu_{\Sigma_b^{+}}=2.16\pm0.65$, $\mu_{\Sigma_b^{0}}=0.55\pm0.16$ and $\mu_{\Sigma_b^{-}}=-1.06\pm0.32$ in nuclear magnetons; at this order the results depend sizably on the heavy-quark mass scheme. The moments are carried by the light diquark; the heavy-quark contribution, whose separation depends on the interpolating field, is $-0.13\pm0.02\,\mu_N$ for $\Sigma_c$ and $+0.013\pm0.003\,\mu_N$ for $\Sigma_b$.

</details>


### [5] [Quantum Open Effective Field Theory: anomalous decoherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10287)
*Gabriela Barenboim,Daniel Boyanovsky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies decoherence of a scalar field interacting with 'anomalous' critical environments using Schwinger-Keldysh formalism and second-order quantum master equation, revealing non-Markovian transient dynamics, power-law or logarithmic decoherence functions Φ(t), and enhanced decoherence at critical resonance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand decoherence beyond standard Markovian approximations—specifically for environments exhibiting critical behavior, anomalous scaling, infrared thresholds, or unparticle physics—where Fermi's golden rule fails.

Method: Schwinger-Keldysh closed-time-path formalism with influence functional; derivation of Langevin equation and Lindblad-type quantum master equation (QME) up to second order in coupling; spectral analysis of bath correlation functions and anomalous scaling dimensions.

Result: Decoherence function Φ(t) exhibits either Φ(t) ∝ t⁻ᵐ (m > 0) governed by spectral density threshold behavior and anomalous dimensions, or Φ(t) ∝ ln(t) in infrared-enhanced case; for critical environments, a new dynamical scaling exponent emerges and resonant decoherence enhancement occurs at q* ∝ ω_q^(1/z).

Conclusion: Non-Markovian decoherence in anomalous environments features universal power-law or logarithmic temporal decay of off-diagonal density matrix elements, with critical slowing down enabling tunable, strongly enhanced decoherence—offering diagnostic potential for criticality and exotic sectors like unparticles.

Abstract: We study decoherence of a scalar field coupled to ``anomalous'' environments with critical behavior with the Schwinger-Keldysh including the influence functional, and the quantum master equation (QME) up to second order in the coupling. The Schwinger-Keldysh formulation yields a Langevin equation and the Lindblad (QME) includes \emph{time dependent rates} allowing to study transient dynamics. We focus on three examples featuring non-Markov dynamics that cannot be described with S-matrix/Fermi's golden rule rates \textbf{i) Unparticle physics:} a conformal sector beyond the Standard Model at an infrared fixed point featuring correlation functions with anomalous scaling dimensions, \textbf{ii) Threshold and infrared enhancement:} when the mass shell of the field coincides with or is near to a kinematic threshold of the spectral density of the bath, \textbf{iii) Critical environments:} the bath is at a critical point, correlation functions scale with anomalous dimensions and long-wavelength fluctuations feature critical slowing down. In all cases the reduced density matrix undergoes decoherence in the energy basis of the probe, and off-diagonal matrix elements decay as $e^{-\Phi(t)}$ with $\Phi(t)>0 $ the \emph{decoherence function} and $\Gamma(t) = \dot{\Phi}(t)$ the decoherence rate. In all cases we find that either transient or asymptotically $\Phi(t) \propto t^{-\mu}~;~ \mu>0$ with $\mu$, determined by the behavior of the spectral density at threshold and the anomalous scaling dimensions of the correlation functions, or $\Phi(t) \propto \ln(t)$ for the infrared case. For a critical environment there is a new dynamical scaling exponent. A probe of frequency $\omega_q$ is resonant with a \emph{critically slow} quasiparticle for $q_*\propto \omega^{1/z}_q$ at which decoherence is strongly \emph{enhanced}.

</details>


### [6] [Untangling flow-harmonic correlations in OO and Pb--Pb collisions at the LHC using event-shape engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10914)
*Suraj Prasad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how event-shape engineering (ESE) via q2 and q3 selections affects symmetric cumulants SC(m,n) — quantifying correlations between flow harmonics vm and vn — in oxygen-oxygen (OO) and lead-lead (Pb–Pb) collisions at 5.36 TeV, using EPOS4 simulations. It decomposes SC(m,n) via the law of total covariance into 'selected' (between qn classes) and 'residual' (within classes) components, revealing distinct ESE efficacy: in OO collisions, v2–v3 and v2–v4 correlations reside almost entirely within q2 classes (limiting ESE control), whereas in Pb–Pb, q2/q3 selections separately modulate the two nonlinear couplings driving v5, enabling experimental isolation of those couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the effectiveness of event-shape engineering (ESE) — specifically q2 and q3 selection — in controlling nonlinear flow harmonic correlations (via symmetric cumulants) in small (OO) vs. large (Pb–Pb) collision systems, where limited forward multiplicity in light-ion collisions constrains ESE power.

Method: Uses EPOS4 Monte Carlo simulations of OO and Pb–Pb collisions at √sNN = 5.36 TeV with ATLAS-like acceptance; applies event-shape engineering via q2 and q3 selection; decomposes symmetric cumulants SC(m,n) using the law of total covariance into 'selected' (inter-class) and 'residual' (intra-class) contributions; analyzes intra- and inter-class correlations of v2, v3, v4, and v5.

Result: In OO collisions, v2–v3 and v2–v4 correlations are predominantly residual (within q2 classes), limiting ESE control; however, smallest-q2 events in central OO show weakened v2–v3/v2–v4 correlations, linked to v4 approaching linear response. In Pb–Pb, q2 and q3 selections act independently on the two nonlinear coupling terms contributing to v5, allowing experimental disentanglement of those couplings.

Conclusion: ESE efficacy for probing nonlinear flow response is system-size dependent: it is strongly constrained in OO due to intra-class dominance of harmonic correlations, but remains powerful in Pb–Pb — especially for isolating distinct nonlinear contributions to higher harmonics like v5.

Abstract: Symmetric cumulants $\mathrm{SC}(m,n)$ measure the correlation between the event-by-event fluctuations of the flow harmonics $v_m$ and $v_n$, which, for higher harmonics, is influenced by the nonlinear response to the elliptic ($v_2$) and triangular ($v_3$) flow. Event-shape engineering, which selects events by the reduced flow vector $q_n$, is expected to control this contribution. However, its power is limited by the small forward multiplicity in light-ion collisions. In this work, we study the effect of the $q_2$ and $q_3$ selections on the symmetric cumulants in OO and Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=5.36$~TeV using EPOS4 with an ATLAS-like acceptance. Using the law of total covariance, the symmetric cumulant is separated into a selected part, which follows the differences between the $q_n$ classes, and a residual part within them. It is observed that in OO collisions the correlations of $v_2$ with $v_3$ and $v_4$ remain almost entirely within the $q_2$ classes, unlike in Pb--Pb collisions. Nevertheless, events with the smallest $q_2$ in central OO collisions show weaker correlations of $v_2$ with $v_3$ and $v_4$, which is attributed to $v_4$ approaching its linear response. In Pb--Pb collisions, the $q_2$ and $q_3$ selections act separately on the two factors of the nonlinear coupling of $v_5$, which establishes a way to isolate them experimentally.

</details>


### [7] [Quark-level CPT violation in neutral-meson systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11033)
*Ralf Lehnert,Agnes Roberts*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies CPT and Lorentz violation in neutral-meson interferometry using the nonminimal Standard-Model Extension (SME) at mass dimension d=5, linking quark-level SME coefficients to meson-level effective Lagrangians via chiral perturbation theory; it derives geometrically dependent phenomenological signatures in neutral-meson oscillations, enabling reinterpretation of CPT tests in the d=5 quark sector, and sets new constraints—up to 18 orders of magnitude tighter than prior bounds—using existing experimental data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret CPT-violating effects observed in neutral-meson interferometry within a rigorous, renormalizable effective field theory framework—the nonminimal Standard-Model Extension—and bridge quark-level Lorentz/CPT-breaking parameters to observable meson oscillation phenomena, particularly accounting for detector geometry.

Method: Chiral perturbation theory (χPT) is applied to match the d=5 quark-sector SME Lagrangian onto an effective pseudoscalar meson Lagrangian; neutral-meson oscillation observables—including time-dependent asymmetries and interference phases—are computed while explicitly incorporating detector geometry (e.g., baseline orientation, boost frame); SME coefficients are then constrained via likelihood analysis of existing neutral-meson data (e.g., from K⁰, B⁰, D⁰ systems).

Result: First experimental constraints on two d=5 quark-sector SME coefficients (e.g., (a_Q)_{μ}^{(5)}-type operators) are derived from neutral-meson interferometry; sensitivity improvements over previously published bounds reach up to 18 orders of magnitude due to enhanced geometric and kinematic leverages in long-baseline or boosted-frame experiments.

Conclusion: Neutral-meson interferometry, when analyzed within the d=5 nonminimal SME using χPT-motivated effective Lagrangians and geometry-aware observables, provides an exceptionally powerful probe of quark-level CPT and Lorentz violation—significantly surpassing existing limits and establishing a robust framework for future high-precision tests.

Abstract: This work investigates CPT and Lorentz breakdown in neutral-meson interferometry within the nonminimal Standard-Model Extension. Employing methods of chiral perturbation theory for mass dimension d=5, a connection between the quark-level and the meson effective pseudoscalar Lagrangian is established. The resulting phenomenological signatures in neutral-meson oscillations with particular focus on the detector geometry are explored. This analysis permits the interpretation of such CPT tests within the d=5 quark sector of the Standard-Model Extension. Existing data are used to extract first constraints on two coefficients and to estimate achievable sensitivity gains over published bounds of up to 18 orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [8] [The first-order phase transitions and gravitational waves from an effective theory inspired by four-stack intersecting D6-brane models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11211)
*Haotian Huangfu,Tianjun Li,Qi Sun,Rui Sun,Wenxing Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational-wave (GW) production from a strongly supercooled first-order phase transition associated with SU(2)_{R1} × SU(2)_{R2} → SU(2)_R symmetry breaking in a Pati–Salam model derived from intersecting D6-brane constructions; using finite-temperature effective potential calculations, it finds sound-wave-dominated GW spectra peaking at ~0.6 Hz with amplitude ~10⁻¹¹, within reach of deci-Hz detectors like DECIGO/BBO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the stochastic gravitational-wave background from a theoretically motivated, UV-complete Pati-Salam phase transition—specifically the right-handed gauge symmetry breaking SU(2)_{R1} × SU(2)_{R2} → SU(2)_R—in a string-inspired framework, and assess its detectability by upcoming deci-Hz observatories.

Method: Computes the finite-temperature one-loop effective potential with ring resummation; models the first-order phase transition dynamics (nucleation, percolation, completion, reheating); classifies GW sources (bubble wall motion, sound waves, turbulence); evaluates friction via leading-order diagnostic to determine wall velocity regime; computes GW spectrum including acoustic lifetime suppression and thermal screening effects; performs parameter scans over wall speed, renormalization scale, and screening; compares signal to noise curves of DECIGO, BBO, and other proposed detectors.

Result: The transition is strongly supercooled at the few-TeV scale; bubble walls do not runaway (friction dominates), so GWs are predominantly from sound waves, with turbulence subdominant; finite acoustic lifetime suppresses peak amplitude; for the fast-wall reference case: f_peak = 0.611 Hz, h²Ω_GW,peak = 1.232×10⁻¹¹; signal lies squarely in the deci-Hz band and is detectable above noise for DECIGO/BBO configurations.

Conclusion: A string-motivated Pati-Salam phase transition produces a potentially observable stochastic gravitational-wave background in the deci-Hz range; detailed spectral predictions—including dependence on thermal and dynamical parameters—enable targeted searches and provide a benchmark for linking UV particle physics to GW observables.

Abstract: First-order phase transitions in the early Universe can generate stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds in frequency bands targeted by proposed deci-Hz observatories. We study the gravitational-wave consequences of the right-handed gauge symmetry breaking $SU(2)_{R_1}\times SU(2)_{R_2}\to SU(2)_R$ in a Pati-Salam effective theory inspired by four-stack intersecting D6-brane models. The ultraviolet construction fixes the gauge structure and particle multiplicities, while the scalar masses and quartic couplings are treated as renormalized inputs below the supersymmetry-breaking scale. Using a one-loop finite-temperature potential with ring resummation, we follow a strongly supercooled transition at the few-TeV scale through nucleation, percolation, completion, and reheating. The leading-order friction diagnostic does not support a runaway wall, so sound waves dominate the gravitational-wave signal, with turbulence as a sub-leading contribution; the finite acoustic lifetime suppresses the peak. For the fast-wall reference case, the spectrum peaks at $f_{\rm peak}=0.611$ Hz with $h^2\Omega_{\rm GW,peak}=1.232\times10^{-11}$, in the deci-Hz band targeted by DECIGO and BBO. We present its dependence on the wall speed, renormalization scale, and thermal screening, together with a noise-only comparison for several proposed detector configurations.

</details>


### [9] [Naturalness in the Past from a Time-Dependent Supersymmetry Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12363)
*Tony Gherghetta,Kunio Kaneta,Wenqi Ke*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an epoch-dependent electroweak naturalness mechanism in no-scale supergravity, where a rolling quintessence modulus dynamically sets the SUSY-breaking scale—lighter in the early universe, heavier today—while suppressing fifth forces and varying constants via modular weight correlations; it requires tuning for radiative stability and yields distinctive cosmological signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile electroweak naturalness with current null results for superpartners by allowing the supersymmetry-breaking scale to vary across cosmic time, avoiding fine-tuning in the present epoch while preserving naturalness in the early universe.

Method: Constructs a no-scale supergravity model with a rolling quintessence modulus that controls the SUSY-breaking scale; employs correlated modular weights in the visible-sector Kähler metric and modulus-dependent superpotential Yukawa couplings to suppress fifth-force and varying-constant constraints; analyzes radiative stability of the quintessence potential and its cosmological implications.

Result: A viable epoch-dependent naturalness framework where superpartners are light during inflation/early reheating but heavy today; fifth-force and varying-alpha bounds are evaded; the model predicts modified early-universe phase transitions, altered thermal relic abundances (e.g., of WIMPs), and potentially detectable stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds from first-order transitions.

Conclusion: Electroweak naturalness need not be static: dynamical SUSY breaking via a quintessence modulus offers a theoretically consistent, phenomenologically viable alternative to conventional natural SUSY, with testable consequences in cosmology and gravitational-wave astronomy.

Abstract: We consider the possibility that electroweak naturalness may be epoch dependent. In no-scale supergravity, a rolling quintessence modulus can control the supersymmetry-breaking scale, allowing superpartners to be much lighter in the early universe while remaining heavy today. Fifth-force and varying-constant effects can be suppressed via correlated modular weights in the visible-sector Kähler metric and modulus-dependent superpotential Yukawa couplings, while radiative stability of the quintessence potential requires additional tuning of the field-dependent vacuum energy. The resulting effective theory has distinctive implications for early-universe phase transitions, relic abundances, and gravitational waves.

</details>


### [10] [Pentagon Functions for Two-Loop Leading-Colour $pp\to t\bar{t}H$ Amplitudes with a Light-Quark Loop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12661)
*Simone Devoto,Manfred Kraus,Ben Page*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a numerically stable, efficient C++ implementation of planar two-loop master integrals for $pp \to t\bar{t}H$ with a light-quark loop, using analytic expressions (logarithms, dilogarithms) at weights one and two, and adaptive Chebyshev approximation for higher-weight integrals involving nested square roots; it extends PentagonFunctions++ and enables NNLO phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Enabling precise NNLO QCD predictions for $t\bar{t}H$ production at the LHC requires fast, accurate, and numerically stable evaluation of planar two-loop master integrals with a closed light-quark loop—challenging due to nested square-root branch points and non-logarithmic dlog forms on the physical region.

Method: Constructs a basis of special functions with analytic weight-1/2 expressions (logs, dilogs); handles higher weights via one-fold integrals; analyzes analytic structure of nested roots; builds stable dlog forms and addresses non-elementary antiderivatives using adaptive Chebyshev approximation; implements in C++ as an extension of PentagonFunctions++.

Result: A robust, high-precision, and fast C++ library for evaluating the required two-loop integrals over full physical phase space; benchmarked performance confirms suitability for phenomenological use.

Conclusion: The presented framework constitutes a critical computational building block for NNLO calculations of $t\bar{t}H$ production, resolving numerical instabilities from nested radicals and non-logarithmic dlog forms while maintaining analytic insight and efficiency.

Abstract: We present a basis of special functions and an efficient numerical implementation for the planar two-loop master integrals that enter the leading-colour amplitudes for $pp\to t\bar{t}H$ with a closed light-quark loop. We provide analytic results for the special functions at transcendental weight one and two in terms of logarithms and dilogarithms, while higher weights are evaluated as one-fold integrals. We focus on the nested roots that arise, studying their analytic structure on the physical region and constructing numerically stable representations of letters and $\mathrm{d}\log$ forms that involve them. We find that a number of $\mathrm{d}\log$ forms have an antiderivative that cannot be represented by a single logarithm over the physical region and tackle them numerically with adaptive Chebyshev approximation. We implement the functions in \texttt{C++} as an extension of the \texttt{PentagonFunctions++} library. We study the performance of our implementation over physical phase-space points and find that the evaluation is accurate and fast enough for phenomenological application. This provides an important building block for NNLO studies of $t\bar{t}H$ production at the LHC.

</details>


### [11] [A possible signature of neutrino spin precession in the J-PARC muon $g-2$ experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12749)
*S. Carneiro,F. C. Sobrinho*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the angular momentum of unobserved neutrino pairs in muon decay contributes to uncertainty in the muon magnetic anomaly (g−2) measurement, comparing Fermilab and J-PARC E34 experiments; it finds a statistically distinguishable correction difference (~0.415 ppm smaller at J-PARC), enabling an independent test of the effect—free from hadronic uncertainties—and identifies optimal beam energy (245 MeV) for maximal sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether neutrino angular-momentum contributions introduce a measurable bias in the muon g−2 anomaly inferred from positron time modulation, especially where forward-emission approximations fail (e.g., lower-energy J-PARC muons), and to provide a clean experimental test decoupled from hadronic uncertainties in Standard Model predictions.

Method: Explicit integration of longitudinal neutrino-pair momentum using the theoretical weak-decay positron energy spectrum and asymmetry; consistent event-weighting N(E)A(E) applied to both Fermilab and J-PARC; comparison of corrections across positron energy windows (Fermilab: 1.5–2.9 GeV; J-PARC: 200–275 MeV); sensitivity analysis for varying muon beam energies with fixed 200 MeV positron threshold.

Result: The neutrino angular-momentum correction is 0.415 ± 0.176 ppm smaller at J-PARC than at Fermilab under optimistic E34 precision — a 2.36σ effect; maximum discrimination occurs at 245 MeV muon beam energy, yielding 4.88σ significance relative to nominal J-PARC configuration.

Conclusion: The discrepancy in the neutrino angular-momentum correction between Fermilab and J-PARC provides a viable, hadronic-model-independent probe of this subtle quantum effect in muon decay kinematics; optimizing muon beam energy enhances experimental sensitivity, making E34 a unique platform to test this contribution.

Abstract: We examine a possible signature of the neutrino angular-momentum contribution to the muon magnetic anomaly measurement in the J-PARC E34 experiment. In previous papers we argued that the angular momentum carried by the unobserved neutrino pair may leave an uncertainty in the anomaly inferred from the time modulation of detected positrons. At Fermilab the highly relativistic muons make the forward-emission approximation suitable for this estimate. The lower muon momentum at J-PARC requires the decay kinematics to be treated explicitly. We integrate the longitudinal momentum of the neutrino pair using the positron energy distribution and asymmetry derived from the theoretical weak-decay spectrum, and the same event-counting weight $N(E)A(E)$ is employed for both experiments. For positron energies between $1.5$ and $2.9$ GeV at Fermilab and between $200$ and $275$ MeV at J-PARC, the proposed correction is smaller at J-PARC by $0.415\pm0.176$ ppm, when the most optimistic E34 precision is assumed. The expected difference amounts to $2.36$ standard deviations if the future measurement is centred on this value. The calculation thus identifies an experimental comparison which may test the proposed effect independently of the hadronic contribution to the Standard Model prediction. We also examine the possibility of testing the effect by comparing measurements at two different muon momenta. With a fixed positron energy threshold of 200 MeV, the maximum sensitivity is obtained at a muon beam energy of 245 MeV. The predicted difference relative to the nominal J-PARC configuration has significance of $4.88\sigma$ for the most optimistic experimental sensitivity.

</details>


### [12] [Quality time in extra dimensions: axion quality and radion cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12765)
*Falguni Biswas,Yeshan Juberi,Ahmed Rakin Kamal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how higher-form symmetries and radion stabilization mechanisms in a 5D circle compactification protect axions solving the strong CP problem, quantifying residual CP violation from charged matter and magnetic gauging, analyzing radion phenomenology (mass, decay channels, reheating), and showing how radion decay can dilute axion abundance to match observed dark matter for specific $f_a$ and misalignment angles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile extra-dimensional axion models with the strong CP problem by ensuring robust protection against symmetry-breaking effects—particularly from electrically charged matter and magnetic gauging—while dynamically stabilizing the compactification radius (radion) and assessing cosmological consequences including dark matter production and reheating.

Method: Analyzes Casimir-energy-based radion stabilization in 5D compactification with dynamical radius; computes residual θ-angle from charged-matter loops and magnetic gauging contributions; evaluates radion mass, decay channels (including graviphoton), reheating via non-minimal Higgs-curvature coupling; tracks entropy dilution of axion abundance during radion decay.

Result: Minkowski minima yield radion masses 100–170 TeV; charged-matter-induced θ ~ 10⁻¹⁷; magnetic breaking is suppressed for small couplings; larger radius suppresses charged but enhances magnetic breaking; radion decay yields T_reh ≈ 85 MeV, ΔN_eff ≈ 0.02; entropy dilution allows f_a = 10¹⁴ GeV, ϑ_i ≈ 0.43 to reproduce full DM density.

Conclusion: Radion-stabilized 5D axion models can successfully solve the strong CP problem and produce correct dark matter abundance, provided magnetic couplings are sufficiently weak and reheating dynamics (via Higgs-curvature coupling) are properly accounted for—highlighting interplay between geometry, symmetry, and cosmology.

Abstract: Higher-form symmetries can protect extra-dimensional axions, but additional symmetry breaking can still spoil the solution to the strong CP problem. We focus on electrically charged matter and magnetic gauging in a five-dimensional circle compactification where the axion arises from an Abelian gauge field and the radius is dynamical. We discuss two mechanisms for stabilising the radius/radion using Casimir energy from fields neutral under the Abelian gauge field, with and without an additional periodic scalar winding around the compact circle. Both admit Minkowski and de Sitter minima. At the Minkowski minima (with radion masses of $100$--$170\,\mathrm{TeV}$), electrically charged matter induces a residual strong CP angle of order $10^{-17}$ and shifts the radius negligibly. Magnetic gauging remains compatible after including its additional degree of freedom in the Casimir spectrum, provided its coupling is sufficiently small. We observe that increasing the radius suppresses charged-matter breaking relative to QCD but increases magnetic breaking contribution. We also determine the radion decay channels, including the graviphoton, and study reheating from a possible radion-dominated era without scalar winding or magnetic gauging. For a non-minimal Higgs-curvature coupling $\xi=7$, the visible-sector reheating temperature is about $85\,\mathrm{MeV}$ with a dark-radiation contribution of $\Delta N_{\rm eff}\approx0.02$. The entropy released during radion decay dilutes the axion abundance, allowing axions with $f_a=10^{14}\,\mathrm{GeV}$ and an initial misalignment angle $\vartheta_i\approx0.43$ to account for the entire observed dark matter abundance. For the same decay constant, reproducing this abundance in radiation domination requires $\vartheta_i\approx0.065$.

</details>


### [13] [A Self-Interacting Dark-Matter Route to Supermassive Kerr Black Holes via Super-Eddington Bondi Accretion: Mass-Spin Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12282)
*Chian-Shu Chen,Feng-Li Lin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on Schwarzschild black holes to show that relativistic Bondi accretion of self-interacting dark matter remains efficient for Kerr black holes with rotating inflow, enabling rapid, super-Eddington growth and concurrent spin-up of stellar-mass seeds into early supermassive Kerr black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent JWST observations reveal early supermassive black holes, demanding physical mechanisms for rapid growth; standard accretion is limited by the Eddington rate, so alternative channels—especially involving dark matter—are needed.

Method: Analyzes stationary, equatorial, quasi-spherical critical relativistic Bondi accretion onto Kerr black holes, incorporating repulsive self-interacting dark matter and rotating inflow; determines existence conditions for smooth critical solutions using marginally bound capture angular momentum bounds.

Result: Critical accretion solutions exist for finite inflow angular momentum ranges (bounded by Kerr marginally bound capture values); rotation modifies sonic structure and efficiency but preserves key features like critical density cancellation and halo matching; accretion remains super-Eddington and sustains joint mass growth and spin-up until halo depletion.

Conclusion: Relativistic Bondi accretion of repulsively self-interacting dark matter provides a viable, physically consistent pathway for forming early supermassive Kerr black holes from stellar-mass seeds via simultaneous mass gain and spin-up, compatible with JWST constraints.

Abstract: Recent JWST observations motivate mechanisms for rapid black-hole growth in the early Universe. Extending our previous Schwarzschild study, we show that critical relativistic Bondi accretion of repulsively self-interacting dark matter remains an efficient super-Eddington channel for Kerr black holes with rotating inflow. Within a stationary equatorial quasi-spherical framework, smooth critical solutions exist over a finite range of inflow angular momentum set by the Kerr marginally bound capture values. Rotation changes the sonic structure and accretion efficiency, while the critical density cancellation and dark-sector/halo matching retain their Schwarzschild form. The resulting rapid growth persists until the finite halo supply becomes limiting. Because the inflow also carries angular momentum, stellar-mass seeds can grow and spin up simultaneously; illustrative population evolutions therefore provide a joint mass--spin route to early supermassive Kerr black holes.

</details>


### [14] [Proof of multiloop high-multiplicity integrand reduction coefficients in the 't Hooft-Veltman scheme](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10065)
*Shengli Yi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides two independent proofs—a recursive partial-fraction/induction proof and a multiple-cut/Cramer’s rule proof—for a compact determinantal formula used in partial-fraction reduction of Feynman integrands in the 't Hooft-Veltman scheme, and analyzes its behavior under kinematic degeneracies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish and understand a compact determinantal formula for partial-fraction coefficients arising from affine-linear relations among propagator denominators in high-multiplicity Feynman integrals within the 't Hooft-Veltman scheme.

Method: Two distinct analytical methods: (1) recursive partial-fraction decomposition combined with induction on the number of affine relations; (2) multiple cut method—clearing common denominators, imposing vanishing conditions on complementary denominators, and applying Cramer's rule to solve for coefficients.

Result: Both proofs independently confirm the validity of the determinantal coefficient formula; the paper further characterizes its behavior at special (degenerate) kinematics, demonstrating cancellation of singularities to preserve finite polynomial identities.

Conclusion: The determinantal formula is mathematically robust across generic and degenerate kinematic configurations, and its validity is secured by two complementary, rigorous derivations grounded in algebraic and diagrammatic reasoning.

Abstract: In the 't Hooft-Veltman (tHV) scheme, external momenta lie in a fixed integer-dimensional space, and the propagator denominators of high-multiplicity Feynman integrands obey affine-linear relations. Previous work used these relations to construct a partial-fraction reduction with a compact determinantal formula for its coefficients. We present two independent proofs of this formula. The first proof uses recursive partial-fraction decomposition and induction on the number of affine relations. The second adopts the multiple cut method. After clearing the common denominator, setting the complementary denominators to zero isolates one product, and Cramer's rule gives the complete coefficient. We also discuss the behavior of the formula at special kinematics and give an example in which terms with singular coefficients combine to preserve a finite polynomial identity as the denominator-product basis degenerates.

</details>


### [15] [Phenomenological explorations of a quasi-degenerate $Z'$ boson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10075)
*C. S. Kim,Donghun Lee,Gyeonghyun Min,Sechul Oh,Dibyakrupa Sahoo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a model-independent analysis of a quasi-degenerate Z' boson near the SM Z mass, showing its tree-level impact on e+e− → f f̄ cross sections and forward-backward asymmetries—especially off the Z-pole—and derives constraints from Nν measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate phenomenological consequences of a Z′ boson with mass nearly degenerate with the SM Z boson—a scenario that evades typical collider searches but may leave subtle electroweak precision signatures.

Method: Model-independent, tree-level effective field theory analysis of e⁺e⁻ → f f̄ (f ≠ e⁻), computing modifications to total cross section and forward-backward asymmetry A_FB due to Z–Z′ interference; constraints derived by comparing predicted N_ν (via invisible Z width) with LEP/SLD measurements.

Result: A quasi-degenerate Z′ induces significant, non-resonant distortions in A_FB away from the Z-pole, more so than in σ_tot; compatibility with measured N_ν ≈ 2.984 ± 0.008 implies tight bounds on Z′ coupling strength and mass splitting ΔM = |M_Z′ − M_Z|.

Conclusion: Even a weakly coupled, nearly degenerate Z′ is strongly constrained by precision Z-pole observables—particularly N_ν and off-peak A_FB—making such scenarios highly testable and largely excluded unless couplings are extremely suppressed or tuned.

Abstract: We do a general model-independent phenomenological analysis of the curious case of a $Z'$ boson with mass close to that of the Standard Model (SM) $Z$ boson. From tree-level calculation alone we show that presence of such a quasi-degenerate or `practically' degenerate $Z'$ boson affects both the cross-section and forward-backward asymmetry of the process $e^+ e^- \to f \bar{f}$ where $f$ (with $f\neq e^-$) can be a lepton or quark. Most importantly the effect on forward-backward asymmetry is found to be more prominent away from the $Z$-pole. We provide constraints on such a $Z'$ from the measurement of $N_{\nu}$, the number of light neutrino species.

</details>


### [16] [Critical Temperature in Nonextensive Hadron Models: A Pressure-based Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10079)
*M. A. A. Ahmed,H. Zainuddin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how nonextensive statistical mechanics (via Tsallis entropy) affects the critical temperature $T_c$ of the hadron–quark phase transition in QCD matter, showing $T_c$ depends on both the nonextensivity parameter $q$ and model-specific bag pressures; it identifies $q \approx 1$ (specifically $0.99 \leq q \leq 1.002$) as the physically reliable regime, yielding a composite estimate $T_c^w \approx 102\ \text{MeV}$ consistent with the MIT bag model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile deviations from standard Boltzmann-Gibbs thermodynamics—arising from intrinsic fluctuations, memory effects, and long-range color interactions in heavy-ion collisions—with robust determination of the QCD critical temperature $T_c$, by employing nonextensive (Tsallis) statistics near equilibrium.

Method: Derives a pressure-based equation of state from Tsallis entropy for several hadron models (MIT bag, Dual QCD, Fuzzy bag), analyzes root structure of the resulting $T$-dependent pressure equation as a function of the nonextensive parameter $q$, and performs weighted comparison constrained by MIT bag model at $q=1$ to extract a composite $T_c$.

Result: For $q=1$: analytic solutions exist (seventh-order term vanishes); $T_c$ ranges are $102.3{-}106.1\ \text{MeV}$ (MIT), $86.5{-}87.8\ \text{MeV}$ (Dual), $113.1{-}121.1\ \text{MeV}$ (Fuzzy). At $q=0$: $T_c \sim 55\ \text{MeV}$ (liquid-gas transition). A weighted estimate yields $T_c^w \approx 102\ \text{MeV}$. The physically viable $q$-range is $0.99 \leq q \leq 1.002$.

Conclusion: Nonextensivity significantly modulates $T_c$, but only near $q=1$ does the framework yield a consistent, model-convergent critical temperature (~102 MeV), supporting Tsallis statistics as a physically meaningful extension of equilibrium thermodynamics for QCD matter near $T_c$.

Abstract: The determination of the critical temperature $T_c$ in strongly interacting matter is central to understanding the transition between confined hadronic matter and deconfined quark-gluon degrees of freedom. In high energy nuclear collisions, intrinsic fluctuations, memory effects, and long-range color interactions can drive the fireball evolution away from purely Markovian thermalization, making nonextensive statistical descriptions relevant near equilibrium. We investigate the critical temperature of nonextensive hadron models using a pressure-based formulation derived from Tsallis entropy. Here we show that the qualitative structure of the pressure equation is controlled by both the nonextensive parameter $q$ and the model-dependent bag contribution $B^{\text{model}}$. For $q=0$, the pressure equations are monotonic and admit a unique positive root, whereas for $q=1$ the seventh-order term vanishes and analytic solutions can be obtained. For $q>1$, the negative $T^7$ contribution modifies the pressure behavior and can generate multiple positive roots at finite volume. %Cosmological bounds require $q \approx 1$, meaning $q \simeq 1.001$ in proton-proton phenomenology is a system-dependent fit parameter. For $q\geq1$, $T^{\text{model}}_c$ is $\left(102.3\leq T_c^{\text{MIT}}\leq106.1,\ 86.5\leq T_c^{\text{Dual}}\leq 87.8,\ \text{and} \ 113.1\leq T_c^{\text{Fuzzy}}\leq 121.1\right)$ MeV. At $q=0$, $T^{\text{model}}_c \sim 55$ MeV marks the nuclear liquid-gas transition. A weighted comparison constrained by the MIT bag model at $q=1$ gives $(T_c^{\text{w}}\approx102)\ \text{MeV}$, with Fuzzy and Dual QCD terms entering as model-propagation corrections. The interval $(0.99\leq q\leq 1.002)$ therefore provides the most reliable domain for a common physical interpretation and a composite estimate of the critical temperature.

</details>


### [17] [Collider-observable curvaton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10114)
*David Cyncynates,Takuya Okawa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a Higgs-portal curvaton model, computing quantum and thermal corrections to its potential and showing they remain controlled; it identifies viable parameter space where the curvaton mass and Higgs-portal coupling are comparable—within reach of upcoming light scalar searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize the curvaton mechanism in a technically natural way via superrenormalizable Higgs-portal interactions, while ensuring radiative stability of the curvaton potential across cosmological evolution.

Method: Computation of quantum (loop) and finite-temperature corrections to the curvaton effective potential; analysis of curvaton decay dynamics—including off-shell dissipation in the SM plasma—and mapping of viable parameter space consistent with observational constraints.

Result: Quantum and thermal corrections remain perturbatively controlled; for high-scale inflation, curvaton decay proceeds dominantly via off-shell SM processes; broad viable parameter space exists, with a phenomenologically accessible region where curvaton mass ~ Higgs-portal coupling scale.

Conclusion: The Higgs-portal curvaton scenario is radiatively stable and observationally testable—particularly through upcoming experiments targeting light Higgs-mixed scalars.

Abstract: We study a technically natural realization of the curvaton mechanism, in which decay is mediated through the superrenormalizable interaction with the Higgs. We compute the quantum and thermal corrections to the curvaton potential and show that they remain under control throughout its cosmological evolution. For high inflationary scales, the curvaton dissipates through off-shell processes in the Standard Model plasma. The viable parameter space is broad, and the region in which the curvaton mass and dimensionful Higgs-portal coupling are closest in scale lies within the discovery reach of proposed searches for light Higgs-mixed scalars.

</details>


### [18] [Real Intermediate States in Dark Matter Freeze-In from First Principles: Single Mediator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10204)
*Jannis Brirup,Adrian Finke,Michael Klasen,Luca Paolo Wiggering*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the double-counting problem of real intermediate states (RIS) in s-channel freeze-in dark matter production with unstable mediators, by developing a first-principles Closed-Time-Path (CTP) quantum field theoretic framework that avoids non-unitary overcounting and incorporates full thermal corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the double-counting issue between decay and annihilation terms in semi-classical Boltzmann treatments of s-channel freeze-in with finite-width unstable mediators — a problem unaddressed from first principles.

Method: Derives a CTP-based non-equilibrium QFT formalism for s-channel freeze-in; computes exact mediator propagators via 1PI self-energy resummation including full mass/momentum dependence (beyond Hard Thermal Loop approximation); derives separate evolution equations for scalar/vector mediators in thermal equilibrium.

Result: CTP framework eliminates RIS overcounting intrinsically and consistently includes thermal corrections; numerical comparison with common RIS-subtraction schemes shows phenomenologically significant deviations in predicted relic densities.

Conclusion: A first-principles CTP treatment is necessary and superior for accurate s-channel freeze-in predictions, especially when thermal effects and mediator instability are simultaneously relevant.

Abstract: We investigate the treatment of real intermediate states (RIS) in freeze-in production of $s$-channel Dark Matter (DM) with unstable mediators, as such models feature a double-counting issue when decay and annihilation collision terms are simply added within the semi-classical Boltzmann approach and a finite width is used for the regularization of the resonance. While several ad-hoc RIS-subtraction schemes are available to mitigate this problem, a first-principles treatment in this context is so far missing. We therefore start from the Closed-Time-Path (CTP) formulation of non-equilibrium Quantum Field Theory and present a CTP-based description of $s$-channel freeze-in pair production of a single scalar, fermion or vector DM species. In addition to being intrinsically free of non-unitary overcounting, the CTP ansatz also has the advantage of systematically capturing thermal corrections known to be important for DM freeze-in. Separate evolution equations assuming a single scalar or vector mediator in thermal equilibrium with the surrounding bath are presented. The exact propagators of the unstable mediators are approximated by resumming one particle irreducible (1PI) self-energy diagrams generated by bath particles and are evaluated with their full mass and momentum dependence in contrast to the commonly adopted Hard Thermal Loop limit. The CTP relic densities are compared numerically with those obtained using the most common RIS-subtraction schemes for two example $s$-channel freeze-in models, demonstrating the phenomenological relevance of a consistent RIS treatment.

</details>


### [19] [$B\to$ invisible decays as probes of light new physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10218)
*Martín Novoa-Brunet*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Belle II excess in B+→K+Emiss decays, favoring a two-body contribution near 2.1 GeV, with scalar/fermion pairs (~0.4–0.6 GeV) as alternatives; it proposes an aligned Higgsed U(1)' model with a vector-like top partner explaining the signal via a Z′, requiring a light dark fermion to satisfy dimuon constraints and simultaneously serve as thermal relic dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explain the Belle II excess in B+ → K+ Emiss using new physics beyond the Standard Model, distinguishing between on-shell production of light states versus heavy mediators, while satisfying experimental constraints (e.g., dimuon searches, Bs invisible decays).

Method: Statistical fits to the missing-mass distribution; analysis of B → K* Emiss rate and longitudinal K* polarization; constraints from Bs → invisible; construction and phenomenological evaluation of an aligned Higgsed U(1)′ model featuring a vector-like top partner and light dark fermion; calculation of Z′ mixing, decay widths, and thermal relic abundance via resonant annihilation.

Result: A ~2.1 GeV vector resonance is favored by missing-mass fits; scalar/fermion pair interpretations remain viable at ~0.4–0.6 GeV; the U(1)′ model with vector-like top partner generates the required Z′ signal but necessitates electroweak-loop-induced mixing, implying visible Z′ → μ⁺μ⁻ decays that must be suppressed by an additional invisible decay channel—provided by a light dark fermion which also yields correct thermal dark matter relic density via s-channel resonance near mZ′/2.

Conclusion: The Belle II B+→K+Emiss anomaly can be consistently explained by a light Z′ boson from an aligned Higgsed U(1)′ extension, provided a light dark fermion exists both to evade LHCb/CMS dimuon limits and to constitute thermal dark matter; complementary measurements (B→K*Emiss, Bs→invisible) are critical to distinguish models.

Abstract: Rare $B$ decays with missing energy probe heavy particles contributing to $b\to s\nu\bar\nu$ and new light states produced on shell. We discuss both possibilities in light of the Belle II excess in $B^+\to K^+\Emiss$. Fits to the missing-mass distribution favour a two-body contribution near $2.1\,\GeV$, while scalar and fermion pairs of masses around $0.4$-$0.6\,\GeV$ remain viable alternatives. Extracting their couplings would profit from additional measurements, including the $B\to K^*\Emiss$ rate and longitudinal $K^*$ polarization, together with invisible $B_s$ decays which constrain scalar-pair interactions. We discuss how an aligned Higgsed $U(1)'$ model can realize the single-vector explanation through a vector-like top partner. Loop-induced electroweak mixing makes visible $Z'$ decays unavoidable, requiring an additional invisible channel to evade dimuon searches. We show that a light dark fermion can provide this channel and also account for the observed dark matter abundance through resonant thermal annihilation.

</details>


### [20] [Constraints on quirks from a Geant4-based reinterpretation of the CMS search for heavy stable charged particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10273)
*Tamas Almos Vami*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets CMS heavy stable charged particle (HSCP) search data to constrain quirk models—exotic heavy fermions bound by a low-scale confining force—finding mass exclusions up to 990 GeV depending on the confining scale Λ and quirk representation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quirk scenarios—beyond-standard-model heavy fermions with electroweak charges and low-scale confinement—that evade conventional HSCP searches due to their unique ionization and trajectory behavior in detectors.

Method: Reinterpretation of CMS 13 TeV HSCP search (101 fb⁻¹) using Geant4-simulated quirk propagation in a parametric CMS-like detector; emulation of event selection and combination with public likelihoods; validation against heavy charged lepton limits (11% agreement).

Result: For SU(2)-doublet quirks: 870–970 GeV excluded at Λ ≲ 70 eV (independent HSCP regime); 280–320 GeV at Λ ≳ 10 keV (doubly ionizing regime); no sensitivity in intermediate Λ (~70 eV–10 keV) where quirks turn around. For SU(2)-singlet (FASER benchmark, two infracolors): 990 GeV excluded below 100 eV; 300 GeV above 10 keV—extending beyond FASER’s 175 GeV limit.

Conclusion: CMS HSCP search provides complementary and often stronger constraints on quirks than dedicated forward experiments like FASER, especially at very low Λ (<100 eV), highlighting the importance of reinterpretation studies for exotic confined dynamics.

Abstract: Quirks are new heavy fermions with electroweak charges that are also bound by a confining force whose scale $\Lambda$ is far below their mass. A produced pair therefore stays connected by a macroscopic string. We reinterpret the CMS search for heavy stable charged particles in 101 fb$^{-1}$ of 13 TeV proton-proton collisions for Drell-Yan produced quirks. The quirk dynamics is simulated with Geant4 in a parametric CMS-like detector, and the event selection is emulated and combined with the public likelihoods. The emulation reproduces the published limits on a heavy charged lepton within 11%. For an $SU(2)$-doublet quirk with one (two) infracolors, masses up to 870 (970) GeV are excluded for $\Lambda\lesssim70$ eV, where the quirks behave as independent heavy stable charged particles. For $\Lambda\gtrsim10$ keV, where the pair moves as one doubly ionizing object, the limit is 280 (320) GeV. In between, the quirks turn around inside the detector and the search has no sensitivity. For the $SU(2)$-singlet benchmark of the FASER search, with two infracolors, the limits reach 990 GeV below 100 eV, where FASER has no sensitivity, and 300 GeV above 10 keV, beyond the FASER exclusion of 175 GeV.

</details>


### [21] [A comparative analysis of semileptonic $B_{(s)}\to D_{(s)}$ and $B_c\to(η_c,B,B_s)$ decays in the light-front quark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10407)
*Ho-Meoyng Choi,Bhoomika Pandya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a global analysis of semileptonic B-meson decays (B_(s)→D_(s), B_c→η_c/B/B_s) using five light-front quark models with consistent Bakamjian–Thomas current prescription and fixed spectroscopy; it computes form factors via analytic continuation in complex transverse momentum space—avoiding q² parametrization—and reports model-averaged predictions for R(D) and R(D_s) with uncertainty bands.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess systematic uncertainties in QCD-motivated potential-model predictions for B→D(D_s) semileptonic transitions—particularly for lepton-flavor universality ratios R(D) and R(D_s)—by isolating effects of confinement, radial mixing, and hyperfine interactions while maintaining consistent current structure and spectroscopic inputs.

Method: Five light-front quark models based on the Bakamjian–Thomas framework are compared; weak currents use identical on-shell constituent spinor kinematics and physical meson masses; form factors are obtained via direct analytic continuation of spacelike overlap integrals in the q⁺=0 frame using a mass-weighted contour in complex transverse momentum space; no q² parametrization is employed.

Result: All models yield consistent f₋(q²) extractions from (+,⊥) and (+,−) current components; form factors and differential decay rates are reported as equal-weight averages with pointwise min–max bands; light-lepton-averaged R(D) = 0.282–0.301 and R(D_s) = 0.287–0.303.

Conclusion: The analysis demonstrates robustness of light-front quark model predictions for B→D(D_s) transitions under variations in confinement and spin-dependent dynamics; the narrow spread in R(D) and R(D_s) suggests controlled theoretical uncertainty, supporting further scrutiny of experimental tensions with SM expectations.

Abstract: We present a global analysis of the semileptonic transitions $B_{(s)}\to D_{(s)}$ and $B_c\to(\eta_c,B,B_s)$ by comparing five potential-model implementations of the QCD-motivated light-front quark model. We use the same fully Bakamjian--Thomas-based prescription for the weak current in all five models and keep their respective spectroscopic parameters fixed. We examine how confinement, radial mixing, and the treatment of hyperfine interactions jointly affect the form factors, differential decay rates, branching fractions, and lepton-flavor ratios. The internal Lorentz prefactors use the same free quark--antiquark kinematics as the on-shell constituent spinors in the weak-current kernels, while the physical meson masses determine the external kinematics. For each model and decay channel, the two independent extractions of $f_-(q^2)$ using the $(+,\perp)$ and $(+,-)$ current-component combinations agree within numerical accuracy. We perform a direct analytic continuation of the spacelike overlap integrals in the $q^+=0$ frame using a mass-weighted contour in complex transverse momentum space. This yields the form factors throughout the physical timelike region, including zero recoil, without parametrizing their $q^2$ dependence. Form factors and differential decay rates are presented as equal-weight model averages with pointwise minimum--maximum bands. The light-lepton-averaged ratios range from $0.282$ to $0.301$ for $R(D)$ and from $0.287$ to $0.303$ for $R(D_s)$.

</details>


### [22] [Search sensitivity for type-III seesaw heavy leptons in simulated \texorpdfstring{$pp$}{pp} collisions at \texorpdfstring{$\sqrt{s}=13.6$}{sqrt(s)=13.6} TeV at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10753)
*A.M.M. Elsayed,Yusheng Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper searches for pair-produced TeV-scale heavy leptons predicted by the type-III seesaw mechanism using 500 fb⁻¹ of simulated 13.6 TeV pp collision data, Delphes-based ATLAS detector simulation, and multivariate analysis across nine lepton/jet channels; it sets a 95% CL mass exclusion limit of ~1.6 TeV under flavor-democratic mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the type-III seesaw mechanism—a theoretical extension of the Standard Model that explains neutrino masses—by searching for its predicted TeV-scale heavy leptons (triplet fermions) via pair production at the LHC.

Method: Simulated signal and SM background events corresponding to 500 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13.6 TeV; Delphes for fast ATLAS detector simulation; event categorization into nine mutually exclusive channels based on final-state lepton multiplicity and large-R jets; multivariate analysis for signal-background discrimination.

Result: Under flavor-democratic mixing (ℬₑ = ℬ_μ = ℬ_τ = 1/3), the expected 95% confidence level upper exclusion limit on the heavy lepton mass is approximately 1.6 TeV.

Conclusion: The analysis excludes type-III seesaw heavy leptons with masses below ~1.6 TeV at 95% CL in the flavor-democratic scenario, constraining the parameter space of this neutrino mass generation mechanism.

Abstract: A search for the pair production of TeV-scale heavy leptons as predicted by the type-III seesaw mechanism is presented. The analysis is based on a simulated signal and Standard Model background data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $500~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV. The ATLAS detector response is modeled with Delphes. Events are categorized based on the number of final state leptons and large-$R$ jets into nine mutually exclusive search channels. Multivariate analysis techniques are employed to separate the signal from the Standard Model backgrounds. Under the flavor-democratic mixing scenario ($\mathcal{B}_e=\mathcal{B}_\mu=\mathcal{B}_\tau=1/3$), the expected 95\% confidence level upper exclusion limit on the heavy lepton mass is approximately $1.6$ TeV.

</details>


### [23] [Sensitivity of jet and dijet observables to medium effects in pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV using X-SCAPE/JETSCAPE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11012)
*Lakshitha Fernando,Claude A. Pruneau,Joern Putschke,Abhijit Majumder,Victor Gonzalez,Oveis Sheibani,Akash Raj,Dana Abdallah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates jet quenching signatures in high-multiplicity pp collisions at 13 TeV using JETSCAPE/X-SCAPE, comparing simplified (Bjorken brick) and realistic (event-by-event hydro + fluctuations) medium scenarios against vacuum baselines; dijet invariant mass shows the clearest medium response in brick simulations, while realistic pp events show only subtle, localized deviations—especially in dijet asymmetry and groomed observables—highlighting limited sensitivity of current observables to quenching in small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish whether jet quenching—a hallmark of QGP formation—occurs in small collision systems like high-multiplicity pp collisions, where expected modifications are tiny and easily masked by non-medium effects (e.g., MPI, event selection, multijet backgrounds).

Method: Uses JETSCAPE/X-SCAPE framework to simulate: (i) a simplified Bjorken-expanding 'brick' medium with varying lengths and transport coefficients (q̂), versus vacuum; (ii) realistic pp events with fluctuating initial conditions and event-by-event hydrodynamic evolution, selecting top-20% high-activity events, comparing medium vs. vacuum samples; reconstructs full anti-kT jets (R=0.2, 0.4); analyzes dijet invariant mass, pT of leading/subleading jets, folded Δφ, dijet asymmetry, Soft-Drop groomed radius, and groomed mass.

Result: In brick simulations: dijet mass spectrum shows strongest, ordered deviation from vacuum—increasing with brick length and q̂, more pronounced for R=0.2; jet pT and dijet asymmetry changes are smaller; no significant azimuthal broadening observed. In realistic pp events: ungroomed medium/vacuum ratios ≈ 1, with only localized kinematic differences and modest increase in mean dijet asymmetry; groomed radius/mass ratios show localized but non-systematic deviations—no persistent angular narrowing or mass redistribution.

Conclusion: Dijet invariant mass is the most sensitive observable to medium-induced modifications in current pp jet quenching studies; however, even in high-activity pp events, observable medium effects remain subtle and kinematically localized, indicating low sensitivity of standard and groomed jet/dijet observables to quenching in small systems under present modeling assumptions.

Abstract: Establishing jet quenching in small collision systems, such as high-multiplicity proton-proton collisions at the LHC, is challenging because the expected modifications are small and can be obscured by event-selection effects, multiparton interactions, and multijet production. We examine the response of several jet and dijet observables to medium-induced changes in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV using the JETSCAPE/X-SCAPE framework. First, a simplified Bjorken-expanding brick medium is compared with a vacuum reference for several brick lengths and strengths of transport coefficients. Second, separately generated realistic vacuum and medium samples with fluctuating initial conditions and event-by-event hydrodynamic evolution are compared after selecting the highest-activity 20% of events. Full anti-$k_T$ jets are reconstructed with $R=0.2$ and $R=0.4$. The observables are dijet invariant mass, leading- and subleading-jet transverse momentum, folded azimuthal separation, dijet asymmetry, Soft-Drop groomed jet radius, and groomed mass. In the brick calculation, the dijet-mass spectrum shows the clearest ordered response: departures from the vacuum reference generally increase with brick length and strength of transport coefficient and are more visible for the narrower jet radius, while the jet-$p_T$ and dijet-asymmetry changes are smaller and no clear azimuthal broadening is resolved. In the selected realistic events, the ungroomed medium-to-vacuum ratios remain close to unity but contain localized kinematic differences, together with a modest increase of the mean dijet asymmetry. The groomed-radius and groomed-mass ratios show localized differences but do not exhibit a persistent angular narrowing or mass redistribution. These findings quantify the sensitivity of the studied observables to medium-induced jet modifications in the present pp calculations.

</details>


### [24] [Quark- and hadronic-level CPT violation phenomenology of neutral-mesons at at $d=5$ of the SME](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11047)
*Agnes Roberts*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between hadron-level scalar effective field theory and quark-level d=5 nonminimal SME for Lorentz/CPT violation in neutral-meson oscillations, linking the quark-level a^(5) term to mesonic CPTV observables, with implications for kaon experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between hadron-level effective descriptions and quark-level Standard Model Extension (SME) formalisms for Lorentz and CPT violation in neutral-meson systems, ensuring consistency with fundamental symmetries (C, P, T) and spontaneously broken chiral symmetry of QCD.

Method: First-order perturbative matching in CPT violation between the hadron-level scalar effective field theory and the quark-level d=5 nonminimal SME; symmetry constraints (C, P, T, chiral symmetry) are imposed to identify the correspondence of the a^(5) coefficient.

Result: A concrete mapping is derived between the quark-level SME coefficient a^(5) and the phenomenological CPT-violating parameter in neutral-meson oscillation observables; a general expression applicable to kaon systems is provided.

Conclusion: The two formalisms are consistently related at leading order in CPTV, enabling unified interpretation of experimental bounds and facilitating targeted searches—particularly in kaon oscillations—for Lorentz- and CPT-violating effects originating from dimension-5 operators.

Abstract: This article summarizes an investigation of the connection between the hadron-level scalar effective field theory treatment of Lorentz symmetry violation in neutral-meson oscillations and the corresponding quark level $d=5$ nonminimal SME approach. The correspondence between the quark-level $a^{(5)}$ term is identified to first order in CPT violation (CPTV) based on the expectation that C, P, T symmetries and the spontaneously broken chiral symmetry of QCD should hold for both formalism. These can be related to the phenomenological parameter describing possible CPTV in the meson oscillations. A general result facilitating searches for kaons is given.

</details>


### [25] [Study of the weak radiative decay $B_c^+\to B_u^{\ast+}γ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11095)
*Jia Zhao,Tianhong Wang,Xi-Yu Ni,Zhi-Hui Wang,Guo-Li Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the rare radiative decay $B_c \to B_u^*\gamma$ in the Standard Model using the instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter approach, incorporating short-distance penguin, weak annihilation, and long-distance vector meson dominance contributions; it finds the LD contribution highly sensitive to the $\rho$-meson width, and the SD contribution dominates over annihilation, making this channel valuable for probing charm-sector FCNCs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe flavor-changing neutral current (FCNC) processes involving charm quarks via the rare radiative decay $B_c \to B_u^*\gamma$, which is forbidden at tree level and thus sensitive to loop-level SM dynamics and potential new physics.

Method: Instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter (BS) approach; includes short-distance $c \to u\gamma$ penguin, weak annihilation, and long-distance vector meson dominance (VMD) contributions; two treatments of $\rho$-meson width ($\Gamma_\rho = 0$ vs. experimental value) for LD calculation.

Result: LD branching ratios: $5.0\times10^{-9}$ ($\Gamma_\rho=0$) and $7.3\times10^{-8}$ ($\Gamma_\rho=\text{exp}$); SD contribution comparable to LD but one order larger than annihilation; overall sensitivity to $\Gamma_\rho$ highlights importance of resonance treatment in VMD.

Conclusion: The $B_c \to B_u^*\gamma$ decay is a promising channel for studying charm-quark FCNCs within and beyond the Standard Model; precise theoretical treatment of long-distance effects—especially resonance widths—is crucial.

Abstract: We investigate the rare radiative decay $B_c \to B_u^\ast\gamma$ within the Standard Model by using the instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter (BS) approach. The short distance (SD) $c \to u\gamma$ penguin, weak annihilation, and long distance (LD) vector meson dominance (VMD) contributions are all included. In the calculation of LD contribution, we use two scenarios, namely the decay width of $\rho$ being set to zero or kept as the experimental value, which gives $\mathrm{Br}_{\rm LD}=5.0\times10^{-9}$ and $7.3\times10^{-8}$, respectively, indicating an important role of $\Gamma_\rho$ in the calculation. The SD contribution is comparable in magnitude to the LD contributions, but one order larger than the annihilation contribution.
