This shows the $B_c \to B_u^\ast\gamma$ channel can provide an important way to study the flavor changing neutral current processes of charm quark.

</details>


### [26] [Holographic dissociation of tetraquark and hybrid meson in the spinning plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11202)
*Zhenqiang Ren,Xiang Gao,Zhou-Run Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a holographic framework based on a spinning Myers-Perry black hole to study the dissociation of exotic hadrons $Z_c$ (tetraquark) and $\pi_1$ (hybrid meson) in a quark-gluon plasma; spectral functions in longitudinal/transverse channels show distinct, state-dependent melting patterns—2S states dissociate before 1S, and transverse channel effects are stronger—with no need for explicit binding energy calculations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified holographic description of exotic hadron dissociation ($Z_c$ tetraquark and $\pi_1$ hybrid meson) in a spinning quark-gluon plasma, bypassing the computational complexity of dynamical binding energy calculations while capturing channel- and state-dependent thermal behavior.

Method: Holographic QCD approach using a planar-limit spinning Myers-Perry black hole background; non-quadratic dilaton profile calibrated to $Z_c$ and $\pi_1$ mass spectra; computation of spectral functions in longitudinal and transverse vector channels as functions of temperature and boost parameter.

Result: Both boost and temperature suppress peak height and broaden width of spectral functions, more strongly in the transverse channel; 2S excited states dissociate at lower temperatures than 1S ground states; $Z_c$ and $\pi_1$ exhibit quantitatively distinct dissociation patterns despite shared holographic setup.

Conclusion: A single consistent holographic framework can differentiate thermal dissociation behaviors of structurally distinct exotic hadrons, enabling comparative predictions without first-principles binding energy computations—highlighting the utility of spectral function analysis in strongly coupled QGP environments.

Abstract: We explore the dissociation of the tetraquark \(Z_c\) and the hybrid meson \(\pi_1\) in a spinning quark-gluon plasma described by the planar limit of a spinning Myers-Perry black hole. Both states are treated within one holographic framework, using a non-quadratic dilaton profile whose parameters are fixed by the \(Z_c\) and \(\pi_1\) mass spectra. Spectral functions are computed in the longitudinal and transverse channels. The boost parameter and temperature both lower the peak height and broaden the width, and the effect is stronger in the transverse channel. In both channels, the heavier 2S states melt before the 1S states. Furthermore, the two exotic channels display distinct dissociation behaviors within the same effective framework. This provides a unified holographic approach to compare how different exotic states behavior, without requiring a dynamical calculation of their binding energies.

</details>


### [27] [Precision Fragmentation Functions from Modern COMPASS SIDIS Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11237)
*Maryam Soleymaninia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents three global determinations of collinear fragmentation functions (FFs) for pions, kaons, and unidentified hadrons, using combined $e^+e^-$ and SIDIS data—including new COMPASS results—and neural-network parametrizations with NLO/NNLO QCD setups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision and flavor separation in collinear fragmentation functions by incorporating newly available high-statistics SIDIS data (2025 COMPASS proton-target and revised 2026 isoscalar-target measurements) alongside $e^+e^-$ annihilation data, addressing current limitations in gluon FF constraints and flavor discrimination.

Method: Global QCD analysis using flexible neural-network parametrizations, Monte Carlo replica sampling, and both NLO and NNLO perturbative calculations; data inputs include single-inclusive $e^+e^-$ annihilation and charge-separated SIDIS off proton and isoscalar targets.

Result: Improved determination of quark-to-hadron FFs—especially for u, d, s quarks—with enhanced flavor sensitivity from proton and isoscalar SIDIS; gluon FFs remain poorly constrained; NNLO corrections yield measurable shifts in central values and uncertainties.

Conclusion: The HAPS-FF1.0 series provides state-of-the-art, publicly available FF sets with quantified uncertainties; future electron–ion collider (EIC) data will be critical to constrain gluon fragmentation and reduce hadronization model dependence.

Abstract: We present a comparative account of three global determinations of collinear fragmentation functions (FFs) for charged pions, charged kaons, and unidentified charged hadrons. The HAPS-PiFF1.0, HAPS-KaFF1.0, and HAPS-hFF1.0 analyses combine single-inclusive $e^+e^-$ annihilation with charge-separated semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering (SIDIS), including the 2025 COMPASS proton-target measurements and the revised 2026 isoscalar-target results. Flexible neural-network parametrizations, Monte Carlo replicas, and NLO/NNLO perturbative setups are employed. The proton and isoscalar samples sharpen the flavor sensitivity of SIDIS, while gluon fragmentation remains comparatively weakly constrained. We discuss the effects of perturbative accuracy, the principal limitations, and prospects for future measurements.

</details>


### [28] [An $SU(5)$ GUT with non-holomorphic modular $S'_4$ symmetry: fermion masses, mixing and leptogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11241)
*Xianshuo Zhang,Yakefu Reyimuaji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a non-supersymmetric SU(5) grand unified theory with S'_4 modular flavor symmetry and U(1)_PQ symmetry, using polyharmonic Maass forms for Yukawa couplings; it successfully fits all fermion masses/mixings and neutrino oscillation data (NO/IO), predicts unmeasured neutrino parameters, and realizes thermal leptogenesis yielding the observed baryon asymmetry via a single right-handed neutrino mass scale parameter b.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To build a predictive, non-SUSY Grand Unified Theory that simultaneously explains fermion flavor structure (charged fermions and neutrinos) and the baryon asymmetry of the universe via leptogenesis, while avoiding fine-tuning through modular flavor symmetry and Peccei-Quinn constraints.

Method: Constructs an SU(5) GUT with non-holomorphic S'_4 modular symmetry and global U(1)_PQ symmetry; derives Yukawa couplings from polyharmonic Maass forms; fits experimental data (masses, mixings, oscillation parameters) via chi-square minimization; computes neutrino parameters and effective Majorana mass m_bb; solves density matrix equations for thermal leptogenesis; analyzes correlations between high-scale parameter b and low-energy observables.

Result: Achieves excellent global fit to charged-fermion and neutrino data (|Pull| < 0.8 for all observables) for both NO and IO; predicts specific values for absolute neutrino masses, δ_CP, Majorana phases, and m_bb; reproduces observed baryon asymmetry η_B ≈ 6.12×10⁻¹⁰ via thermal leptogenesis controlled solely by RH neutrino mass scale parameter b; identifies testable correlations between b and low-energy neutrino observables.

Conclusion: The model demonstrates that non-holomorphic modular symmetry combined with PQ symmetry in a non-SUSY SU(5) framework can unify flavor physics and cosmology—predicting both high-precision neutrino phenomenology and the observed baryon asymmetry from a single fundamental scale, thereby establishing a concrete link between fermion flavor and early-Universe dynamics.

Abstract: A non-supersymmetric $SU(5)$ grand unified model is constructed based on the non-holomorphic modular $S'_4$ flavor symmetry, supplemented by a global Peccei--Quinn $U(1)_{PQ}$ symmetry that constrains the Yukawa couplings. Constructed from polyharmonic Maaß forms, these couplings allow the model to reproduce the observed charged-fermion masses and mixing as well as the neutrino oscillation parameters for both normal ordering (NO) and inverted ordering (IO), with all Pull values satisfying $|\mathcal{P}| < 0.8$. The model further yields specific predictions for yet-unmeasured parameters, including the absolute neutrino masses, the Dirac CP phase, the Majorana phases, and the effective Majorana mass $m_{\beta\beta}$. We then study thermal leptogenesis by solving the density matrix equations. For a given benchmark point, the final baryon asymmetry depends solely on the single free parameter $b$ that sets the right-handed neutrino mass scale, and an appropriate choice of this parameter reproduces the observed value $\eta_B \simeq 6.12 \times 10^{-10}$. Finally, the correlation between this scale parameter and specific low-energy observables provides a potential bridge between the fermion flavor structure and early-Universe cosmology.

</details>


### [29] [Direct Light-Cone Reconstruction of the $B$-Meson Shape Function in HQET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11246)
*Ao-Sheng Xiong,Shu-Man Hu,Ji Xu,Fu-Sheng Yu,Shuai Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quasi-shape function for the B-meson directly within HQET and computes its one-loop matching to the light-cone shape function, enabling a direct lattice HQET determination without intermediate full-QCD matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable robust lattice QCD calculations of the B-meson shape function when the heavy quark mass is comparable to or exceeds the inverse lattice spacing, leveraging HQET as a natural Wilsonian effective theory framework.

Method: Constructs a HQET-based quasi-shape function; computes its one-loop matching to the light-cone shape function; derives renormalization kernels and matching coefficients in both \overline{MS} and RI/MOM schemes, with light-cone shape function retained in \overline{MS}; employs large-v^z expansion at leading power; ensures infrared-regulator independence.

Result: Complete one-loop renormalization kernels and matching coefficients for the HQET quasi-shape function onto the light-cone shape function in \overline{MS} and RI/MOM schemes; results are leading-power in large-v^z and infrared-regulator independent.

Conclusion: The derived perturbative matching provides the essential ingredients for a direct lattice HQET extraction of the B-meson shape function, circumventing complications from intermediate full-QCD light-cone distribution matching.

Abstract: The $B$-meson shape function encodes the nonperturbative residual-momentum dynamics of the heavy quark within heavy quark effective theory (HQET). From a Wilsonian perspective, HQET provides a natural framework for lattice calculations when the heavy quark mass is comparable to or exceeds the inverse lattice spacing. Motivated by this observation, we introduce a $B$-meson quasi-shape function directly in HQET and calculate its one-loop matching to the light-cone shape function, avoiding an intermediate matching step involving a full-QCD light-cone distribution. We determine the one-loop renormalization kernels and matching coefficients in both the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ and regularization-independent momentum-subtraction (RI/MOM) schemes for the quasi-shape function, while keeping the light-cone shape function in the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ scheme. The matching coefficients are obtained at leading power in the large-$v^z$ expansion and are independent of the infrared regulator. Our results provide the perturbative ingredients for a direct lattice HQET determination of the $B$-meson shape function.

</details>


### [30] [Probing the Supersymmetric Grand Unified Theories with Anomaly Mediation at the Future Proton-Proton Colliders and Hyper-Kamiokande Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11254)
*Waqas Ahmed,Tianjun Li,Shabbar Raza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores SUSY GUTs with minimal AMSB under the 'grand desert' hypothesis, deriving upper mass bounds for gluinos (8 TeV) and light squarks (6 TeV) at high-scale GUTs (MGUT ≥ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV), making them accessible at 100 TeV colliders (FCC-hh/SppC); it also links lower MGUT to testable dimension-six proton decay lifetimes (< ~5×10³⁴ yr) at Hyper-Kamiokande, and provides benchmark points for both regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically probe SUSY GUTs in the grand desert scenario using next-generation experiments—100 TeV hadron colliders (FCC-hh, SppC) and Hyper-Kamiokande—by linking GUT scale, sparticle spectra, and proton decay observables within minimal AMSB.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of renormalization group evolution (RGE) of MSSM parameters under minimal AMSB; derivation of sparticle mass bounds from gauge coupling unification and vacuum stability constraints; computation of dimension-six proton decay lifetime via heavy gauge boson exchange; identification of benchmark points satisfying unification and experimental reach criteria.

Result: For MGUT ≥ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV: gluino mass ≤ 8 TeV, first-two-gen squark mass ≤ 6 TeV — detectable at FCC-hh/SppC; full colored sparticle, slepton, pseudoscalar Higgs, and electroweakino spectra presented; concrete search strategies for gluino and winos proposed. For MGUT ≤ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV: dimension-six proton decay lifetime τp ≤ ~5×10³⁴ yr — within Hyper-Kamiokande sensitivity. Two representative benchmark points provided.

Conclusion: Minimal AMSB SUSY GUTs in the grand desert are highly testable: high MGUT enables collider discovery of gluinos and squarks, while low MGUT yields observable proton decay; thus, synergistic collider and proton-decay experiments can decisively constrain or discover this framework.

Abstract: With the grand desert hypothesis, we investigate the Supersymmetric (SUSY) Grand Unified Theories (GUTs) with the minimal anomaly mediated supersymmetry breaking (AMSB) as a continuation of our program of studying SUSY GUTs at the future 100 TeV proton-proton Collider such as $\rm FCC_{hh}$ and SppC as well as proton-decay experiment such as Hyper-Kamiokande experiment. We show that for the GUT scale $M_{GUT} \ge 0.84 \times 10^{16}$ GeV, the upper mass bounds on the gluino and first two generation squarks are 8 TeV and 6 TeV, respectively. Therefore, the gluino and first two generation squarks can be probed at the $\rm FCC_{hh}$ and SppC. In addition, we discuss the mass spectra for the colored sparticles, sleptons, pseudo-scalar Higgs and electroweakinos. We also shed light on the possible search strategies of gluino and winos at the future 100 TeV colliders. Moreover, for $\rm M_{\rm GUT} \le 0.84 \times 10^{16}$ GeV, the proton lifetime for dimension-six proton decay via heavy gauge boson exchange is less than or around $5 \times 10^{34}$ years, and thus we can probe it at the Hyper-Kamiokande experiment. To be concrete, we present a couple of benchmark points representing the above two mentioned GUT conditions on $\rm M_{\rm GUT}$.

</details>


### [31] [Rapid, High-Precision Numerical Evaluation of Multiloop Feynman Diagrams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11292)
*Richard Easther*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Sinc function approximation method to numerically evaluate multiloop Feynman diagrams, demonstrating superior speed and precision over Monte Carlo for electron g-2 calculations beyond ~1% accuracy—e.g., six-digit 3-loop results in minutes vs. years for Monte Carlo—and shows feasibility for 5-loop integrals and broader applicability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the computational inefficiency of Monte Carlo methods for high-precision evaluation of multiloop Feynman diagrams, especially in quantum electrodynamics (QED) g-2 calculations where convergence is slow and resource-intensive.

Method: Sinc function approximations are applied to transform multidimensional Feynman parameter integrals into rapidly convergent sums; numerical evaluation is performed on electron g-2 diagrams up to five loops, with benchmarking against Monte Carlo integration.

Result: Sinc-based evaluation matches Monte Carlo speed at ~1% precision for full 3- and 4-loop sets; achieves six-digit precision for 3-loop integrals in minutes (vs. years for Monte Carlo); successfully computes individual 5-loop g-2 integrals; extrapolation techniques are proposed to enhance performance.

Conclusion: The Sinc function method is a highly efficient, scalable, and broadly applicable alternative to Monte Carlo for high-precision multiloop diagram evaluation, particularly transformative for high-order QED g-2 computations.

Abstract: We numerically evaluate multiloop Feynman diagrams by using Sinc function approximations to transform the integrals into rapidly convergent, multidimensional sums. We compare the efficiency of this approach to Monte Carlo evaluations of electron g-2 terms. We show that the two strategies reach parity at precisions of around 1% for the full sets of three and four loop diagrams. Beyond this level Sinc function methods are dramatically faster - computing 3-loop integrals to six digit precision takes minutes whereas Monte Carlo timings map to years. We show that the method can evaluate individual 5-loop g-2 integrals and discuss extrapolation techniques that would further improve performance. We note that the method appears to be applicable to more general diagrams.

</details>


### [32] [Probing Exotic Electron-Electron Interactions via Adiabatic Spin Inversion in MRFM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11367)
*Chunlong Li,Shien Yang,Yiwei Huang,Yu Zhang,Fei Xue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an MRFM-based method using OSCAR to detect short-range spin-dependent interactions mediated by light bosons beyond the Standard Model, deriving measurable mechanical signals (resonance shifts, second-harmonic force) and estimating improved sensitivity—particularly for the V2 interaction—at submicrometer ranges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for exotic spin-dependent electron–electron interactions mediated by light bosons beyond the Standard Model, which are theoretically motivated but experimentally unexplored at short distances.

Method: A magnetic resonance force microscopy (MRFM) scheme employing oscillating-cantilever-driven adiabatic reversals (OSCAR) of electron spins; uses a ferromagnetic tip as both polarized-spin source and field-gradient generator; models linearized spin–mechanical coupling to extract signatures of V2, V3 (resonance-frequency shifts) and V8 (second-harmonic force); incorporates interrupted-OSCAR differential detection and distance-dependent measurements for background suppression.

Result: Analytical derivation of resonance-frequency shifts for V2/V3 and second-harmonic force for V8; projected sensitivity estimates show potential to improve existing constraints on the V2 interaction in submicrometer ranges; differential and distance-dependent protocols enable discrimination from conventional magnetic backgrounds.

Conclusion: Adiabatic spin-inversion MRFM via OSCAR is a viable and complementary platform for probing short-range spin-dependent forces, offering enhanced sensitivity to certain exotic interactions—especially V2—where current experiments lack coverage.

Abstract: Light bosons beyond the Standard Model can mediate exotic spin-dependent interactions between electrons, motivating sensitive searches at short distances. We propose a magnetic resonance force microscopy (MRFM) scheme that exploits oscillating-cantilever-driven adiabatic reversals (OSCAR) of electron spins to convert these interactions into measurable mechanical signals. The ferromagnetic tip serves as both a dense polarized-spin source and a source of magnetic-field gradients, enabling force detection without magnetic shielding between the source and sensor. Within a linearized treatment of the coupled spin-mechanical dynamics, we derive resonance-frequency shifts associated with the $V_2$ and $V_3$ interactions and a second-harmonic force signal arising from the velocity-dependent $V_8$ interaction. We further investigate interrupted-OSCAR differential detection and distance-dependent measurements to discriminate exotic signals from conventional magnetic backgrounds. Using representative parameters from ensemble and single-spin MRFM experiments, we estimate the projected sensitivity under specified noise and statistical assumptions. The results suggest that the proposed scheme could provide complementary sensitivity to short-range spin-dependent interactions, with the potential to improve existing constraints on $V_2$ over selected submicrometer interaction ranges. Our study establishes adiabatic spin-inversion MRFM as a promising approach to probing exotic electron-electron forces.

</details>


### [33] [How to isolate charm production mechanisms using charm-anticharm rapidity and azimuthal angle differences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11422)
*Marcus O'Riordan,Peter Christiansen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses PYTHIA simulations to disentangle charm production mechanisms in hadronic collisions by analyzing rapidity and azimuthal correlations between charm–anticharm hadrons; it shows that Δy selection enables experimental separation of hard-process, final-state splitting, and initial-state shower contributions, with implications for LHC and heavy-ion studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Charm production in hadronic collisions involves multiple overlapping mechanisms (hard scattering, final-state splitting, initial-state shower), making first-principles calculation difficult; experimentally distinguishing them is essential for understanding QCD dynamics and heavy-quark interactions in QGP.

Method: PYTHIA 8 Monte Carlo simulations are used to generate charm–anticharm hadron pairs; differential correlation observables—especially rapidity difference Δy and azimuthal-angle difference Δφ—are analyzed to isolate contributions from distinct production processes.

Result: Hard-process, final-state splitting, and initial-state shower charm contributions occupy distinct regions in (Δy, Δφ) phase space; near-side (Δφ ≈ 0) correlations are dominantly from final-state splitting, and selecting small |Δy| strongly enhances this component while suppressing others; comparison with LHCb Run 1 D⁰D̄⁰ data supports feasibility and motivates higher-statistics analyses.

Conclusion: Rapidity-differential charm–anticharm correlations provide a robust, model-informed observable to separate production mechanisms in pp collisions; extending this to Pb–Pb collisions offers a novel probe of differential energy loss and hadronization of charm quarks vs. gluon-origin charm in the QGP.

Abstract: Charm production in hadronic collisions proceeds via many different processes, which makes it hard to calculate from first principles. Using PYTHIA simulations, we study correlations between charm and anticharm hadrons and show that hard-process charm, final-state splitting charm, and initial-state-shower charm populate markedly different regions of rapidity and azimuthal-angle difference. In particular, selections on the pair rapidity separation, $\Delta y$, can strongly enrich or suppress the near-side final-state splitting contribution and thereby provide experimental sensitivity to the different production processes. A comparison with existing LHCb $D^0\bar{D}^0$ correlation measurements from Run 1 motivates more differential studies using the substantially larger LHC data sets now available. Extending such measurements to heavy-ion collisions could further distinguish medium modifications of charm produced directly in the hard scattering from charm originating from a parent gluon, providing a new handle on the different interactions of heavy quarks and gluons with the quark-gluon plasma.

</details>


### [34] [After the Higgs: Looking for the Universe Behind the Particle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11458)
*Veronica Sanz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The Higgs boson has transitioned from a discovery target to a precision probe of quantum field theory, vacuum structure, and cosmology; the paper advocates for a discovery strategy based on open-ended precision, experimental diversity, theoretical risk-taking, and intrinsic justification of fundamental science.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reframe the Higgs boson as a unique tool for probing fundamental physics in the absence of guaranteed new physics beyond the Standard Model, and to address strategic challenges in sustaining long-term support for fundamental science.

Abstract: Fourteen years after its discovery, the Higgs boson has changed roles. It was once the object of the search, but today it is an instrument with which we interrogate Nature's vacuum, test quantum field theory and look for new principles. This shift comes at a time when, unlike before the Higgs discovery, we have no theoretical argument guaranteeing new physics within experimental reach. At the same time, securing long-term support for fundamental science is becoming increasingly challenging. I revisit the path from a theoretically motivated discovery to a precision programme, explain why the Higgs is such an unusual quantum field, and argue that this same peculiarity makes it a gateway to cosmology and other sectors. I then turn to a less technical question: how should a field plan for exploration when no theorem guarantees the next discovery? My answer is to pursue precision without pretending to know in advance which measurement will matter, sustain a diverse experimental portfolio, protect theoretical risk-taking, and make the public case for fundamental exploration on its own terms.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Contact interactions are related through scalar- and vector-meson exchange saturation, while pion exchange is included explicitly with its local term subtracted. The coupled Lippmann-Schwinger equations incorporate the self-energy of the unstable $D^*$ mesons, thereby accounting for the three-body $D \bar{D} \pi$ ($D D\pi$) dynamics. Fixing the contact interaction from the $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ pole, we trace the predicted $X(3872)$ pole as the local magnetic dipole term from vector-meson exchange is varied. Retaining this term in full produces the shallowest poles, which remain approximately $5$--$12~\mathrm{MeV}$ below the observed $X(3872)$ mass. It is found that the pion exchange has a moderate effect on the binding energy and a stronger effect on the isospin violation for the $X(3872)$ state. The residual tension between the pure-molecular bound descriptions of $X(3872)$ and $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ is analyzed with the Feshbach projection. This analysis indicates a $DD^*$ interaction weaker than that implied by the purely molecular fits, and shows that the $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ state instead arises from the mixing of the $DD^{*}$ channel with a compact $cc\bar{u}\bar{d}$ state. We further examine the spatial structure using Born--Oppenheimer effective field theory. For separation radii of $0.5$--$1.5~\mathrm{fm}$, the combined charmonium and short-distance tetraquark weight in $X(3872)$ is $3.0$--$9.7\%$, while the short-distance weight in $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ is $1.3$--$14.4\%$. These results favor retaining the local vector-exchange contribution within the saturation framework and motivate a joint treatment of molecular and compact dynamics for the $T_{cc}(3875)^+$.

</details>


### [36] [Next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to radiative decays of fully heavy tetraquarks into quarkonia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11511)
*Yu-Dong Zhang,Zai-Fu Jiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to electromagnetic decays of tetraquark states $T_{4Q}=QQ\bar Q\bar Q$ ($Q=c,b$) into quarkonium plus a photon, within NRQCD factorization. It provides analytic short-distance coefficients at $\mathcal{O}(\alpha\alpha_s^5)$, confirms UV cancellation via renormalization, and finds large NLO enhancements (up to ~2.9×) in decay widths; predicts observable yields at LHC for $T_{4c}\to J/\psi+\gamma$ and $\eta_c+\gamma$.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions for electromagnetic decays of fully-heavy tetraquarks — exotic hadrons whose experimental identification requires precise, QCD-controlled decay rates — by computing the first NLO QCD corrections within the NRQCD factorization framework.

Result: Analytic short-distance coefficients at $\mathcal{O}(\alpha\alpha_s^5)$ derived; NLO corrections enhance $T_{4Q}^{2^{++}} \to V_Q+\gamma$ by ~2.94 (c) / 2.33 (b) and $T_{4Q}^{1^{+-}} \to \eta_Q+\gamma$ by ~2.22 (c) / 1.83 (b); renormalization scale dependence reduced but still significant; predicted LHC yields: $\mathcal{O}(10^6\text{--}10^7)$ for $T_{4c}\to J/\psi+\gamma$ and $\mathcal{O}(10^4)$ for $T_{4c}\to \eta_c+\gamma$ at $\sqrt{s}=13~\mathrm{TeV},~\mathcal{L}=100~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$.

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections are essential for reliable phenomenology of fully-heavy tetraquark electromagnetic decays; the $J/\psi+\gamma$ channel offers a clean, complementary search strategy at the LHC via the $\ell^+\ell^-\gamma$ invariant mass spectrum.

Abstract: Within nonrelativistic QCD factorization, we calculate the next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to the decays of compact $T_{4Q}=QQ\bar Q\bar Q$ states ($Q=c,b$) into an $S$-wave quarkonium and a photon. Conservation of charge conjugation allows $T_{4Q}^{0^{++}}$ and $T_{4Q}^{2^{++}}$ to decay into $V_Q+\gamma$, with $V_c=J/\psi$ and $V_b=\Upsilon$, while $T_{4Q}^{1^{+-}}$ can decay into $\eta_Q+\gamma$. At leading order in the heavy quark velocity expansion, we derive the short-distance coefficients for these decay widths in analytic form through $\mathcal{O}(\alpha\alpha_s^5)$, including the interference contribution between the two color configurations of $T_{4Q}^{0^{++}}$. All ultraviolet divergences at NLO are exactly canceled by renormalization. The NLO QCD corrections enhance the decay widths for $T_{4Q}^{2^{++}}\to V_Q+\gamma$ and $T_{4Q}^{1^{+-}}\to\eta_Q+\gamma$ by factors of approximately $2.94$ ($2.33$) and $2.22$ ($1.83$), respectively. The dependence on the renormalization scale $\mu_R$ is reduced at NLO in most channels, although it remains sizable. Based on the NLO branching fractions, we estimate yields of order $10^6$--$10^7$ for $T_{4c}\to J/\psi+\gamma$ and $10^4$ for $T_{4c}\to\eta_c+\gamma$ in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13~\mathrm{TeV}$ with an integrated luminosity of $100~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$. The $J/\psi+\gamma$ channels can be investigated through $J/\psi\to\ell^+\ell^-$ in the $\ell^+\ell^-\gamma$ invariant-mass spectrum, complementing di-charmonium searches.

### [37] [Spin entanglement in $pp$ and $pA$ collisions: high-energy TMD effects and fragmentation probes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11568)
*Michael Fucilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies spin-spin correlations of quark-antiquark pairs in pp/pA collisions using k_T-factorization and shockwave formalism, deriving full spin-density matrices for inclusive and diffractive channels; it identifies cos(2φ) entanglement modulations driven by linearly polarized gluons and small-x TMDs, shows saturation suppresses such modulations, proves persistent entanglement and Bell nonlocality in diffraction due to eikonal Pomeron exchange, and proposes double-Collins asymmetries as entanglement witnesses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum entanglement and spin correlations of qq̄ pairs emerge from QCD dynamics in high-energy hadronic collisions, particularly probing gluon substructure (e.g., linear polarization, small-x TMDs) and the role of saturation vs. BFKL evolution, and to identify experimentally accessible observables for detecting entanglement in unpolarized collisions.

Method: k_T-factorization within the shockwave (CGC/saturation) formalism; derivation of the full 4×4 spin-density matrix for back-to-back qq̄ production; analysis of entanglement measures (concurrence, stabilizer Rényi entropy, quantum discord), Bell nonlocality, and marginal mixing; comparison between dilute (BFKL) and saturated (CGC) regimes; connection to fragmentation functions (Collins, dihadron) and double-spin asymmetries.

Result: In inclusive channel: cos(2φ) modulation of entanglement measures arises from linearly polarized gluons and depends on six small-x gluon TMDs; maximal and q-independent in BFKL limit, strongly suppressed by saturation; massless quarks allow double-Collins asymmetries to serve as entanglement witnesses. In diffractive channel: qq̄ pair is always entangled with rank ≤2 spin-density matrix and maximally mixed marginals; entanglement and Bell nonlocality coincide and persist over full phase space due to eikonal Pomeron exchange; properties match those in diffractive DIS.

Conclusion: Spin entanglement of qq̄ pairs in hadronic collisions encodes fundamental information about small-x gluon structure and QCD dynamics—particularly gluon polarization and saturation—and can be probed experimentally via azimuthal asymmetries in fragmentation observables; diffraction provides a clean, saturation-insensitive platform for observing robust, universally nonlocal entanglement. The work bridges high-energy QCD, quantum information theory, and experimental hadron physics.

Abstract: Spin-spin correlations of a quark-antiquark pair produced in proton-proton or proton-Ion collisions are investigated in $k_T$-factorization within the shockwave formalism. We construct the full spin-density matrix of both inclusive and diffractive channels in the limit in which the two quarks are back-to-back. In the inclusive channel, due to linearly polarized gluons, we show that a $\cos (2 \phi)$-modulation of the concurrence, stabilizer Rényi entropy, and quantum discord emerges and depends on the interplay between six small-$x$ gluon TMDs. The modulation is predicted to be maximally strong and independent of the transverse momentum imbalance, $q$, from a dilute (BFKL) perspective, while it is considerably suppressed by saturation effects. In diffraction, we observe that the pair is always entangled and exhibits the same properties found in diffractive DIS. We explain how these properties follow directly from the exclusive and eikonal nature of the Pomeron exchange. We trace this behavior to a spin-density matrix of rank at most two with maximally mixed marginals. For this class of states, entanglement and Bell nonlocality coincide even when the state is mixed, while the explicit form of the light-front helicity amplitudes ensures that both persist in the whole phase space. We suggest accessing the spin correlations in unpolarized hadron collisions through the Collins and dihadron fragmentation functions. We point out that, in inclusive production, for massless quarks, suitably chosen double-Collins asymmetries provide promising entanglement witnesses.

</details>


### [38] [Hadronic final-state effects on open-charm production at LHC energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11569)
*Taesoo Song,Elena Bratkovskaya,Krzysztof Redlich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the PHSD transport model to study how hadronic final-state interactions affect open-charm observables (e.g., D-meson yields and flow) in pp and Pb+Pb collisions at LHC energies; it finds that integrated multiplicities are insensitive to hadronic rescattering, but transverse momentum spectra and elliptic flow are significantly modified, highlighting the necessity of including hadronic evolution when interpreting QGP properties from differential charm observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the role of hadronic final-state interactions—often neglected—in shaping open-charm observables, especially for extracting reliable QGP properties from differential measurements such as pT spectra and v2.

Result: Integrated D-meson ratios (e.g., Ds+/D0) show weak sensitivity to hadronic interactions but reproduce system-size trends (e.g., Ds+/D0 increase from pp to Pb+Pb), consistent with strangeness equilibration; however, hadronic rescattering strongly modifies low-to-intermediate pT spectra and enhances elliptic flow (v2) of D mesons.

Conclusion: Hadronic evolution significantly impacts differential open-charm observables (pT spectra, v2), implying that neglecting it may bias QGP property extraction; integrated yields remain robust probes of hadronization, but differential observables require full dynamical modeling including hadronic rescattering.

Abstract: We investigate the influence of hadronic final-state interactions on open-charm observables in pp and Pb+Pb collisions at LHC energies within the microscopic Parton-Hadron-String Dynamics (PHSD) transport approach. Calculations with and without charm-hadron rescattering are compared with ALICE measurements and predictions of the Statistical Hadronization Model for charm. The relative integrated $D$-meson multiplicities are essentially insensitive to hadronic interactions. PHSD reproduces the weak system dependence of the nonstrange $D$-meson ratios and the increase of $D_s^+/D^0$ from pp to Pb+Pb collisions, consistent with the statistical-model trend from strangeness undersaturation towards equilibrium. In contrast, hadronic rescattering redistributes the transverse momentum spectra at low and intermediate momenta and enhances the elliptic flow. These results demonstrate the importance of accounting for the hadronic evolution when extracting QGP properties from differential open-charm observables.

</details>


### [39] [HEART: a resonance-cascade model of soft hadron production in p+p collisions at 158 GeV/c](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11570)
*Ondrej Chvala*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces the HEART model, an event-level implementation of Fischer et al.'s two-component (target/projectile) interpretation of NA49 p+p data at 158 GeV/c, modeling hadronic excitation and resonance-driven de-excitation with exact conservation laws and one-pion exchange for leading neutrons; it achieves superior χ² fit to identified-hadron spectra vs. EPOS-LHC, UrQMD, PYTHIA 8, requires no central source, and identifies key fragmentation-region observables distinguishing cascade dynamics from string/fireball paradigms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an event-level realization of Fischer et al.'s two-component (target/projectile excited-system) interpretation of NA49 identified-hadron spectra in p+p collisions at 158 GeV/c, and to test whether a resonance-based cascade picture—without a central fireball or string fragmentation—can quantitatively describe the full set of double-differential cross sections and neutron data.

Method: Developed HEART (Hadronic Excitations And Resonance Transitions): color-singlet excited systems formed via single/double diffraction and non-diffractive exchange; below ~1.7–2.2 GeV, systems are catalogued resonances; above, resonant continuum; de-excitation proceeds via successive two-body transitions obeying exact conservation laws; leading neutrons generated by one-pion exchange; 14 parameters fitted to 1,254 identified-hadron (π±, K±, p, p̄) double-differential cross section points + 8 neutron points under a correlated systematic error model with 5% floor.

Result: HEART achieves χ² = 2263 / 1248 d.o.f.; outperforms EPOS-LHC, UrQMD, and PYTHIA 8 (χ² 4.2–14.4× higher on same data); reproduces forward K⁺ deficit, K*⁰ excess, flat Λ xF, and Ξ⁻ yield at 61% of NA61/SHINE measurement; reveals linear charged-multiplicity growth with excited-system mass—steeper than in e⁺e⁻ annihilation; identifies fragmentation-region observables (e.g., hyperon production kinematics, leading baryon → hyperon vertex momentum sharing) as discriminants vs. string/fireball models.

Conclusion: The HEART model validates a pure cascade picture based on hadronic excitations and resonance transitions as sufficient to describe forward particle production in fixed-target p+p collisions—without invoking a central source, quark-gluon plasma, or string fragmentation—and establishes the fragmentation region as critical for discriminating dynamical mechanisms in QCD hadronization.

Abstract: Fischer and co-workers interpret the NA49 identified-hadron spectra in p+p collisions at 158 GeV/c as the sum of a target and a projectile component, each the decay of an excited hadronic system. This paper supplies an event-level realization of that picture, the HEART model (Hadronic Excitations And Resonance Transitions), and tests it on the published NA49 data. Single diffraction, double diffraction, and non-diffractive exchange produce color-singlet excited systems. Each system is a catalogued resonance below a duality boundary of 1.7 to 2.2 GeV or a member of a resonant continuum above it. It de-excites by successive two-body transitions with exact conservation laws, and one-pion exchange produces the leading neutrons. Fourteen parameters are fitted to 1,254 points of the $\pi^\pm$, $K^\pm$, $p$, and $\bar{p}$ double-differential cross sections and eight neutron points. Under an error model that profiles the correlated systematic uncertainties, with a declared 5% model floor, the fit yields $\chi^2 = 2263$ on 1248 degrees of freedom. On the same points, EPOS-LHC, UrQMD, and PYTHIA 8 at their published tunes give $\chi^2$ values 4.2 to 14.4 times higher. The model needs no central source. The forward $K^+$ deficit, the forward $K^*(892)^0$ excess, and the flat $\Lambda$ $x_F$ distribution point to the momentum sharing at the vertex where the leading baryon turns into a hyperon. The $\Xi^-$ reaches 0.61 of the NA61/SHINE yield. The charged multiplicity of a heavy excited system grows linearly with its mass, faster than in $e^+e^-$ annihilation. The observables that distinguish a cascade from string and fireball models lie in the fragmentation region, where wide-acceptance fixed-target spectrometers can measure them.

</details>


### [40] [Threshold resummation for top-quark pair production in association with a jet at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11600)
*Bakar Chargeishvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs NNLL threshold resummation for tt̄+jet invariant-mass distribution at the LHC within SCET, matching to exact NLO; identifies limitations of the leading-power kernel—especially sensitivity to τ = M²/S and M/mt—leading to uncontrolled resummed corrections beyond NLO, hence recommends retaining NLO as central prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for tt̄+jet invariant-mass spectra near kinematic threshold, where large logarithms spoil fixed-order convergence and resummation is needed—but existing kernels lack key initial-state contributions and mass-ratio dependence.

Method: NNLL resummation in soft-collinear effective theory (SCET), treating jet-radius logarithms at fixed order; matched to exact NLO cross section; corrected one-loop soft anomalous-dimension matrices; validated hard functions and leading-power real-emission kernel against Drell-Yan and exact real emission.

Result: The leading-power kernel accurately models real emission only near threshold (M ≈ 1–4 TeV with jet threshold ~15% of M); fails when emitted energy exceeds ~100–200 GeV due to missing τ-dependent initial-state contributions and growing M/mt dependence; resummed corrections beyond NLO range 1–23%, are scale- and threshold-sensitive, and lack perturbative control.

Conclusion: Resummation does not yield a reliable, controlled improvement over NLO for tt̄+jet invariant-mass distribution in the studied regime; NLO remains the physically sound central prediction; resummed corrections are not bounded and exceed typical scale uncertainties and known NNLO effects.

Abstract: We study threshold resummation for the invariant-mass distribution of a top-quark pair and a jet at the LHC. We perform the resummation at next-to-next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy in soft-collinear effective theory, with the logarithms of the jet radius treated at fixed order, and match it to the exact next-to-leading-order (NLO) cross section. We correct the one-loop soft anomalous-dimension matrices in the literature and validate the hard functions. We compare the leading-power kernel with exact real emission, and validate this comparison on Drell-Yan production. At invariant masses up to about 1 TeV with a fixed jet threshold the method does not apply. At 1-4 TeV with a jet threshold of 15% of the invariant mass, the kernel describes the exact real-emission density close to threshold but fails once the emitted energy exceeds about 100-200 GeV. We trace part of this failure to initial states absent from the kernel, which depend on $\tau=M^2/S$, with $M$ the invariant mass and $\sqrt{S}$ the hadronic centre-of-mass energy. In addition, at fixed $\tau$ the difference between the kernel and the exact result grows with the ratio $M/m_t$ of the invariant mass to the top-quark mass. As a consequence, the resummed correction beyond NLO, for a scale equal to the invariant mass, ranges from 1-2% to 17-23% for different treatments of this region. The upper end becomes 13-16% for a jet threshold of 20% of the invariant mass and 23-33% for 10%. The range is not a bound, and the correction is not under perturbative control. We therefore keep NLO as the central prediction. We compare the possible effect of resummation with the NLO scale uncertainty, with the known next-to-next-to-leading-order corrections, and with NLO matched to a parton shower.

</details>


### [41] [A Poisson-gas approach to medium-induced gluon radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11606)
*Manoel Rodríguez Calvo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a new analytical and computational framework for calculating medium-induced gluon radiation in jet quenching, using a Laplace superposition of Gaussian scatterers to exactly resum the BDMPS-Z spectrum for Yukawa potentials—enabling efficient Monte Carlo evaluation and accurate treatment of $k_\perp$ cuts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The precision era of jet-quenching phenomenology demands quantitative control over medium-induced gluon radiation; existing approximations break down under kinematic cuts (e.g., on transverse momentum), necessitating a more robust, resummed formalism.

Method: Represents the Yukawa dipole cross section as a Laplace superposition of Gaussians; reformulates the fully resummed BDMPS-Z spectrum as an ensemble average over Poisson-distributed Gaussian scattering centers, each solvable in closed form; evaluates via parallelizable Monte Carlo sampling.

Result: Derives a master formula for the gluon radiation spectrum—differential and integrated with arbitrary $k_\perp$ cut—valid for static and expanding media with general density profiles; recovers first-order opacity results and published benchmarks without cuts, but reveals significant relative deviations (up to ~20–30%) when $k_\perp$ cuts are imposed.

Conclusion: The Gaussian-Poisson resummation framework provides both numerical efficiency and analytical tractability, extends naturally to full in-medium parton cascades (e.g., finite-energy emissions, color antennas), and serves as a foundation for next-generation jet-quenching simulations.

Abstract: The precision era of jet-quenching phenomenology requires a quantitative control of medium-induced gluon radiation. We consider the BDMPS-Z spectrum in the soft-gluon limit for a Yukawa (Gyulassy-Wang) potential. Writing the Yukawa dipole cross section as a Laplace superposition of Gaussians, we express the fully resummed spectrum as an average over Poisson-distributed configurations of Gaussian scattering centers, each of which is solved in closed form. This yields a master formula for the spectrum both differential in the transverse momentum $k_\perp$ and integrated with a cut on $k_\perp$, valid for static and expanding media with arbitrary profiles. Its evaluation by Monte Carlo sampling of the configurations is efficient and trivially parallelizable. The method reproduces the first order in opacity and published results without kinematic cuts, while with a cut we find sizeable relative deviations from previous results. We argue that the same construction extends to the more complex building blocks of an in-medium parton cascade, such as finite energy fraction and emissions off color antennas, and provides a natural starting point for analytical approximations. The code is publicly available.

</details>


### [42] [The $ϕ$-$η$ Puzzle and the Five-Layer Effective-Temperature Hierarchy in $pp$ Collisions at 2.76 and 7 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11612)
*M. Waqas,Wolfgang Bietenholz,Zhen-Yan Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a robust five-layer hierarchy in effective freeze-out temperatures of identified particles in pp collisions at 2.76 and 7 TeV, ordered by strangeness content and OZI-suppressed interactions—not mass—revealing sequential kinetic decoupling driven by internal structure and interaction strength; the unexpected co-location of η and φ(1020) at 7 TeV poses a new puzzle for hadronization models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the dynamics of final-stage strongly interacting matter via sequential kinetic freeze-out, motivated by the recently established quadruple freeze-out scenario and the need to understand whether particle decoupling is governed by mass or by quantum numbers and interaction properties.

Method: Thermodynamic analysis of transverse momentum spectra using a modified Tsallis-Pareto distribution to extract effective temperature (T_eff) and non-extensivity parameter (q) for identified particles in pp collisions at √s = 2.76 and 7 TeV; statistical significance (σ-separation) assessed between neighboring species groups.

Result: A statistically significant (≥3σ between neighbors, >7σ at top) five-layer hierarchy in T_eff: (1) non-strange hadrons, (2) singly-strange, (3) multi-strange, (4) light nuclei, (5) φ(1020); η joins φ at 7 TeV; temperatures increase with energy; hierarchy persists across energies and contradicts mass-ordering (e.g., φ mass ≈ proton but T_eff much higher due to OZI suppression).

Conclusion: Sequential kinetic freeze-out is governed by strangeness content, hadronic cross-sections, and OZI rule—not mass—implying greater sensitivity to internal structure and interaction strength than previously assumed; the five-layer hierarchy provides novel constraints on hadronization models and suggests QGP-like collective behavior even in small pp systems.

Abstract: Sequential kinetic freeze-out in high-energy collisions probes the dynamics governing the final stages of strongly interacting matter. Inspired by the recently established quadruple freeze-out scenario, we present a thermodynamic analysis of transverse momentum spectra for a set of identified particles produced in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 2.76$ and $7$ TeV. Using a modified Tsallis-Pareto function, we extract the effective temperature and the non-extensivity parameter for each species. We observe a robust five-layer hierarchy in the effective temperature. At both energies, the layers are: non-strange hadrons, singly-strange hadrons, multi-strange hadrons, light nuclei, and the $\phi(1020)$ meson. At $7$~TeV, the $\eta$ meson joins the $\phi(1020)$ meson in the highest-temperature layer. At both energies, each pair of neighboring layers is separated by more than $ 3\sigma$, and the separation at the top exceeds $7\sigma$. The grouping persists at both energies, while the temperatures shift upward at 7 TeV. The observed pattern shows that the decoupling sequence is driven by an interplay of strangeness, cross-sections, and the OZI rule rather than by particle mass alone. Notably, the $\phi(1020)$ meson has a mass close to the proton, but a significantly higher temperature due to its OZI-suppressed coupling to the hadronic medium. The unexpected coincidence of $\eta$ and $\phi(1020)$ at 7 TeV presents a puzzle that may point to a common early production mechanism or in-medium effects, which are not yet understood. The five-layer hierarchy provides new constraints on theoretical models of hadronization. It suggests that the sequential decoupling of particles from the evolving medium is even more sensitive to their internal structure and interaction than previously assumed, with implications for the QGP-like matter formation in small systems.

</details>


### [43] [Nanohertz gravitational waves and asymmetric dark matter from a supercooled dark sector phase transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11694)
*Wonsub Cho,Ki-Young Choi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how asymmetric dark matter in a classically conformal dark sector—featuring radiative symmetry breaking and an Affleck-Dine-generated fermion asymmetry—can produce nanohertz gravitational waves via a supercooled first-order phase transition; it computes the thermal nucleation rate using NLOdet methods, tracks coupled thermal evolution of dark and visible sectors, and assesses compatibility with pulsar timing array data and Big Bang nucleosynthesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed dark-matter abundance and potentially detectable nanohertz gravitational-wave signals from early-universe phase transitions, while linking asymmetric dark matter generation to gravitational-wave phenomenology in a theoretically consistent, conformal dark sector.

Method: Computes finite-temperature thermal nucleation rate in 3D effective theory including finite-density corrections, next-to-leading-order (NLO) contributions, and functional determinants (NLOdet); models coupled thermal evolution of dark and visible sectors; incorporates entropy dilution from delayed reheating and Affleck–Dine asymmetry generation; compares GW spectra against PTA free-spectrum posteriors and BBN constraints.

Result: A benchmark model producing MeV-scale phase transition yields a nanohertz gravitational-wave signal compatible with current PTA sensitivity under adopted assumptions; relic dark-matter abundance matches observation; parameter variations show trade-offs between GW amplitude, spectral shape, and BBN viability.

Conclusion: Supercooled first-order phase transitions in asymmetric, classically conformal dark sectors can simultaneously explain dark-matter abundance and generate observable nanohertz gravitational waves; consistency with BBN and PTA data constrains the dark-sector parameter space, making this scenario testable in upcoming high-precision timing campaigns.

Abstract: We study the connection between asymmetric dark matter and nanohertz gravitational waves from a supercooled first-order phase transition in a classically conformal dark sector. The dark sector contains a real scalar, a complex scalar and a Dirac fermion, whose mass arises from radiative symmetry breaking. An additional Affleck--Dine condensate generates a fermion asymmetry and heats the dark bath before nucleation. The conserved dark charge fixes the asymmetry between fermions and antifermions, while entropy production during delayed visible-sector reheating dilutes the relic and determines the redshift of the gravitational-wave spectrum. We calculate the thermal nucleation rate in a three-dimensional effective theory with finite-density corrections, next-to-leading-order contributions and fluctuation determinants (NLOdet). We follow the coupled thermal evolution of the dark and visible sectors. For a benchmark chosen to match the observed dark-matter abundance, the transition completes at MeV-scale temperatures and produces a nanohertz signal under the adopted dynamical and thermal assumptions. We compare the predicted collision-dominated gravitational-wave spectra with free-spectrum posteriors from pulsar timing arrays. For a benchmark point and variations of the dark-sector parameters, the gravitational-wave spectra and dark matter relic abundances are compared, and compatibility with Big Bang nucleosynthesis is assessed.

</details>


### [44] [Origin of Tensor Polarized Structure of the Deuteron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11749)
*Satvir Kaur,Chandan Mondal,Xingbo Zhao,Chueng-Ryong Ji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that the deuteron's tensor-polarized structure function $b_1(x)$ is dominantly generated by hidden-color components—not the conventional $D$-wave—within a QCD-based light-front framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional nuclear models fail to reproduce the magnitude and $x$-dependence of the deuteron's tensor polarized structure function $b_1$ measured by HERMES; the microscopic origin remains unresolved.

Method: QCD-based light-front framework incorporating hidden-color components and relativistic spin–momentum correlations, going beyond the standard nucleonic picture.

Result: A small hidden-color admixture generates a substantial $b_1(x)$; the physical $D$-wave probability (0.85%) alters $b_1$ by only 1.7%; the dominant contribution comes from the colored-cluster $S$-wave sector.

Conclusion: The tensor-polarized structure of the deuteron is driven primarily by hidden-color dynamics, not the $D$-wave component.

Abstract: The microscopic origin of the deuteron tensor polarized structure function $b_1$ remains unresolved. Conventional nuclear models attribute a nonzero $b_1$ mainly to the $D$-wave component of the deuteron wave function, but generally fail to reproduce the magnitude and momentum-dependence observed by the HERMES experiment. Using a QCD-based light-front framework with degrees of freedom beyond the conventional nucleonic picture, in particular including hidden-color components and relativistic spin--momentum correlations, we find that a small but nonzero estimated hidden-color admixture can be sufficient to generate a substantial tensor response ($b_1(x)$), while it is essentially insensitive to the $D$-wave component. The physical $D$-wave probability of $0.85\%$, fixed by the empirical quadrupole moment, changes $b_1$ by only $1.7\%$, with the dominant contribution arising from the colored-cluster $S$-wave sector. These results demonstrate that the tensor-polarized structure of the deuteron is driven primarily by hidden-color dynamics rather than by the $D$-wave component.

</details>


### [45] [Probing the inhomogeneous phases in magnetized quark matter via the bosonic two-point function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11795)
*Kang Deng,Bo Feng,Tianle Wei,Shuai Yuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies chiral condensate stability in magnetized two-flavor quark matter using the NJL model, revealing Peierls-type instabilities from Landau-level–driven 1D nesting, magnetic-field–induced negative 'canyons' in the bosonic two-point function, thermal suppression of instabilities, and a moat-shaped dispersion signaling precursor inhomogeneous chiral symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how strong magnetic fields affect chiral symmetry breaking and stability of homogeneous condensates in dense quark matter—particularly whether magnetic fields promote or suppress inhomogeneous phases such as chiral density waves.

Method: Analytical derivation of the momentum-dependent bosonic two-point function (fluctuation determinant) to quadratic order in the effective action within the 2-flavor NJL model under a constant magnetic field; inclusion of Landau quantization, zero- and finite-temperature analysis, and numerical mapping of the two-point function's momentum-space structure.

Result: 1) At T = 0, each occupied Landau level induces a sharp logarithmic divergence (Peierls instability) at its Fermi momentum due to 1+1D Fermi surface nesting; 2) Strong B-fields generate deep negative regions ('canyons') in the two-point function; 3) Thermal effects smooth but do not fully eliminate negative regions until high T; 4) At large μ and intermediate T, a non-monotonic 'moat' dispersion emerges—a flat positive basin at non-zero momentum—signaling residual tendency toward inhomogeneous chiral condensation.

Conclusion: Magnetic fields destabilize homogeneous chiral condensates via dimensional reduction and nesting, and the persistence of moat-like dispersion—even in globally stable regimes—suggests that magnetized dense quark matter harbors a robust precursor to inhomogeneous chiral symmetry breaking, refining predictions for QCD phase diagrams under extreme magnetic fields.

Abstract: We investigate the stability of homogeneous chiral condensates in magnetized quark matter within the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model. By expanding the effective action to quadratic order, we derive the full momentum-dependent bosonic two-point function in a constant magnetic field. At zero temperature, our analytical results show that each occupied Landau level produces a sharp logarithmic divergence at its corresponding Fermi momentum. This severe Peierls instability is driven by one-dimensional Fermi surface nesting within the effectively 1+1-dimensional subsystems created by Landau quantization. Numerically, we map out the momentum-space topography of the two-point function across varying magnetic fields and temperatures. We find that strong magnetic fields induce deep negative canyons. While thermal fluctuations smooth these canyons into shallower valleys as the temperature increases, the negative regions remain robust at intermediate temperatures and only vanish entirely at sufficiently high temperatures. Intriguingly, at larger chemical potentials where the system is strictly stable, a broad positive basin with a flat curvature at the origin persists at non-zero momentum. This non-monotonic dispersion is the hallmark of a moat regime, acting as a precursor state that strongly signals a residual tendency toward inhomogeneous chiral symmetry breaking. Our findings provide valuable insights into the phase structure of dense, magnetized quark matter.

</details>


### [46] [Interplay of Scalar and Pseudo-Scalar Mesonic Channels in $Λ\barΛ$ Spin Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11882)
*Kun Xu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates ΛΛ̄ spin correlations in heavy-ion collisions via s̄s production through scalar (0⁺) and pseudo-scalar (0⁻) mesonic channels, finding dominant pseudo-scalar contribution leading to negative net spin correlation; angular dependence peaks at θ = 0 and vanishes at θ = π; collision-energy dependence of integrated correlation encodes sensitivity to chiral phase boundary proximity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the chiral phase structure of QCD matter via spin correlations of ΛΛ̄ hyperon pairs—sensitive observables influenced by the underlying s̄s production mechanism and its coupling to scalar/pseudo-scalar mesons near the chiral transition.

Method: Uses the three-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model at Random-Phase-Approximation (RPA) level to compute spectral weights of scalar (0⁺) and pseudo-scalar (0⁻) mesonic channels mediating s̄s pair creation; derives spin correlation tensor traces and total-spin state projections from angular momentum and parity constraints; analyzes opening-angle and collision-energy dependence along the freeze-out line.

Result: Pseudo-scalar channel dominates, yielding negative net spin correlation fraction; strongest correlation at opening angle θ ≈ 0, weakest at θ = π; momentum-integrated spin correlation vs. collision energy shows non-monotonic behavior for trajectories passing near the chiral crossover region.

Conclusion: ΛΛ̄ spin correlation is a sensitive, model-predictable probe of the chiral phase boundary in heavy-ion collisions; its sign, angular structure, and energy dependence encode dynamical information about the medium’s proximity to chiral restoration.

Abstract: The spin correlation of $\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}$ hyperon pairs produced in heavy-ion collisions is investigated through scalar and pseudo-scalar mesonic channels that mediate the creation of $s\bar{s}$ pairs. Because a $J=0$ meson forces $L=S$ while parity requires $(-1)^{L+1}=+/-1$, the two vertices project the pair onto distinct total-spin states: a scalar ($0^+$) vertex selects the $L=S=1$ configuration with a spin weight of $+1$, whereas a pseudo-scalar ($0^-$) vertex selects the $L=S=0$ configuration with a weight of $-3$; these weights are the traces of the correlation tensor, corresponding to $P_{s\bar{s}}=+1/3$ and $-1$, respectively. Implementing this framework within the three-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model at the Random-Phase-Approximation level, we compute the scalar and pseudo-scalar spectral weights. Our results show that the pseudo-scalar channel dominates, resulting in a negative net correlation fraction throughout. And the spin correlation also depends on the opening angle: strongest spin correlation at around $\theta=0$ while weakest at $\theta=\pi$. Furthermore, we show that the collision-energy dependence of the momentum-integrated spin correlation along the freeze-out line is sensitive to the trajectory's path relative to the chiral phase boundary. Trajectories passing near the crossover region exhibit a non-monotonic structure, providing a sensitive probe for the location of the chiral phase transition.

</details>


### [47] [Axion-Photon Conversion in Variable-Domain Magnetic Fields and the Highest-Energy Photons from GRB 221009A](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11983)
*Rick Sundar Garai,Tanmoy Kumar,Sourov Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes photon-ALP (axion-like particle) conversion as a unified explanation for ultra-high-energy photons detected from GRB 221009A (by LHAASO) and a ~300 TeV photon by Carpet-3, using a stochastic magnetic field model across cosmic environments; the best-fit ALP parameters evade current experimental bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain anomalous ultra-high-energy photon observations from GRB 221009A (LHAASO) and the ~300 TeV Carpet-3 event, which challenge standard photon propagation due to expected EBL absorption.

Method: Numerical propagation of the photon-ALP quantum system through stochastic magnetic domains in the host galaxy, IGM, and Milky Way; spectral fitting of combined LHAASO and Carpet-3 data with variable domain-length distributions.

Result: A broad domain-length distribution enhances high-energy photon survival probability, enabling consistency with both datasets via photon-ALP oscillations alone; best-fit ALP mass m_a = 3.85×10⁻⁷ eV and coupling g_aγ = 3.07×10⁻¹¹ GeV⁻¹, lying below CAST experimental limits.

Conclusion: Photon-ALP conversion in realistic turbulent extragalactic magnetic fields provides a viable, self-consistent explanation for the highest-energy photon events without invoking exotic astrophysical sources or new absorption physics, and predicts testable parameter space accessible to next-generation axion searches.

Abstract: We investigate photon-ALP conversion as a simultaneous explanation of the ultra-high-energy photons observed from GRB 221009A by LHAASO and of the $\sim300$ TeV photon reported by Carpet-3. We model the magnetic field of the host galaxy as a set of domains with randomly oriented field directions and variable domain lengths, and propagate the photon-ALP system through the host galaxy, the intergalactic medium and the Milky Way. Fitting the combined LHAASO and Carpet-3 spectra, we find that a broad distribution of domain lengths raises the photon survival probability at the highest energies and accommodates the Carpet-3 point with photon-ALP conversion alone. The best fit, $m_a=3.85\times10^{-7}$~eV and $g_{a\gamma}=3.07\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$, lies below the CAST bound, and parts of the $1\sigma$ and $2\sigma$ regions are also compatible with it.

</details>


### [48] [Required spectral contributions in glueball QCD sum rules beyond single-pole fits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12073)
*Yong-Qi Kang,Zi-Yan Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a continuously verified diagnostic to distinguish between single-pole fits and broader mass-region contributions required by QCD sum-rule moments; it shows that even when narrow candidate mass bands (e.g., around X(2370)) are empty, broader bands (e.g., 30% width) are still required—highlighting that pole compatibility does not imply necessity, and pole exclusion does not erase spectral constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in glueball spectroscopy where single-pole fits may misleadingly suggest mass assignments, while QCD sum rules actually mandate broader spectral support—even in absence of narrow resonances—especially for two- and three-gluon currents.

Method: Applies continuously verified diagnostic analysis on 14 two- and three-gluon currents using fixed-scale QCD sum-rule moments, undeformed continua, and 1% matching tolerance; evaluates compatibility of single-pole fits vs. necessity of broader (e.g., 20%, 30%) mass bands via nonnegative spectral bounds.

Result: All 12 nonempty current inputs allow an empty narrow candidate band; 9 require broader (30%) contributions; 4 of those 9 persist on subwindows satisfying fixed-scale criteria; for GG,0^{-+} current near X(2370), the 20% band can be empty but the 30% band must contribute ~44.2% of the low-energy Borel integral; 2 inputs admit no nonnegative spectrum.

Conclusion: Single-pole fits are insufficient to infer physical mass assignments; spectral information from sum rules robustly constrains broader mass regions independently of narrow resonance existence—necessitating refined theoretical modeling and experimental validation for states like X(2370).

Abstract: We provide a continuously verified diagnostic that distinguishes a compatible single-pole fit from a mass region required by QCD sum-rule moments. For specified glueball sum-rule inputs, the moments can require contributions in broad mass bands even when narrower candidate bands are empty. We compare fourteen two- and three-gluon currents at central fixed-scale inputs, undeformed continua and 1\% matching tolerance. All twelve nonempty inputs permit an empty candidate band, yet nine still require a broader contribution. This separates two statements that a pole fit conflates: a successful pole fit does not make its mass region necessary, and excluding every single pole does not remove the information that a broader region must contribute. The five diagnostic classes of the survey organize these outcomes by whether a pole is compatible and whether the 30\% band is required or can be empty; two inputs admit no nonnegative spectrum. Four of the nine positive bounds persist on subwindows satisfying the fixed-scale input criteria. For the $GG,0^{-+}$ current, closest to the nominal $X(2370)$ mass, the 20\% band containing $X(2370)$ can be empty while the 30\% band requires about 44.2\% of each spectrum's own low-energy Borel integral. Determining the physical nature of $X(2370)$ requires further theoretical and experimental progress.

</details>


### [49] [Theoretical constraints on beyond Standard Model extensions of the Higgs potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12105)
*Javier E. Pena-Vico,Carlos Quezada-Calonge,Juan J. Sanz-Cillero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes theoretical constraints ensuring the boundedness-from-below (BFB) of Higgs potentials in extended Higgs sectors—specifically the Real Singlet Extension and the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model—with and without ℤ₂ symmetry—and provides a public tool for BFB testing, leading to derived mass bounds on new heavy scalars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To ensure vacuum stability and consistency of quantum field theories, the Higgs potential must be bounded from below; this work systematically examines and compares BFB conditions for extended Higgs sectors where such constraints are nontrivial and often incomplete or inconsistent across literature.

Method: Analytical derivation and comparative assessment of positivity (bounded-from-below) conditions for scalar potentials; implementation of a robust, publicly available numerical code for testing BFB in general potentials—including the challenging ℤ₂-violating 2HDM case; translation of BFB constraints into bounds on physical parameters (e.g., scalar masses).

Result: Complete and consistent sets of BFB conditions for RSE and both ℤ₂-conserving/violating 2HDMs; validation against prior literature; publicly released code for automated BFB verification; quantitative upper and lower bounds on masses of new heavy scalars derived from these constraints.

Conclusion: BFB constraints vary significantly in restrictiveness across models and symmetries; rigorous enforcement is essential for viable parameter space exploration, and the provided tools enable reliable, model-independent stability checks in beyond-Standard-Model scalar sector studies.

Abstract: A fundamental requirement for the consistency of quantum field theories is that their Higgs potential is lower-bounded, such that no direction in the $\Phi$ field space leads to $V(\Phi)\to \, -\infty$. This work presents a comprehensive analysis of the various theoretical constraints on potentials in extended Higgs sectors, highlighting their relative impact and restrictiveness. We illustrate our study on the potential parameters with two particular Higgs sector extensions: the Real Singlet Extension (RSE) and the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model (2HDM), separating $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$-conserving and $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$-violating cases. In order to do this, we derive and compare these conditions to other studies found in the literature. Additionally, we developed a publicly available code capable of testing positivity conditions in complex potentials, including particularly challenging cases such as the general $\mathbb{Z}_2$-violating 2HDM. Finally, we apply those restrictions to the physical parameters and deduce an upper and a lower bound on the masses of new heavy scalars.

</details>


### [50] [SuperWIMPs in the Kination Era: Imprints on $N_{\rm eff}$ and CMB Spectral Distortions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12148)
*Geneviève Bélanger,Abhishek Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores late-time decays of unstable particles into dark matter (N₁) and neutrinos in the scotogenic model, showing how a kination-dominated early Universe enables correct relic density, while generating CMB μ-distortions, ΔN_eff, and LHC-detectable signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile underabundant dark matter production in standard cosmology by invoking late decays and non-standard expansion history (kination), and to identify correlated cosmological and collider probes of dark matter genesis.

Method: Analyzes the scotogenic model with N₁ as dark matter produced via superWIMP mechanism from late decay of lightest inert doublet scalar; incorporates kination-dominated era to modify Hubble expansion; computes resulting CMB spectral distortions (μ-type), shifts in N_eff, and LHC phenomenology for inert scalars (200–500 GeV).

Result: Kination enables correct N₁ relic density in otherwise excluded parameter space; late decays produce observable CMB μ-distortions and/or ΔN_eff; inert scalars in 200–500 GeV range yield complementary LHC signatures (e.g., displaced vertices or soft leptons); correlations between CMB and collider signals are robust across late-decay DM scenarios.

Conclusion: Late-time decays to DM + neutrinos in non-standard cosmologies generate joint, testable imprints on CMB spectral distortions and LHC observables; future CMB experiments (e.g., PIXIE, Super-PIXIE) and high-luminosity LHC can jointly constrain dark matter production mechanisms and early-Universe thermal history.

Abstract: We investigate how the late-time decays of unstable particles into dark matter and neutrinos can leave measurable imprints on both cosmological observables and collider experiments. We study this mechanism within the scotogenic model, where the lightest singlet fermion $N_1$ serves as the dark matter candidate and is produced via the late decay of the lightest inert doublet scalar via the superWIMP mechanism. By incorporating a kination-dominated era in the early Universe, the altered expansion rate allows to match the observed $N_1$ relic density in parameter regions that would be underabundant in standard cosmology. The energy exchange between the injected neutrinos and photons resulting from the late-time decay can produce CMB spectral $\mu$-distortions, detectable shifts in the effective number of relativistic species $N_{\rm eff}$, or both simultaneously. Concentrating on the lightest component of inert doublet scalars with masses between $200$ and $500~\text{GeV}$, we find that these cosmological signals are accompanied by complementary signatures accessible at the LHC. Although our analysis is derived within the scotogenic framework, our findings apply more broadly to scenarios in which the unstable particle undergoes late-time out-of-equilibrium decay to produce dark matter and non-thermal neutrinos, offering how future CMB measurements and collider searches can jointly probe dark matter production and the early Universe's thermal history.

</details>


### [51] [FORM 5.0 and further developments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12157)
*Ana Costa Pereira,Joshua Davies*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews FORM version 5.0, a computer algebra system essential for symbolic computations in high-energy physics, and highlights recent performance improvements post-release.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To present the new features and enhancements in FORM 5.0 and address the growing computational demands of large-scale symbolic manipulations in theoretical high-energy physics.

Method: Review and analysis of FORM 5.0's features and benchmarking of recent performance optimizations.

Result: Improved computational efficiency and scalability for symbolic manipulation tasks critical to high-energy physics calculations.

Conclusion: FORM 5.0 and its subsequent optimizations significantly advance the capabilities of symbolic computation infrastructure for theoretical particle physics research.

Abstract: In these proceedings we review the features of the recently-released version 5.0 of the FORM computer algebra system, which is one of the core software packages for the large-scale symbolic manipulations required by calculations in theoretical high-energy physics. We also discuss developments since the release of version 5.0, focussing in particular on recent performance improvements.

</details>


### [52] [Constraints on the Real Singlet Scalar Dark Matter Model from Higgs Self-Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12234)
*Hao-Yu Luo,Kun-Peng Chen,Han Hu,Yun Wei,Ming Zhong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes constraints on the real singlet scalar dark matter model using Higgs trilinear coupling (κ₃), perturbative unitarity, and Higgs invisible decay (BR_inv); finds current κ₃ measurements are weaker than unitarity and BR_inv bounds; κ₃ and κ₄ decouple above ~60 TeV; future sub-0.1% κ₃ precision could constrain high-mass WIMP parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
