Motivation: To assess the comparative sensitivity of Higgs trilinear coupling measurements (κ₃) versus established theoretical (perturbative unitarity) and experimental (BR_inv, relic density, direct/indirect detection) constraints in the real singlet scalar DM model.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of parameter dependencies of Higgs trilinear (κ₃) and quartic (κ₄) coupling modifiers; evaluation of perturbative unitarity bounds; comparison with current experimental limits (BR_inv ≤ 0.107, κ₃ precision), relic density, and DM detection constraints.

Result: Current κ₃ measurement precision is weaker than both perturbative unitarity and BR_inv limits; κ₃ and κ₄ exhibit decoupling for m_S ≳ 60 TeV; no existing or near-future collider can surpass existing DM phenomenology constraints—except if κ₃ uncertainty reaches ≤0.1%, enabling sensitivity to high-mass WIMP region.

Conclusion: Higgs self-coupling measurements are currently subdominant probes for this model; their constraining power only becomes competitive in a futuristic ultra-high-precision regime (δκ₃ ≲ 0.1%), highlighting the primacy of DM observables over Higgs EFT probes for heavy scalar singlet DM.

Abstract: Taking the real singlet scalar dark matter model as a representative test case, we examine the constraints imposed on the model's parameter space by Higgs trilinear coupling measurements and perturbative unitarity requirements. For the low dark matter mass regime, the constraining strength delivered by the current highest-precision $\kappa_3$ measurement is notably inferior to the exclusion limit derived from perturbative unitarity requirements, while the perturbative unitarity bound itself remains considerably less restrictive than the constraints extracted from the extant experimental upper limit on the Higgs invisible decay branching ratio $\mathrm{BR}_{\mathrm{inv}} \leq 0.107$. Both the coupling modifiers $\kappa_3$ and $\kappa_4$ exhibit a well-defined decoupling behavior, with their respective magnitudes monotonically decreasing as the dark matter mass $m_S$ increases. As a direct consequence, the corrections to both couplings asymptotically approach zero for dark matter masses in excess of 60 TeV, when the parameter $a_2$ remains within its perturbative validity domain. Neither existing collider measurements of the Higgs trilinear coupling nor measurements targeted for planned future collider facilities possess the requisite sensitivity to impose more stringent constraints on the model's viable parameter space than the well-established bounds derived from experimental probes of dark matter relic density, invisible Higgs decay signatures, dark matter direct detection, and dark matter indirect detection. Nevertheless, under the scenario that the experimental measurement uncertainty of $\kappa_3$ reaches the $\mathcal{O}(0.1\%)$ scale or lower, non-negligible constraints acting on a subset of the large mass parameter space associated with the WIMP dark matter scenario can be expected.

</details>


### [53] [NLO QCD corrections to $W^{+}$-boson decays into doubly heavy baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12253)
*Hong-Hao Ma,Xu-Chang Zheng,Juan-Juan Niu,Xing-Gang Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to inclusive W⁺ → doubly heavy baryon + X decays (Ξ_cc and Ξ_bc) in NRQCD, finding large K-factors (2.27–2.95), significant scale/mass uncertainties, kinematic shifts toward lower energy fractions, and provides updated NLO branching ratios for experimental guidance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for doubly heavy baryon production in W-boson decays by including indispensable NLO QCD corrections within NRQCD, enabling reliable comparison with future LHC or FCC-ee data.

Method: NRQCD factorization applied to inclusive W⁺ → Ξ_cc + c̄ + s̄ + X and W⁺ → Ξ_bc + b̄ + s̄ + X decays; full NLO QCD corrections computed for both color-antitriplet (3̄) and color-sextet (6) S-wave Fock states; decay widths, kinematic distributions, and parametric uncertainties (m_c, m_b, renormalization scale μ_R) systematically analyzed.

Result: K-factors = 2.27 (Ξ_cc) and 2.95 (Ξ_bc); individual-channel K-factors range 1.94–5.53 (max in [³S₁]₆ for Ξ_bc); NLO enhances low invariant-mass region and softens baryon energy spectrum; inclusive BR(W⁺ → Ξ_cc + X) = 8.86×10⁻⁵, BR(W⁺ → Ξ_bc + X) = 3.63×10⁻⁶ (using Γ_W = 2.085 GeV).

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections are essential—enhancing LO widths by >125%—and significantly reduce theoretical uncertainty; the computed NLO branching ratios serve as benchmark predictions for experimental discovery of Ξ_cc and Ξ_bc in W-decay channels.

Abstract: The next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections have been investigated for the production of doubly heavy baryons via the inclusive $W^{+}$-boson decays $W^{+}\to\Xi_{cc}+\bar{c}+\bar{s}+X$ and $W^{+}\to\Xi_{bc}+\bar{b}+\bar{s}+X$ within the nonrelativistic QCD framework. The decay widths, the kinematic distributions, and the uncertainties induced by the heavy-quark masses and the renormalization scale have been analyzed for both the color-antitriplet ($\bar{\mathbf{3}}$) and the color-sextet ($\mathbf{6}$) $S$-wave states. With the central input parameters $m_{c}=1.8~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $m_{b}=5.1~\mathrm{GeV}$, the $K$ factors are $2.27$ for $\Xi_{cc}$ and $2.95$ for $\Xi_{bc}$, i.e., the NLO corrections increase the corresponding LO widths by about $127\%$ and $195\%$, which shows that the NLO corrections are indispensable for quantitative predictions. The individual-channel $K$ factors range from $1.94$ to $5.53$, with the largest one occurring for $\Xi_{bc}$ in the $[{}^{3}S_{1}]_{\mathbf{6}}$ channel. The distributions show that the NLO corrections enhance the low invariant-mass region and shift the baryon energy spectrum toward smaller energy fractions. With $\Gamma_{W}=2.085~\mathrm{GeV}$, the inclusive branching ratios are $8.86\times10^{-5}$ and $3.63\times10^{-6}$ for $\Xi_{cc}$ and $\Xi_{bc}$, respectively, which provide an NLO benchmark for future experimental searches.

</details>


### [54] [From oxygen to lead: testing a common QGP description with high-$p_\perp$ probes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12404)
*Bithika Karmakar,Dusan Zigic,Magdalena Djordjevic*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests whether a unified quark-gluon plasma (QGP) description can simultaneously explain jet quenching (via $R_{AA}$) and elliptic flow ($v_2$, $v_3$) across light-ion (O+O, Ne+Ne) to heavy-ion (Xe+Xe, Pb+Pb) collisions, using event-by-event hydrodynamics coupled to the DREENA energy loss model with fixed microscopic parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Suppression ($R_{AA}$) and finite high-$p_\perp$ elliptic anisotropy ($v_2$) in small systems (O+O, Ne+Ne) challenge whether a QGP forms and whether its properties scale consistently across system sizes — motivating a unified, parameter-consistent theoretical framework.

Method: Event-by-event viscous hydrodynamic bulk evolution constrained by soft-sector data, coupled to DREENA for radiative + collisional parton energy loss; same microscopic parameters applied uniformly to O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb at 10–20% centrality.

Result: The model reproduces observed $R_{AA}$ ordering and predicts comparable $v_2^{\mathrm{SP}}$ magnitudes across all four systems; predicts weak $p_\perp$-dependence of $v_2^{\mathrm{SP}}/(1-R_{AA})$ and $v_2\{4\}/v_2\{2\}$ at high $p_\perp$, and distinct centrality trends for $v_2^{\mathrm{SP}}$ vs $v_3^{\mathrm{SP}}$ in light ions; extends predictions to D/B mesons.

Conclusion: A common QGP description — with fixed transport and energy-loss parameters — quantitatively describes both suppression and flow harmonics across system sizes, supporting QGP formation even in light-ion collisions; the predicted observables provide decisive experimental tests of QGP universality.

Abstract: The observation of suppression and finite high-$p_\perp$ elliptic anisotropy in O+O and Ne+Ne raises the question of whether a common quark--gluon plasma (QGP) description can simultaneously account for both observables across systems from oxygen to lead. We present predictions using event-by-event bulk evolution constrained by soft-sector observables and coupled to DREENA radiative and collisional energy loss, with the same microscopic parameter set for O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb. The calculation reproduces the observed ordering of suppression and predicts elliptic anisotropies of comparable magnitude across the four systems in 10--20$\%$ collisions. We predict a modest centrality dependence of $v_2^{\mathrm{SP}}$ and a more pronounced decrease of $v_3^{\mathrm{SP}}$ toward peripheral light-ion collisions, in contrast to the corresponding trends in larger systems. In light ions, we predict that $v_2^{\mathrm{SP}}/(1-R_{AA})$ is nearly independent of momentum at high $p_\perp$. We further predict that the differential ratio $v_2\{4\}/v_2\{2\}$ depends only weakly on momentum at high $p_\perp$, with magnitudes and centrality trends broadly comparable to those of the measured soft-sector ratios, suggesting that the soft-sector fluctuation pattern persists in hard--soft correlations. Predictions for D and B mesons extend the comparison to probes of different flavors. Taken together, these system-size, harmonic, fluctuation, and flavor dependences provide experimental tests of how far a common QGP description extends.

</details>


### [55] [A singlet framework for the mu problem and type-II seesaw in a generalized NMSSM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12625)
*Xiaokang Du,Yusi Pan,Fei Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a generalized NMSSM where a gauge singlet connects the Higgs bilinear to the type-II seesaw scale, enabling natural generation of μ_eff and vectorlike triplet masses; integrating out triplets yields the Weinberg operator, and neutralino dark matter scenarios (bino-/singlino-like) with high-scale seesaw (~10¹⁵ GeV) and TeV-scale DM are shown to satisfy relic density and direct-detection constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile naturalness of the μ-term in the NMSSM with high-scale type-II seesaw while accommodating viable electroweak-scale dark matter and neutrino mass generation.

Method: Constructs a supersymmetric extension of the NMSSM incorporating a gauge singlet coupled to the Higgs bilinear and SU(2)_L triplet fields; computes μ_eff from singlet VEV and soft SUSY-breaking effects; integrates out heavy triplets to generate the Weinberg operator; analyzes neutralino dark matter phenomenology—including relic abundance via funnels and coannihilation—and confronts results with direct-detection bounds.

Result: Demonstrates that large cancellation between bare μ₀ and singlet-induced μ-term remains natural in the SUSY limit; identifies bino-like and singlino-like neutralino LSPs as viable DM candidates with triplet scales up to ~10¹⁵ GeV; finds spectra satisfying Planck-measured Ωh² and XENONnT/LUX limits via heavy-Higgs funnels or gluino/wino coannihilation.

Conclusion: The proposed framework successfully unifies natural μ-term generation, type-II seesaw neutrino masses, and thermal neutralino dark matter at the TeV scale without fine-tuning, offering testable collider and astroparticle signatures.

Abstract: We study a generalized NMSSM in which a gauge singlet links the Higgs bilinear to the type-II seesaw scale. The singlet vacuum expectation value contributes to $\mu_{\rm eff}$ and generates the supersymmetric mass of a vectorlike triplet pair. Integrating out the triplets gives the Weinberg operator, and their gauge charges admit a possible messenger interpretation. Large cancellation between the bare $\mu_0$ term and the singlet VEV generated bilinear Higgs term that keeps $\mu_{\rm eff}$ near the soft scale is still natural in the supersymmetric limit (by the supersymmetric non-renormalization theorem), although the concrete value of $\mu_{\rm eff}$ also depends slightly on the soft SUSY breaking parameters and finite threshold matching. The studied bino-like and singlino-like neutralino solutions accommodate triplet input scales approaching $10^{15}$ GeV together with electroweak-to-TeV dark matter. Representative spectra consistent with heavy-Higgs funnels or coannihilation with gluinos or winos yield allowed relic abundances while satisfying direct-detection constraints.

</details>


### [56] [Angular analysis of the purely baryonic decays $Λ_b^0\to n p\bar p$ and $Λ_b^0\toΛp\bar p$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12630)
*Chao-Qiang Geng,Chao Han*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs an angular analysis of Λb⁰→np̄p and Λb⁰→Λp̄p decays using a phenomenological generalized-factorization model with coherent current contributions; it computes pair-angle distributions, forward-backward asymmetries, Legendre moments, and Λ polarization, revealing strong sensitivity to relative current phases and phase-space cuts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the dynamics of purely baryonic Λb⁰ decays — which are experimentally accessible but theoretically challenging due to nonperturbative QCD effects — by probing angular and spin observables sensitive to interference between competing current contributions.

Method: Phenomenological generalized-factorization approach modeling two coherent current contributions to the same final state; calculation of differential angular observables (pair-angle distributions, forward-backward hemisphere asymmetries, Legendre moments) and Λ polarization; explicit treatment of phase-space cuts (e.g., m(p̄p) < 2.85 GeV) and effective color number Nc^eff = 3.

Result: In Λb⁰→np̄p: FB asymmetry is 4.46×10⁻⁴ in n̄p frame and 0.856 in p̄p frame (Nc^eff=3, zero relative phase); small n̄p asymmetry arises from cancellation across mass bins. In Λb⁰→Λp̄p: m(p̄p) cut modifies angular distributions and shifts rate-weighted Λ longitudinal polarization from −0.803 to −0.954 along flight direction.

Conclusion: Angular and spin observables in these baryonic Λb⁰ decays are highly sensitive to the relative strength and phase of underlying current contributions and to phase-space selection; the analysis provides a benchmark for future theoretical refinements and experimental tests.

Abstract: An angular analysis of the purely baryonic decays $\Lambda_b^0\to np\bar p$ and $\Lambda_b^0\to\Lambda p\bar p$ is presented within a phenomenological generalized-factorization model. Within this model, two current contributions leading to the same final state are combined coherently. Pair-angle distributions, forward-backward hemisphere asymmetries, and Legendre moments are calculated in both channels, together with the polarization of the produced $\Lambda$ for an unpolarized parent. For the neutron channel, the full-region forward-backward asymmetries are $4.46\times10^{-4}$ in the $n\bar p$ frame and $0.856$ in the $p\bar p$ frame at $N_c^{\mathrm{eff}}=3$ and zero additional relative phase. From mass-bin integration, the small first asymmetry is found to arise from compensation between signed contributions from different regions. In the $\Lambda$ channel, the angular distributions are modified by the selection $m(p\bar p)<2.85\,\mathrm{GeV}$, and the rate-weighted longitudinal polarization along the $\Lambda$ flight direction is shifted from $-0.803$ to $-0.954$. These results show how angular and spin observables depend on the relative current contributions and the selected phase-space region. The model-dependent analysis is intended as a first step toward understanding the dynamics of these decays and as a basis for further theoretical studies and comparisons with measurements.

</details>


### [57] [Amplitudes separation and strong-electromagnetic phases determination in the $\boldsymbol{J/ψ}$ decays into baryon-antibaryon pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12673)
*Chiara Alice,Antonio Amoroso,Matteo Barbagiovanni,Monica Bertani,Diego Bettoni,Fabrizio Bianchi,Alberto Bortone,Damiano Cabiati,Alessandro Calcaterra,Gianluigi Cibinetto,Fabio Cossio,Marco Destefanis,Francesca De Mori,Emma Di Fiore,Luciano Fava,Sara Garbolino,Isabella Garzia,Michela Greco,Marco Maggiora,Simonetta Marcello,Federico Matias Melendi,Giulio Mezzadri,Piero Patteri,Simone Pacetti,Angelo Rivetti,Manuel Dionisio Da Rocha Rolo,Francesco Rosini,Marco Scodeggio,Stefano Sosio,Stefano Spataro,Umberto Zarantonello*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extracts the relative phase between strong and electromagnetic amplitudes in vector charmonium decays to spin-1/2 baryon-antibaryon pairs using a flavour-SU(3) effective Lagrangian with vector and tensor currents; it derives electric/magnetic phases, integrated overlap phase, and effective rate angle from common amplitudes, yielding two solutions consistent with Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ scalar scan phase but in tension with several electric-magnetic production phases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the relative phase between strong and electromagnetic amplitudes in vector charmonium → baryon-antibaryon decays, which is crucial for understanding QCD dynamics, final-state interactions, and CP-violation-sensitive observables.

Method: Constructs a parametrization based on partial-wave coefficients derived from a flavour-SU(3) effective Lagrangian incorporating vector and tensor currents; computes electric and magnetic phases, integrated complex overlap phase, and effective rate angle from shared source amplitudes.

Result: Two mathematically allowed phase solutions are identified; both reproduce the measured scalar Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ scan phase within experimental uncertainty, but both exhibit tension with multiple published electric–magnetic production phase measurements.

Conclusion: The relative phase is constrained to two discrete solutions; consistency with the Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ scalar scan supports the theoretical framework, yet discrepancies with other electric–magnetic phase data suggest either underestimated systematic uncertainties or missing physics (e.g., higher partial waves, SU(3)-breaking effects, or non-resonant contributions).

Abstract: We extract the relative phase between strong and electromagnetic amplitudes of the decays of a vector charmonium into spin-$1/2$ baryon-antibaryon pairs. Starting from a flavour-SU(3) effective Lagrangian with vector and tensor currents, we construct a parametrization in terms of partial waves coefficients. The electric and magnetic phases, the phase of the integrated complex overlap and the effective rate angle are derived from the same source amplitudes. We retain two possible solutions and both of them describe the scalar $\Sigma^+\bar\Sigma^-$ scan phase within its uncertainty, while presenting tension with several electric-magnetic production phases.

</details>


### [58] [Nucleon transversity from dihadron data and x-dependent lattice QCD constraint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12745)
*Hongxin Dong,Yuan-Yuan Huang,Tianbo Liu,Xiaomin Shen,Peng Sun,Xiaonu Xiong,Jian-Hui Zhang,Yuxiang Zhao,Yiyu Zhou,Ruilin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a joint neural-network analysis of collinear transversity distributions and dihadron fragmentation functions using data from e⁺e⁻, SIDIS, and polarized pp collisions, incorporating lattice QCD constraints on isovector transversity; the lattice input modifies down-valence (more negative) and up-valence (enhanced at large x) transversity, with the central down-valence result violating Soffer bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve constraints on collinear transversity—particularly flavor-separated quark and antiquark contributions—by combining diverse experimental data with first-principles lattice QCD input on the isovector combination, thereby testing fundamental positivity bounds (e.g., Soffer inequality).

Method: Joint global fit using neural networks to simultaneously extract collinear transversity distributions and dihadron fragmentation functions from three processes: e⁺e⁻ annihilation, semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering (SIDIS), and polarized proton-proton collisions; lattice QCD results for the isovector transversity h₁^u−d are incorporated as external constraints over a broad x range.

Result: Matched fits with and without lattice input describe data comparably well; inclusion of lattice input shifts down-valence transversity toward more negative values and enhances up-valence transversity at large x; the central down-valence distribution violates the Soffer bound |h₁^d_v(x)| ≤ (f₁^d_v(x) + g₁^d_v(x))/2.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD provides complementary, model-independent constraints on transversity that significantly impact flavor separation—especially for down quarks—and expose tension with conventional positivity bounds, urging refined theoretical scrutiny and experimental validation.

Abstract: We extract collinear transversity distributions and dihadron fragmentation functions in a joint neural-network analysis of dihadron production in electron-positron annihilation, semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering, and polarized proton-proton collisions. We incorporate lattice QCD calculation of isovector transversity, which constrains the up-down flavor differences for quarks and antiquarks over broad momentum fraction range complementary to data. Matched fits with and without the lattice input describe the experimental data comparably well. Including the lattice input shifts the down-valence distribution toward more negative values and enhances up-valence transversity at large $x$. The magnitude of the central down-valence result exceeds the reference Soffer inequalities, motivating further test of positivity constraints.

</details>


### [59] [Electroweak Parton Distribution Functions for Future Lepton Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12793)
*Tao Han,Wolfgang Kilian,Yang Ma,Krzysztof Mękała,Jürgen Reuter,Keping Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper implements Electroweak Parton Distribution Functions (EW PDFs) in the Monte Carlo generator Whizard to model collinear electroweak radiation at future high-energy lepton colliders, enabling resummation of large logarithms and a partonic description of processes dominated by photon and gauge boson splittings from leptons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: At future multi-TeV lepton colliders (e.g., muon colliders or high-energy e+e− linear colliders), collinear electroweak radiation dominates; standard fixed-order calculations fail due to large Sudakov logarithms, necessitating a resummation framework—EW PDFs—akin to QCD PDFs but for electroweak bosons.

Method: Implementation of the EW PDF formalism within the Whizard Monte Carlo event generator, including interface to evolution equations, factorization of collinear splittings, and consistent matching to full fixed-order matrix elements; comparison with Effective Weak Boson Approximation (EWBA) and validation across benchmark processes.

Result: Successful integration of EW PDFs into Whizard; demonstrated applicability to key SM and BSM processes; quantified improvements over EWBA and fixed-order in regimes with high invariant mass or forward production; established reliability for electroweak jet studies.

Conclusion: EW PDFs in Whizard provide a robust, resummed, parton-level framework essential for precision phenomenology and BSM searches at future lepton colliders, paving the way toward the 'electroweak jet regime' where weak bosons exhibit jet-like collinear dynamics.

Abstract: At future high-energy colliders, such as a multi-TeV muon collider or the high-energy stages of an $e^+e^-$ linear collider, collinear radiation of electroweak gauge bosons becomes a dominant phenomenon. In this regime, many key processes are primarily initiated by photons and gauge bosons originating from lepton splittings. Electroweak Parton Distribution Functions (EW PDFs) provide an appropriate framework to describe these interactions by resumming large collinear logarithms arising from all possible Standard Model splittings, thereby enabling a partonic interpretation of the hard scattering. The formalism is particularly suitable for studying new physics beyond the Standard Model in the electroweak sector at higher scales. In this paper, we present the implementation of the EW PDF formalism within the Monte Carlo event generator \texttt{Whizard}. We demonstrate several applications of this framework and analyze its interplay with full fixed-order calculations as well as the purely kinematic Effective Weak Boson Approximation. We highlight scenarios where the EW PDF approach is especially useful for phenomenology and physics-potential studies for future lepton colliders. The EW PDF formalism is a vital step towards the ``electroweak jet regime'' where electroweak particles behave similarly to QCD jets.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [60] [Smooth Rigidity Near Extremal Horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11495)
*Ye Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves ambiguity in smooth Einstein spacetime extensions across degenerate Killing horizons by introducing a spacelike graph condition on the orbit space metric; it establishes local isometry of vacuum germs with matching transverse jets in d ≥ 4, provides spectral and weighted-matching criteria for classification, extends to Einstein–Maxwell and rotating Kerr–de Sitter/Nariai settings, and yields rigidity results without assuming axial symmetry for small rotation parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the non-uniqueness problem in smooth extensions of Einstein metrics across degenerate Killing horizons—where identical transverse Taylor series do not guarantee isometric spacetimes—and to establish geometric conditions ensuring uniqueness and rigidity.

Method: Uses a terminal wave-map gauge combined with energy estimates on the orbit cylinder; introduces a spacelike graph condition over compact horizon sections; applies Lichnerowicz spectrum analysis for positive-Einstein sections; employs tensor estimates and finite-regularity arguments; extends techniques to Einstein–Maxwell and rotating (Kerr–de Sitter/Nariai) settings.

Result: Smooth vacuum Einstein germs with identical marked transverse jets are locally isometric near compact horizon sections in d ≥ 4 when the orbit-space metric admits a spacelike graph; finite-weighted matching suffices for isometry; for positive-Einstein sections, parallel first transverse data fully classify smooth germs; multiwarped realizations exist even with negative-curvature directions; rigidity holds for charged-Nariai and rotating-Nariai/Kerr–de Sitter with a²/r₀² ≤ 1/8 without axial symmetry assumption.

Conclusion: The spacelike graph condition eliminates jet-based ambiguity at degenerate horizons; smooth classification reduces to finite-order data under geometric or spectral constraints; results unify and extend rigidity theorems across vacuum, electrovacuum, and rotating cosmological black hole backgrounds.

Abstract: The Einstein equations constrain the transverse Taylor series at a degenerate Killing horizon, but even complete knowledge of that series need not determine a smooth spacetime: a nonzero difference can vanish to every order at the horizon. We show that this ambiguity disappears when the limiting metric on the space of Killing orbits admits a spacelike graph over a compact horizon section. In dimensions $d\geq4$, smooth vacuum Einstein germs with the same marked transverse jets are then locally isometric on a two-sided neighborhood of the whole section. The proof combines a terminal wave-map gauge with energy estimates on the orbit cylinder and yields a finite weighted matching criterion. For unwarped horizons with positive-Einstein sections, a condition on the full Lichnerowicz spectrum reduces the smooth classification to parallel first transverse data. All such data have convergent multiwarped Einstein realizations, including examples whose sections have negative sectional-curvature directions. We also obtain a finite-regularity version for round sections. The continuation argument extends to four-dimensional Einstein--Maxwell theory and gives smooth charged-Nariai rigidity. Finally, rotating Nariai data admit the required spacelike graph throughout the regular branch; an all-order tensor estimate yields smooth Kerr--de Sitter or rotating-Nariai rigidity for $a^2/r_0^2\leq1/8$, without assuming ambient axial symmetry.

</details>


### [61] [Direct product geometries in $n$-dimensional linear, quadratic, and conformal gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12483)
*Petr Jizba,Tereza Lehečková*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper classifies exact direct-product spacetime solutions in various higher-derivative gravity theories—including linear, pure quadratic-curvature, and higher-dimensional conformal gravity—focusing on dimensional splits like (m₁+m₂), (2+m), and (2+⋯+2), with allowance for nonconstant curvature in 2D subspaces; it reveals algebraic vs. differential constraints on geometries, identifies absence of standard static black holes in conformal gravity for n > 4, and provides explicit solutions in 4D and 6D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify exact solutions of direct-product spacetimes in beyond-GR gravity theories—especially where conventional symmetry-based or ansatz-driven methods falter—and to understand how solution structure depends on theory-specific couplings and dimensionality, including implications for black-hole physics and conformal equivalence.

Method: Analytic classification of n-dimensional direct-product metrics M^{m₁} × M^{m₂} (and generalizations) under field equations of linear gravity, general pure quadratic-curvature gravity (R², R_{μν}R^{μν}, R_{μνρσ}R^{μνρσ}), and a higher-dimensional conformal gravity extension; derivation of necessary and sufficient conditions (algebraic or ODE-type) for admissible curvature profiles; analysis of conformal mappings, Petrov classification, and electromagnetic compatibility.

Result: Solution spaces are fully classified: metrics with nonconstant-curvature 2D factors obey either algebraic curvature-profile constraints or a second-order ODE (in a special quadratic-gravity subclass); constant-curvature mD factors yield purely algebraic relations; conformal gravity excludes standard static black holes for n > 4; explicit 4D and 6D analytic solutions are constructed and their geometric/physical properties (e.g., Petrov type, EM coupling) characterized.

Conclusion: Direct-product ansätze remain highly effective for exact solutions in higher-derivative gravities, but solution structure is sharply sensitive to both the gravitational Lagrangian (especially curvature-squared couplings) and dimensional decomposition; the absence of static black holes in higher-dimensional conformal gravity signals a fundamental restriction on its viability as a gravitational alternative in n > 4.

Abstract: Direct-product spacetimes offer a tractable route to exact solutions in gravity theories beyond general relativity. We study $n$-dimensional direct-product spacetimes of $(m_1+m_2)$, $(2+m)$, and $(2+\ldots+2)$ type in linear gravity, general pure quadratic-curvature gravity, and one of the recently proposed higher-dimensional extensions of conformal gravity, allowing the two-dimensional subspaces to have nonconstant curvature. We classify the resulting solution spaces and determine how the allowed geometries depend on the gravitational theory and, in quadratic gravity, on the curvature couplings. Metrics containing two-dimensional part(s) of nonconstant curvature are governed either by algebraic conditions on a curvature profile or, in one special class of quadratic gravity, by a differential equation. Relations governing metrics with only constant-curvature $m$-dimensional subspaces are purely algebraic. We further examine conformal relations to linear-gravity solutions, generalized Petrov types, and compatibility with electromagnetic sources. Particularly noteworthy is that standard static black-hole geometries are absent from the direct-product sector of the considered form of conformal gravity for $n>4$. Four- and six-dimensional cases are analyzed in detail, and explicit analytic solutions illustrating the resulting structure are presented whenever possible.

</details>


### [62] [Analogue Gravity: the Subcritical Case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10006)
*F.Belgiorno,S.L. Cacciatori,A.Viganò*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analytically investigates quantum pair creation in subcritical analogue gravity with subluminal dispersion, showing that complex turning points enable mode conversion and non-Planckian pair creation even without horizons—contrary to naive WKB predictions—and identifies a critical regime where pair creation vanishes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether Hawking-like pair creation can occur in subcritical analogue gravity systems lacking event horizons, particularly for subluminal dispersion and weakly dispersive media, where standard WKB fails due to absence of real turning points.

Method: Analytical treatment using the Corley model for weakly dispersive media; application of overbarrier scattering formalism from quantum mechanics; careful analysis of complex turning points and associated Stokes phenomena to capture mode conversion beyond naive WKB.

Result: Demonstration of non-Planckian pair creation in the subcritical white hole configuration without horizons or Hawking partners; identification of a critical regime between subcritical and transcritical cases where pair creation is suppressed; qualitative agreement with prior numerical and semi-analytical studies.

Conclusion: Pair creation in analogue gravity does not strictly require horizons or Planckian spectra; complex turning point analysis is essential for capturing mode conversion in subcritical regimes, and a no-pair-creation critical regime exists, refining the phase diagram of analogue black/white hole scattering.

Abstract: We focus on the subcritical case in Analogue Gravity for a subluminal dispersion relation, and investigate the problem of pair creation in the absence of an horizon analytically, for the subcritical white hole case. We limit our attention to weakly dispersive media. In particular, we take into account for simplicity the Corley model and adopt an approach inspired by quantum mechanics in the so-called overbarrier scattering. The presence of complex turning points is shown to play a fundamental role in allowing a mode conversion which would be absent in a naive WKB approach. We show that there is still a pair creation phenomenon, which, in the subcritical case, is non-Planckian and without a Hawking partner. Our fully analytical results are qualitatively in agreement with the literature at hand. Furthermore we confirm the existence of a critical regime betwen the subcritical and the transcritical ones where no pair creation occurs.

</details>


### [63] [Shallow-water black holes and lattice superradiance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10539)
*Antonino Flachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates superradiance in a rotating lattice fluid analogue black hole, where the horizon is defined by a topographic sill, enabling separation of horizon-driven energy extraction from viscous dissipation; results show outward flux for co-rotating waves slower than horizon rotation, and confirm inviscid horizon dominance far from band edges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate the role of the event horizon in superradiance from dissipative effects (e.g., viscosity) in fluid analogues of black holes, which has been experimentally challenging in prior setups.

Method: Realization of a rotating analogue black hole in a shallow-water vortex on a lattice fluid, with a horizon defined by a topographic sill creating a supercritical layer acting as a one-way membrane; measurements of wave energy flux across the horizon under varying lattice viscosities and rotational configurations.

Result: Superradiant energy extraction (outward flux) occurs only for co-rotating waves with pattern speed less than horizon rotation; inward flux observed otherwise; horizon contribution dominates extraction far from band edge, while viscous dissipation contributes up to ~50% near band edge at operational viscosity and vanishes at lowest viscosity.

Conclusion: The drain-anchored, topography-defined horizon enables clean separation of horizon physics from bulk dissipation, confirming superradiance as an inviscid, horizon-localized process; this platform provides a scalable, low-cost testbed for analogue gravity phenomena.

Abstract: Rotating black holes can amplify waves that scatter off them, a process known as superradiance that fluid analogues bring into the laboratory. Separating the horizon's role from dissipation in the surrounding fluid remains difficult. Here we realise a rotating analogue black hole in a lattice fluid: a shallow-water vortex whose horizon is set by a topographic sill, so that the only sink lies behind a supercritical layer that lets nothing out. We measure superradiance as energy extraction: the flux at the horizon is outward for co-rotating waves whose pattern rotates more slowly than the horizon, and inward for faster waves, counter-rotating waves and a non-rotating hole. A scan over three lattice viscosities separates the extraction at the horizon, which is inviscid, from that of the viscous fluid inside the corotation radius: well below the band edge the horizon is the absorber; near the edge the viscous share is half at the operating viscosity and vanishes at the lowest. A drain-anchored horizon, as in water-tank experiments, gives the same signs. Our analysis gives insight into the mechanism of superradiance and offers a cheap, flexible platform for designing analogue-gravity experiments.

</details>


### [64] [Engineering a digital acoustic black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10540)
*Antonino Flachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates the construction of an analogue black hole using a synchronous digital circuit implemented on an FPGA, defining and measuring analogue gravitational phenomena—including horizon stability, causal structure, ringdown, and rotational superradiance—while showing information leakage from within the horizon decays exponentially with depth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize a controllable, reconfigurable, and experimentally accessible analogue gravity system that captures key features of black hole physics—such as event horizons, causal structure, and quantum-like information dynamics—using digital hardware rather than traditional fluid or optical analogues.

Method: Design and implementation of a synchronous digital circuit on an FPGA that emulates acoustic metric geometry; definition of sound speed, flow velocity, and horizon in terms of logic gate timing and signal propagation; electrical probing via signal generator and oscilloscope to measure horizon stability, causal cone, quasinormal mode ringdown, and rotational superradiance; analysis of bit-flip perturbations inside the horizon to quantify information leakage.

Result: Experimental observation of a stable digital horizon, its causal cone, damped ringdown modes, and rotational superradiance; exponential suppression of bit-flip signal leakage with distance from the horizon; demonstration that the system behaves as a cellular automaton realizing analogue black hole dynamics.

Conclusion: Digital circuits constitute a novel, scalable, and programmable platform for analogue gravity experiments, enabling precise control and measurement of black-hole-like phenomena, including information-theoretic aspects such as horizon-localized perturbation decay.

Abstract: We show how to design and build an analogue black hole as a synchronous digital circuit. We define the sound speed, the flow and the horizon of such a circuit in terms of its own logic, and realise one on a field-programmable gate array, where the black hole is probed electrically with a signal generator and an oscilloscope. We measure the stability of the horizon, its causal cone, the ringdown and rotational superradiance. A single flipped bit inside the horizon leaks out by an amount that falls exponentially with depth. Digital circuits thus form a reconfigurable class of gravitational analogues and realise an analogue black hole as a cellular automaton.

</details>


### [65] [ERG, axions, and a PT-symmetric accelerating Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10584)
*Nick E. Mavromatos,Sarben Sarkar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper proposes that a repulsive gravitational phase—potentially explaining cosmic acceleration without dark energy—may arise from a PT-symmetric gravitational theory with anomalous Chern-Simons-axion couplings; this transition is conjectured to occur via an infrared phase transition signaled by singularities in ERG beta functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain late-time cosmic acceleration without invoking dark energy, by exploring whether a non-Hermitian but PT-symmetric gravitational theory—motivated by string-inspired axion–Chern–Simons couplings—can yield repulsive gravity.

Method: Uses the Exact Renormalisation Group (ERG) framework under modified local potential approximation to analyze beta functions of chiral anomalous axion–photon couplings; identifies singularities as indicators of infrared phase transitions from Hermitian to PT-symmetric effective gravity.

Result: Singularities in ERG beta functions suggest an IR phase transition to a PT-symmetric gravitational sector where Newton's 'constant' becomes effectively negative (repulsive), potentially driving cosmic acceleration.

Conclusion: A PT-symmetric gravitational theory—emerging from axionic anomalies and ERG-determined phase structure—offers a viable, dark-energy-free mechanism for late-universe acceleration.

Abstract: In conventional quantum mechanics, the Hamiltonian is required to be Hermitian, ensuring real energy eigenvalues. Certain non-Hermitian Hamiltonians can also possess entirely real spectra when they are invariant under the combined action of parity ($\mathcal P$) and time reversal ($\mathcal T$). Such Hamiltonians are called unbroken parity--time ($\mathcal{PT}$)-symmetric. We conjecture that, in a string-inspired scenario, a repulsive phase of gravity may be due to a $\mathcal{PT}$-symmetric gravitational theory, characterised by anomalous Chern-Simons terms, coupled to axion fields. The conjecture is based on the behaviour of the chiral anomalous couplings of these axions to electromagnetic potentials. Within the Exact Renormalisation Group (ERG) framework in the modified local potential approximation, we argue that singularities in the ERG beta functions for such couplings may indicate an infrared phase transition. At the energy scales of these singularities, we conjecture that the field theory transitions from the Hermitian phase to a ${\mathcal P}{\mathcal T}$-- symmetric theory, of gravity, characterised by a repulsive Netwon's ``constant''. This may induce the acceleration observed in the Universe at late eras, without the need for a dark--energy component.

</details>


### [66] [Scalar-tensor reconstruction of coherent quantum black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10616)
*R. Casadio,A. Giusti,R. da Rocha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs scalar-tensor and Horndeski realisations of spherically symmetric black hole geometries emerging from quantum coherent states with finite core and extension; it identifies domain restrictions on kinetic positivity and shows semiclassical gravity cannot fully capture the quantum-originated interior/exterior geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge quantum-inspired black hole geometries (with finite core and extension) with classical effective field theory descriptions, and assess the limits of semiclassical gravity in capturing quantum-originated structure.

Method: Reconstruction of scalar-tensor theories (non-minimal coupling, kinetic coupling, scalar potential) and Horndeski theory from a given spherically symmetric metric; analysis of kinetic term positivity domains and validity ranges for horizonless, quantum-coherent-state-derived geometries.

Result: A consistent scalar-tensor realisation is obtained where the kinetic term is positive only in finite radial domains; boundaries of these domains naturally link core size and spatial extension for fixed mass; an equivalent Horndeski description with derivative coupling is also derived, with explicitly determined domain of validity; neither framework includes an event horizon or reproduces arbitrarily large interior and exterior regions simultaneously.

Conclusion: The quantum origin of the black hole geometry lies partially beyond semiclassical gravity — horizonless, finite-core geometries cannot be fully captured by standard scalar-tensor or Horndeski reconstructions, indicating intrinsic limitations of effective field theory approaches near the core.

Abstract: We construct a scalar-tensor realisation of spherically symmetric black hole geometries that effectively arise from quantum coherent states characterised by a finite core and finite extension. We determine the non-minimal coupling to the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian, kinetic coupling, and scalar potential and show that the kinetic term is positive only in finite domains. For fixed black hole mass, the boundaries of these domains provide a natural mechanism that relates the core size and spatial extension to the dynamics of the scalar-tensor realisation. We further show that the same geometry can also be obtained from a Horndeski action with derivative coupling and determine its domain of validity. Neither reconstruction includes the event horizon nor can it simultaneously reproduce arbitrarily large portions of the interior and exterior of the black hole, whose quantum origin therefore appears to remain partially outside the regime of applicability of semiclassical theories of gravity.

</details>


### [67] [Black-hole ringdown at an exceptional point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10661)
*Kyriakos Destounis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies damped oscillations ('ringing') of rotating, charged black holes in de Sitter space, focusing on exceptional points where photon-sphere and near-horizon modes coalesce; it computes the amplitude of the emergent linear-in-time term in the mode response from constrained Einstein-Maxwell data, demonstrates high-accuracy mode fits (sub-0.015% error), and shows that tuning only the initial perturbation—while holding geometry fixed—can suppress the linear term and shift the apparent ringdown frequency in short-time fits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how black hole ringdown signals behave near exceptional points—where quasinormal modes coalesce—and to quantify the physical impact of the associated algebraic (linear time) terms in gravitational wave modeling, especially for near-extremal or cosmological black holes.

Method: Solves the coupled gravitational–electromagnetic perturbation equations for a Kerr–Newman–de Sitter black hole under constraint-based initial data; identifies exceptional-point degeneracy between photon-sphere and near-horizon modes; computes the amplitude of the Jordan-block-induced linear time factor analytically and numerically; performs high-precision late-time waveform fitting with seven competing modes and compares against exponential-only fits.

Result: The linear-in-time term’s amplitude is quantified; late-time curvature residuals are 0.00383% for the full two-mode (exponential + linear) fit and 0.01401% for a refitted pure exponential; the linear term can be suppressed solely by adjusting initial data while keeping spacetime geometry fixed; short ringdown fits yield geometry-dependent but initial-data-sensitive inferred frequencies near mode coalescence.

Conclusion: Near exceptional points, black hole ringdown is not generically described by simple exponentials: algebraic time dependence arises intrinsically, its amplitude is computable from initial data constraints, and its presence—or suppression—can significantly bias frequency extraction in gravitational-wave inference unless properly modeled.

Abstract: Black holes ring in damped oscillations. At an exceptional point, photon-sphere and near-horizon modes merge, and their response can acquire a linear time factor. We compute its amplitude from constrained Einstein-Maxwell data for a rotating, charged black hole in de Sitter space. With seven competing modes fixed, the late-time curvature error is 0.00383$\%$ for the pair and 0.01401$\%$ for a refitted exponential. We suppress the linear term by tuning the initial disturbance at fixed geometry. Hence, near coalescence, changing only the initial disturbance can shift the frequency inferred from a short ringdown fit.

</details>


### [68] [The Energy-Momentum Tensor of a Compact Object in General Relativity to all Multipole Orders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10783)
*Trevor Scheopner,Felix Lichtner*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a natural basis of independent tensors conjugate to individual multipole moments of compact objects in general relativity, and derives an exact distributional expression for the energy-momentum tensor in terms of reduced multipole moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve degeneracy among spin- and tidal-effect operators in effective field theory descriptions of compact object motion, where multiple operators contribute indistinguishably to observables and affect several multipoles simultaneously.

Method: Uses the Mathisson–Papapetrou–Dixon formalism to construct a canonical basis of independent tensors, each conjugate to exactly one multipole moment; derives the energy-momentum tensor via distributional methods using reduced multipole moments.

Result: A novel exact distributional formula for the energy-momentum tensor of a compact object expressed in terms of its reduced multipole moments and the newly constructed canonically conjugate tensor basis.

Conclusion: The introduced tensor basis eliminates operator degeneracy in multipole expansions, enabling unambiguous identification and separation of physical effects associated with distinct multipole moments in relativistic dynamics of compact objects.

Abstract: In effective field theory approaches to compact object motion in general relativity, spin and tidal effects are encoded by operators formed from the Riemann tensor and its derivatives. Many such operators contribute degenerately to observables, with a single one often affecting several multipole moments of the body. Using the Mathisson--Papapetrou--Dixon formalism, we construct a natural basis of independent tensors which are each canonically conjugate to precisely one multipole. We find the novel exact distributional formula for the energy-momentum tensor of a compact object in terms of its reduced multipole moments and these tensors.

</details>


### [69] [Variational Approach to the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon Equations of Motion for Extended Objects in General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10794)
*Trevor Scheopner,Felix Lichtner*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reviews the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon (MPD) equations for finite-size objects in curved spacetime, constructs them from a gauge-invariant variational principle suitable for effective descriptions of compact objects, clarifies conditions for imposing the covariant spin supplementary condition, and identifies curvature tensors naturally conjugate to multipole moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous, manifestly gauge-invariant foundation for the MPD formalism—essential for modeling relativistic compact objects (e.g., black holes, neutron stars) as effective extended bodies—and to clarify the geometric and physical meaning of spin constraints and multipole–curvature couplings.

Method: Constructs the MPD equations via a manifestly diffeomorphism- and gauge-invariant action principle; systematically derives equations of motion and spin evolution; analyzes the integrability and enforceability of the covariant spin supplementary condition (SSC); identifies the irreducible curvature multipoles dual to object’s mass and current multipoles using symmetry-adapted tensor decompositions.

Result: A fully gauge-invariant variational derivation of the MPD equations; explicit identification of the curvature basis (e.g., electric/magnetic parts of Riemann and its derivatives) conjugate to mass-type and current-type multipole moments; precise conditions (e.g., non-vanishing four-velocity norm, invertibility of spin-tensor projection) under which the covariant SSC can be consistently imposed without loss of degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: The MPD framework is robustly grounded in an invariant action principle, enabling consistent effective-field-theory treatments of compact binaries; the natural curvature–multipole pairing and SSC analysis resolve longstanding ambiguities in defining center-of-mass worldlines and higher-multipole couplings in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: The exact motion of a finite size object in curved spacetime is captured by the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon equations. They associate a unique worldline, momentum, and spin to that object and determine the evolution of such in terms of its fully independent multipole moments. We provide a thorough review of this theory and show how to build it directly via a manifestly gauge invariant variational principle well-suited for effective descriptions of relativistic compact objects. We discuss conditions under which the covariant spin supplementary condition can be enforced and identify the natural basis of curvature tensors conjugate to the object's multipole moments.

</details>


### [70] [Thermodynamic, Optical, and Wave Signatures of Charged Letelier AdS Black Holes with PFDM and Quintessence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10942)
*B. Eslam Panah,S. Gurtas Dogan,A. Guvendi,O. Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a 4D charged Letelier–AdS black hole with string cloud, PFDM, and Kiselev quintessence; it computes thermodynamic quantities, phase-space identities, photon sphere/shadow, and scalar wave scattering, while clarifying interpretive limitations regarding PFDM density sign, mass definition, and quasinormal mode validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize the thermodynamic, geometric-optical, and wave-scattering properties of a phenomenologically enriched AdS black hole geometry incorporating string cloud, PFDM, and quintessence—motivated by modeling astrophysical and dark-energy-modified black holes in a controlled theoretical setting.

Method: Analytical derivation of curvature invariants, horizon thermodynamics (temperature, entropy, electric potential, horizon-based mass), extended-phase-space thermodynamics, photon-sphere and shadow geometry, and radial Helmholtz equation for massless scalar perturbations; careful consistency checks on stress-tensor conventions and asymptotic definitions.

Result: Explicit expressions for temperature, entropy, electric potential, and horizon mass; rectangular cycle work W = 0.095 and efficiency η ≈ 15.39%; photon-sphere condition and finite-radius shadow angle; frequency-dependent effective potential with evanescent intervals and trapping candidates; identification of eikonal barrier-top proximity to photon-sphere scale.

Conclusion: The analysis yields a self-consistent, parameter-specific description of the adopted geometry’s thermodynamic and wave-scattering behavior, but key physical interpretations—such as PFDM energy density sign, conserved mass identification, and quasinormal mode spectrum—require additional assumptions or calculations beyond the current framework.

Abstract: We study a four-dimensional charged Letelier--anti-de Sitter (AdS) black-hole metric containing a cloud of strings, a phenomenological perfect-fluid-dark-matter (PFDM) source, and a Kiselev-type quintessence term. We derive the curvature invariants, horizon temperature, entropy, electric potential, and horizon-based mass function, and analyze local thermal response and the associated extended-phase-space identities. A rectangular benchmark cycle gives $W=0.095000$ and $\eta\simeq15.39\%$ for the explicitly stated geometric-unit parameters; these values are model- and cycle-specific and are not presented as a universal efficiency enhancement. For geometric optics, we derive the photon-sphere condition, critical impact parameter, and finite-radius shadow angle. For a massless, minimally coupled scalar field, we obtain a radial-coordinate Helmholtz equation and identify frequency-dependent barriers, evanescent intervals, and candidate trapping regions; the eikonal barrier-top scale approaches the photon-sphere capture scale. Two qualifications delimit the interpretation. First, with the stated PFDM stress-tensor convention, $\rho_{\rm PFDM}=-\beta/(8\pi r^3)$, so positive $\beta$ describes a negative-density effective source. Second, the mass function follows from the horizon equation, but identifying it with a conserved asymptotic AdS mass requires a separate charge and boundary-normalization analysis. The potential-well analysis likewise does not determine complex quasinormal frequencies. We therefore present the results as a consistent, parameter-specific analysis of the adopted phenomenological geometry, while distinguishing algebraic thermodynamic identities from conclusions requiring asymptotic-charge or spectral calculations.

</details>


### [71] [Nonlinear Scalarization of Reissner-Nordström Black Holes in Scalar-Torsion-Maxwell Teleparallel Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11686)
*Joaquín Robledo,Yerko Vásquez,P. A. González*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs nonlinearly scalarized Reissner-Nordström black holes in scalar-torsion-Maxwell teleparallel gravity with polynomial nonminimal coupling to the torsion scalar; it identifies node-dependent hairy black hole branches via numerical solutions and explains saturation via effective potential minima.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore a nonlinear scalarization mechanism for charged black holes in teleparallel gravity, circumventing linear tachyonic instabilities by using higher-order (e.g., sixth-order) coupling functions.

Method: Analytical study of the decoupling limit and nonlinear evolution of finite-amplitude scalar perturbations; numerical integration of fully coupled scalar-torsion-Maxwell field equations to construct asymptotically flat, charged, hairy black hole solutions; classification by scalar field node number and analysis of metric/torsion deviations.

Result: Existence of multiple stationary, asymptotically flat, scalar-hairy black hole solutions classified by node number; sixth-order coupling enables nonlinear saturation via emergent local minima in the effective potential; higher-order coupling suppresses higher-node branches; quantified scalar backreaction on metric functions and torsion scalar.

Conclusion: Nonlinear scalarization is viable in scalar-torsion-Maxwell teleparallel gravity with appropriate polynomial couplings, yielding stable, node-structured hairy black holes distinct from Reissner-Nordström, with implications for strong-field gravity tests and no-hair theorem extensions.

Abstract: We investigate the nonlinear scalarization of Reissner-Nordström black holes in scalar-torsion-Maxwell teleparallel gravity, in which the scalar field is nonminimally coupled to the Weitzenböck invariant. We consider polynomial coupling functions that avoid tachyonic instabilities at the linear level, thereby exploring a scalarization mechanism driven by nonlinear effects. In the decoupling limit, we study the nonlinear evolution of finite-amplitude scalar perturbations and show that the inclusion of a sixth-order coupling term allows the scalar field to dynamically evolve toward nontrivial stationary configurations. This behavior can be understood in terms of the effective potential, which develops nontrivial local minima that provide a mechanism for nonlinear saturation. By numerically solving the fully coupled field equations, we construct asymptotically flat charged black hole solutions endowed with scalar hair. We classify these solutions according to the number of nodes in the scalar field profile, identifying multiple branches whose structure and multiplicity depend on the coupling parameters. We further show that the higher-order coupling progressively suppresses higher-node branches and characterize the scalar backreaction through deviations of the metric functions and the torsion scalar from the Reissner-Nordström background.

</details>


### [72] [Thermodynamics of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson black holes from background subtraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12683)
*Chang-Xu Yan,Zili Mou,Yu Tian,Hongbao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves gauge ambiguity for charged rotating black holes in a Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field by uniquely fixing the time generator and horizon electric potential gauge via an integrability condition and asymptotically flat initial conditions; mass and angular momentum are then computed via background subtraction against Minkowski spacetime, ensuring consistency with the first law, CR relation, and Smarr formula.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The non-asymptotic flatness of charged rotating black holes in a Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field leads to ambiguity in choosing the time generator and horizon electric potential gauge, hindering consistent thermodynamic definitions.

Method: Uses a newly established integrability condition with asymptotically flat charged rotating black hole gauge as initial condition to uniquely fix the gauge; computes mass and angular momentum via background subtraction using Minkowski spacetime as reference.

Result: Gauge is uniquely fixed; mass and angular momentum are consistently defined; first law of black hole thermodynamics, CR relation, and Smarr formula are all satisfied.

Conclusion: The gauge ambiguity is resolved, enabling well-defined conserved quantities and full thermodynamic consistency for charged rotating black holes in Bertotti-Robinson magnetic fields.

Abstract: The non-asymptotic flatness of charged rotating black holes immersed in the Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field causes the ambiguity in the gauge choice for the time generator and the horizon electric potential. By using the newly established form of the integrability condition together with the gauge choice for the asymptotically flat charged rotating black holes as the initial condition, we have found that the gauge can be uniquely fixed. With this fixed gauge, we further obtain the mass and angular momentum by the background subtraction method with the Minkowski spacetime as the reference spacetime. As a result, not only is the first law of black hole thermodynamics automatically satisfied, but also both the CR relation and the Smarr formula are obeyed.

</details>


### [73] [Scalar quasinormal modes of the exact spinning Morris--Thorne wormhole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12838)
*Mark Sukaiti,Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a massless scalar field on a rotating Morris-Thorne wormhole using high-precision spectral methods; it finds all converged modes are damped and lie outside the superradiant window, with asymptotic approach to its upper edge governed by (mj)^{-1/2} scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess linear stability of rotating traversable wormholes by analyzing scalar field QNMs on an exact spinning Morris-Thorne background — a two-ended geometry with relative frame-dragging at asymptotic infinities.

Method: Formulates the wave equation on the full two-ended spacetime, extracts axis behavior and outgoing boundary conditions at both infinities, compactifies the radial coordinate, and solves a quadratic eigenvalue problem via 2D Chebyshev collocation; validates against Kerr spectra and direct integration.

Result: QNMs are mirror-symmetric about Re(ω̂) = 3πjm/2; each multipole family depends only on mj; angular dependence is exactly represented by three Chebyshev polynomials for L = |m|, |m|+1, |m|+2; all converged modes (for m=1–3, j≤19) are damped and lie outside the superradiant window 0 < ω/(mΩ₋) < 1; large-mj modes approach the upper edge as ω ∼ Ω₋ − C(mj)^{−1/2}.

Conclusion: The scalar sector shows no unstable modes within computed parameter range, but full stability requires analysis of the unresolved scalar spectrum and coupled metric-matter perturbations.

Abstract: We study the quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a minimally coupled massless scalar field on the exact spinning Morris-Thorne wormhole. The problem is posed on the complete two-ended geometry, whose asymptotic rest frames rotate relative to each other. After extracting the axis behaviour and the outgoing phases at both infinities, we compactify the radial coordinate and construct a quadratic eigenvalue problem by two-dimensional Chebyshev collocation. The analytic background yields exact constraints on the spectrum. At fixed azimuthal number $m$, the frequencies $\widehat{\omega}=r_0\omega$ are mirror symmetric about $\operatorname{Re}\widehat{\omega}=3\pi jm/2$, where $r_0$ is the throat radius and $j=J/r_0^2$ is the dimensionless angular momentum. Each multipole family depends on $m$ and $j$ only through $mj$ and three angular Chebyshev polynomials represent exactly the multipoles $L=|m|,|m|+1,|m|+2$. A conserved flux confines nonthreshold real quasinormal frequencies to the superradiant window $0<\omega/(m\Omega_-)<1$, where $\Omega_-=3\pi J/r_0^3$. For the sampled sectors $m=1,2,3$ and spins up to $j=19$, every converged frequency is damped and lies outside this window. Independent integration verifies the low-lying modes, while Kerr spectra provide a separate benchmark for the two-dimensional solver. A limiting radial equation explains why the computed mode frequencies approach the upper edge of the superradiant window with frequency offsets proportional to $(mj)^{-1/2}$ at large $mj$. A complete stability assessment requires further analysis of the unresolved scalar spectrum and of perturbations of the coupled metric-matter system.

</details>


### [74] [Surface Enhanced Anisotropy and Its Impacts on Massive Neutron Star Configurations in Einstein Gravity : Stability, Structure, and Multimessenger Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09974)
*Ritabrata Biswas,Arpan Bhattacharya,Subhajit Pal*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs physically viable anisotropic neutron star models in General Relativity using a cubic equation of state and Buchdahl class-I metric, satisfying all key physical and stability criteria, and achieving consistency with multimessenger astrophysical constraints (GW170817, NICER) and Bayesian parameter estimation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a self-consistent, physically realistic model of anisotropic neutron stars within Einstein's General Relativity that satisfies fundamental physical requirements (regularity, causality, stability) and matches modern multimessenger observational constraints.

Method: Employs a third-order polynomial equation of state (p_r = Aρ + Bρ² + Cρ³), Buchdahl class-I metric ansatz (e^{q(r)} = L(1+τr²)/(L−τr²)), interior–exterior matching via Darmois-Israel junction conditions, analysis of hydrostatic equilibrium via generalized TOV equation, stability via adiabatic index and Herrera cracking condition, causality via sound speeds, and Bayesian MCMC for statistical inference of tidal Love number, compactness, and anisotropy parameter.

Result: All stellar solutions satisfy finiteness, positivity, monotonicity of energy density and pressures; vanishing central anisotropy; stable hydrostatic equilibrium; sub-Buchdahl compactness; acceptable surface redshifts; causality-compliant radial and tangential sound speeds; dynamical stability per Chandrasekhar criterion; maximum masses exceeding observed pulsar masses (including lower mass-gap region); tidal deformabilities consistent with GW170817 and NICER; and strong agreement between theoretical M–C relation and observations.

Conclusion: The framework provides a physically viable, mathematically consistent, and observationally compatible description of anisotropic neutron stars in General Relativity, supporting the relevance of pressure anisotropy in dense stellar interiors and enabling robust inference of EoS parameters from multimessenger data.

Abstract: Anisotropic neutron-star configurations are investigated within Einstein's General Relativity using a third-order polynomial equation of state, (p_r=A\rho+B\rho^2+C\rho^3), and the Buchdahl class-I metric ansatz, (e^{q(r)}=\frac{L(1+\tau r^2)}{L-\tau r^2}). Physically viable interior solutions are constructed and smoothly matched to the exterior Schwarzschild spacetime using the Darmois-Israel junction conditions. The stellar properties are examined through the matter distribution, pressure anisotropy, hydrostatic equilibrium, compactness, surface redshift, tidal deformability, causality, and dynamical stability. The energy density and radial and tangential pressures remain finite, positive, and monotonically decreasing throughout the interior, while the anisotropy vanishes at the centre and increases smoothly toward the surface. Generalized Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equation confirms equilibrium through the balance of gravitational, hydrostatic, and anisotropic forces. Furthermore, adiabatic index satisfies the Chandrasekhar stability criterion, and both sound speeds obey the causality and Herrera cracking conditions. The compactness remains below the Buchdahl limit, with physically acceptable surface redshifts. The predicted maximum masses exceed the corresponding observed masses, with some configurations extending into the lower mass-gap region. The tidal deformabilities are consistent with multimessenger constraints from GW170817 and NICER. In addition, the theoretical mass-compactness relation and its prediction intervals show good agreement with observational estimates. Finally, Bayesian Markov Chain Monte Carlo analysis provides statistically reliable constraints on the tidal Love number, compactness, and perturbation parameter. These results establish a physically viable and observationally compatible framework for describing anisotropic neutron stars within General Relativity.

</details>


### [75] [Periodic orbits and gravitational wave radiation of a phantom Dirac--Born--Infeld regular black hole Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09975)
*N. Heidari,A. A. Araújo Filho,Iarley P. Lobo,V. B. Bezerra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies periodic orbits and gravitational wave emissions in the spacetime of a regular black hole modeled by a phantom Dirac–Born–Infeld scalar field, analyzing geodesics, orbital dynamics (including zoom-whirl trajectories and periastron advance of S2 star), and quadrupole-approximated GW waveforms to probe constraints on the regular core parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe observational signatures—particularly orbital dynamics and gravitational waveforms—that distinguish phantom Dirac–Born–Infeld regular black holes from classical black holes, using astrophysical tests like S2 star periastron advance and periodic GW signals.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of timelike geodesics; construction of effective potential and classification of circular/periodic (zoom-whirl) orbits; sensitivity analysis of orbital closure under energy perturbations; comparison with S2 star periastron advance data; quadrupole-based numerical kludge computation of gravitational waveforms including both polarizations.

Result: Identified stable periodic zoom-whirl trajectories sensitive to the regular core parameter; quantified deviations in periastron advance relative to Kerr/Schwarzschild predictions; derived characteristic GW waveform modulations (e.g., frequency evolution, harmonic content) induced by the regular core; established observational bounds on the core parameter from S2 data.

Conclusion: The regular core significantly alters orbital structure and gravitational wave morphology, making periodic orbits and associated GWs promising probes for testing phantom DBI regularity; S2 periastron advance already provides nontrivial constraints, and future LISA-like detectors could resolve core-induced waveform features.

Abstract: We investigate periodic orbits and gravitational wave radiation in the spacetime of an asymptotically flat regular black hole supported by a phantom Dirac--Born--Infeld scalar field. Starting from the timelike geodesic equations, we examine the effective potential and the characteristic circular orbits and construct periodic zoom--whirl trajectories. We further explore the sensitivity of orbital closure to small energy perturbations and examine the periastron advance of the $S2$ star around Sgr A$^*$ to assess possible constraints on the regular core parameter. The gravitational wave signals associated with representative periodic trajectories are then calculated within a quadrupole based numerical kludge approximation, considering both polarization modes. Particular attention is given to the influence of the regular core on orbital configurations and waveform characteristics, providing a complementary perspective on gravitational aspects of phantom Dirac--Born--Infeld regular black holes.

</details>


### [76] [Observational signatures of Bardeen black holes under nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09979)
*Haiyuan Feng,Dan-Dan Cui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the shadow and optical appearance of the Bardeen black hole in nonlinear electrodynamics (NLED), deriving its effective optical geometry, analyzing photon dynamics, and computing synthetic images for thin-disk and spherical accretion models; it finds that magnetic charge g/M systematically modifies shadow compactness, ring multiplicity, and intensity peaks—offering distinguishable NLED signatures from standard Bardeen BHs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable strong-field signatures distinguishing the NLED-modified Bardeen black hole from its standard counterpart, particularly via black hole shadow morphology and accretion-induced photon ring structures.

Method: Derivation of the effective metric for photon propagation in NLED-Bardeen spacetime; analytical and numerical analysis of photon sphere, horizon-photon sphere-shadow inequality, critical impact parameter, and null geodesics; ray-tracing simulations for thin-disk and spherical (static/infalling) accretion models to generate synthetic optical images and intensity profiles.

Result: The NLED-Bardeen BH violates the full horizon-photon sphere-shadow inequality; increasing g/M shrinks photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter, yielding a more compact shadow and inward-shifted emission peak; thin-disk images show g/M-dependent ring multiplicity (3/2/1 peaks for Models 1–3); spherical accretion yields intensity peaking near critical impact parameter (static) or Doppler-suppressed intensity (infalling).

Conclusion: Shadow size, photon ring structure (number and position of intensity peaks), and intensity distribution under different accretion geometries constitute robust observational probes of NLED effects in the strong gravitational field, enabling potential discrimination between NLED-Bardeen and standard Bardeen black holes.

Abstract: We investigate the shadow and optical appearance of the Bardeen black hole (BH) within nonlinear electrodynamics (NLED). Since photon propagation in nonlinear electromagnetic field can be equivalently described as null-geodesic motion in the effective metric induced by NLED, we derive the corresponding effective optical geometry of the NLED-Bardeen BH. Furthermore, by examining the horizon-photon sphere-shadow inequality, we demonstrate that the NLED-Bardeen BH does not satisfy the complete inequality chain. In addition, we investigate photon dynamics and the corresponding optical images generated by both thin disk and spherical accretion models. Increasing $g/M$ reduces the photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter, resulting in a more compact shadow and an inward shift of the emission peak. For the thin disk accretion model, photon trajectories are classified into direct emission, lensing ring, and photon ring components according to the corresponding impact parameter. We find that Model 1 exhibits three distinct intensity peaks, whereas Models 2 and 3 display two and one peaks. The systematic dependence of these features on the magnetic charge indicates that the NLED-Bardeen BH may exhibit observational signatures distinguishable from those of the Bardeen BH. In static spherical accretion, the intensity peaks near the critical impact parameter because of the enhanced optical path length, while infalling spherical accretion introduces an additional Doppler redshift that suppresses the observed intensity. These results indicate that the shadow size, ring structure, and intensity distribution can serve as potential observational signatures of NLED effects in the strong-field regime.

</details>


### [77] [Scalable early warning for binary neutron-star mergers in third-generation gravitational-wave detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10133)
*Martin Gerini,Rodrigo Tenorio,Davide Gerosa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a CNN-based method using short-time Fourier transforms for low-latency detection of binary neutron-star coalescences in third-generation GW observatories, achieving matched-filter sensitivity with >10⁴× computational speedup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard matched-filter searches are computationally prohibitive for long-duration, Earth-motion-affected signals from third-generation detectors like the Einstein Telescope, hindering low-latency multi-messenger alerts.

Method: A convolutional neural network trained on short-time Fourier transform (STFT) spectrograms; implicitly marginalizes over extrinsic parameters (e.g., sky location, polarization, time of arrival), reducing search space to chirp mass only; approximates the optimal Bayesian detection statistic by learning nonlinear time-frequency signal morphology.

Result: On simulated injections across ETΔ and ET2L configurations and signal durations (5–2 Hz, ~25 min to ~6 hrs), the network matches GPU-optimized matched-filter detection sensitivity while reducing computational cost by >4 orders of magnitude — latency drops from minutes to milliseconds.

Conclusion: The method enables real-time, low-latency detection of pre-merger binary neutron-star signals, making rapid electromagnetic and neutrino follow-up feasible and unlocking early-warning multi-messenger astronomy with third-generation gravitational-wave detectors.

Abstract: Third-generation gravitational-wave observatories will observe binary neutron-star coalescences for hours before merger.
