Over these extended time windows, Earth's motion and the long signal durations make standard search methods computationally demanding for low-latency detection. We propose an approach based on short-time Fourier transforms and convolutional neural networks that implicitly marginalizes over extrinsic parameters, effectively reducing the search to the chirp mass. The network learns the signals' nonlinear time-frequency morphology, approximating the optimal Bayesian detection statistic. Using a large-scale campaign of simulated signal injections, we compare the network's performance with a GPU-optimized matched-filtering baseline for both the ET$\Delta$ and ET2L configurations of the Einstein Telescope and signal durations from approximately 25 minutes ($f_{\rm min} = 5$ Hz) to approximately 6 hours ($f_{\rm min} = 2$ Hz). The network achieves comparable detection sensitivity while reducing computational costs by over four orders of magnitude, bringing processing times from minutes to milliseconds. This efficiency paves the way for rapid early-warning alerts for multi-messenger astronomy in the third-generation detector era.

</details>


### [78] [Stealth scalars on prescribed dark-energy backgrounds: reconstruction, branch regularity, and perturbative bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10231)
*José Blanco,Cuauhtemoc Campuzano,Víctor H. Cárdenas,Giovany Cruz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reconstructs homogeneous stealth scalar fields on FLRW cosmological backgrounds with constant or evolving dark-energy equations of state, showing that exact stealthness (vanishing scalar stress tensor) is distinct from global regularity and from identifying a scalar-tensor term with the prescribed dark-energy density; it proves dynamical non-invariance of that identification in mixed flat constant-w cosmologies, provides hypergeometric solutions for wCDM, reduces to a comoving-distance quadrature at conformal coupling, and analyzes stability, propagation speed (luminal), and solar-system constraints (e.g., Cassini).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether and how homogeneous scalar fields can act as 'stealth' sources—i.e., vanish identically in their stress-energy contribution while still being dynamically present—on realistic cosmological backgrounds, especially in light of dark energy modeling and observational constraints.

Method: Analytical reconstruction of homogeneous scalar field profiles satisfying the stealth condition (vanishing stress tensor) on prescribed FLRW backgrounds; derivation of a Riccati equation by eliminating the self-interaction potential; use of hypergeometric functions for wCDM; conformal-coupling reduction to a comoving-distance integral; analysis of perturbations, propagation speed via scalar–tensor effective metric, and solar-system bounds via Cassini parameter constraints.

Result: Stealthness is mathematically distinct from regularity and from sector-preserving identification with dark energy; such identification is not dynamically invariant in mixed constant-w cosmologies; regular solutions exist for radiation-free wCDM separated by a background-dependent threshold; at conformal coupling, stealth reconstruction simplifies to an exact quadrature valid even with radiation; CPL phantom-divide crossing does not introduce singularities in the background stealth equation; high-frequency scalar perturbations propagate luminally under positive effective Planck mass; Cassini constraints severely suppress late-time gravitational responses if no screening occurs.

Conclusion: Homogeneous stealth scalar fields are viable on broad classes of FLRW backgrounds, but their physical consistency requires careful distinction between mathematical stealth, regularity, and dark-energy mimicry; observational constraints—especially from solar-system tests—strongly limit their gravitational impact unless screening mechanisms are invoked.

Abstract: We reconstruct homogeneous stealth scalar fields on prescribed Friedmann--Lemaître--Robertson--Walker backgrounds with constant or evolving dark-energy equations of state. The scalar stress tensor vanishes, so the expansion history is supported by an independent background sector. Eliminating the self-interaction potential yields a Riccati equation for the scalar profile. We use it to distinguish exact stealthness from global regularity and from the stronger identification of a term in the scalar stress tensor with the prescribed dark-energy density. We prove that this sector-preserving identification is not dynamically invariant in a mixed flat constant-$w$ cosmology. For radiation-free $w$CDM backgrounds, a hypergeometric representation allows the regular and singular branches to be separated by a background-dependent threshold. At conformal coupling the reconstruction instead reduces to an exact comoving-distance quadrature, valid for arbitrary regular flat histories, including radiation. A CPL phantom-divide crossing does not singularize the background stealth equation, although the perturbations of the prescribed dark-energy sector require an independent treatment. The scalar--tensor description gives luminal high-frequency propagation under a positive effective Planck mass. If the field tracks its cosmological value locally without screening, Cassini constraints strongly suppress the corresponding late-time gravitational responses.

</details>


### [79] [Relativistic Simulations of Self-Gravitating Radiation Fluids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10320)
*Bing-Jyun Tsao,Pablo Laguna,Octavio Guerrero*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new numerical method to simulate pure radiation fluids in general relativistic hydrodynamics by treating radiation as a zero-rest-mass perfect fluid, integrated into the Whisky/Einstein Toolkit framework; it validates the implementation via shock-tube tests and demonstrates physical applications including black hole accretion and unstable photon star equilibria.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate, self-consistent numerical simulations of radiation-dominated systems (e.g., early universe, gamma-ray bursts, accretion flows) in strong-gravity regimes where radiation pressure dominates and gravitational backreaction cannot be neglected.

Method: Extends the Whisky code (within Einstein Toolkit) to model radiation as a self-gravitating, massless perfect fluid: modifies source terms in the Einstein and hydrodynamic equations, adapts the primitive variable recovery scheme for ultra-relativistic EOS (P = ρ/3), and updates Riemann solvers (e.g., HLLE or Roe) to handle stiff radiation-fluid characteristics; validation via 1D relativistic shock tube tests.

Result: Successful reproduction of analytical shock-tube solutions; stable evolution of radiation accretion onto a Schwarzschild black hole showing Bondi-like inflow and horizon crossing; numerical construction of spherically symmetric, unstable photon stars—small perturbations trigger either gravitational collapse to a black hole or dispersal to infinity.

Conclusion: The radiation-fluid formalism is robust and physically meaningful within full GR hydrodynamics; it provides a viable alternative to moment-based radiative transfer for regimes where scattering is negligible and radiation behaves collectively as a fluid, enabling new studies of radiation-driven dynamics in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: We present an approach for numerical simulations of pure radiation fluids within the general relativistic hydrodynamics framework. The approach models radiation as a self-gravitating perfect fluid with vanishing rest mass, enabling simulations of radiation fluids in strong-gravity spacetimes. Building on the existing relativistic hydrodynamics code Whisky, within the Einstein Toolkit framework, we introduce modifications to incorporate radiation fluids, including updates to the source terms, recovery scheme, and Riemann solvers. Modifications are validated using standard shock tube tests. We present results for radiation accretion onto a single black hole, as well as a photon star solution for radiation in unstable hydrostatic equilibrium, demonstrating both collapse and dispersion under perturbations.

</details>


### [80] [Generalized Second Law of Thermodynamics in $f(Q)$ Symmetric Teleparallel Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10604)
*Nadeem Azhar,Kayoomars Karami*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper verifies the generalized second law (GSL) of thermodynamics on the cosmological apparent horizon in f(Q) symmetric teleparallel gravity, deriving a compact master formula for total entropy production and showing GSL holds for three observationally viable f(Q) models; robustness stems from the sign of Ḣ (no phantom crossing), while energy condition violations differ across models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the thermodynamic consistency of f(Q) gravity—specifically whether the generalized second law holds on the cosmological apparent horizon—given its growing role as a viable alternative to ΛCDM and GR, and to clarify the interplay between horizon thermodynamics, modified gravity, and energy conditions.

Method: The authors adopt Wald-type horizon entropy with prefactor Af_Q/4G plus a correction term derived from the modified Friedmann equations in f(Q) gravity. They analytically derive the apparent horizon entropy S_A, compute its time derivative Ṡ_A symbolically, combine it with matter entropy rate Ṡ_m, and obtain a closed-form master equation for T_A Ṡ_tot. They then evaluate the GSL (Ṡ_tot ≥ 0) for power-law, exponential, and logarithmic f(Q) models using observational constraints and analyze associated energy conditions (NEC, WEC, DEC, SEC) via effective energy-momentum components.

Result: A compact, exact master formula T_A Ṡ_tot = 4πḢ(Ḣ f_Q + H ḟ_Q)/H⁴ (8πG=1) is derived and verified; GSL holds at all redshifts for all three f(Q) models. Crucially, for pressureless matter satisfying NEC, GSL validity reduces solely to Ḣ ≥ 0 — i.e., no phantom crossing. Energy condition analysis shows: power-law and exponential models violate only SEC; logarithmic model violates NEC, WEC, and DEC for z > −1, and SEC for z < 3.84.

Conclusion: The GSL is thermodynamically robust in f(Q) gravity under standard matter assumptions, not due to model-specific tuning but because of the fundamental link between Ḣ and total entropy production — reinforcing that cosmic acceleration without phantom behavior ensures thermodynamic consistency. However, the logarithmic model’s broad energy condition violations signal potential instabilities or pathological causal structure, warranting further scrutiny beyond thermodynamics.

Abstract: We investigate the validity of the generalized second law (GSL) of thermodynamics on the cosmological apparent horizon in $f(Q)$ symmetric teleparallel gravity, using the Wald-type entropy prefactor $Af_Q/4G$ together with a correction term derived directly from the modified Friedmann equations. We derive, and verify by explicit symbolic computation, a closed-form expression for the apparent horizon entropy in $f(Q)$ gravity and show that its time derivative combines with the matter entropy rate into a remarkably compact master formula, $T_A\dot S_{\rm tot} = 4\pi\dot H(\dot H f_Q + H\dot f_Q)/H^4$ (in units $8\pi G=1$), which correctly reduces to the known general-relativistic limit. We apply this formalism to three observationally viable $f(Q)$ models --- power-law, exponential, and logarithmic. We find that the GSL is satisfied throughout cosmic history for all three models, and relate this robustness to a general, theory-independent structural condition: for ordinary pressureless matter obeying the null energy condition, GSL validity reduces to the sign of $\dot H$, i.e., to the absence of a phantom crossing in the total effective expansion history. The geometric sector exhibits model-dependent energy conditions, power-law and exponential models violate only the strong condition, while the logarithmic model violates the null, weak, and dominant conditions for \(z>-1\), and the strong condition for \(z<3.84\).

</details>


### [81] [A note on Positivity and Rigidity for an Expanding Quasi-Local Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10880)
*Eric Ling,Annachiara Piubello*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper strengthens endpoint rigidity for the expanding quasi-local energy $E_\lambda$ on constantly umbilic initial data, proving that $E_\lambda = 0$ at the threshold $\lambda = \alpha$ implies isometry to a Euclidean 3-ball of radius $\leq 1/\lambda$; it also constructs a counterexample showing negativity and failure of rigidity beyond $\alpha$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve open questions from [ALP26] regarding the sharpness of positivity and rigidity for the expanding quasi-local energy $E_\lambda$, particularly whether vanishing at the threshold $\lambda = \alpha$ fully characterizes Euclidean balls and whether positivity fails beyond that threshold.

Method: Combines the Shi-Tam rigidity theorem for Brown-York energy with a strict concavity argument for $E_\lambda$ in $\lambda$; constructs an explicit geometric counterexample violating positivity and rigidity beyond $\alpha$.

Result: Proof that $E_\lambda = 0$ at $\lambda = \alpha$ implies $(\Omega,g)$ is isometric to a closed Euclidean 3-ball of radius $\leq 1/\lambda$; explicit counterexample demonstrating $E_\lambda < 0$ for $\lambda > \alpha$ and $E_\lambda = 0$ without rigidity.

Conclusion: The endpoint rigidity for $E_\lambda$ is sharp and geometrically precise at $\lambda = \alpha$, but the conjectured global positivity and rigidity fail beyond this threshold—establishing sharpness of the threshold and clarifying the energy's geometric limitations.

Abstract: We study positivity and rigidity for an expanding quasi-local energy $E_\lambda$ associated with constantly umbilic initial data $(\Omega,g,k=\lambda g)$, introduced in earlier work [ALP26] as an analogue of the Brown-York energy adapted to an expanding flat de Sitter background. Under nonnegative scalar curvature and suitable boundary assumptions, our previous results established positivity for $\lambda$ below a geometrically determined threshold $\alpha$, while vanishing $E_\lambda$ at $\lambda=\alpha$ forced the boundary to be round. In this paper, we strengthen this endpoint rigidity statement by showing that, if $E_\lambda=0$ when $\lambda=\alpha$, then $(\Omega,g)$ is isometric to a closed Euclidean 3-ball of radius at most $1/\lambda$. The proof combines the rigidity theorem of Shi-Tam for the Brown-York energy with a strict concavity argument. We also construct an explicit counterexample to the positivity and rigidity conjecture formulated in [ALP26], showing that the energy can become negative beyond this threshold and that vanishing energy need not imply rigidity.

</details>


### [82] [Qubit representation of the mass observable in quantized Schwarzschild interior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10933)
*Babak Vakili*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a quantum-computational framework to represent and measure the mass observable of a Wheeler–DeWitt quantized Schwarzschild black-hole interior using finite-dimensional truncated oscillator states, Pauli decomposition, and variational quantum algorithms; it quantifies resource scaling and demonstrates experimental feasibility on small qubit systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge canonical quantum gravity (specifically Wheeler–DeWitt quantization of black-hole interiors) with near-term quantum computation by enabling concrete simulation and measurement of gravitational observables like mass.

Method: Constructs finite-dimensional representations of the mass operator in the physical Hilbert space of correlated two-mode oscillator states; maps these to qubit operators via Pauli decomposition; analytically derives scaling of Pauli term count and measurement settings with truncation size; implements a two-qubit VQE for spectral validation and designs a quantum-circuit protocol to verify the Wheeler–DeWitt constraint independently.

Result: Analytical scaling laws for Pauli term count (~N^2) and independent measurement settings (~N) with truncation dimension N; successful two-qubit VQE reproducing exact spectrum; explicit quantum circuit for constraint verification; quantitative resource characterization for encoding/measuring black-hole mass.

Conclusion: The work establishes a rigorous, implementable quantum-computational pathway for simulating gravitational observables in canonical quantum gravity, demonstrating that key features of Wheeler–DeWitt quantum cosmology—such as constraint satisfaction and observable spectra—can be faithfully encoded and probed on current and near-term quantum hardware.

Abstract: We develop a quantum-computational framework for the mass observable of the Wheeler--DeWitt quantized Schwarzschild black-hole interior. Working in the physical sector spanned by correlated two-mode oscillator states, we construct finite-dimensional representations of the mass operator and map them onto qubit operators through Pauli decomposition. We analyze the resulting representations for increasing truncation sizes and establish analytically the scaling of the number of nonvanishing Pauli terms and independent measurement settings. These results provide a quantitative characterization of the quantum resources required to encode and measure the black-hole mass observable. As a representative benchmark, we implement a two-qubit variational quantum eigensolver for the smallest nontrivial truncation and find excellent agreement with the exact finite-dimensional spectrum. We further describe a quantum-circuit procedure for verifying the Wheeler--DeWitt constraint independently of the mass estimation. Our results provide a direct connection between Wheeler--DeWitt quantum gravity and quantum computation and establish a structured route toward the quantum simulation of gravitational observables.

</details>


### [83] [Dynamical entropy on local causal horizons: the Einstein equation of state and canonical-energy flux](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11006)
*Roh-Suan Tung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs two geometric entropy functionals—HWZ's S_loc and a new S_L—for twist-free null hypersurfaces, showing that their exact off-shell thermodynamic balance laws (under local equilibrium assumptions and stress-tensor conservation) are equivalent to the Einstein equation (with undetermined cosmological constant); S_L is uniquely selected by positivity, cut-locality, and first-order normalization, and reduces to HWZ’s dynamical entropy near equilibrium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive the Einstein equation from thermodynamic principles on null hypersurfaces without assuming global equilibrium or final stationary states—specifically, to eliminate reliance on asymptotic equilibrium cuts and instead enforce exact, local, off-shell balance laws for entropy functionals.

Method: Constructs two entropy functionals: (i) the Hollands-Wald-Zhang (HWZ) functional S_loc = (4G)⁻¹∫(1 − Vθ)dA, which satisfies an exact Raychaudhuri identity with no linear θ term; and (ii) a new logarithmic functional S_L = (4G)⁻¹∫(1 + ln J − Vθ)dA_ref defined on a reference area element, shown to obey an exact nondecreasing balance under null convergence. Uses local Unruh temperature, stress-tensor conservation, and Jacobson-style heat–entropy matching; derives equivalence to Einstein’s equation via variational and affine-parameter monotonicity arguments; analyzes origin dependence and anchoring invariance.

Result: S_loc yields Einstein’s equation (with undetermined Λ) when equating its equilibrium variation to boost heat, assuming local Unruh temperature and conservation. S_L satisfies an exact positive-definite balance law whose monotonicity under null convergence is equivalent to the full Einstein equation (given conservation and normalization). The density 1 + ln J − Vθ is uniquely selected among two-function candidates by cut-locality, positivity, and first-order HWZ matching. Both functionals depend on affine origin, but their physically invariant content is identified.

Conclusion: The Einstein equation emerges as a necessary and sufficient condition for the exact, local, off-shell thermodynamic balance of suitably constructed geometric entropy functionals on null hypersurfaces—without requiring global equilibrium or caustic avoidance beyond the domain of validity. This strengthens the thermodynamic foundation of gravity and clarifies the physical role of affine parametrization and coarse-graining in gravitational entropy.

Abstract: For a twist-free null hypersurface, the Hollands-Wald-Zhang (HWZ) representative $S_{\rm loc}=(4G)^{-1}\int(1-V\theta)dA$, local on each cross-section (cut), obeys an exact off-shell Raychaudhuri identity with no term linear in the expansion, so no final equilibrium cut is needed. Given an entropy density $1/4G$, the Unruh temperature $\kappa/2\pi$ of an approximate local boost, local equilibrium cuts, and stress-tensor conservation, equating the leading equilibrium change of $S_{\rm loc}$ to matter boost heat yields $R_{kk}=8\pi GT_{kk}$ for every null direction, hence the Einstein equation with undetermined cosmological constant, an equation of state in Jacobson's conditional sense. On a fixed caustic-free generator bundle with $dA=J\,dA_{\rm ref}$, the functional $S_{\rm L}=(4G)^{-1}\int(1+\ln J-V\theta)dA_{\rm ref}$ obeys an exact balance per reference cell with curvature, shear, and expansion terms all positive, so under null convergence $S_{\rm L}$ is nondecreasing for $V\geq0$ until the first caustic. Conversely, requiring this balance for positive affine parameter on every small bundle and null direction is equivalent to the Einstein equation, given conservation and the stated normalization. Cut-locality, positivity, and first-order HWZ normalization select the density $1+\ln J-V\theta$ uniquely within a two-function class. Both functionals depend on the affine origin; we give their shift laws, identify the anchoring-invariant content, and show the origin is physical data fixed by the equilibrium cut. Near equilibrium, $S_{\rm L}$ agrees with the HWZ dynamical entropy at first order, and the temperature-weighted change of its Hessian equals the HWZ modified canonical-energy flux for stationary-vacuum perturbations. At finite amplitude, $S_{\rm L}$ is only a monotone geometric functional; reading it as entropy requires a coarse-graining postulate, stated but not derived.

</details>


### [84] [Covariant Hamiltonian Gravity in Spherical Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11099)
*Cong Zhang,Zhoujian Cao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper constructs an effective Einstein equation from covariant Hamiltonian gravity in spherical symmetry, showing that prescribed static, spherically symmetric metrics (e.g., generalized Vaidya) solve it as vacuum solutions; it demonstrates that standard null dust (Vaidya) can source dynamical black holes—including regular ones—beyond GR, and argues that quantum gravity corrections alone cannot explain Hawking evaporation without violating the null energy condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether standard matter sources (e.g., null dust) can consistently drive dynamical black-hole geometries in effective gravitational theories beyond general relativity, and to assess the role of quantum gravity corrections in black-hole mass loss and evaporation.

Method: Covariant Hamiltonian formulation of gravity in spherical symmetry; derivation of an effective Einstein equation whose vacuum solutions include a chosen one-parameter family of static, spherically symmetric metrics; explicit construction and analysis of generalized Vaidya solutions and their associated energy-momentum tensor and energy conditions.

Result: Generalized Vaidya metrics are exact vacuum solutions of the effective Einstein equation; their induced energy-momentum tensor matches the standard Vaidya (null dust) form; such solutions support dynamical black-hole spacetimes—including regular black holes—and require violation of the null energy condition for ingoing mass loss, implying quantum gravity corrections alone cannot reproduce Hawking evaporation.

Conclusion: Standard null dust is compatible with dynamical black-hole geometries in effective gravity frameworks beyond GR; however, modeling Hawking-like evaporation demands energy condition violation, suggesting that semiclassical or thermodynamic effects—not just quantum gravity corrections—are essential for consistent black-hole mass loss.

Abstract: Building upon the covariant Hamiltonian gravity in spherical symmetry, we construct the effective Einstein equation governing gravitational dynamics. For any prescribed one-parameter family of static, spherically symmetric metrics, this equation can be constructed such that the prescribed family constitutes their vacuum solutions. As an application, we construct generalized Vaidya solutions to the effective Einstein equation and show that the corresponding energy-momentum tensor takes precisely the standard Vaidya form. The results demonstrate that standard null dust sources can serve as matter source for dynamical black-hole geometries beyond general relativity, including regular black holes. Furthermore, this work suggests that quantum gravity corrections alone may not account for Hawking-like evaporation, as mass loss in the ingoing Vaidya solution necessarily requires a violation of the null energy condition within our effective framework.

</details>


### [85] [Exceptional Points in Scalar Quasinormal Modes of Rastall String-Fluid Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11366)
*Ming Zhang,Xiao-Mei Wang,Ya-Nan Wang,Miao Wang,Lei Zhang,Xufen Zhang,De-Cheng Zou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates exceptional points (EPs) in scalar quasinormal modes of Rastall string-fluid black holes using a Pöschl–Teller perturbation; it identifies a resolution-stable second-order EP on the regular branch via high-precision spectral numerics and multiple defectiveness diagnostics, but finds no convergent EP for the logarithmic branch.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously identify and characterize exceptional points—non-Hermitian degeneracies where eigenvalues and eigenvectors coalesce—in the quasinormal mode spectrum of a modified black hole spacetime, testing robustness of EPs under numerical discretization and continuum limits.

Method: Introduces a localized Pöschl–Teller perturbation to the scalar wave potential with tunable bump position $a$ and amplitude $\varepsilon$; solves the hyperboloidal QNM eigenvalue problem using two-domain Chebyshev collocation and arbitrary-precision linear algebra; applies five complementary EP diagnostics: complex discriminant, eigenvector coalescence, Jordan-chain residuals, square-root splitting of eigenvalues, and closed-loop mode exchange.

Result: A resolution-stable second-order EP is found on the regular ($A=1$, $\eR=-1$) branch across spectral resolutions $N=24$ to $N=60$; time-domain constrained semidiscrete evolution confirms the characteristic non-exponential Jordan response $(C+Dt)e^{i\omega_{\rm EP}t}$; no convergent EP is found on the logarithmic ($A=2$) branch—the defective points observed in finite truncations fail to converge under refinement.

Conclusion: Exceptional points can be robustly realized in black hole QNM spectra under controlled perturbations, provided the background satisfies sufficient regularity (here, the regular Rastall branch); their existence is not generic across all parameter branches, highlighting the interplay between spacetime geometry, boundary conditions, and spectral defectiveness.

Abstract: In this paper, we study exceptional points (EPs) in scalar quasinormal modes(QNMs) of Rastall string-fluid black holes by adding a localized Pöschl--Teller perturbation to the scalar potential, with the bump position $a$ and amplitude $\eb$ serving as independent controls. We solve the hyperboloidal problem with two-domain Chebyshev collocation and arbitrary-precision linear algebra, and use the complex discriminant, eigenvector coalescence, Jordan-chain residuals, square-root splitting and closed-loop mode exchange as complementary tests of defectiveness. For the regular $A=1$, $\eR=-1$ branch, the two-parameter search locates a resolution-stable second-order EP from $N=24$ to $N=60$, while constrained semidiscrete evolution gives the Jordan response $(C+Dt)e^{\ii\omega_{\rm EP}t}$. For the logarithmic $A=2$ branch, defective points occur in each finite truncation but their coordinates do not converge, so we make no continuum claim for this branch.

</details>


### [86] [Negative Dynamical Friction in Dark Matter Spikes around Rotating Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11380)
*Wei Xiong,Peng-Cheng Li,Bin Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Dark matter spikes around rotating massive black holes can induce negative dynamical friction—supplying energy to EMRIs—leading to significant phase shifts (~100 rad) and environmental redshift corrections comparable in magnitude but opposite in sign to first-order conservative self-force; both effects are crucial for precision EMRI modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how dark matter environments affect EMRI waveforms, particularly through non-vacuum dynamical friction and conservative metric perturbations, which are critical for LISA data analysis accuracy.

Method: Analytical modeling of orbital-energy exchange and conservative metric response from dark matter spikes around Kerr black holes; computation of phase accumulation and frequency-domain redshift corrections for prograde circular EMRIs.

Result: Negative dynamical friction occurs beyond the ISCO for sufficiently high black-hole spin; for a 4-year Milky Way–like EMRI (q=10⁻⁵), it yields ~10² rad phase shift relative to vacuum; fixed-frequency environmental redshift correction is comparable to and opposite the first-order conservative self-force.

Conclusion: Both environmental energy exchange (dynamical friction) and conservative metric response from dark matter must be included in high-precision EMRI waveform models for gravitational-wave observatories like LISA.

Abstract: Dark matter may affect extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) through orbital-energy exchange and a conservative metric response. Here we show that dark matter spikes around rotating massive black holes can drive negative dynamical friction, supplying orbital energy to secondary black holes on stable prograde circular orbits. The energy-gain region extends beyond the innermost stable circular orbit once the black-hole spin exceeds a model-dependent threshold. For an illustrative four-year inspiral with Milky Way--like halo parameters and mass ratio $q=10^{-5}$, the total dynamical-friction contribution produces a phase difference of order $10^2$ radians relative to vacuum. For the same system, the fixed-frequency environmental redshift correction is comparable in magnitude and opposite in sign to the first-order conservative self-force correction. Both environmental energy exchange and the conservative metric response are therefore essential to precision EMRI modeling.

</details>


### [87] [Pushing the accuracy of the quasi-circular baseline for the SEOBNR family of models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11449)
*Cheng Foo,Alessandra Buonanno,Héctor Estellés,Lorenzo Pompili*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: SEOBNRv5iHM is an improved quasi-circular, aligned-spin gravitational-waveform model in the effective-one-body framework, featuring a new phenomenological intermediate region for mode-specific ringdown attachment—yielding significantly lower unfaithfulness against numerical relativity (e.g., 4.5× improvement over SEOBNRv5HM for the (2,2) mode) and enhanced accuracy for high-mass and multi-mode waveforms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve accuracy of binary black hole waveform models for gravitational-wave source characterization and astrophysical inference, especially as detector sensitivities increase and higher-order modes become more relevant.

Method: Developed SEOBNRv5iHM by modifying SEOBNRv5HM: removed non-quasi-circular corrections and introduced a phenomenological intermediate region enabling mode-dependent ringdown attachment time (including at each mode's amplitude peak); calibrated and validated against 591 aligned-spin, quasi-circular NR waveforms from SXS Catalog 3.

Result: For the (2,2) mode, median maximum unfaithfulness vs. NR is 4.25e-5 (10–300 M⊙), 4.5× lower than SEOBNRv5HM and 3–8× lower than other semi-analytical models; for M ≥ 150 M⊙, median drops to 9.7e-6 — comparable to NRHybSur3dq8. With ℓ_max = 5 and inclination π/3, median max unfaithfulness is 4.2e-4 — 2.6× better than SEOBNRv5HM and 6–11× better than other semi-analytical models.

Conclusion: SEOBNRv5iHM sets a new standard for quasi-circular, aligned-spin EOB waveform accuracy, particularly in late inspiral and merger-ringdown; its improvements will propagate to spin-precessing and eccentric extensions, reducing systematic errors in future GW data analysis.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave observations have opened a new era of astronomy, with accurate modeling of binary black hole signals becoming increasingly important for source characterization and astrophysical inference as detector sensitivities improve. We present SEOBNRv5iHM, an updated quasi-circular, aligned-spin inspiral-merger-ringdown gravitational-waveform model with higher-order modes, constructed within the effective-one-body formalism and building upon SEOBNRv5HM. The improvements mainly concern the late inspiral and the merger-ringdown: we remove the non-quasi-circular corrections used in previous models and instead introduce a phenomenological intermediate region between the inspiral and the ringdown, which allows the ringdown attachment time to be chosen independently for each mode, including at the peak of the mode's amplitude. The model is calibrated and validated against 591 quasi-circular aligned-spin waveforms from the third Simulating eXtreme Spacetimes catalog. For the $(2,2)$ mode, when considering the maximum unfaithfulness against numerical relativity over a total mass range of 10-300$M_\odot$, SEOBNRv5iHM achieves a median of $4.25\times10^{-5}$, a factor of 4.5 below SEOBNRv5HM, and a factor of 3-8 below other semi-analytical models, with $84\%$ of cases below $10^{-4}$. For $M\ge150 M_\odot$, the median drops to $9.7\times10^{-6}$, comparable to the numerical-relativity surrogate NRHybSur3dq8. For polarization mismatches at inclination $\pi/3$, with modes up to $\ell_\mathrm{max} = 5$, the median maximum unfaithfulness is $4.2\times10^{-4}$, a factor of 2.6 below SEOBNRv5HM, 1.9 below IMRPhenomTHM, and 6-11 below the other semi-analytical models. Since spin-precessing and eccentric models are built on top of the quasi-circular, aligned-spin baseline, these improvements will make future SEOBNR models more accurate, reducing waveform systematics in upcoming analyses.

</details>


### [88] [Quasinormal modes of a scale-dependent black hole in 5D spacetime with cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11584)
*Ángel Rincón,Zdeněk Stuchlík*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a 5D scale-dependent black hole with positive cosmological constant under scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations; it derives analytical solutions, analyzes effective potentials, applies WKB approximation for QNM frequencies, and confirms stability (negative imaginary parts) across all perturbations for small quantum corrections parameterized by ε.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore quantum-gravity corrections to black hole dynamics via scale-dependent gravity, specifically how small deviations from General Relativity (parameterized by ε) affect quasinormal mode spectra and stability in five dimensions with Λ > 0.

Method: Derives exact analytical solutions for the 5D scale-dependent black hole metric; constructs effective potentials from the lapse function for scalar, EM, and Dirac perturbations; employs first-order approximation in ε for the lapse function; computes QNMs using the WKB semi-analytical method (up to 6th order); compares against classical (ε = 0) case.

Result: Computed QNM frequencies for all three perturbation types across varying small ε; results tabulated and plotted; all modes exhibit negative imaginary parts, confirming linear stability of the scale-dependent black hole under these perturbations.

Conclusion: The five-dimensional scale-dependent black hole with positive cosmological constant remains stable under scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations for small quantum corrections (low ε), and its QNM spectrum exhibits systematic, ε-dependent deviations from the classical GR limit—providing a potential observational signature for scale-dependent gravity.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a scale-dependent black hole in five dimensions with a positive cosmological constant, considering i) scalar, ii) electromagnetic, and iii) Dirac perturbations. After deriving exact analytical solutions within the framework of scale-dependent gravity in five dimensions and selecting a different family of solutions (cf. \cite{Contreras:2019fwu}), we analyze the corresponding effective potentials (given by the lapse function) for each perturbation type. We employ a first-order approximation for the lapse function, as it closely matches the exact case for small $\varepsilon$. Since quantum corrections are expected to be small, our focus is on a relatively low range of the scale-dependent parameter $\varepsilon$. We confirm that the WKB semi-analytical approach can be used to compute the QNMs for these perturbations to the background geometry. We calculate the QNM frequencies for the classical case (for comparison) and for cases where the scale-dependent parameter $\varepsilon$, which quantifies deviations from General Relativity, is non-zero. The results are summarized in a series of tables and figures. Finally, we find that, regardless of the perturbation type, the solutions are stable, as the imaginary part of each QNM frequency is negative.

</details>


### [89] [Horizon-penetrating rotating black-hole configurations in VCDM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11775)
*Antonio De Felice*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs stationary, rotating, and possibly charged black-hole solutions in VCDM (Vector-Tensor Gravity with a preferred foliation), showing that regular black-hole metrics do not guarantee regularity of the preferred foliation; it analyzes constant-mean-curvature (CMC) foliations crossing horizons, introduces a horizon-regular flux formulation, performs numerical validation across 17 configurations and flux scans, establishes geometric and analytic bounds (e.g., on lapse and inner limits), and clarifies limitations regarding cosmological interpretation and interior dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether regular black-hole metrics in Vector-Connection-Dynamical-Matter (VCDM) gravity ensure regularity of the theory's intrinsic preferred foliation — a key structural element — and to explore how stationary black holes embed in this geometrically constrained framework.

Method: Analytic construction of stationary rotating/charged solutions on the constant-auxiliary Einstein-Maxwell branch of VCDM; derivation of Kerr-Newman and Kerr-Newman-de Sitter geometries with nonzero constant-mean-curvature (CMC) foliations; development of a horizon-regular flux formulation for inner data and asymptotics; numerical survey of 17 configurations plus mean-flux scans; residual analysis, geometric reconstruction, grid convergence tests; derivation of analytic bounds (e.g., lapse positivity, inner limit conditions).

Result: CMC foliations generically cross both future black-hole and cosmological horizons; horizon-regular flux formulation yields well-posed inner data and distinguishes hyperboloidal/expanding asymptotics; numerical results show power-law scaling (~2) in local lapse/shear differences; an exact lapse bound excludes zeros in the regular exterior ($\Delta_r > 0$); singular finite-proper-distance endpoints are distinguished from asymptotic trumpet ends; conditional bounds established for spacelike inner limits.

Conclusion: Regularity of the black-hole metric does not imply regularity of the preferred foliation in VCDM; the preferred foliation is dynamically sensitive to flux parameters even when the background geometry is fixed; stationary constructions constrain outer and asymptotic structure but leave the rotating interior endpoint and cosmological matching undetermined.

Abstract: A regular black-hole metric need not define a regular configuration of VCDM, whose preferred foliation is part of the gravitational solution. We construct stationary rotating, possibly charged configurations on the constant-auxiliary Einstein-Maxwell branch. Their Kerr-Newman and Kerr-Newman-de Sitter geometries carry nonzero constant-mean-curvature foliations that cross the future black-hole horizon and, in the de Sitter case, the future cosmological horizon. A horizon-regular flux formulation specifies the inner data and the distinct hyperboloidal and expanding asymptotic ends. Seventeen survey configurations are supplemented by a mean-flux scan at fixed four-geometry and paired flat/de Sitter comparisons down to $\kappa M = 0.01$, where $K_0 = 3\kappa$. Varying the flux changes the preferred-foliation shear without changing the background seen by standard test matter. The paired local lapse and shear differences have effective powers close to two over the computed range, with the lapse comparison requiring a fixed time normalization. Continuous residuals, geometric reconstructions, and selected grid comparisons test the numerical solutions. An exact lapse bound excludes zeros in the regular stationary exterior block with $\Delta_r > 0$. We distinguish singular finite-proper-distance endpoints from asymptotic trumpet ends and give conditional bounds on a stationary spacelike inner limit. A local cosmological interpretation requires additional matching assumptions; dynamical selection and the rotating interior endpoint are not determined by the stationary construction.

</details>


### [90] [Quasinormal Modes of Black holes in the generalized Schwarzschild-de Sitter solution of $f(R)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11895)
*Dhruba Jyoti Gogoi,Himanshu Chaudhary,Tiberiu Harko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of scalar, vector, and axial tensor perturbations in a modified f(R) gravity Schwarzschild-type black hole solution featuring linear and logarithmic metric corrections; using high-order WKB and analytical methods, it finds parameter-dependent deviations from GR predictions—especially nonlinear scaling of QNM frequencies/damping with parameter A—and confirms consistency with Schwarzschild QNMs for suitable parameter choices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test viability of a specific f(R) gravity black hole solution against gravitational-wave observations by analyzing its quasinormal mode spectrum—crucial for distinguishing modified gravity from general relativity via ringdown signals.

Method: Applies higher-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB) approximation and complementary analytical techniques to compute quasinormal modes for scalar, vector, and axial tensor perturbations on a static spherically symmetric f(R) black hole metric with linear + log corrections; constructs contour plots in parameter space and derives time-domain waveforms.

Result: QNMs closely reproduce Schwarzschild values for appropriate parameter choices; parameter A induces nonlinear increase in both oscillation frequency and damping rate; parameter η exhibits A-dependent opposite effects—enhancing deviation for small A but suppressing it for large A, especially in scalar perturbations; contour plots and time-domain profiles confirm these trends.

Conclusion: The f(R) model remains phenomenologically viable for certain parameter ranges, but scalar perturbations serve as a sensitive discriminant due to their distinct parametric response—highlighting the importance of multi-polar and multi-type perturbation analysis in testing modified gravity with gravitational-wave data.

Abstract: We analyze scalar, vector, and {axial} tensor perturbations in a Schwarzschild-type static spherically symmetric solution of modified $f(R)$ gravity. This solution differs from the Schwarzschild metric of general relativity through the presence of a linearly growing term and a logarithmic correction, and it depends on four integration constants. Using the higher-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB) approximation and an analytical method, we calculate the quasinormal modes (QNMs) for all three types of perturbations. Our results show that, for appropriately chosen parameter values, the QNMs closely match those of the Schwarzschild black hole. In particular, the model parameter $A$ produces a nonlinear increase in both the oscillation frequency and damping rate of ringdown gravitational waves. A similar effect arises for the parameter $\eta$ when $A$ is small, while for larger $A$ values the effects are opposite, where the scalar perturbations display a distinct deviation compared to the vector and {axial} tensor cases. Contour plots of QNMs in the parameter space support these findings. Finally, time-domain profiles are obtained to illustrate the dynamical evolution of the gravitational wave signal.

</details>


### [91] [Efficiency enhancement in the Unruh quantum Otto engine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11917)
*Yongjie Pan,Baocheng Zhang,Qingyu Cai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates efficiency enhancement in an Unruh quantum Otto engine (UQOE) composed of two unentangled, differently accelerated Unruh-DeWitt detectors interacting with a Minkowski vacuum scalar field. Efficiency gain arises not from entanglement but from proper-time asymmetry affecting population-weighted energy exchange; cyclic operation is identified with separable post-heat-absorption states; detector separation modulates entanglement and constrains achievable efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether entanglement generation is necessary for efficiency enhancement in relativistic quantum thermal machines, specifically testing this in an Unruh quantum Otto engine where detectors experience different proper times due to acceleration.

Method: Analyzes a UQOE with two initially unentangled UDW detectors undergoing non-uniform acceleration in Minkowski vacuum; employs diagonal free Hamiltonian; defines work/heat w.r.t. one detector’s proper time; identifies cyclic operating points satisfying initial-state passivity, cycle closure, and energy conservation; examines role of inter-detector separation on entanglement and coherence.

Result: Identifies cyclic regimes where efficiency exceeds that of conventional UQOE; shows separable (non-entangled) state after heat absorption yet enhanced efficiency; demonstrates that proper-time asymmetry—not coherence or entanglement—drives enhancement; reveals separation-dependent trade-off: larger separation suppresses entanglement but restricts viable parameter space for cycle closure and limits max efficiency gain.

Conclusion: Entanglement generation is not a necessary condition for efficiency enhancement in Unruh quantum Otto engines; relativistic time dilation (proper-time mismatch) alone can enable thermodynamic advantage, even with separable intermediate states.

Abstract: We investigate the efficiency enhancement of an Unruh quantum Otto engine (UQOE) composed of two initially unentangled Unruh-DeWitt (UDW) detectors. The two detectors have different accelerations and interact with a massless scalar field in the Minkowski vacuum. We choose a diagonal free Hamiltonian for the UQOE and define its work and heat with respect to the proper time of one of the detectors. Consequently, the coherence terms generated through the interaction with the scalar field do not directly contribute to the work and heat of the UQOE. Instead, the efficiency enhancement arises from the relative proper-time factor, which assigns different weights to the population changes of the two detectors. We then identify cyclic operating points that satisfy the conditions of initial-state passivity, cycle closure, and energy conservation. Their efficiency is higher than that of a conventional UQOE, and the state of the UQOE after the heat-absorption stage is separable. Finally, we study the effect of the separation between the two detectors on the engine efficiency. During the interaction of the two detectors with the field, increasing their separation reduces the generated entanglement; however, this separation enters the nonlocal coherence terms, thereby restricting the range of parameter settings compatible with cycle closure and influencing the achievable efficiency enhancement. Our results demonstrate that the UQOE can achieve enhanced efficiency even when its state after the heat-absorption stroke is separable, showing that entanglement generation is not a necessary condition for efficiency enhancement.

</details>


### [92] [Deep-Learning-Assisted Bayesian Inference for Extreme-Mass-Ratio Inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11922)
*Jian-Jun Song,Zhong-Wu Xia,Wen-Hong Ruan,Zu-Cheng Chen,Hongwei Yu,Puxun Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a deep-learning-assisted Bayesian inference framework for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs), combining neural-network-guided localization, particle swarm optimization, and MCMC to efficiently recover posteriors without prior knowledge of true parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: EMRIs have highly multimodal and narrow likelihood surfaces, making standard Bayesian inference computationally prohibitive without accurate initial parameter guesses—unavailable in real observations.

Method: A hybrid framework integrating a pre-trained neural network for coarse parameter-space localization, particle swarm optimization for refinement, and MCMC for high-fidelity posterior sampling.

Result: The method recovers posteriors consistent with reference MCMC runs initialized at true parameters, achieving full inference in ~10 hours per simulated six-month EMRI signal after network training.

Conclusion: Deep learning can effectively guide Bayesian inference for challenging gravitational-wave sources like EMRIs, eliminating dependence on oracle initialization while maintaining accuracy and reducing computational cost.

Abstract: Extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) pose a major challenge to Bayesian parameter inference because of their complex, highly multimodal likelihood surfaces. Locating narrow high-likelihood regions is particularly difficult without knowledge of the true source parameters, which is unavailable in real observations. We develop a deep-learning-assisted Bayesian inference framework that combines neural-network-guided parameter-space localization, particle swarm optimization, and Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) sampling. Applied to simulated six-month EMRI signals, the framework recovers posterior distributions in close agreement with reference MCMC analyses initialized near the true parameter values, without requiring such knowledge for initialization. Once the neural network has been trained, the complete inference pipeline takes approximately 10 hours per signal.

</details>


### [93] [Single and repetitive Penrose processes in genuine uncharged Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11943)
*Ruo-Cen Wu,Hanjun Zou,Meng Xu,Yun-Long Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an analytical framework for the Penrose process in the uncharged Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR0) black-hole spacetime embedded in a Bertotti-Robinson electromagnetic vacuum, distinguishing thermodynamic and geometric energies, solving particle decay dynamics in the ergoregion, and quantifying energy extraction efficiency under magnetic field influence—showing decreasing efficiency and eventual termination of repetitive extraction due to orbital constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously model energy extraction via the Penrose process in a physically realistic, magnetized near-horizon black-hole geometry (KBR0), where standard Kerr-based analyses break down due to strong electromagnetic coupling and near-horizon conformal symmetry; specifically, to reconcile thermodynamic mass generation with Killing energy, resolve decay kinematics in the ergoregion, and quantify magnetic-field effects on extraction viability and efficiency.

Method: Analytical derivation of the KBR0 metric and its Killing vectors; decomposition of particle four-momentum into coordinate (Killing) and thermodynamic (mass-generator) energy components; multibranch parameter mapping from thermodynamic states to metric parameters (spin, magnetic field); exact solution of equatorial neutral particle decay at radial turning points inside the ergoregion; derivation of six physical feasibility constraints (including negative-energy capture, infall accessibility, and angular momentum balance); numerical evaluation of three efficiency metrics (η_EROI, η_EUE, η_thermo) across magnetic field and spin parameter space.

Result: 1) Exact analytical decay solution satisfying all six physical constraints in the KBR0 ergoregion; 2) Monotonic decrease of η_EROI and η_EUE with increasing external magnetic field for both near-extremal and low-spin cases; 3) Increase of η_thermo (rotational reservoir consumption rate) with magnetic field; 4) Two distinct boundary mechanisms limiting single extraction: infall obstruction and loss of negative-energy fragment; 5) All repetitive extraction sequences terminate due to local-slope failure or far-field incident-branch closure; 6) Final black-hole state lies on a universal integral curve in parameter space, independent of parent-particle specific energy.

Conclusion: The Penrose process in the magnetized KBR0 spacetime is fundamentally constrained by electromagnetic coupling: stronger magnetic fields suppress net energy gain and accelerate rotational energy depletion without improving utilization efficiency; repetitive extraction is self-limiting and geometrically bounded, preserving most initial rotational energy—indicating that near-horizon electromagnetic environments severely restrict practical energy harvesting from black holes.

Abstract: We establish an analytical framework for the Penrose process in the genuine uncharged Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR0) black-hole spacetime within a Bertotti-Robinson (BR) electromagnetic vacuum, consistently linking particle decay, radial transport, thermodynamic states, and geometric reconstruction by distinguishing coordinate Killing energy from thermodynamic mass-generator energy and performing multibranch parameter mapping from thermodynamic states to background metric parameters. Furthermore, we present the analytical solution for the decay of equatorial massive neutral particles at a common radial turning point inside the ergoregion, the orbital accessibility criteria, and the six physical constraints required for a complete extraction event. Numerical calculations demonstrate that for both near-extremal and lower-spin inputs, in both single and repetitive Penrose processes, the net energy return on investment ($\eta_{\text{EROI}}$) and the rotational energy utilization efficiency ($\eta_{\text{EUE}}$) decrease with increasing external magnetic field, whereas the rotational energy reservoir consumption rate per unit incident energy ($\eta_{\text{thermo}}$) increases. For the inputs considered, the feasible domain and the upper bound of the initial magnetic field for single extraction are constrained by two boundary mechanisms: the obstruction of parent-particle infall and the loss of negative energy in the captured fragment. Finally, for repetitive extraction processes within the surveyed regime, all sequences terminate due to orbital accessibility obstructions, explicitly local-slope failure and far-field incident-branch closure, with the final black-hole state retaining most of its initial rotational energy reservoir. Moreover, the black-hole state evolves along a common integral curve independent of the parent particle's specific energy, which solely alters the sequence endpoint.

</details>


### [94] [Deviations from Kerr: Critical curves and photon-ring critical parameters for general observer inclinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12050)
*Hao-Peng Yan,Xiang-Qian Li,Xiao-Jun Yue*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a first-order analytic framework to compute deviations of photon-ring observables (critical curve, demagnification, time delay, azimuthal advance) from Kerr geometry in stationary, axisymmetric, separable spacetimes, using a Kerr-relative decomposition of radial metric functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantify how photon-ring observables—key targets for EHT and future very-long-baseline interferometry—depart from Kerr predictions in viable black hole alternatives, enabling model-independent tests of the Kerr hypothesis.

Method: Kerr-relative decomposition of radial metric functions; analytic perturbative expansion of null geodesic equations; computation of signed normal displacement of critical curve, demagnification exponent, time delay, and azimuthal advance at common screen position angle; use of intrinsic relations among exact observables to isolate geometric vs. instability contributions.

Result: Critical curve displacement factorizes as $\tilde u_n = \tilde F_{\mathrm K} \mathscr C$, where $\tilde F_{\mathrm K}$ is purely Kerr-dependent and $\mathscr C$ encodes optical metric deviations; demagnification depends additionally on radial Lyapunov exponent (instability), while time delay and azimuthal advance share intrinsic relations with the critical curve; first-order predictions are validated against exact finite-deformation profiles and independent numerical ray-tracing on regular photon-shell branches.

Conclusion: The formalism provides a robust, geometrically transparent, and computationally efficient tool for interpreting high-resolution black hole shadow and photon-ring measurements in terms of underlying spacetime structure, distinguishing between angular and radial metric deformations.

Abstract: We study critical curves and photon-ring critical parameters in stationary, axisymmetric spacetimes with separable null geodesics and Kerr angular structure. A Kerr-relative decomposition of the radial metric functions gives analytic first-order departures of these observables for observers at general inclination. The critical curve's signed normal displacement factorizes as $\tilde u_n=\tilde F_{\mathrm K}\mathscr C$, where $\tilde F_{\mathrm K}$ depends only on the Kerr reference geometry and $\mathscr C$ is an explicit combination of optical metric deviations. The demagnification exponent, time delay, and azimuthal advance are evaluated at a common position angle on the observer screen. Intrinsic relations among the exact observables express the optical information shared by the curve, time delay, and azimuthal advance, while demagnification additionally depends on radial instability. Applications to representative metrics illustrate how different radial deformations produce distinct departures from Kerr around the critical curve. Comparisons with exact finite-deformation profiles and independent numerical calculations verify the first-order predictions on regular, accessible exterior photon-shell branches.

</details>


### [95] [Next-to-next-to-leading gravitational radiation-reaction corrections to quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic compact binary orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12093)
*Donato Bini,Luc Blanchet,Guillaume Faye,Andrea Geralico,Quentin Henry,Sara Rufrano Aliberti,Emeric Seraille*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes radiation-reaction corrections at 4.5PN order for both quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic orbits of compact binaries using the Lagrange variation-of-constants method, confirming analytic equivalence of local effects between the two orbital regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently incorporate next-to-next-to-leading-order (4.5PN) radiation-reaction effects—including non-local tail and recoil contributions—into orbital dynamics of compact binaries, bridging gaps between elliptic and hyperbolic treatments.

Method: Applies Lagrange’s method of variation of constants to integrate the recently derived 4.5PN radiation-reaction acceleration (in harmonic coordinates, both general and center-of-mass frames); handles quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic cases separately with tailored perturbation equations and orbital parameters; evaluates non-local contributions from 4PN tails and 4.5PN gravitational-wave recoil.

Result: Derives complete 4.5PN RR corrections to orbital elements for both quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic motion; analytically confirms equivalence of local RR effects across the two regimes via analytic continuation.

Conclusion: The local 4.5PN radiation-reaction dynamics are unified across bound and unbound orbits, supporting a coherent PN framework for gravitational-wave modeling in both inspiral and scattering scenarios.

Abstract: We apply the Lagrange method of variation of constants to compute the radiation-reaction (RR) corrections to the quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic motion of compact binary systems at 4.5PN order, i.e., at next-to-next-to-leading order in the RR. We rely on the recent determination of the 4.5PN RR acceleration, in a general frame and in the center-of-mass frame, in harmonic coordinates. The non-local effects, due to the tails at 4PN order and to the gravitational-wave recoil at 4.5PN order, are carefully evaluated. Although the calculation is implemented differently in the quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic cases (different perturbation equations and choices of orbital parameters), we verify by analytic continuation that the results for the local effects are equivalent in the two cases.

</details>


### [96] [Gravity Without Absolutes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12489)
*Julian Barbour,Maria I. R. Lourenço*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper argues that reductionism, long dominant in physics since Newton, may need replacement by holism due to stagnation and foundational issues; using Newtonian gravity as a test case, it shows that applying Newton’s second law in absolute space/time yields infinite arbitrary solutions for N-body universes, and that Leibniz’s Principle of Sufficient Reason (PSR) eliminates this arbitrariness—implying necessarily infinite particle number and a shift from causation to correlation as fundamental.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of major advances in physics over the last half-century and persistent conceptual problems in current paradigms motivate a re-examination of reductionism’s foundational role—and a potential shift toward holism.

Method: Analytical reconstruction of Newtonian mechanics: treating Newton’s second law as central, examining its implications in absolute space and time for closed N-body universes (not subsystems), and applying Leibniz’s Principle of Sufficient Reason to constrain solutions and infer ontological consequences.

Result: Newton’s second law alone admits infinitely many arbitrary solutions for N-body universes under reductionist assumptions; PSR eliminates this arbitrariness and implies that physically admissible universes must contain infinitely many particles, leading to a reconceptualization where correlation—not causal evolution—may underlie apparent dynamical behavior.

Conclusion: Holism, grounded in principles like PSR rather than reductionist mechanical laws, offers a promising ontological and methodological alternative for advancing fundamental physics.

Abstract: By formulating his theory of universal gravitation in absolute space and time, Newton demonstrated the power of reductionism; it has been behind every major advance in physics since then. However, the lack of significant advances over the last half-century, together with unresolved problems in the dominant paradigms of physics and cosmology, suggest that holism may need to replace reductionism as the way forward. The ontological simplicity and historical significance of Newtonian gravity makes it the ideal theory for examining this possibility. Taking Newton's second law of motion to be the core of his theory, we show that its use within the reductionist framework of absolute space and time introduces an infinity of arbitrary solutions if used to describe $N$-body universes rather than subsystems within them. We show that Leibniz's principle of sufficient reason (PSR) eliminates the infinite arbitrariness and transforms the predicted nature of $N$-body universes; they are all shown to be unexpectedly interesting. An even greater change is enforced if one asks what principle could determine the number of particles in the universe. The PSR suggests it can only be infinite. We suggest that, in such a universe, correlation could replace causation while still giving the appearance that each state has been created by dynamical evolution from the past, whereas its actual explanation may lie entirely within the present state. While much work remains to develop these ideas into a viable theory, we are confident that it advances the case for holism.

</details>


### [97] [Numerical-Relativity Particle-in-Cell Simulations: Self-Gravitating Collisionless Matter in Dynamical Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12831)
*Jiaxi Wu,Elias R. Most*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new numerical-relativity particle-in-cell (NRPIC) framework for solving the Einstein–Vlasov system in 3D, enabling fully kinetic, collisionless relativistic simulations coupled to dynamical spacetime evolution using Z4c and moving-puncture gauge; validated on dust collapse, prolate collapse, cluster stability, and binary black hole formation/merger in a collisionless environment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational collapse, black-hole dynamics in multi-scale systems, and extremal black hole formation demand fully nonlinear, kinetic treatments of matter—beyond fluid approximations—coupled self-consistently to general-relativistic spacetime evolution.

Method: Develops a 3D numerical-relativity particle-in-cell (NRPIC) solver for the Einstein–Vlasov system, built on AthenaK; uses Z4c formulation with moving-puncture gauge; implements conservative stress-energy deposition on AMR grids; introduces a Boris-type geodesic integrator leveraging an electrodynamic analogy (Lorentz-force form of geodesic equation).

Result: Validated against spherical dust collapse; successfully simulates prolate collapse through caustics into black holes; demonstrates nonlinear stability/migration of relativistic clusters; performs first full 3D simulation of two overdensities collapsing within a shared collisionless envelope, leading to black-hole binary inspiral, merger, and post-merger accretion.

Conclusion: The NRPIC framework enables accurate, scalable, and physically faithful modeling of collisionless relativistic matter in strong-field gravity, opening new avenues for studying gravitational instability, structure formation, and black-hole astrophysics beyond fluid assumptions.

Abstract: Gravitational collapse and instability, black-hole dynamics in systems with large scale separations, and questions surrounding extremal black hole formation require full nonlinear studies using accurate and adapted numerical tools. To this end, we present a new algorithm to study collisionless relativistic systems using a fully kinetic description of matter coupled to the evolving spacetime. In detail, we present a numerical-relativity particle-in-cell (NRPIC) framework for solving the Einstein--Vlasov system in three spatial dimensions. Implemented on top of the performance-portable \texttt{AthenaK} code, the method couples particle dynamics to Z4c spacetime evolution in moving puncture gauge through conservative stress-energy deposition on adaptively refined meshes. Our implementation makes use of advances in the computational modeling of relativistic plasmas using modern PIC models, now made available to general-relativistic gravity via an equivalent electrodynamic formulation. We introduce a Boris-type particle pusher that exploits the electrodynamic (Lorentz force) form of the geodesic force. We validate the coupled evolution against spherical dust collapse, follow prolate collapse through collisionless caustics into black-hole formation, and investigate the stability and nonlinear migration of relativistic clusters. Highlighting its potential to study gravitational instability in NR, we simulate the collapse of two overdensities within a common collisionless envelope, following black-hole formation, binary inspiral and merger, and subsequent mass accretion onto the remnant.

</details>


### [98] [Beyond $Λ$CDM: Probing Structure Growth and Observational Viability of Generalized ${f\,(Q , \mathcal{L}_{m})}$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12839)
*Raj Kumar Das,Sivasish Paul*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a generalized power-law $f(Q, \mathcal{L}_m)$ gravity model with additive minimal coupling, deriving the Hubble function via novel parametrization; it introduces non-conserved matter continuity (interpreted as particle creation) for $\nu \neq 1$, constrains parameters using OHD, BAO, and SNe Ia data, and analyzes linear structure growth via $f\sigma_8$; Bayesian evidence shows background data moderately favour certain realisations, but growth data strongly prefer $\nu = 1$, i.e., standard conservation and recovery of $\Lambda$CDM-like behaviour.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the viability of generalized additive minimal coupling in symmetric teleparallel $f(Q, \mathcal{L}_m)$ gravity beyond background cosmography, particularly probing whether non-conservation of the matter continuity equation (as induced by $\nu \neq 1$) can reconcile observational tensions or improve fits — and to assess the critical role of structure growth data in discriminating such extensions from $\Lambda$CDM.

Method: Derives the background Hubble function $H(z)$ from a generalized power-law $f(Q, \mathcal{L}_m) = -Q^\mu + \alpha \mathcal{L}_m^{\,\nu} + \beta$; formulates and solves the non-conserved continuity, Euler, and perturbed field equations for linear scalar perturbations; performs joint likelihood analysis using OHD, DESI DR2 BAO, three SNe Ia samples (Pantheon+, Union3, DES-SN5YR), and $f\sigma_8$ growth data; computes Bayesian evidence relative to $\Lambda$CDM to quantify model preference.

Result: Background data alone yield moderate Bayesian support for some $\nu \neq 1$ realisations; however, inclusion of $f\sigma_8$ data drives $\nu \to 1$ with high significance, restoring matter conservation and effectively collapsing the model toward $\Lambda$CDM; the growth probe strongly disfavours the extra freedom introduced by $\nu \neq 1$, indicating tension between background and growth observables in this extension.

Conclusion: While generalized $f(Q, \mathcal{L}_m)$ gravity with $\nu \neq 1$ offers flexibility at the background level — interpretable as particle creation — linear structure growth data ($f\sigma_8$) robustly constrain $\nu$ to unity, underscoring that growth observables are decisive in testing non-minimal couplings in symmetric teleparallel gravity and reinforcing the empirical adequacy of standard matter conservation in $\Lambda$CDM.

Abstract: $f(Q, \mathcal{L}_m)$ gravity is a well-known extension of $f(Q)$ gravity, obtained through non-minimal or additive minimal coupling in the action's Lagrangian density. Background-level cosmography within this framework, along with comparisons to $\Lambda$CDM, has been the focus of prior studies. The present work considers a generalized power-law form of the additive minimal coupling, $f(Q,\mathcal{L}_m) = -Q^\mu + \alpha \mathcal{L}_m^{\,\nu} + \beta$, from which the Hubble function $H(z)$ is derived through a novel parametrization. When $\nu \neq 1$, the matter continuity equation is rendered non-conserved, a feature that can be interpreted as an effective particle-creation mechanism. Constraints on the model parameters are obtained from Observational Hubble Data, DESI DR2 BAO measurements, and three SNe Ia compilations (Pantheon+, Union3, DES-SN5YR), allowing their cosmographic effects to be examined. Beyond the background level, the perturbed field, Euler, and continuity equations are derived under non-conservation, and their impact on structure growth is studied through a joint analysis incorporating the $f\sigma_8$ probe. Bayesian evidence, computed relative to $\Lambda$CDM, shows that the model is favoured by background data in several cases, with moderate support for one realisation, whereas the inclusion of $f\sigma_8$ drives $\nu\to1$ and decisively disfavours the additional freedom, highlighting the importance of growth data in testing such extensions.

</details>


### [99] [Mapping of Spacetimes with Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12857)
*Estuti Shukla,David Radice,Rossella Gamba,Sebastiano Bernuzzi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a neural network method to learn coordinate transformations from numerical relativity simulations to analytical gauge targets, enabling gauge-invariant comparison and perturbative waveform modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gauge freedom in numerical relativity hinders coordinate interpretation and cross-simulation comparison; a data-driven method is needed to map numerical metrics to physically meaningful analytical gauges without assuming transformation forms.

Method: A neural network is trained end-to-end to learn a smooth, invertible coordinate map by minimizing the discrepancy between the pullback of the source metric (numerical) and the target metric (analytical), using only metric tensor components as input/output—no parametric ansatz for the transformation.

Result: The method recovers known coordinate maps for Schwarzschild and Kerr analytically; for moving-puncture simulations, it yields time-dependent maps to ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein Schwarzschild and approximate Kerr backgrounds; curvature perturbations constructed via the learned map, when evolved with the Teukolsky equation, reproduce full nonlinear waveforms with high fidelity.

Conclusion: Learned coordinate maps provide a robust, gauge-agnostic bridge between numerical spacetimes and analytical backgrounds, enabling perturbative modeling, cross-code validation, and physical interpretation of simulation outputs.

Abstract: Gauge freedom in numerical relativity complicates the interpretation of simulation coordinates and the comparison of different descriptions of the same spacetime. We present a data-driven neural network method for learning coordinate maps from numerical metric data to a prescribed target gauge. The network is trained by matching the transformed source metric to the target metric, without assuming a functional form for the coordinate transformation. We first validate the method on analytical Schwarzschild and Kerr geometries, recovering known transformations up to the isometries of the target spacetime. We then apply it to numerical black hole evolutions in moving-puncture coordinates. For a non-spinning black hole, the learned map relates the time-dependent numerical metric to a stationary Schwarzschild metric in ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates. For a spinning puncture, the method provides an approximate identification with a Kerr background despite departures from Kerr in the numerical spacetime. We use this identification to construct curvature perturbations and evolve them with the Teukolsky equation, obtaining waveforms that closely match those extracted from the full nonlinear simulation. These results demonstrate that learned coordinate maps can relate numerical spacetimes to analytical backgrounds, with potential applications to cross-code validation and black hole perturbation theory.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [100] [A Free-Field Realization of Boundary Symmetries in Discrete Jackiw--Teitelboim Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10074)
*H.T. Özer,Aytül Filiz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a finite-periodic Miura free-field realization of the classical Virasoro–dilaton sector for sl(2,R) Jackiw–Teitelboim gravity, using a single logarithmic Cartan field and discrete Miura map to define lattice spin-two current and periodic dilaton; it derives an induced mixed Poisson bracket, tests consistency with continuous symmetry, and provides a free-field analog of JT boundary transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a free-field, lattice-based realization of the Virasoro–dilaton algebra underlying JT gravity—specifically, to reconcile discrete Miura structure with the classical boundary symmetry without ad hoc assumptions about dilaton Poisson brackets.

Method: Uses discrete Miura map and its free-field Poisson bracket on a finite periodic lattice; constructs spin-two current from a single logarithmic Cartan field; determines dilaton as a periodic composite via inhomogeneous difference equation and monodromy condition; derives induced mixed Poisson bracket; analyzes transfer problem, quadratic adjoint invariant, and finite Fourier algebra vs. continuous Virasoro–dilaton transformation.

Result: An explicit, self-consistent finite-periodic free-field realization yielding the Virasoro–dilaton sector with correct conformal weight (−1) for the dilaton; emergence of an induced mixed Poisson bracket; verification that the finite Fourier algebra reproduces continuous transformation laws; identification of boundary transformation analogs—but no identification of Miura Poisson structure with canonical BF boundary-charge algebra.

Conclusion: The construction successfully realizes the classical Virasoro–dilaton algebra in a discrete, free-field setting compatible with JT gravity’s boundary symmetries, offering a novel lattice framework—but stops short of equating the Miura Poisson structure with the canonical BF charge algebra.

Abstract: We investigate a finite-periodic Miura free-field realization of the classical Virasoro--dilaton sector relevant to sl(2,R) Jackiw--Teitelboim (JT) gravity. Starting from a single logarithmic Cartan field, we use the established discrete Miura map and its free-field Poisson bracket to construct the lattice spin-two current. The dilaton is then realized as a periodic composite of the same Cartan variables, selected by an inhomogeneous difference equation and its monodromy condition. This construction yields an explicit induced mixed Poisson bracket without postulating an independent dilaton bracket.
