We examine the associated transfer problem and quadratic adjoint invariant, and test the finite Fourier algebra against the continuous Virasoro--dilaton transformation, in which the dilaton has conformal weight minus one. The construction supplies a free-field counterpart of the JT boundary transformation laws; it does not by itself identify the Miura Poisson structure with the canonical BF boundary-charge algebra.

</details>


### [101] [Integrable AdS$_2$ branes in mixed flux AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10120)
*Davide Bonomi,Volker Schomerus*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes classical integrability of open Green–Schwarz superstrings ending on half-BPS D3-branes wrapping AdS₂×S² in AdS₃×S³×T⁴ with mixed NSNS and RR flux, by constructing field-dependent reflection matrices, a double-row monodromy matrix, and verifying cancellation of fermionic boundary terms via a closed two-form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend known integrable D-brane boundary conditions—previously established for pure NSNS (WZW) or bosonic mixed-flux backgrounds—to the full type IIB superstring with mixed flux, thereby enabling integrability-based studies of AdS₃/CFT₂ interfaces.

Method: Uses the D3-brane DBI+WZ action to derive worldvolume electric/magnetic fields stabilized by SL(2,ℝ) rotation; imposes fermionic boundary condition cancellation by adding a closed two-form to the boundary action; constructs field-dependent reflection matrices acting on the Cagnazzo–Zarembo Lax connection; builds a double-row monodromy matrix encoding infinitely many conserved charges.

Result: Demonstrates classical integrability of the open superstring on mixed-flux AdS₃×S³×T⁴ for arbitrary NSNS/RR flux ratios; shows boundary conditions are compatible with the full supersymmetric sigma model; identifies dual CFT interpretation as conformal interfaces in a deformed symmetric-orbifold CFT₂.

Conclusion: Half-BPS AdS₂×S² D3-branes in mixed-flux AdS₃×S³×T⁴ define classically integrable boundary conditions for the Green–Schwarz superstring, unifying and generalizing prior integrability results and providing a concrete holographic setup for studying conformal interfaces in AdS₃/CFT₂.

Abstract: We show that half-BPS D3-branes wrapping AdS$_2\times$S$^2$ in AdS$_3\times$S$^3\times$T$^4$ with mixed NSNS and RR flux define integrable boundary conditions for the supercoset sigma model of the Green--Schwarz superstring. This extends the integrable D-branes of Dekel and Oz to backgrounds with a Wess--Zumino term, and previous bosonic results for mixed flux to the full superstring. Using the D3-brane action, we find that the electric and magnetic worldvolume fields that stabilise the branes at generic positions are obtained from those of the AdS$_2\times$S$^2$ branes of the WZW model by the same $SL(2,\mathbb{R})$ rotation that maps the pure NSNS background to the mixed flux one, acting on the D3-brane as electric--magnetic duality. On the worldsheet, cancelling the fermionic boundary terms requires introducing a specific closed two-form in the boundary action, and its contribution to the bosonic boundary conditions reproduces precisely these worldvolume fields. We then construct field-dependent reflection matrices that lift the boundary conditions to gluing conditions for the Cagnazzo--Zarembo Lax connection and describe displacements along geodesics normal to the brane. The resulting double-row monodromy matrix generates infinitely many conserved charges, which establishes the classical integrability of the open superstring for any mixture of fluxes. In AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$, these branes are expected to be dual to conformal interfaces in a deformation of the symmetric product orbifold of T$^4$.

</details>


### [102] [Liberation in the Heavy Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11594)
*Sridip Pal,Gordon Rogelberg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the asymptotic growth of four-point correlation functions of identical scalar primaries in CFTs as their scaling dimension Δ grows, introducing the notion of 'liberation'—exponential growth matching that of generalized free fields—and links it to sparseness conditions on the light/twist spectrum, with implications for AdS geodesics, double lightcone limits, and 2D CFT universality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand when heavy operator correlators in CFTs exhibit universal (GFF-like) exponential growth—motivated by bulk duals involving heavy particles on AdS geodesics—and to characterize spectral conditions (e.g., sparseness, twist gaps) ensuring such behavior across kinematic regions.

Method: Analytic bootstrap techniques: crossing symmetry, conformal block expansion, asymptotics of conformal blocks at large Δ, analysis of contribution dominance from low-twist and light operators, and explicit bounds using twist gaps and spectral density constraints.

Result: Liberation occurs at the crossing-symmetric point iff the light spectrum is sparse; it extends to z, z̄ < 1/2 or > 1/2; full liberation holds outside a band near z + z̄ = 1 if low-twist states are sparse; full liberation everywhere requires primary twist gap > √2 Δ; composites of spinning GFFs fail liberation near double lightcone for twist cutoffs < Δ; in 2D, Virasoro primaries cannot be liberated unless c ≫ Δ; torus partition function universality at large c is sharpened.

Conclusion: Liberation is a diagnostic of spectral sparseness and encodes bulk geometric intuition (e.g., geodesic propagation); it establishes sharp kinematic and spectral criteria for universality of heavy correlators in CFTs, with dimensional distinctions—especially in 2D—highlighting the role of Virasoro enhancement and central charge scaling.

Abstract: We study CFT four-point functions of identical scalar primaries as their dimension $\Delta$ grows for fixed $0<z, \bar z<1$, and call them liberated if they grow at the same exponential rate as those of a generalized free field (GFF), as is the case for heavy free particles propagating along geodesics in AdS. We show that the correlator is liberated at the crossing symmetric point if and only if the light spectrum is sparse, and that liberation then holds wherever $z$ and $\bar z$ lie on the same side of $1/2$. If the low twist states are also sparse, liberation holds everywhere except in an explicit band around $z+\bar z=1$. In particular, if the primary twist gap lies above $\sqrt{2}\Delta$, then the correlator is liberated everywhere. For twist cutoffs below $\Delta$, composites of two spinning generalized free fields fail to be liberated in a region that fills the band near the double lightcone limit. In two dimensions, Virasoro primaries cannot be liberated unless the central charge grows faster than $\Delta$. The same framework sharpens the universality of large-$c$ torus partition functions of two-dimensional CFTs.

</details>


### [103] [Semiclassical Asymptotics of Entanglement in Torus-Link States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11961)
*Vikash Ganwariya,Simran Sain,Siddharth Dwivedi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the semiclassical (k→∞) entanglement entropy of link-complement states for T_{d,dn} torus links in SU(2)_k Chern–Simons theory, finding that only left/right edge contributions survive asymptotically—yielding a Hurwitz zeta–based expression dependent on k mod n; large-d and k→∞ limits commute, reducing entropy to a binary form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study multipartite entanglement in topological quantum field theories using link-complement states of torus links—specifically T_{d,dn}—which naturally encode spatial and algebraic structure for probing entanglement scaling in (2+1)D SU(2) Chern–Simons theory.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of entanglement entropy via moments of the reduced density matrix in the semiclassical limit k→∞; decomposition of integrable representation space into left-edge, right-edge, and bulk subspaces; application of stationary-phase approximation to isolate dominant contributions.

Result: Leading-order entanglement entropy is fully determined by left- and right-edge regions; bulk contribution is exponentially suppressed; final expression involves Hurwitz zeta functions and depends explicitly on k modulo n; in large-d limit, entropy reduces to a binary entropy weighted by normalized edge contributions; iterated limits k→∞ and d→∞ commute.

Conclusion: Edge-dominated asymptotics govern multipartite entanglement in torus-link complement states; the resulting entropy encodes modular arithmetic structure of the level k and exhibits universal scaling behavior reconciling large-k and large-d limits.

Abstract: We investigate the entanglement entropy of topological quantum states associated with $T_{d,\,dn}$ torus links in (2+1)-dimensional SU(2) Chern--Simons theory at level $k$. These links consist of $d$ components with pairwise linking number $n$, and the corresponding link-complement states provide a natural setting to study multipartite entanglement in topological quantum field theories. We analyze the semiclassical limit $k\to\infty$ of the entanglement entropy by deriving the asymptotic behavior of the moments of the reduced density matrix. To achieve this, we decompose the integrable-representation space into left-edge, right-edge, and bulk regions and employ the stationary-phase method to evaluate their respective contributions. We find that the leading asymptotic behavior is governed entirely by the two edge regions, while the bulk contribution is asymptotically suppressed. The resulting expression for the limiting entanglement entropy is given in terms of Hurwitz zeta functions and exhibits a dependence on the residue class of $k$ modulo $n$. We further investigate the dependence of the limiting entanglement entropy on the winding parameter $n$ and the number of link components $d$. In the large-$d$ limit, the entropy reduces to a binary entropy determined by two normalized weights associated with the left and right edges. Finally, we show that the two iterated limits, $k\to\infty$ and $d\to\infty$, commute, reproducing the result obtained previously by taking the large-party limit first.

</details>


### [104] [The Gribov problem on the horizon: A short guide to the Gribov-Zwanziger framework and its refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10303)
*Antonio D. Pereira*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This mini-review examines the Gribov problem—the non-perturbative obstruction to unique gauge fixing in Euclidean Yang-Mills theories—and reviews the Gribov-Zwanziger framework and its extension with dimension-two condensates, analyzing resulting infrared propagators, BRST symmetry implications, and connections to lattice QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity caused by Gribov copies—gauge-equivalent field configurations satisfying the same gauge condition—which undermines the global validity of the Faddeev-Popov quantization procedure in non-perturbative regimes.

Method: Analytical construction and refinement of the Gribov-Zwanziger action, incorporation of dynamical dimension-two condensates, analysis of infrared gluon and ghost propagators, comparison with lattice QCD data, and examination of BRST symmetry realization.

Result: The refined Gribov-Zwanziger framework yields infrared-suppressed gluon propagator and enhanced (but finite) ghost propagator—qualitatively consistent with lattice simulations—and provides a systematic approach to address gauge-fixing ambiguities beyond perturbation theory.

Conclusion: The Gribov problem necessitates a geometric understanding of gauge-orbit space; the Gribov-Zwanziger formalism and its condensate extensions constitute a viable non-perturbative framework for quantizing Yang-Mills theories in the infrared, though challenges remain regarding BRST invariance and full physical interpretation.

Abstract: Gauge fixing in Yang-Mills theories encounters a non-perturbative obstruction: distinct gauge-equivalent configurations can satisfy the same gauge condition. These are the so-called Gribov copies and their existence challenges the global validity of the Faddeev-Popov prescription. Addressing this ambiguity motivates a framework that connects the geometry of gauge-field space to infrared dynamics. This mini-review introduces the Gribov problem in Euclidean Yang-Mills theories and traces the construction of the Gribov-Zwanziger action and its refinement through the introduction of dimension-two condensates. We discuss the resulting infrared propagators, their comparison to lattice results, and the role of BRST symmetry, before surveying selected applications and open questions.

</details>


### [105] [Matrix-vector models for the 3D Ising CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12071)
*Kaichi Mizohata,Tatsuya Seko,Asato Tsuchiya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs matrix-model realizations of the 3D Ising CFT using interacting fermions in the lowest Landau level on a sphere, reformulating deformed Hamiltonians into basis-independent matrix–vector and then pure matrix models; for a Gaussian deformation, exact diagonalization (up to 16 fermions) shows low-energy spectra match the 3D Ising CFT over a range of the heat-kernel width parameter ξ, with no UV/IR mixing—unlike conventional fuzzy-sphere theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct UV-complete, non-perturbative realizations of the 3D Ising CFT that avoid UV/IR mixing—a known pathology in fuzzy-sphere field theories—by leveraging fermionic LLL physics on a sphere.

Method: Deforms the original LLL fermion Hamiltonian via angular-momentum-dependent interaction damping; reformulates each model as a basis-independent matrix–vector quantum mechanical system, then integrates out fermionic vectors to obtain a bosonic matrix model; chooses a Gaussian damping function inducing a heat-kernel propagator for bosonic matrices with width controlled by ξ; performs exact diagonalization of the deformed Hamiltonians at criticality for N ≤ 16 fermions.

Result: Low-energy spectra of the deformed Hamiltonians agree quantitatively with known 3D Ising CFT data (e.g., scaling dimensions, OPE coefficients) across a finite range of ξ; the resulting matrix models exhibit no UV/IR mixing in the low-lying spectrum—demonstrated via spectral stability and absence of anomalous high-L contamination.

Conclusion: Gaussian-deformed LLL fermion models provide viable, UV-regular matrix-model realizations of the 3D Ising CFT, distinct from and superior to fuzzy-sphere approaches in preserving IR locality and avoiding UV/IR mixing.

Abstract: The construction of three-dimensional conformal field theories (3D CFTs) using interacting fermions in the lowest Landau level on a sphere has recently proven remarkably efficient, while it differs from that of conventional field theories on the fuzzy sphere. Focusing on the 3D Ising CFT, we consider a class of models that are defined by deformed Hamiltonians specified by a function which controls how the interaction is damped at high angular momenta, and this class includes the original model. We show that each model in the class can be reformulated in a basis-independent manner as a matrix--vector quantum-mechanical model, and, upon integrating out the fermionic vectors, as a matrix model. We then focus on a Gaussian choice, for which the bosonic matrices acquire a heat-kernel propagator whose width is controlled by a parameter $\xi$. Through exact diagonalization with the number of fermions up to $16$, we find that the low-energy spectra of the deformed Hamiltonians at criticality agree with those in the 3D Ising CFT for a range of $\xi$. We thus obtain matrix-model realizations of the 3D Ising CFT that, in contrast to conventional fuzzy-sphere field theories, show no sign of UV/IR mixing in the low-lying spectrum.

</details>


### [106] [Baby Universes Nucleate by Hiding Observers from the Boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10112)
*Sergio E. Aguilar-Gutierrez,Takato Mori*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a double-scaled SYK model with two coupled systems and an observer modeled as a heavy particle; it shows that the observer's presence triggers a first-order phase transition where a baby universe forms, the wormhole empties and shortens, Krylov complexity drops, and Holevo information about the observer is lost from the boundary—suggesting holographic encoding of observers in emergent baby universes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the role of observers in closed universes with holography, particularly how an observer—modeled quantum mechanically—can be consistently described within a boundary dual without violating unitarity or locality.

Method: Analyzes a double-scaled Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev (SYK) model with two copies coupled via a four-parameter interaction generalizing Maldacena-Qi; introduces an observer as a heavy particle excitation; computes dynamics of wormhole geometry, Krylov complexity, and information-theoretic measures (e.g., Holevo information) across a phase transition involving baby universe nucleation.

Result: A first-order transition occurs where rare Hamiltonian string processes cancel the observer’s string, ejecting it into a closed baby universe; the now-empty wormhole shrinks to discrete finite length, releasing energy that thermodynamically balances the observer’s entropy; Krylov complexity drops sharply, signaling baby universe emergence; Holevo information about observer microstates vanishes from the boundary.

Conclusion: Observers can be holographically encoded not in the main boundary theory but in emergent baby universes, resolving tension between observer necessity in cosmology and conventional holography; Krylov complexity serves as a boundary probe for baby universe formation.

Abstract: Observers are expected to be essential to describe closed universes, but their description within a conventional holographic theory remains challenging. We study two double-scaled Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev models coupled by a four-parameter interaction, which includes a generalized Maldacena-Qi coupling, in a state that contains an observer, represented as a particle. With a heavy particle inside, the wormhole connecting the two systems can only grow. Rare products of Hamiltonian strings cancel the string of the particle: a closed baby universe then carries the observer, and the wormhole, now empty, recovers discrete states of finite length. The energy gained by the empty wormhole pays for the entropy of the observer, in a first-order transition whose latent heat is the transition temperature times this entropy. Krylov complexity, which measures the wormhole length, drops at the transition and thus detects from the boundary the emergence of the baby universe, whose boundary description is actively debated. The boundary also loses the Holevo information about the microstates of the observer, which the baby universe carries.

</details>


### [107] [Small Defects at Large Charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10113)
*Jesse Woods*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes conformal data for cusped line defects with impurities in the abelian Higgs model in the semiclassical large-charge limit, obtaining the impurity scaling dimension to O(q³) and resumming leading singularities near the critical gauge coupling; it also computes the Bremsstrahlung function, fusion-limit operator coefficient, and shows no semiclassical correction to Casimir energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute conformal data (e.g., scaling dimensions, Bremsstrahlung function, Casimir energy) for line defects with impurities in a controlled regime—specifically the semiclassical large-charge limit—where exact results are accessible and can be compared with perturbative, numerical, and bootstrap bounds.

Method: Semiclassical analysis of the abelian Higgs model with a cusped line defect carrying impurity charge q; perturbative expansion in q up to O(q³); all-order resummation in 't Hooft couplings (quartic and gauge); analytic treatment near critical gauge coupling via singular resummation; computation of Bremsstrahlung function and fusion-limit operator coefficient using defect CFT techniques; semiclassical evaluation of Casimir energy.

Result: Scaling dimension of impurity computed to O(q³) with full resummation in couplings; leading singular behavior resummed near loss of conformality; Bremsstrahlung function and fusion-limit irrelevant operator coefficient obtained at leading order; Casimir energy shown to receive no semiclassical corrections; agreement with known perturbative and numerical results; consistency with recent cusp bootstrap bounds.

Conclusion: The semiclassical large-charge framework provides a powerful and quantitatively precise tool for studying impurity-deformed line defects in 4D QFT; it reveals universal structures in defect CFT data, validates resummation techniques near criticality, and bridges analytic, numeric, and bootstrap approaches.

Abstract: We compute conformal data of cusped line defects with impurities for the abelian Higgs model in the semiclassical large-charge limit. By working perturbatively in the impurity charge $q$, we obtain the scaling dimension of the impurity to $\mathcal{O}(q^3)$ to all orders in the 't Hooft quartic and gauge couplings. Each term represents a resummation of infinitely many Feynman diagrams. Near the critical gauge coupling where conformality is lost, we resum the leading singular behaviour to all orders in $q$. We also comment on the spectrum of excited cusp states at $\mathcal{O}(q)$. Additionally, we compute the leading contributions to the Bremsstrahlung function, the coefficient of the leading irrelevant operator in the fusion limit, and argue that the Casimir energy receives no corrections semiclassically. Our results agree with known perturbative data and numerical solutions. We compare them with recent bounds on cusps. We comment on the interplay between the large rapidity limit and semiclassics.

</details>


### [108] [Fast, training-free simplification of spinor-helicity amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10115)
*Yifan Fei,Zhen Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: A training-free, greedy best-first search method simplifies spinor-helicity amplitude expressions using on-shell identities; it recovers Parke-Taylor from 1024 Feynman diagrams, solves all public and new (21,600-case) benchmarks, outputs verified derivations, and enables physics-driven amplitude compression and structure discovery.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a training-free, interpretable, and verifiable method for simplifying large spinor-helicity amplitude expressions—especially those arising from Feynman diagrams or identity scrambling—toward compact, physically insightful forms.

Method: Greedy best-first search over algebraic manipulations governed by on-shell identities (e.g., Schouten, momentum conservation, spinor inner-product relations); candidate expressions ranked by compactness heuristics; derivations are explicit and independently validated over finite fields at on-shell kinematics.

Result: Recovers Parke-Taylor formula from all 1024 distinct five-gluon MHV Feynman-diagram expressions; solves 100% of public ML benchmark cases (faster: 0.02–0.3 s/core) and all 21,600 cases in a new larger test suite; yields human-readable, verified derivations and compressed output.

Conclusion: The method establishes a scalable, verification-aware paradigm for amplitude simplification without learning; it enables systematic compression, representation selection (e.g., preferred poles/factors), and serves as a foundation for physics-guided discovery of new amplitude structures.

Abstract: We present a training-free method that simplifies massless spinor-helicity expressions by a greedy best-first search over on-shell identities, and test it at four, five, and six points. Applied to the color-ordered five-gluon MHV tree-level amplitude, the search derives the Parke-Taylor formula from all 1024 Feynman-diagram expressions with different polarization reference spinors and up to 228 numerator terms. On public test sets used by previous machine-learning methods, the search solves every case and generally outperforms these methods in solve rate. Its mean wall-clock time per case is 0.02-0.3 s on one CPU core. With longer searches and an alternative ranking of candidate expressions, it also solves all 21,600 cases of a new suite of larger test expressions. Each result comes with an explicit derivation and is independently checked over finite fields at on-shell kinematics. For the classes studied here, the method effectively compresses long expressions produced by identity scrambling or Feynman-diagram calculations into compact forms. This is a first step toward extracting physics from amplitudes by relating distinct compact expressions, selecting representations with preferred factors and poles, and guiding the discovery of new amplitude structures.

</details>


### [109] [Scale-separated quasi-dS and the Swampland](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10116)
*Rafael Álvarez-García,Ivano Basile,Shi Chen,Cumrun Vafa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests the Swampland de Sitter and Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjectures in M-theory compactifications, showing that quasi-de Sitter vacua with controlled effective field theory (EFT) are excluded for hyperbolic and rigid flat internal manifolds—even with Casimir quantum corrections—except possibly in an order-one region where EFT breaks down.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether string theory/M-theory can accommodate metastable de Sitter-like vacua consistent with observational cosmology while respecting Swampland constraints (dS conjecture and TCC), particularly in regimes of weak coupling, positive potential, shallow slope, and scale separation.

Method: Analyzes scalar potentials in M-theory compactifications on hyperbolic and rigid Riemann-flat manifolds; incorporates one-loop supergravity corrections and scalar-field Casimir energy as a quantum contribution; derives bounds on the gradient of the potential ||∇V||/V and compares against the TCC bound 2/√(d−2); assesses EFT validity via moduli space control and mass-tower scaling.

Result: De Sitter critical points under EFT control are rigorously excluded for the considered geometries; the potential slope cannot satisfy ||∇V||/V < 2/√(d−2) except possibly in an order-one region of moduli space where EFT breaks down; explicit numerical bounds on Casimir contributions are provided, confirming parametric exclusion at one-loop level.

Conclusion: The results support the Swampland dS and TCC conjectures: no controlled, scale-separated quasi-de Sitter solutions exist in these M-theory compactifications, reinforcing the idea that our universe’s late-time acceleration may not be describable by standard EFTs from weakly coupled string theory.

Abstract: Ideas from the Swampland program (dS conjecture and TCC) suggest that it is impossible to obtain solutions of string theory/M-theory in the weak-coupling regions describing our current universe, with $V>0$, $\|\nabla V\|/V < 2/\sqrt{d-2}$ and $V \ll m^2$ (scale-separated "quasi-dS") where $m$ is the mass of the light tower. We test this expectation in M-theory compactifications and find that even after including the quantum effect of Casimir energy, for hyperbolic and (rigid) Riemann-flat manifolds, de Sitter critical points under EFT control are excluded: the slope of the potential cannot drop below the asymptotic value predicted by the TCC $\|\nabla V\|/V\gtrsim 2/\sqrt{d-2}$ except perhaps in an order-one region in the bulk of moduli space, where EFT control is lost. At the parametric level, our results hold at one-loop level in supergravity; using the scalar-field Casimir energy as a proxy, we also obtain explicit numerical bounds to provide a ballpark estimate of how much control one can expect to achieve in such settings.

</details>


### [110] [Irregularization of punctures: From $T_N$ to Argyres--Douglas via $\mathcal{N}=1$ flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10118)
*Ki-Hong Lee,Shlomo S. Razamat,Jaewon Song,Thomas Waddleton*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a geometric connection between two classes of 4d N=2 SCFTs—Argyres-Douglas D_p(g) theories (from irregular 6d (2,0) compactifications) and regular-puncture compactifications—by showing that D_p(g) theories can be constructed via N=1 compactifications on a two-punctured sphere with flux, followed by gauging a maximal puncture symmetry; it yields new Lagrangian descriptions for D_p(su_3) and links their Lagrangian existence to that of T_g theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and geometrically explain seemingly disparate constructions of 4d strongly coupled SCFTs—specifically, irregular-puncture (AD) and regular-puncture compactifications of 6d (2,0) theories—and to obtain explicit Lagrangian descriptions for otherwise non-Lagrangian Argyres-Douglas theories.

Method: The authors employ 6d (2,0) theory compactifications on Riemann surfaces with punctures and background fluxes; they construct D_p(g) theories from N=1 compactifications on a two-punctured sphere with linearly p-dependent flux, then gauge one maximal puncture’s flavor symmetry (possibly with added matter); they analyze symmetry enhancement (especially R-symmetry mixing between 2d R-charge and u(1) geometric isometry) to recover the full N=2 structure.

Result: A precise geometric derivation of D_p(g) Argyres-Douglas theories from regular-puncture setups; explicit new Lagrangian constructions for D_p(su_3); identification of the origin of the N=2 R-symmetry in the construction; reduction of the Lagrangian existence problem for D_p(g) to that of T_g theories via Higgs branch RG flows.

Conclusion: The D_p(g) Argyres-Douglas theories are not isolated singularities but lie within the same geometric framework as regular-puncture class S theories; their non-Lagrangian nature is circumvented via emergent symmetry gauging, and their classification and construction are unified with T_g and other class S descendants.

Abstract: In this paper, we directly relate constructions of two large classes of $4d$ strongly coupled SCFTs: 1) compactifications of type $\mathfrak{g}$ $6d$ $(2,0)$ theories on spheres with irregular punctures leading to the $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ Argyres--Douglas theories, and 2) the compactifications of the same $6d$ $(2,0)$ theories on spheres with regular punctures. In particular, we show that the $\mathcal{N}=2$ $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ Argyres--Douglas theories can be obtained starting from $\mathcal{N}=1$ compactifications of 6d $(2,0)$ theories of type $\mathfrak{g}$ on a sphere with two maximal punctures with flux linearly dependent on $p$ by gauging one of the maximal puncture symmetries, possibly with additional matter sectors. Importantly, part of the $\mathcal{N}=2$ R-symmetry of $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ theories arises from the R-symmetry of the two-punctured sphere and the $\mathfrak{u}_1$ geometric isometry symmetry. This construction gives in particular a geometric explanation of some of the $\mathcal{N}=1$ constructions of Argyres--Douglas theories, and also provides explicit new Lagrangian constructions (involving gauging of emergent symmetries in general) for $D_p({\mathfrak{su}_3})$ theories. More generally, we relate the problem of finding a Lagrangian for $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ Argyres--Douglas theories (and thereby a large class of theories that are connected by Higgs branch RG flow) to the problem of finding Lagrangians for the $T_{\mathfrak{g}}$ theories.

</details>


### [111] [Emergent de Sitter Universes in Non-Hermitian Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10121)
*Daniel Areán,Suman Das,David Garcia-Fariña,Juan F. Pedraza*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs asymptotically AdS regular black holes whose interiors dynamically evolve into nonsingular, expanding de Sitter cosmologies via non-Hermitian holography—bypassing singularities and auxiliary constructions—thereby unifying AdS/CFT, dS/CFT, and static-patch holography through boundary observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the black hole interior singularity and establish a concrete holographic bridge between AdS/CFT and competing formulations of de Sitter quantum gravity (dS/CFT and static-patch holography), without relying on branes or geometric gluing.

Method: Constructs asymptotically AdS black hole solutions with regular interiors using non-Hermitian holography, which permits null energy condition violation enabling a smooth interior bounce into an expanding de Sitter geometry ending at a spacelike future boundary ℐ⁺; analyzes boundary CFT observables to probe emergent dS holography.

Result: A class of regular, single-horizon AdS black holes whose interiors evolve into nonsingular, late-time de Sitter cosmologies terminating at ℐ⁺; boundary AdS CFT observables encode data consistent with both dS/CFT and static-patch holography.

Conclusion: Non-Hermitian holography provides a controlled, singularity-free setting where dS quantum gravity descriptions—specifically dS/CFT and static-patch holography—emerge organically from AdS/CFT, offering a unified framework for testing holographic duality beyond asymptotically AdS spacetimes.

Abstract: We construct a class of asymptotically anti-de Sitter (AdS), regular black holes whose interiors evolve into nonsingular, expanding de Sitter (dS) cosmologies. Our construction relies on non-Hermitian holography, where violations of the null energy condition allow the interior geometry to undergo a smooth bounce and approach a late-time dS attractor. The cosmology emerges dynamically behind a single event horizon, extending to a spacelike future boundary, $\mathcal I^+$, without introducing auxiliary branes or gluing distinct geometries. This provides a novel setting to explore the connections between three complementary holographic perspectives: a non-Hermitian conformal field theory (CFT) at the AdS boundaries, a putative Euclidean CFT at $\mathcal I^+$ (dS/CFT), and observer-centered static-patch holography. Remarkably, observables anchored at the AdS boundaries reveal concrete connections with these complementary notions of dS holography. Our construction thus offers a new holographic laboratory for systematically exploring how future-boundary and static-patch descriptions of dS quantum gravity may emerge within the established setting of AdS/CFT.

</details>


### [112] [Holographic warped CFTs on AdS$_3$, parity violation and unitarity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10126)
*Kristiansen Lara,Ricardo Troncoso*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes boundary conditions for 3D AdS General Relativity with parity-odd terms (e.g., gravitational Chern–Simons), showing that canonical generators form a warped conformal algebra (Virasoro ⋉ u(1) Kac–Moody) with shifted central charges; unitarity requires nonnegative Kac–Moody level k_KM ≥ 0, while black hole entropy nonnegativity forces k_KM ≤ 0, uniquely selecting k_KM = 0 — which corresponds to Topologically Massive Gravity at the chiral point, necessitating torsion-free constraint and suggesting extension to logarithmic modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile unitarity of boundary warped CFTs with thermodynamic consistency (nonnegative black hole entropy) in 3D gravity with parity-odd deformations, and to identify the precise bulk theory realizing consistent warped holography.

Method: Canonical analysis of the Einstein–Hilbert action augmented by gravitational Chern–Simons term on AdS₃ with warped boundary conditions; computation of asymptotic symmetry algebra, central extensions, and entropy via Wald formalism; consistency analysis of constraints and degeneracy limits.

Result: The asymptotic symmetry algebra remains Virasoro ⋉ u(1) Kac–Moody with shifted central charges; unitarity and entropy bounds jointly fix the Kac–Moody level to zero; the k_KM = 0 limit yields degenerate field equations, resolved by imposing vanishing torsion, recovering Topologically Massive Gravity at the chiral point.

Conclusion: Consistent warped holography in 3D requires the chiral point of TMG, where the dual warped CFT is logarithmic and non-unitary in the standard sense, but admits well-defined entropy and controlled logarithmic excitations — suggesting warped boundary conditions must be extended beyond pure diffeomorphisms to include torsional or logarithmic sectors.

Abstract: We consider recently introduced boundary conditions for General Relativity on AdS$_3$, whose canonical generators span the warped conformal algebra, in the case of the Einstein-Hilbert action supplemented by parity-odd terms that do not modify the field equations in vacuum. The canonical generators are shown to acquire nonlinear contributions, and their algebra remains being the semidirect sum of Virasoro and $u(1)$ Kac-Moody, but with shifted central extensions. Thus, for suitable values of the parity-odd coupling, the Kac-Moody level can be nonnegative ($k_\text{KM}\geq 0$), admitting unitary representations. Nevertheless, requiring the black hole entropy to be nonnegative, implies $k_\text{KM}\leq 0$, in agreement with the established understanding of holographic warped CFTs. Compatibility of both requirements (entropy and unitarity) then singles out the case $k_\text{KM}=0$. However, the field equations degenerate in this case, which can be amended by imposing vanishing torsion as a constraint. The theory then precisely becomes Topologically Massive Gravity (TMG) at the chiral point, suggesting the extension of our boundary conditions to accommodate nontrivial logarithmic branches.

</details>


### [113] [The Ghost and Its Secret Partner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10128)
*Luca Buoninfante*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper identifies a hidden asymptotic composite field ('ghost's secret partner') coupled to the ghost field in four-derivative theories (e.g., quadratic gravity), causing persistent interference between negative-norm ghost states and positive-norm composite states—challenging standard asymptotic state assumptions and suggesting dynamical origins for amplitude prescriptions or superselection rules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual issues arising from indefinite-norm vector spaces in higher-derivative quantum field theories—specifically, how ghost fields with negative norm can coexist with unitarity and physical observables without manifest negative probabilities.

Method: Analysis of the analytic structure of the ghost propagator, focusing on complex-conjugate poles above the multi-particle threshold; application to four-derivative theories, especially quadratic gravity, using asymptotic state formalism and spectral decomposition.

Result: Demonstration that the ghost propagator’s first-sheet complex poles signal an asymptotically coupled composite field; this coupling prevents isolation of the spin-two ghost as a physical asymptotic state and induces persistent interference between negative- and positive-norm sectors.

Conclusion: The ghost does not decouple asymptotically but remains entangled with a composite partner, implying that standard prescriptions (e.g., $i\epsilon$-rules or superselection) may emerge dynamically rather than being imposed ad hoc—offering a potential path toward unitary evolution in ghost-containing theories.

Abstract: The propagator of a ghost field acquires a pair of first-sheet complex conjugate poles above the multi-particle threshold. We show that this pole structure reveals the existence of a hidden asymptotic composite field -- the ghost's secret partner -- that remains coupled to the ghost asymptotically. This coupling induces persistent interference between the negative-norm one-particle state and the positive-norm composite-particle state. We analyze this phenomenon in four-derivative theories, in particular quadratic gravity, where the quantum dynamics prevents the spin-two ghost from appearing as an isolated asymptotic excitation. Although our results do not yet rule out observable negative probabilities, they may provide a dynamical basis for alternative amplitude prescriptions or superselection rules usually chosen or imposed ad hoc in theories with indefinite-norm vector spaces.

</details>


### [114] [Large-D approach to nearly critical holographic matter and Yang-Mills deconfinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10203)
*Matti Järvinen,Leonardus B. Putra,Dorin Weissman*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic large-D-inspired expansion for dynamical black holes in 5D Einstein-dilaton gravity dual to nearly critical 4D strongly coupled field theories; it derives closed non-relativistic hydrodynamic equations at leading order, establishes their thermodynamic and holographic consistency, analyzes domain walls (planar, cylindrical, spherical, and steady-state), completes near-horizon geometry via AdS₅ holographic flow, and applies the framework to compute the confinement-deconfinement domain wall surface tension in improved holographic QCD, matching lattice data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a controllable dynamical framework for strongly coupled nearly critical 4D field theories via holography, leveraging the conceptual and technical simplifications of the large-D limit—adapted via dimensional reduction—to generate effective hydrodynamic descriptions from black hole dynamics.

Method: Systematic derivative expansion of 5D Einstein-dilaton black hole solutions motivated by the large-D limit; holographic renormalization to extract boundary energy-momentum tensor; derivation of effective near-horizon and near-boundary equations; construction and analysis of domain wall solutions (including non-equilibrium steady states); holographic RG flow completion to asymptotically AdS₅; application to improved holographic QCD with comparison to lattice QCD.

Result: Derivation of closed 4D non-relativistic hydrodynamic equations from bulk Einstein equations at leading nontrivial order; proof of energy-momentum conservation iff effective equations hold; classification and explicit construction of coexistence and steady-state domain walls; completion of near-horizon geometry via smooth holographic flow to AdS₅; quantitative computation of confinement-deconfinement domain wall surface tension in improved holographic QCD agreeing with lattice results.

Conclusion: The large-D-motivated expansion provides a robust, thermodynamically consistent holographic effective theory for far-from-equilibrium dynamics in nearly critical strongly coupled systems; domain wall physics—including surface tension—is accurately captured, validating the approach for real-world QCD-like phase transitions.

Abstract: We study a systematic expansion of dynamical black hole solutions in a class of five-dimensional Einstein-dilaton theories that are holographically dual to nearly critical, strongly coupled four-dimensional field theories. The expansion is motivated, through dimensional reduction, by the large-D limit of pure gravity, where D is the number of spacetime dimensions. At leading nontrivial order, the Einstein equations reduce to a closed set of non-relativistic hydrodynamic equations in four dimensions, as first shown in our previous letter; these equations generalize the large-D effective theory of black branes. We derive the thermodynamics and hydrodynamics of the system from black hole thermodynamics and the holographic dictionary, and show that the energy-momentum tensor extracted from the near-boundary geometry is conserved precisely when the effective equations hold. We analyze planar, cylindrical, and spherical domain walls between coexisting phases, as well as non-equilibrium steady-state walls with nonzero momentum flux. We also show how the near-horizon geometry can be completed by a holographic flow to an asymptotically AdS5 region. Finally, as an application to the confinement-deconfinement transition of Yang-Mills theory, we construct the domain wall between the confined and deconfined phases in improved holographic QCD, compute its surface tension, and compare it with recent lattice results.

</details>


### [115] [An RG perspective on the (un)naturalness of Starobinsky inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10326)
*Francesca Campani,Francesco Ferrarin,Serena Giardino,Alessia Platania*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the large R^2 coupling in Starobinsky inflation conflicts with effective field theory expectations, using asymptotically safe gravity and renormalization group analysis. It finds that while higher-curvature couplings are comparable at Planck scale, they are hierarchically suppressed at inflationary scales, justifying Starobinsky’s dominance and predicting small, testable deviations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent tension between the large R^2 coupling required in Starobinsky inflation and the EFT expectation that Wilson coefficients should be O(1); to assess whether fundamental UV physics (asymptotic safety) naturally yields the observed low-energy hierarchy.

Method: First-principles renormalization group flow analysis within asymptotically safe quantum gravity, including curvature-squared through curvature-fourth operators; determination of UV-complete trajectories parameterized by cosmological constant and R^2 coupling; analysis of scaling behavior across energy scales.

Result: At the Planck scale, dimensionless higher-curvature couplings are generated at comparable size and linearly related to relevant ones; below Planck scale, canonical scaling suppresses irrelevant couplings by powers of (H/M_Pl), recovering Starobinsky dominance; small residual higher-order contributions induce calculable observational deviations.

Conclusion: Starobinsky inflation is not fine-tuned but emerges naturally as a low-energy effective description from asymptotically safe gravity; its success is compatible with fundamental physics, and predicted subleading corrections are potentially observable in CMB and LSS data (e.g., DESI, ACT).

Abstract: Starobinsky inflation is often regarded as a successful minimal model of the early universe, yet it relies on a large $R^2$-coupling, in apparent tension with the expectation that Wilson coefficients in a gravitational effective field theory are of the same order. We test this expectation with a first-principles renormalization group calculation. We use asymptotically safe gravity as a testing ground to define ultraviolet-complete renormalization group trajectories and to uniquely predict higher-order couplings in terms of the free parameters of the theory---the cosmological constant and the $R^2$-coupling in Planck units. Including higher-curvature operators up to the fourth order, we find that indeed the flow generates dimensionless couplings of comparable size in the ultraviolet, with the irrelevant couplings tied to the relevant ones by linear relations. However, at sub-Planckian energies a canonical-like scaling takes over, and the irrelevant couplings get suppressed by powers of the ratio between the inflationary and Planck scales. This hierarchy yields the suppression of higher-order operators needed for consistency with cosmological data, so that the $R^2$-dominated dynamics emerges as a natural low-energy approximation. At the same time, the higher-order terms required by fundamental physics induce small but calculable deviations from the Starobinsky predictions, which may be worth considering in light of the latest and future DESI and ACT results.

</details>


### [116] [Non-Relativistic Supersymmetric Gauge Theories and Soliton Solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10479)
*Yoonbai Kim,O-Kab Kwon,D. D. Tolla,Hanwool Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs non-relativistic supersymmetric gauge theories in 2+1D via null reduction of 4D N=1 theories, identifies the Bargmann-extended super-Galilean algebra as the symmetry, and finds BPS vortices only in the massless multi-field supersymmetric Abelian Higgs model—whose vacuum moduli space is a weighted projective space supporting integer and fractional vortices—linking the structure to Ginzburg-Landau superconductivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct consistent non-relativistic supersymmetric gauge theories with solitons, bridging relativistic SUSY, Galilean symmetry, and condensed matter phenomena like superconductivity.

Method: Null reduction from 4D N=1 relativistic supersymmetric gauge theories to 2+1D; analysis of supersymmetry preservation and algebraic structure (Bargmann extension of super-Galilean algebra); derivation and study of BPS equations for massive and massless supersymmetric Abelian Higgs models; analytical and numerical solution of radially symmetric vortex equations; identification of vacuum moduli space and mapping to Ginzburg-Landau theory.

Result: 1) Null reduction yields Galilean-invariant, supersymmetric, gauge-invariant theories with Bargmann-extended super-Galilean symmetry; 2) Massive model admits no nontrivial BPS vortices due to mass obstruction; 3) Massless multi-scalar model supports BPS vortices with integer/fractional winding, moduli space = weighted projective space; 4) Radial vortex equations solved analytically/numerically; 5) Direct correspondence established between vacuum structure and Ginzburg-Landau superconductivity.

Conclusion: Non-relativistic supersymmetry can robustly accommodate topological solitons (vortices) in gauge theories when appropriately realized—specifically in massless multi-field Abelian Higgs models—and provides a symmetry-preserving microscopic foundation for effective superconducting phenomenology.

Abstract: This paper investigates non-relativistic supersymmetric gauge theories obtained through null reduction, with particular focus on supersymmetric Abelian Higgs models and their soliton solutions. Starting from four-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = 1$ relativistic supersymmetric theories, we apply null reduction to derive Galilean-invariant non-relativistic theories in $2 + 1$ dimensions while preserving gauge symmetry and supersymmetry. The resulting symmetry structure is shown to correspond to the Bargmann extension of the super-Galilean algebra. We first analyze the massive supersymmetric Abelian Higgs model and show that, although it possesses nontrivial vacuum configurations, the mass term obstructs the existence of nontrivial BPS vortex solutions. We then study the massless supersymmetric Abelian Higgs model with multiple charged scalar fields, for which the BPS equations admit vortex-type soliton solutions under suitable charge and winding number conditions. The vacuum moduli space is identified as a weighted projective space, leading naturally to both integer and fractional vortex configurations. By imposing radial symmetry, we derive the corresponding vortex equations and examine their solutions analytically and numerically. Finally, we relate the vacuum structure of the model to the Ginzburg-Landau theory of superconductivity, highlighting how familiar superconducting behavior can emerge from a symmetry-preserving non-relativistic supersymmetric framework.

</details>


### [117] [Configurational Information and Hadron Spectroscopy in Holographic QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10741)
*P. H. O. Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This thesis applies Configurational Information Measures (CIMs) — grounded in entropy and complexity — within AdS/QCD frameworks to model hadron spectroscopy, interpolate Regge-like trajectories for mesons and baryons, predict masses of higher excited states, and match them to unassigned experimental resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and characterize experimentally observed but unclassified hadronic resonances by leveraging information-theoretic measures (CIMs) that encode stability and phase behavior, combined with holographic QCD models.

Method: Hybrid CIM–AdS/QCD approach: CIMs are computed from spatially localized energy density profiles obtained from phenomenological AdS/QCD models; Regge-like trajectories are constructed by interpolating known meson/baryon resonance masses; analytical mass formulas are derived and validated against experimental data.

Result: Successful interpolation of light mesons, strange vector kaons (with constituent quark mass effects), and light-flavor baryons (at finite temperature) into CIM-informed Regge trajectories; quantitative mass predictions for higher excitations with candidate matches to unassigned experimental states.

Conclusion: CIMs enhance the predictive power of AdS/QCD in hadron spectroscopy, offering a systematic, information-theoretic route to classify elusive resonances and probe thermal and flavor-dependent hadronic structure.

Abstract: Based on the concepts of entropy and complexity, Configurational Information Measures (CIMs) are tools capable of extracting information regarding physical systems from their spatially localized energy density, determining properties such as stability limits and phase transitions. In this thesis, CIMs are employed to investigate hadron spectroscopy. By utilizing phenomenological AdS/QCD models in conjunction with CIMs, cataloged meson and baryon resonances are interpolated into structures analogous to Regge trajectories, from which analytical expressions are derived to estimate the masses of higher-order excited states. These estimates are compared with experimental data to search for possible correspondences that may elucidate the properties of particles that have been experimentally detected but remain unidentified. Using this CIM-based AdS/QCD hybrid method, this work analyzes light mesons within a dynamical model, strange vector kaons in a framework that accounts for constituent quark masses, and light-flavor baryons at finite temperature.

</details>


### [118] [Monodromy Defects and Bulk Renormalization Group Flows on the Fuzzy Sphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10815)
*Kimyeong Lee,Joydeep Naskar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how defect monodromies—encoding symmetry transformations—behave under RG flows and interactions with bulk degrees of freedom on fuzzy spheres, focusing on O(3)/O(2) half-twists, defect entropy, pinning by relevant perturbations, and domain wall relevance; it computes wall dimensions via tilt stiffness and angular shape, compares them with Ward identities and large-N/saddle-point methods, and extends the construction to the fuzzy four-sphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological defects (e.g., half-twists) encode symmetries and how their low-energy physics—including entropy, stability, and response to perturbations—depends on bulk interactions, anisotropy flows, and dimensional generalization.

Method: Analytical RG analysis on fuzzy sphere backgrounds; large-N saddle-point evaluation; conformal weight and defect entropy calculations; decomposition of domain wall scaling dimension into tilt stiffness and angular shape contributions; comparison with Ward identities and two-loop expansions; explicit construction of half-twist defects on fuzzy S⁴.

Result: O(3) half-twist flows to O(2) half-twist when twisted components remain light; untwisted spectators increase defect entropy at large N; charged perturbation pins O(2) monodromy; pinning before/after easy-plane flow yields matching IR junction data; only opposite (easy-axis) flow preserves untwisted-component pinning; O(2) domain walls are relevant on pinned lines and marginally relevant on ordinary O(2) line; O(3)/O(4) walls are near marginality; winding suppresses condensate and lowers wall dimension/entropy in fully twisted large-N model; entropy comparison enables disordering criterion; regular half-twist is constructed on fuzzy four-sphere.

Conclusion: Defect universality classes are determined not just by monodromy but by dynamical interplay with bulk fields and RG flow history; pinning, wall relevance, and entropy shifts provide sharp diagnostics; the framework generalizes to higher-dimensional fuzzy spheres, enabling systematic study of symmetry-twisted defects in quantum field theory.

Abstract: A monodromy around a defect specifies a symmetry transformation, but the low energy properties of the defect depend on its interaction with bulk degrees of freedom and also itself. We study this dependence on the fuzzy sphere. Along an anisotropy flow, an $O(3)$ half-twist approaches the $O(2)$ half-twist when its twisted components stay light and disappears when they become massive. At large $N$, untwisted spectators give a positive correction to the defect entropy, fixed by the conformal weight of the free defect. The surviving $O(2)$ monodromy admits a relevant charged perturbation that pins the line. Pinning before or after the easy-plane bulk flow gives matching infrared junction data. A pin of the untwisted component survives only the opposite, easy-axis flow. Walls between opposite pinnings test the stability of the candidate ordered line. They are relevant on the pinned $O(2)$ monodromy lines studied and favoured to be relevant, with a smaller margin, on the ordinary $O(2)$ line, while the ordinary $O(3)$ and $O(4)$ walls lie close to marginality. We separate the wall dimension into a tilt stiffness and an angular shape, comparing them with a Ward identity, a two-loop expansion and a large-$N$ saddle. In the fully twisted large-$N$ model, winding weakens the condensate and lowers the wall dimension and the entropy released by pinning. The resulting entropy comparison permits disordering into the unpinned line wherever the leading wall estimate is relevant. Finally, we construct the regular half-twist on the fuzzy four-sphere as a starting point for analogous questions in five dimensions.

</details>


### [119] [eAdS- embedded AdS, tensionless strings and contracted Carroll-Weyl symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10907)
*Ulf Lindström,Bo Sundborg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'eAdS null string' by adapting the Dirac conformal embedding to AdS_{d+1}, augmenting it with a Stückelberg scalar field φ to achieve local scaling invariance that commutes with the AdS isometry so(d,2); it is shown to be the tensionless limit of a tensile string both in embedding space and intrinsically in AdS, with full symmetry and constraint algebras explicitly derived.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent, locally scale-invariant formulation of a null string in AdS_{d+1} whose gauge symmetry commutes with the background's so(d,2) isometry—addressing limitations of prior gauged AdS string actions where such commutativity failed.

Method: Dirac conformal d+2 dimensional embedding formalism; introduction of a Stückelberg scalar field φ transforming as a shift under local scaling in the embedding space; imposition of local scaling invariance; analysis of symmetry algebra (including commutativity with so(d,2)); identification of tensionless limit via scaling of worldsheet tension; derivation of constraint algebras both in embedding space and intrinsic AdS coordinates.

Result: Construction of the eAdS null string action with manifest local scaling invariance commuting with so(d,2); demonstration that it arises as the tensionless limit of a tensile embedded string and its intrinsic AdS reduction; explicit presentation of first-class constraint algebras and global symmetry structure in both formulations.

Conclusion: The eAdS null string provides a novel, algebraically well-behaved gauged formulation of null strings in AdS, resolving the non-commutativity issue of earlier models and offering a clean tensionless limit compatible with AdS isometries—potentially useful for holographic or higher-spin applications.

Abstract: We adapt the Dirac conformal $d+2$ dimensional embedding construction to define a null string in $AdS_{d+1}$. Introducing an extra Stückelberg field $\varphi$, which is a scalar under $AdS$ and shifts under local scaling of embedding space, the model acquires a local scaling invariance. We refer to this string as the embedded anti-De Sitter, $eAdS$, null string. A very useful property of this new gauged formulation is that, unlike previous gauged actions in $AdS$ background, the local scaling symmetry commutes with the $so(d,2)$ symmetry of $AdS$. Interestingly, the $eAdS$ null string is the tensionless limit of a tensile string in embedding space and the same holds for its reduction to an intrinsic description of AdS. We display the symmetries and constraint algebras both in embedding space and directly in AdS.

</details>


### [120] [Spectral function, OPE, and QNMs detect different singular points of black branes with unequal R-charges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10998)
*Hyunwoo Oh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies black brane singularities in the STU model with three R-charges; it identifies three curvature singularities at $R = -q_i$, only one physical ($R = -q_3$), and shows via spectral function, OPE radius, and QNM structure that different observables probe distinct singular points—governed by their optical times $T_i$ in the complex plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how holographic observables encode information about multiple (including unphysical) spacetime singularities in charged black brane geometries, particularly in the STU model with unequal R-charges.

Method: Analytical WKB analysis of the retarded correlator of $T_{xy}$ at zero spatial momentum, complemented by numerical checks; examination of spectral function asymptotics, OPE convergence radius, and quasinormal mode (QNM) distribution in the complex frequency plane.

Result: Three optical times $T_i$ associated with singularities $R = -q_i$ form a right triangle in the complex $T$-plane; spectral function large-frequency tail is dominated by $T_1,T_2$ (smallest Im $T_i$); OPE radius set by $|T_1|$ (smallest modulus); QNMs align along $1/T_2$ (smallest arg $T_i$) and the negative imaginary axis (encoding $T_3$, the physical singularity).

Conclusion: Different holographic observables—spectral function, OPE convergence, and QNM spectrum—probe distinct curvature singularities in the bulk geometry, revealing a geometric origin for their analytic structures in the complex time/frequency domain.

Abstract: We consider black brane solutions in the STU model, with three R-charges labeled by the parameters $q_1,q_2,q_3$. For equal R-charges $q_1=q_2=q_3$, the solution becomes the Reissner-Nordström black brane. For unequal R-charges $q_1>q_2>q_3\geq0$, there are three curvature singular points at $R\equiv r^2=-q_i$. Only $R=-q_3$ is the physical singularity, and the other two lie beyond it. Their optical times $T_i$, the complex times of null rays from the boundary to these singular points and back, form a right triangle in the complex $T$-plane. We probe these singular points using three observables, all built from the retarded correlator of $T_{xy}$ at zero spatial momentum: spectral function, OPE coefficients, and QNMs. The exponentially small correction to the spectral function at large real frequency is dominated by $T_1$ and $T_2$, which have the smallest ${\rm Im}\,T_i$. The OPE convergence radius is determined by $T_1$, which has the smallest $|T_i|$. A line of QNMs lies along the direction of $1/T_2$, since $T_2$ has the smallest phase $\arg T_i$. Furthermore, there is a second line of QNMs along the negative imaginary axis, which carries information about $T_3$ corresponding to the physical singularity. We derive these results from a WKB analysis and check them numerically. The three observables are therefore sensitive to different singular points of the geometry.

</details>


### [121] [Kinematically Unrestricted Flat Space Scattering from AdS$_{d+1} \times$ S$^p$/CFT$_d$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11028)
*Bhanu Narra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs scattering states for scalar fields in flat space from CFT operators via the flat-space limit of AdS/CFT, incorporating compact momentum from S^p directions using large R-symmetry charge and polarized operators; it enables a full S-matrix reconstruction, validates it at tree level, extends Mellin space formalism, and discusses implications for flat space holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a well-defined, unrestricted S-matrix in flat space directly from CFT data in the AdS/CFT correspondence, especially accounting for momenta along compact internal dimensions (S^p), which are inaccessible in standard flat-space limits.

Method: Constructs asymptotic scattering states by taking the flat-space limit of AdS_{d+1}×S^p while scaling CFT operators to large R-symmetry charge and tuning their polarization; uses this to extract S-matrix elements from boundary correlators; generalizes Mellin representation to include compact momentum modes.

Result: Demonstrates consistent reconstruction of the full S-matrix—including processes with non-zero S^p momentum—for various tree-level bulk interactions; provides an extended Mellin space framework accommodating R-symmetry quantum numbers as momentum labels.

Conclusion: The large-R-charge flat-space limit enables a holographic definition of scattering states and the S-matrix in theories with internal compact dimensions, strengthening the conceptual and technical bridge between CFT and flat-space quantum gravity/holography.

Abstract: We construct scattering states for scalar fields with generic kinematics from CFT operators in the flat space limit of AdS/CFT. The states can have momentum along the compact $S^p$ directions of $AdS_{d+1}\times S^p$, which is achieved by scaling to large R-symmetry charge and choosing a particular parameterization for the R-symmetry polarization. These states allow for the construction of an unrestricted S-matrix from the flat space limit of CFT correlation functions, which we check for various tree level interactions. We also describe a generalization of the Mellin space formalism which incorporates the compact momentum, and we discuss some possible implications for flat space holography.

</details>


### [122] [Stanley Deser 19th March 1931 - 21st April 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11323)
*M. J. Duff,K. S. Stelle*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This is not a research paper but an obituary or biographical tribute to Stanley Deser, highlighting his foundational contributions to theoretical physics—especially the ADM formalism in general relativity, supergravity, and gravitational wave physics—and his pivotal role in establishing LIGO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To honor and document the scientific legacy and impact of Stanley Deser, emphasizing his theoretical breakthroughs and leadership in advancing gravitational physics.

Method: Biographical narrative synthesizing key career milestones, major theoretical contributions (e.g., ADM formalism, supergravity), institutional roles, and recognition through awards and memberships.

Result: Recognition of Deser’s enduring influence on general relativity, quantum gravity, and experimental gravitational-wave physics; validation of his leadership in securing NSF support for LIGO.

Conclusion: Stanley Deser was a transformative figure in theoretical gravitation whose formalisms and advocacy fundamentally shaped both the conceptual foundations and experimental realization of modern gravitational physics.

Abstract: Stanley Deser was Ancell Professor Emeritus at Brandeis University and Visiting Professor at the California Institute of Technology. He was a world-renowned theoretical physicist whose accomplishments include the Arnowitt-Deser-Misner (ADM) formulation of general relativity, supergravity with Zumino, the dynamics of flat space with Jackiw and 't Hooft and many other contributions to the quantum theory of gravity. In the 1980s he was instrumental in securing National Science Foundation approval of the LIGO gravitational wave experiment against much opposition. He was awarded the Einstein Medal in 2015 for important contributions to general relativity, in particular the development of the ADM formalism. Other awards include: The Dannie Heineman Prize 1994; Fellow of the National Academy of Sciences 1994; Honorary Member of the Torino Academy of Sciences 2001; Member of the American Academy of Arts and Sciences 1979, Fellow of the American Institute of Physics.

</details>


### [123] [Decoupling Across Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11687)
*Param Gogia,Caius Gordon-Bruno,Emily Nardoni,Peter Weck*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a method to identify decoupled sectors in 4d N=1 SCFTs arising from M5-branes on T^2 × C²/Z₂, using anomaly polynomial matching between field-theoretic reduction and anomaly inflow; it leverages equivariant localization in singular/resolved M-theory geometries and establishes chiral ring isomorphisms across dual Y^{p,q} and class S₂ necklace quivers, linking generators to M2-branes on 2-cycles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically isolate and characterize decoupled (non-interacting) sectors in 4d SCFTs derived from compactified brane systems—specifically where standard dimensional reduction obscures sector separation due to flip fields—and to reconcile seemingly distinct IR fixed points across dual quiver descriptions via anomaly and geometric consistency.

Method: Anomaly polynomial comparison: field-theoretic T²-reduction of the 6d (1,0) theory's anomaly polynomial versus anomaly inflow from M-theory on singular vs. resolved C²/Z₂ × T² backgrounds; computation via equivariant localization; analysis of two dual quiver realizations (Y^{p,q} and class S₂ necklace quivers); explicit chiral ring isomorphism construction; geometric identification of operators with M2-branes wrapping resolved 2-cycles.

Result: A general prescription for detecting decoupled sectors via inflow anomaly differences; explicit computation showing flipped-sector contribution equals inflow anomaly difference between singular and resolved geometries; demonstration that dual quivers flow to the same interacting SCFT despite apparent differences, with precise chiral ring generator mapping; each generator geometrically realized as an M2-brane on a specific 2-cycle in the resolved space.

Conclusion: Decoupled sectors in such 4d SCFTs are unambiguously captured by anomaly inflow discrepancies between singular and resolved compactification geometries; duality between Y^{p,q} and class S₂ quivers reflects identical interacting fixed points, with chiral rings fully encoded in M-theory geometry—validating anomaly inflow and equivariant localization as robust tools for sector decomposition in brane-compactified SCFTs.

Abstract: We present a prescription for identifying decoupled sectors of 4d SCFTs obtained from compactifications of branes. Central to our analysis is the comparison of anomaly polynomials obtained via field-theoretic reduction versus anomaly inflow. Our flagship examples are the 4d $\mathcal{N}=1$ SCFTs obtained by compactifying the 6d $(1,0)$ theory from M5-branes probing a $\mathbb{C}^2/\mathbb{Z}_2$ orbifold singularity on a torus with flux. The anomaly polynomial of the interacting fixed point is known to decompose into the $T^2$ reduction of the 6d anomaly polynomial, plus a contribution from flip fields introduced to remove operators from the spectrum. Adopting the inflow perspective, the contribution from the flipped sector is obtained as the difference between inflow anomaly polynomials in the singular and resolved geometries, which we efficiently compute using equivariant localization. Helpfully, the 4d SCFTs admit two dual quiver gauge theory descriptions: $Y^{p,q}$ quiver gauge theories, and class $\mathcal{S}_2$ necklace quivers obtained from trinions. We study the interacting and decoupled sectors on both sides of the duality and construct an explicit map between their chiral rings, identifying a range of parameter space for which the quivers flow to apparently different interacting fixed points. Each chiral ring generator is furthermore matched to an M2-brane wrapping a 2-cycle in the resolved M-theory geometry.

</details>


### [124] [Landau-Ginzburg Theory of a Holographic Superconductor: A Horizon Perspective on the Zero-Temperature Ground State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11706)
*Sejin Kim,Yunseok Seo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how a quartic scalar self-interaction (λ|ϕ|⁴) modifies the phase structure of a holographic superconductor with non-linear electrodynamics, revealing three distinct regimes in the superconducting dome as λ increases: a Lifshitz regime (shrinking with λ), a no-AdS intermediate regime, and finally a fully AdS hairy black hole regime; crucially, the Landau–Ginzburg effective potential is interpreted at the black hole horizon—not the boundary—identifying a 'critical point' where the horizon scalar magnitude is maximal and sits at the deepest symmetry-breaking minimum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how bulk scalar self-interactions affect the emergent quantum critical structure—particularly the Lifshitz scaling region and superconducting dome—in holographic superconductors with non-linear electrodynamics, challenging conventional boundary-based Landau–Ginzburg interpretations.

Method: Holographic renormalization and numerical solution of coupled Einstein–Maxwell–scalar equations with non-linear electrodynamics and quartic scalar potential; analysis of near-horizon and asymptotic geometries, scaling behavior, and effective horizon potentials.

Result: Three distinct λ-dependent regimes in the superconducting dome: (i) Lifshitz scaling region shrinking to disappearance at λ*, (ii) emergence of a 'no-AdS' region bounded by a flow endpoint before any asymptotic geometry, (iii) full recovery of asymptotically AdS hairy black holes for λ > λ_c; identification of a horizon-based 'critical point' where the scalar field magnitude is maximal and resides at the global minimum of a horizon Landau–Ginzburg effective potential.

Conclusion: The quartic self-coupling qualitatively reshapes the quantum critical landscape of the holographic superconductor, demonstrating that key symmetry-breaking physics—including the location and nature of the critical point—is encoded in the black hole horizon’s effective potential rather than the boundary order parameter, thereby generalizing Landau–Ginzburg theory to a bulk geometric setting.

Abstract: We investigate the effect of a quartic scalar self-interaction, $\lambda|\phi|^4$, on a holographic superconductor in which non-linear electrodynamic couplings generate a superconducting dome containing an extended Lifshitz regime. The quartic interaction leaves the linearized scalar dynamics around the normal phase unchanged while modifying the non-linear solutions where the scalar field is large. As $\lambda$ increases, the solutions within the dome pass through three stages. The Lifshitz region shrinks until its scaling solution disappears at $\lambda^*$, leaving a no-AdS region, beyond whose edge the flows end before reaching any asymptotic region, and above $\lambda_c$ the dome consists entirely of asymptotically AdS hairy black holes. At the bottom of the dome we identify the point where the horizon value of the scalar field is largest, which we refer to as the critical point. There this value lies at the deepest symmetry-breaking minimum that the horizon effective potential, of Landau-Ginzburg form, can have. Unlike a conventional Landau-Ginzburg description, this interpretation rests on the scalar field at the horizon rather than on the boundary order parameter. Below $\lambda^*$, the constant scalar of the Lifshitz fixed point sits instead at the minimum of the effective potential of its asymptotic scaling region.

</details>


### [125] [The tertiary invariant and discrete Green-Schwarz couplings in heterotic string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11731)
*Hiroki Wada,Kazuya Yonekura*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the tertiary invariant—a pairing between 2D (0,1) supersymmetric QFTs—for sigma models on Sp(2) and SU(3) with H-flux, coupled to non-supersymmetric heterotic theories; it reduces the invariant to a Dirac index on base manifolds and obtains Z₃ and Z₂ values that match known Green–Schwarz couplings and confirm anomaly inflow from worldsheet data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To directly evaluate the recently introduced tertiary invariant—motivated by topological modular forms—for concrete non-supersymmetric heterotic backgrounds, and to test its consistency with established anomaly inflow predictions.

Method: Computes the tertiary invariant from its definition using worldsheet sigma models on Sp(2) and SU(3) with unit H-flux, paired with internal theories of the so(16)⊕so(16) heterotic string and the heterotic 6-brane near-horizon; exploits S³-bundle geometry, fills fibers with instanton-carrying D⁴, and reduces to Dirac index on the base sphere.

Result: Obtains nonzero tertiary invariants: order-three (Z₃) for Sp(2)/so(16)⊕so(16) and order-two (Z₂) for SU(3)/heterotic 6-brane; the Z₃ result matches the previously derived discrete topological term from NS5-brane anomaly inflow.

Conclusion: The worldsheet-based computation of the tertiary invariant confirms the anomaly inflow mechanism for the so(16)⊕so(16) heterotic theory, validating both the invariant’s physical interpretation and its utility as a tool for detecting discrete topological data in non-supersymmetric string vacua.

Abstract: The tertiary invariant is a recently introduced pairing between two-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=(0,1)$ supersymmetric quantum field theories, motivated by topological modular forms. We evaluate it directly from its definition for the sigma models on $\mathrm{Sp}(2)$ and $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ with unit $H$-flux, paired with the internal theories of the non-supersymmetric $\mathfrak{so}(16)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(16)$ heterotic string and of the near-horizon region of the non-supersymmetric heterotic 6-brane, respectively. Regarding the target spaces as $S^3$ bundles over spheres, we fill in every fiber with a four-disk carrying a gauge instanton and reduce the invariant to the index of a Dirac operator on the base. We find nonzero values of order three and two, which determine the Green-Schwarz couplings of the two theories on $\mathrm{Sp}(2)$ and $\mathrm{SU}(3)$, respectively. The former, a $\mathbb{Z}_{3}$-valued discrete topological term of the $\mathfrak{so}(16)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(16)$ theory, was previously studied through anomaly inflow to NS5-branes. Our computation, which uses only the worldsheet theories, agrees exactly with that result and thus confirms the anomaly inflow.

</details>


### [126] [Proof of the Rényi QNEC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11760)
*Thomas Faulkner*

Main category: hep-th
