TL;DR: This paper provides a general proof of the Rényi quantum null energy condition (QNEC) using Araki-Masuda weighted $L_p$ norms for all $p > 2$, extending prior algebraic proofs limited to $p = 2$; it introduces a weighted conditional $L_p$ norm tied to quantum expansion/anti-quantity and studies associated weighted conditional entropies, recovering a conditional dynamical entropy at $p = 2$ relevant to black hole physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the quantum null energy condition (QNEC) beyond the $p=2$ case—where an algebraic proof already exists—to all $p > 2$ using Araki-Masuda $L_p$ norms, thereby deepening the connection between quantum information-theoretic quantities and gravitational energy conditions.

Method: Employs weighted Araki-Masuda $L_p$ norms to define a weighted conditional $L_p$ norm that extrapolates quantum expansion/anti-quantity; analyzes properties of the resulting weighted conditional entropy for half-sided modular inclusions and general von Neumann subalgebras with a common cyclic and separating vector.

Result: A rigorous, general proof of the Rényi QNEC for all $p > 2$; characterization of weighted conditional entropy including its behavior under modular inclusions; recovery of a conditional dynamical entropy at $p = 2$, originally introduced in black hole thermodynamics.

Conclusion: The Rényi QNEC holds universally for all $p > 2$ within the Araki-Masuda $L_p$ framework, unifying and extending quantum energy conditions with modular theory and quantum information geometry; the $p=2$ limit recovers physically significant dynamical entropy from black hole physics.

Abstract: We give a general proof of the Rényi QNEC formulated using the Araki-Masuda weighted $L_p$ norms for all $p > 2$. This generalizes the algebraic proof of the QNEC applicable at $p=2$. Our approach utilizes a weighted version of a conditional $L_p$ norm which extrapolates the quantum expansion/ant-quantity. We elucidate some properties of the corresponding weighted conditional entropy both for half-sided modular inclusions and for general von Neumann subalgebras with a common cyclic and separating vector. The $p=2$ limit computes a conditional dynamical entropy originally defined in the context of black hole dynamics.

</details>


### [127] [Discretising 2D Fermionic CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11807)
*Dazhuo Cheng,Hexuan Li,Gong Cheng,Ling-Yan Hung*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends exact fixed-point tensor constructions to fermionic minimal models, analytically building fixed-point tensors for fermionic rational CFTs using boundary-changing operator correlators on triangles, with proper handling of fermionic parity and spin structure; it constructs boundary and strip tensors to extract scaling dimensions, recovers NS/R sectors via modified weights and spin structure encoding, validates accuracy on the c=1/2 Majorana fermion, generalizes via fermionization of bosonic CFTs and modular tensor categories, links discretized spin structures to R/NS signs, interprets OPE coefficients as spin-TQFT state-sums, and provides analytic initial tensors for fermionic tensor network simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an analytic, exact fixed-point tensor formalism for fermionic rational conformal field theories—extending prior bosonic constructions—to enable rigorous, efficient tensor network simulations of fermionic critical phenomena while correctly encoding fermionic statistics, parity, and spin structure (Neveu-Schwarz and Ramond sectors).

Method: Extends Cheng et al.'s exact fixed-point tensor construction by defining fermionic fixed-point tensors as correlation functions of boundary-changing operators on a triangle, incorporating fermionic parity grading and explicit spin structure dependence; constructs boundary tensors and composes them with triangle tensors into strips to compute scaling dimensions; introduces modified weights for shrinkable boundary conditions and systematic spin-structure labeling; leverages fermionization of rational bosonic CFTs and their modular tensor category data for generic discretisation; derives spin-TQFT interpretation of OPE coefficients via state-sum formulation.

Result: Analytic construction of fermionic fixed-point tensors recovering both NS and R sectors; numerical validation showing rapid convergence to exact bulk scaling dimensions (e.g., for c=1/2 Majorana fermion) even at minimal descendant truncation; demonstration that discretized spin structures on triangulated spin surfaces reproduce correct fermionic signs distinguishing R/NS; formulation of a generic fermionization-based discretisation scheme rooted in modular tensor categories; identification of OPE coefficients with spin-TQFT state-sum amplitudes.

Conclusion: The work establishes a rigorous, analytic framework for fixed-point fermionic tensor networks grounded in boundary CFT data and spin structure geometry, unifying fermionic CFT, modular tensor categories, and spin-TQFTs; it yields practical, high-fidelity starting tensors for tensor network studies of fermionic criticality and provides conceptual insight into the topological origin of fermionic signs in lattice realizations.

Abstract: We extend the exact fixed-point tensor construction of Cheng et al. to fermionic minimal models, providing an analytic construction of fixed-point tensors for fermionic rational conformal field theories. The fermionic fixed-point tensor is defined as a correlation function of boundary-changing operators on a triangle, with careful treatment of fermionic parity and spin structure. Boundary tensors are also constructed and combined with triangle tensors to form strips, enabling the extraction of scaling dimensions for boundary-changing operators. The construction incorporates modified weights for shrinkable boundary conditions, systematic depiction of spin structures, recovering both the Neveu-Schwarz and Ramond sectors. As a benchmark, our tensor with a minimal truncation of descendant levels yields convergence to the exact bulk scaling dimensions of many low energy states for the free Majorana fermion ($c=1/2$), demonstrating the efficiency of approximate fixed point tensors from boundary changing operators. We also make use of a more general description of fermionic CFTs based on fermionisation of rational bosonic CFTs, and make a generic prescription for discretisation making use of their connections to modular tensor categories. We show that the discretised spin structures on a spin surface naturally recover the signs needed to recover the R/NS sectors. We propose that the collection of OPE coefficients of the discretised 2d CFT can be naturally interpreted as a state-sum of spin-TQFTs, analogous to the bosonic case. This work provides analytically computed starting points for tensor network simulations of fermionic critical phenomena.

</details>


### [128] [Critical Slowing Down and the Crossover to Quantum Scrambling in Holographic CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11889)
*Mozib Bin Awal,Prabwal Phukon*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies critical relaxation dynamics and quantum information scrambling in the CFT dual to a RN-AdS black hole using holography; it finds universal critical slowing down with exponent Δ ≈ 2/3, extends scaling analysis to include central charge, and identifies a crossover scale between thermodynamic relaxation and fast scrambling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how critical thermodynamic relaxation in boundary CFTs—holographically dual to near-extremal black holes—relates to quantum information scrambling, especially beyond single-parameter (e.g., temperature) scaling and incorporating finite-central-charge effects.

Method: Holographic mapping of RN-AdS black hole thermodynamics to boundary CFT; stochastic modeling of horizon radius via Langevin and Fokker–Planck equations; analysis of autocorrelation time, eigenvalue suppression, and scaling behavior; extension to two-parameter (temperature, central charge) scaling; analytical and numerical comparison of relaxation and scrambling timescales using free-energy landscape.

Result: 1) Critical slowing down with τ ∼ |ε|^(−2/3); 2) Universal scaling under combined variable involving T and central charge C; 3) Analytical crossover scale ε̂ ∼ (C ln C)^(−3/2) between relaxation and scrambling; 4) Numerical confirmation using full nonlinear free energy.

Conclusion: Critical dynamical scaling in holographic CFTs is robust under central-charge variation, and thermodynamic relaxation and quantum scrambling exhibit a well-defined, analytically tractable crossover governed by large-C corrections—unifying critical phenomena, finite-N effects, and quantum chaos in AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We investigate critical relaxation dynamics and their connection to quantum information scrambling in the conformal field theory dual to a Reissner-Nordström anti-de Sitter (RN-AdS) black hole. Employing the holographic dictionary, we formulate the thermodynamics in terms of boundary CFT variables and study the stochastic evolution of the reduced horizon radius using Langevin and Fokker-Planck formalisms. We demonstrate pronounced critical slowing down in the dual CFT, characterized by an enhancement of the autocorrelation time and stochastic fluctuations, accompanied by a suppression of the lowest nonzero Fokker-Planck eigenvalue. The autocorrelation time exhibits a universal power-law divergence, $\tau\sim|\epsilon|^{-\Delta}$, with $\Delta\simeq 2/3$, consistent with the analytical prediction and previous bulk gravitational results. Going beyond the conventional single-parameter analysis, we incorporate variations of the CFT central charge and identify a combined scaling variable involving temperature and central charge, demonstrating that the leading relaxation dynamics retain the same critical exponent along different trajectories in the two-dimensional parameter space. Finally, we explore the interplay between thermodynamic relaxation and quantum information scrambling by comparing the relaxation timescale with the fast-scrambling timescale. The comparison reveals a crossover between thermodynamic relaxation and quantum scrambling for which we derive the corresponding crossover scale analytically and find that, in the large-central-charge limit, it behaves as $\hat{\epsilon}\sim(C\ln C)^{-3/2}$. We also verify this numerically using the full nonlinear free-energy landscape. Our results connect critical dynamical scaling, finite-central-charge effects, and quantum scrambling within the holographic CFT framework.

</details>


### [129] [Proof of the QNEC, without assumptions and under a weight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11920)
*Thomas Faulkner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper completes a rigorous proof of the Quantum Null Energy Condition (QNEC) within the Half-Sided Translations/Modular Inclusions framework, removing a prior restriction requiring finite-null-energy purifications; it establishes convexity of relative entropy along horizon cuts for Hartle-Hawking states and extends QNEC to a weight-based Hilbert space relevant for evaporating black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To remove the restrictive assumption—present in prior QNEC proofs—that a purification with finite null energy must exist, thereby enabling a fully general, mathematically rigorous derivation of QNEC in algebraic quantum field theory settings involving black hole horizons.

Method: Uses the mathematical framework of Half-Sided Translations and Modular Inclusions (HST/HSMI) to prove a theorem on convexity of relative entropy; leverages modular theory and operator-algebraic techniques to extend the result to weights replacing the Hartle-Hawking state.

Result: A complete, assumption-free proof of QNEC in the HST/HSMI setting; demonstration that relative entropy between the exterior field state and the Hartle-Hawking state is convex in the horizon cut parameter; derivation of a generalized QNEC using a weight-based Hilbert space applicable to evaporating black holes.

Conclusion: The QNEC holds generally in this rigorous AQFT framework without auxiliary purification constraints; the convexity property provides a fundamental entropic characterization of horizon dynamics, and the weight-based extension opens a pathway to address backreaction and evaporation in flat-space black holes.

Abstract: We complete a proof of the quantum null energy condition (QNEC) in the mathematical framework of Half-Sided Translations and Modular Inclusions (HST/HSMI) removing an assumption that applied to previous proofs. Here we establish a theorem, which when translated to a physical setting implies that the relative entropy comparing the state of fields outside a stationary black hole with a Hartle-Hawking (HH) state is always a convex function of the horizon cut. Previous proofs only applied when a purification with finite null energy was available. We also obtain a QNEC generalization in which the HH Hilbert space is replaced with one based on a weight which is plausibly of relevance to evaporating black holes in flat space.

</details>


### [130] [Two-Loop $n$-Point Identical Helicity Amplitude for Gravity Minimally Coupled to a Chiral Fermion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12096)
*Zvi Bern,Calvin,Chen,Enrico Herrmann,Anthony Morales,Radu Roiban,Michael S. Ruf*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the two-loop identical-helicity four-point amplitude in gravity coupled to a chiral fermion—where the self-dual anomaly vanishes—and extends it to n points using a chiral bootstrap in gauge theory and KLT relations, yielding rational amplitudes proportional to tree-level gravity with an R³ insertion; results are verified up to 13 points for crossing symmetry and soft limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent progress in two-loop identical-helicity gauge-theory amplitudes where the Bardeen–Cangemi (or Costello–Paquette) self-dual integrability anomaly vanishes motivates extending this analysis to gravity coupled to a chiral fermion, which similarly evades the anomaly.

Method: Computes the infrared-subtracted two-loop four-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity + chiral fermion; identifies its rational structure and proportionality to pure-gravity tree amplitude with an R³ operator insertion; bootstraps n-point gauge-theory tree amplitudes with single F³ insertion via chiral methods; maps to gravity via Kawai–Lewellen–Tye (KLT) relations to obtain n-point two-loop gravity+fermion amplitudes; validates crossing symmetry and soft limits up to n=13.

Result: The two-loop n-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity minimally coupled to a chiral fermion is purely rational and exactly equal to the tree-level n-point gravity amplitude with a single R³ insertion—derived in closed form via chiral bootstrap + KLT; explicitly confirmed for crossing symmetry and soft behavior through 13 points.

Conclusion: The absence of the self-dual anomaly enables a clean, rational, and fully determined two-loop identical-helicity sector in gravity+fermion, tightly linked to R³-deformed tree amplitudes; this establishes a new exact correspondence between loop-level quantum gravity amplitudes and higher-curvature tree-level deformations.

Abstract: Motivated by recent developments in the two-loop identical-helicity sector of gauge theory when the one-loop self-dual integrability anomaly of Bardeen and Cangemi, or the equivalent twistor-space version of Costello and Paquette, vanishes, we compute the two-loop four-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity coupled to a single chiral fermion, for which the corresponding self-dual anomaly also vanishes. We show that the infrared-subtracted amplitude is purely rational and proportional to the identical-helicity tree amplitude of pure gravity with a single insertion of the operator $R^3$. Using this connection, we obtain a closed-form expression for the two-loop $n$-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity minimally coupled to a fermion. We do so by first obtaining the corresponding identical-helicity gauge-theory tree amplitude with a single $F^3$ insertion using a chiral bootstrap. We then convert these $n$-point gauge theory amplitudes using Kawai-Lewellen-Tye to obtain gravity amplitudes with an $R^3$ insertion, which are necessarily equal to the desired two-loop identical helicity amplitudes of gravity coupled to a chiral fermion. As a nontrivial check, we explicitly verify crossing symmetry and the expected soft limits of our expressions through 13 points.

</details>


### [131] [Rotating the Replica: Twisted Rényi Coefficients from CFT Operator Counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12133)
*Jyotirmoy Mukherjee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Twisting Rényi entropies by spatial rotation encodes CFT data on spinning operator counting; angular-momentum-twisted Rényi entropies for a 4D ball are fully determined by rotating-sphere thermal partition functions (including Weyl anomaly), verified in free and holographic $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extract conformal field theory data—specifically, asymptotic spectra of spinning operators—from entanglement measures via symmetry twisting, bridging entanglement entropy, thermal physics, and conformal anomalies.

Method: Angular-momentum twisting of vacuum Rényi entropies for a spherical region in 4D; systematic local derivative expansion of the rotating-sphere thermal partition function; matching logarithmic coefficients of twisted Rényi entropies to thermal data; cross-verification in free CFTs and holographic $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM via AdS/CFT.

Result: Exact all-order correspondence between logarithmic terms in angular-momentum-twisted Rényi entropies and rotating-sphere thermal free energy, including full Weyl-anomaly contribution; confirmed in both free scalar/fermion theories and strongly coupled large-$N$ $\mathcal{N}=4$ Yang–Mills.

Conclusion: Twisted Rényi entropies serve as a precise probe of spinning operator spectra and encode universal thermal and anomaly data, unifying entanglement, geometry, and representation theory in CFTs.

Abstract: We show that twisting vacuum Rényi entropies by a spatial rotation reveals conformal field theory data governing the asymptotic counting of spinning operators. For a ball in four dimensions, the logarithmic coefficients of these angular-momentum-twisted Rényi entropies are determined by rotating-sphere thermal data to all orders in the local derivative expansion, including the Weyl-anomaly contribution. We test this relation in free theories and in strongly coupled, large-$N$ $\mathcal N=4$ Yang--Mills theory using holography.

</details>


### [132] [Einstein--Yang--Mills Plane Waves: Uniqueness and an Exact Curvature Double Copy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12204)
*Metin Gurses,Tahsin Cagri Sisman,Bayram Tekin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes Coleman-Güven plane waves in Yang-Mills–gravity systems to arbitrary dimensions D ≥ 4, identifying uniqueness of Coleman’s linear transverse profile via bounded energy density and Riemann–field-strength algebraic identity; constructs exact AdS solutions; shows spherical transverse geometry leads to singularities; analyzes regularity in quadratic/Gauss–Bonnet gravity; and establishes universality across generic higher-curvature and gauge theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend the known exact non-abelian plane wave solutions of Yang–Mills theory coupled to gravity beyond four dimensions, clarify the geometric and gauge-theoretic constraints that select Coleman's linear profile, and assess their robustness under modifications of gravity (e.g., AdS background, quadratic curvature terms) and transverse geometry.

Method: Analytical construction and classification of plane-wave ansätze in D-dimensional spacetimes with various transverse geometries (flat, spherical, hyperbolic); explicit computation of energy density, Riemann tensor, and Yang–Mills field strength; use of gauge invariance and algebraic identities (e.g., R_{μνρσ} = F^a_{μν} F^a_{ρσ}); examination of regularity and boundedness conditions; extension to quadratic and Gauss–Bonnet gravity via modified field equations.

Result: Coleman’s linear transverse profile is uniquely selected (up to gauge) by two independent criteria: bounded energy density and the identity R_{μνρσ} = F^a_{μν} F^a_{ρσ}; exact closed-form AdS solutions exist for all D ≥ 4 but violate the Riemann–color-square identity; spherical transverse space always yields singularities; hyperbolic transverse space admits regular waves only if light defocusing occurs or higher-curvature terms dominate Einstein gravity; all solutions admit consistent deformations to generic gravity and gauge theories.

Conclusion: Coleman’s linear profile is fundamentally privileged in D-dimensional Yang–Mills–gravity plane waves—not merely as a solution but as the unique one satisfying physically and geometrically natural boundedness and algebraic consistency conditions; its robustness across diverse gravitational frameworks underscores its structural significance in exact non-perturbative solutions of non-abelian gauge-gravity systems.

Abstract: Coleman found plane waves of Yang--Mills theory that are genuinely non-abelian in four dimensions and yet obey linear field equations, and Güven showed that they remain exact when gravity is included. We study these waves in $D$ dimensions. Coleman's profile, which is linear in the transverse coordinates, is singled out twice: up to gauge, it is the only profile with bounded energy density, and the only one for which the full Riemann tensor equals the color-contracted square of the Yang--Mills field strength that sources it. In anti-de Sitter space, we construct a closed-form family for every $D\geq4$; there, the full Riemann tensor cannot equal this color square. Waves whose transverse space is a sphere are always singular. In quadratic and Gauss--Bonnet gravity, a hyperbolic transverse space avoids this only if the waves defocus light or the higher-curvature terms become comparable to the Einstein term. All solutions extend, with changed coefficients, to generic gravity and gauge theories.

</details>


### [133] [Zeta function in Coulomb background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12294)
*M.Bordag,I.G. Pirozhenko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the renormalized one-loop vacuum energy of a massive scalar field inside a spherical cavity with Dirichlet boundary conditions and a Coulomb potential α/r, using zeta-function regularization and heat-kernel subtraction; it determines new Coulomb-dependent heat-kernel coefficients, establishes finiteness and physical normalization of the result, and identifies its mass dependence—including vanishing for large m and divergence for m→0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define a physically meaningful, finite renormalized vacuum energy for a massive scalar field in a Coulomb background within a bounded region—a setting where standard infinite-volume QFT methods fail and Coulomb-specific divergences require novel treatment of heat-kernel coefficients.

Method: Zeta-function regularization: spectrum derived from zeros of Kummer function; zeta function analytically continued to s = −1/2; renormalization via subtraction of divergent part from heat kernel expansion; Coulomb contributions to heat-kernel coefficients extracted by matching analytic continuation; uniform asymptotics partially retained; digamma integral representation used for continuation.

Result: Finite, physically normalized renormalized vacuum energy; explicit α- and α²-dependent heat-kernel coefficients determined; infinite-volume limit yields E₀^{ren,∞}(α,m) = α e(α/m), with e dimensionless; energy vanishes as m→∞ (satisfying normalization) and diverges as m→0 (indicating breakdown for massless case).

Conclusion: A consistent, finite one-loop vacuum energy can be defined for a massive scalar in a Coulomb background with spatial confinement; the calculation reveals essential role of Coulomb-modified heat-kernel structure and confirms necessity of a different vacuum state for the massless limit.

Abstract: We calculate the one-loop vacuum energy of a massive scalar field confined to the interior of a sphere with Dirichlet boundary conditions and subject to a Coulomb background potential $\alpha/r$. The spectrum is determined by the zeros of a confluent hypergeometric (Kummer) function, and the corresponding zeta function is constructed and analytically continued to $s=-1/2$. The renormalization is performed by subtracting the divergent part of the zeta function, defined through the heat kernel expansion, which removes the non-decreasing powers of the mass and leaves a finite, physically normalized vacuum energy. While the heat kernel coefficients for the pure Bessel (Coulomb-free) case are well known, the Coulomb contributions to the coefficients are not; we determine them by matching the analytic continuation of the zeta function to the general form of the divergent part, obtaining terms proportional to $\alpha$ and $\alpha^2$. A key technical point is that the uniform asymptotic expansion of the mode generating function cannot be used in full; a part must be retained unexpanded, and an integral representation of the digamma function is used to carry out the analytic continuation. In the infinite-volume limit the renormalized vacuum energy tends to a constant $E_0^{ren,\infty}(\alpha,m)=\alpha e(\alpha/m)$, with a dimensionless function $e$ that depends only on the ratio $\al/m$. This limit shows that a finite, physically meaningful renormalized vacuum energy can be defined for a massive scalar field in the background of a Coulomb source. For $m\to\infty$ it vanishes in accordance with the normalization condition, while for $m\to0$ it diverges, so that the present calculation does not apply to a massless field, pointing to the need of another vacuum state in that case.

</details>


### [134] [On a soliton with a non-normalisable zero mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12387)
*Guido Franchetti,Stefano Bolognesi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the holomorphic baby Skyrme model as a tractable soliton system exhibiting a non-normalisable zero mode—specifically, a massless phase modulus arising from the soliton tail—treated via a radial (1+1)D effective action on the half-plane with boundary conditions set by the soliton core; analysis employs WKB and numerical methods, and the framework is proposed as a prototype for similar non-normalisable modes in higher-dimensional systems like monopoles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and analyze a simple, solvable model exhibiting a non-normalisable zero mode—a feature relevant to more complex topological solitons (e.g., certain monopoles in 3+1D) where standard moduli space approximations fail due to infrared divergences or lack of normalisability.

Method: Analyzes the holomorphic baby Skyrme model using radial reduction to a (1+1)D effective action on the half-plane; imposes boundary conditions determined by the soliton core; solves the resulting effective dynamics via WKB approximation and numerical evaluation; extends the moduli space approximation to include a boundary term at a finite cutoff scale.

Result: Demonstrates that the 1-soliton has a massive size modulus but a massless, non-normalisable phase modulus due to the asymptotic soliton tail; shows the radial effective action framework consistently captures this mode; provides explicit WKB and numerical validation of the boundary-coupled effective dynamics.

Conclusion: The holomorphic baby Skyrme model serves as a controlled prototype for non-normalisable zero modes; its treatment via a boundary-influenced (1+1)D effective action offers a viable generalisation of the moduli space approximation applicable to systems where zero modes decay too slowly for standard L^2-based quantisation.

Abstract: We consider the holomorphic baby Skyrme model, which we propose as a simple tractable example of a soliton with a non-normalisable zero mode. The $1$-soliton has the same structure as a lump of fixed size. The size modulus of the sigma model becomes massive, while the phase modulus remains massless. The latter is non-normalisable in the $(0+1)$-dimensional sense because of the soliton tail. A suitable way to treat it is to consider the radial $(1+1)$-dimensional effective action on the half-plane. The soliton core sets the boundary condition for the effective model, which we evaluate with the WKB approximation and also numerically. We discuss the analogue of the effective moduli space approximation with a $1+1$ model and a boundary term at a certain cutoff scale. This may serve as a prototype for more complex systems featuring non-normalisable zero modes such as certain kinds of monopoles in $3+1$ dimensions.

</details>


### [135] [First-order origins of second-order supersymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12418)
*Stevan Cebrian,Mikhail S. Plyushchay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs one-dimensional second-order supersymmetry via cyclic reduction of a two-fermion Witten-type model with centrally extended N=4 supersymmetry; it links quantum corrections to Schwarzian derivatives via isothermal coordinates, classifies real vs. complex factorizations by the sign of the quadratic invariant C, and explores geometric realizations—including on the hyperbolic plane and flat cylinder—with consistent inner products and first-order structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically derive and geometrically interpret second-order supersymmetric quantum mechanics (SSQM) from higher-dimensional or fermionic extensions, resolving ambiguities in quantum corrections, factorization reality conditions, and invariant classification—particularly distinguishing cases where the central charge C is non-positive versus positive.

Method: Uses cyclic (Kaluza–Klein) reduction of a 2D surface with a cyclic coordinate; employs Witten’s fermionic mechanics with two Grassmann modes and centrally extended N=4 algebra; applies isothermal coordinates to map auxiliary Hill equations to Laplace/wave equations; computes quantum corrections via Schwarzian derivatives; diagonalizes one-fermion intermediates; implements Pauli reductions and curvature-dependent analysis on the hyperbolic plane; constructs Hermitian two-component intermediates and transverse deformations on flat cylinders.

Result: Derives explicit second-order supercharges and partner Hamiltonians with correlated quantum corrections; identifies C ≤ 0 (Riemannian) and C ≥ 0 (indefinite) regimes with distinct factorization properties (real vs. complex scalar); obtains curvature-dependent invariants and extra charges for Pöschl–Teller/free-particle pairs on ℍ²; establishes local first-order chains for arbitrary C using positive-definite inner products; realizes C > 0 first-orderly on a flat cylinder with real transverse deformation.

Conclusion: Second-order SSQM arises naturally from geometric and fermionic symmetries, with the sign of the quadratic invariant C dictating analytic structure (real/complex factorizations), geometric realization (Riemannian vs. indefinite), and admissible first-order mechanisms—unifying previously disparate approaches under a unified cyclic-reduction framework with precise quantum-corrected geometry.

Abstract: One-dimensional second-order supersymmetry is constructed from conserved first-order operators on a surface with a cyclic coordinate. In Witten's mechanics with two fermionic modes, a centrally extended $N=4$ algebra permits a conserved product connecting the zero- and two-fermion sectors. Cyclic reduction gives the second-order supercharge and the correlated quantum corrections in the charge and partner Hamiltonians. Isothermal coordinates identify the auxiliary Hill equation with the separated Laplace or wave equation for the gauge-transformed charge kernel, and express the correction as a Schwarzian derivative. The central momentum fixes the quadratic invariant $C$: the Riemannian construction gives $C\leq0$, and its indefinite counterpart gives $C\geq0$, with a separately chosen positive adjoint after reduction. Diagonalizing the one-fermion intermediate recovers real scalar factorizations for $C\leq0$ and complex ones for $C>0$. Pauli reductions provide a second first-order mechanism; on the hyperbolic plane they reveal a curvature-dependent invariant and additional charges of a regular free-particle--Pöschl--Teller pair in a selected momentum sector. In one dimension, a formally Hermitian two-component intermediate with a positive inner product yields local first-order chains for arbitrary $C$. For a constant superpotential, a real transverse deformation on a flat cylinder also gives a first-order surface realization of $C>0$ with a positive inner product.

</details>


### [136] [Cubic Curvature Corrections to Taub-NUT-AdS Black Holes and Their Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12482)
*Yu-Qi Chen,Hai-Shan Liu,Hong Lu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how cubic curvature corrections affect the thermodynamics of 4D Taub-NUT-AdS black holes, deriving first-order analytic solutions and confirming thermodynamic consistency via two Reall-Santos methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the robustness of Taub-NUT-AdS black hole thermodynamics under higher-derivative (cubic curvature) corrections in four dimensions.

Method: Constructs the first-order analytic solution in higher-derivative couplings; computes corrected Hawking temperature and Wald entropy; determines NUT charge and Gibbs free energy using Maxwell relations and two independent Reall-Santos methods—field redefinition and background subtraction.

Result: Both Reall-Santos methods yield identical Gibbs free energies, fully fixing the previously undetermined function of the NUT parameter and consistently determining mass and NUT charge.

Conclusion: The thermodynamic structure of Taub-NUT-AdS black holes remains consistent and well-defined under cubic curvature corrections.

Abstract: We investigate cubic curvature corrections to four-dimensional Taub-NUT-AdS black holes and their thermodynamics. We construct the complete analytic solution to the first order in the higher-derivative couplings, and derive the corrected Hawking temperature and Wald entropy. The NUT potential remains unchanged, while reconstructing the NUT charge and Gibbs free energy through the Maxwell relation leaves just one undetermined function of the NUT parameter. We then independently evaluate the Gibbs free energy using two improved Reall-Santos methods, based on either the field redefinition or the background subtraction. After matching the thermodynamic variables, both methods yield identical results and fix the undetermined function, providing a consistent determination of the mass and NUT charge. Our work therefore establishes that the thermodynamics of Taub-NUT-AdS black hole withstands the higher-derivative corrections.

</details>


### [137] [Deep Inelastic Scattering and Proton Structure Functions in AdS/QCD models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12693)
*Rafael A. Costa-Silva,Henrique Boschi-Filho*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates holographic fermionic deep inelastic scattering (DIS) in a deformed AdS model, using SUSY partner potentials to ensure isospectrality; it shows that quadratic approximation reproduces soft-wall model results for structure functions F₁,₂(q²,x), and higher-order approximations improve agreement with experimental data at Bjorken-x = 0.65–0.85.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study fermionic DIS in a deformed AdS background while preserving spectral equivalence between chiral modes via supersymmetric quantum mechanics (SUSY QM), bridging bottom-up holography with QCD phenomenology.

Method: Imposes SUSY partner condition on Schrödinger-like potentials for left/right chiral fermionic modes; applies systematic truncation of the deformed AdS metric; computes DIS structure functions F₁,₂(q²,x) in quadratic and higher-order approximations; compares against full numerical solution and experimental data.

Result: Quadratic approximation yields identical F₁,₂(q²,x) as the soft-wall model; higher-order approximations progressively improve agreement with experimental data—especially at large Bjorken-x (x = 0.65, 0.75, 0.85).

Conclusion: The SUSY-motivated truncation scheme provides a controllable holographic framework for fermionic DIS, where increasing approximation order enhances fidelity to QCD data, validating the deformed AdS approach as a flexible extension of the soft-wall model.

Abstract: In this work we study the holographical fermionic deep inelastic scattering in a deformed AdS model. Imposing that the potentials that emerge from the Schrödinger-like equations for the fermionic chiral left and right modes are SUSY partners to preserve isospectrality, we implement a systematical truncation of such potentials. Then, we show that in the quadratic approximation of the deformed AdS model, the fermionic DIS and the proton structure functions $F_{1,2}(q^2,x)$ are identical with the results obtained from the soft wall model. Further, we obtain and compare high-order approximations for the fermionic potentials with the full solution of the deformed AdS model and show, as expected, that as greater the order of approximation, the better are the results for $F_{1,2}(q^2,x)$ when compared with experimental data explicitly for the Bjorken parameter values $x=0.85, 0.75$ and $0.65$.

</details>


### [138] [On Thermodynamic Role of the Gauss-Bonnet Topological Term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12698)
*Marek Liška,David Kubiznak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the thermodynamic role of the Gauss-Bonnet (GB) topological term in 4D gravity, showing that its purported entropy contribution depends on horizon boundary action choice; it identifies a boundary prescription where Wald entropy matches Jacobson-Myers entropy even off-shell; confirms no GB-induced corrections to conserved charges from outer boundaries; and reveals that GB coupling modifies the Hawking-Page phase transition—inducing a zeroth-order transition at high T/P for positive coupling—suggesting observational signatures; results extend qualitatively to critical-dimensional Lovelock theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in prior claims about Gauss-Bonnet's thermodynamic effect in 4D—specifically, whether its topological nature implies a universal entropy shift—and to clarify how boundary terms, horizon non-stationarity, and phase structure are affected.

Method: Analytical derivation using Wald’s Noether charge formalism for entropy and conserved quantities; explicit construction and comparison of horizon boundary actions; thermodynamic analysis of black hole solutions in GB-modified gravity, including computation of free energy and phase diagrams; generalization to critical-dimensional Lovelock theories.

Result: 1. The horizon entropy contribution from the 4D Gauss-Bonnet term is not intrinsic but depends on the choice of horizon boundary action; 2. A specific horizon boundary action ensures Wald entropy equals Jacobson-Myers entropy, even for non-stationary horizons; 3. No additional contributions to ADM-like conserved charges arise from smooth outer boundaries, irrespective of boundary conditions or asymptotics; 4. The GB coupling shifts the Hawking-Page critical temperature and induces a zeroth-order phase transition for α > 0; 5. These features generalize qualitatively to other Lovelock terms in their critical dimensions.

Conclusion: The thermodynamic impact of topological Lovelock terms—though vanishing in equations of motion—is physically meaningful and controllable via boundary prescriptions; their effects on entropy and phase structure are robust and potentially observable, urging careful treatment of boundary terms in gravitational thermodynamics and effective gravity models.

Abstract: We revisit the thermodynamic implications of the Gauss-Bonnet topological term in four spacetime dimensions. It has been previously claimed in the literature that this term, although it does not modify the equations of motion, shifts the black hole entropy by a contribution proportional to the Euler characteristic of the horizon. We argue that this conclusion is sensitive to the choice of the boundary action on the horizon. We then identify a choice for which the Wald entropy expression naturally reproduces the Jacobson-Myers entropy, even for non-stationary black holes. We also show that there are no further contributions to conserved quantities from smooth outer boundaries, regardless of the choice of boundary conditions there and of the asymptotic behaviour of the spacetime. Moreover, we demonstrate that the topological Gauss-Bonnet term changes the critical temperature of the Hawking-Page phase transition, opening, in principle, a way for detecting its presence in the gravitational action. In fact, for a positive Gauss-Bonnet coupling constant, we observe a zeroth-order Hawking phase transition at high temperatures and pressures. While our discussion focuses on the case of the Gauss-Bonnet term in 4D, the results are more general and qualitatively apply to other Lovelock Lagrangians in their respective critical dimensions.

</details>


### [139] [Boundary Formula for Volume Response of Entanglement Wedge Polygons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12715)
*Chen-Te Ma*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the semiclassical quadratic response of the entanglement wedge vacuum volume in AdS₃ to time-symmetric coherent scalar matter, showing it depends on the unweighted energy integral—unlike entropy differences, which involve nonnegatively weighted energy integrals—and demonstrates distinguishability of volume responses even when all six interval entropies match up to second order; it further validates the result via CFT calculation for a dimension-3/2 primary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how bulk geometric quantities—specifically the vacuum volume of an entanglement wedge polygon—respond to matter perturbations in AdS/CFT, and to clarify the distinction between volume and entropy as probes of bulk physics, especially regarding weighting of energy contributions and topological robustness.

Method: Semiclassical gravity in AdS₃ with time-symmetric coherent scalar field perturbations; analysis of entanglement wedge volume via holographic entanglement entropy and modular Hamiltonians; comparison of quadratic responses of six-interval entropy combinations versus volume; continuum limit of narrowing quartets for reconstruction; independent boundary CFT computation for a dimension-3/2 primary operator using conformal symmetry and replica trick techniques.

Result: The vacuum volume’s quadratic response is governed by the unweighted integral of the energy density, while six-interval entropy differences measure a nonnegatively weighted version; thus, distinct perturbations can yield identical entropy responses (up to O(ε²)) but opposite volume responses; a continuum reconstruction of volume from quartet limits is achieved with controlled noise; CFT calculation for h = 3/2 reproduces the exact edge-length formula for the polygon.

Conclusion: Entanglement wedge volume is a fundamentally distinct bulk observable from entanglement entropy: it is topologically constrained and sensitive to the full (unweighted) energy distribution, making it a finer probe of bulk matter configuration—even when entropy data is insufficient to resolve it. This highlights volume’s role as a complementary, non-redundant geometric diagnostic in holography.

Abstract: The vacuum volume of an entanglement wedge polygon is topological. We determine its semiclassical quadratic response to time-symmetric coherent scalar matter in AdS$_3$. A contrast of six interval entropies measures a nonnegatively weighted energy integral, whereas volume depends on the unweighted integral. For negative mass squared above the stability bound, perturbations with the same quadratic coefficient of global energy can match all six vacuum entropies through second order yet have opposite volume responses. A continuum of narrowing quartets reconstructs the volume response with controlled noise. An independent CFT calculation for a dimension-$3/2$ primary reproduces an exact polygon edge formula.

</details>


### [140] [M-theory anomaly localization and holographic $c$-extremization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12802)
*Carolina Matté Gregory*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes characteristic class contributions to the trial anomaly polynomial for supersymmetric AdS₃×M₈ backgrounds with M₈ a projective bundle over a 6-manifold, uses c-extremization to identify the projective-invariant denominator J and derive central charge corrections linear in unshifted flux, validates results via fibre integration and localization, and connects them to known field-theory anomalies on wrapped-M5/S⁴ bundles—subject to unitarity, boundary, mixing, and anomaly-completeness conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine the central charges of 2D N=(0,2) SCFTs dual to supersymmetric AdS₃×M₈ string/M-theory backgrounds, especially when M₈ is a projective bundle P(O⊕L)→B₆, and to reconcile geometric anomaly inflow computations with field-theoretic expectations including flux quantization and mixed anomalies.

Method: Computes cubic and C₃∧X₈ terms in the trial anomaly polynomial; performs c-extremization incorporating Abelian currents from C₃; identifies the projective-invariant J as the denominator; evaluates push-forward of X₈ to extract pure R-symmetry, mixed R–Betti, and gravitational anomalies; verifies results via direct projective fibre integration and localization; tests against three flux-quantized backgrounds—including non-polynomial ones—and compares with known N³ and N-order anomalies in the wrapped-M5/S⁴ setup.

Result: Derives central charge formula c⁽⁰⁾ = −I₃ + 3I₊I₋/J; shows X₈ push-forward yields central-charge corrections linear in unshifted flux; finds mixed R–Betti anomaly contributes equally to both left/right central charges and is generically required; confirms mixed coefficient matches both fibre integration and localization; reproduces known field-theory anomalies up to N³ and N orders on S⁴-bundle backgrounds.

Conclusion: The geometric anomaly inflow framework consistently yields physically meaningful central charges for AdS₃/CFT₂ dualities involving projective eight-manifolds, provided the dual SCFT satisfies unitarity, appropriate boundary conditions, full global current mixing, and completeness of anomaly sectors; a Euclidean relative-equivariant interpretation remains conditional and is treated separately.

Abstract: We compute the cubic and $C_3\wedge X_8$ caracteristic class contributions to the trial anomaly polynomial of supersymmetric $AdS_3\times M_8$ backgrounds with $M_8=P(\mathcal O \oplus \mathcal L)\to B_6$. Including the Abelian currents descending from $C_3$, we identify the projective invariant $J$ as the denominator arising from their elimination by $c$-extremization and recover the central charge $c^{(0)}=-I_3+3I_+I_-/J$. The $X_8$ push-forward determines the pure R-symmetry, mixed R--Betti and gravitational anomalies. Evaluating it at the classical extremum gives the associated central-charge corrections, linear in the overall unshifted flux. The mixed term contributes equally to both central charges and is required in general. We evaluate these formulae for three flux-quantized backgrounds, including non-polynomial solutions. Direct projective fibre integration reproduces the mixed coefficient obtained by localization. On the wrapped-$M5$ $S^4$ bundle, the same couplings reproduce the independently known field-theory anomaly through orders $N^3$ and $N$. Their interpretation as full CFT central charges requires a unitary SCFT with a normalizable vacuum, suitable supersymmetric boundary conditions, a complete mixing sector of global currents and control of omitted anomaly sectors. A conditional Euclidean relative-equivariant interpretation is discussed separately.

</details>


### [141] [Global Green-Schwarz response on quaternionic sphere bundles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12810)
*Yuqi Li,Hao-Yu Sun*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the global dependence of the normalized Green-Schwarz phase in 10D string theory on background data—specifically, three-sphere bundles of quaternionic lines over spin 7-manifolds—with two Spin(16) gauge bundles and an integral differential trivialization. It combines a local Green-Schwarz integral and a real eta invariant (via 12D APS filling) to yield an integral relative characteristic number; the anomaly response is one-third of that number mod ℤ. Under vanishing H³, H⁴(Q; ℚ), it equals 8×Crowley-Goette invariant; calibration on Sp(2) recovers the known 1/3-phase; distinct String structures realize all three possible phase values, revealing detectable global topological data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how the normalized Green-Schwarz phase—though locally captured by anomaly polynomials—depends globally on background topology and differential cohomology, especially for quaternionic line bundles over spin 7-manifolds where conventional local analysis is insufficient.

Method: Computes the full anomaly functional via decomposition into a local term (Green-Schwarz integral) and a global spectral term (real eta invariant), evaluated using a 12-dimensional Atiyah-Patodi-Singer filling manifold; employs integral differential trivializations of a specific cohomology class (½p₁(T) + c₂ of complexified quaternionic bundle); leverages Crowley-Goette invariants and String structure classification.

Result: The additive anomaly response is ⅓ of an integral relative characteristic number modulo ℤ; under H³=H⁴=0, it equals 8×Crowley-Goette invariant; Sp(2)-calibration yields exactly 1/3; distinct integral String structures on a fixed 10-manifold realize all three possible phase values (0, 1/3, 2/3 mod ℤ), proving sensitivity to global background data beyond local anomaly polynomials.

Conclusion: The normalized Green-Schwarz phase encodes nontrivial global topological information—detectable via integral String structures and refined differential cohomology—that cannot be reduced to local anomaly polynomials alone; this has implications for anomaly cancellation in F-theory compactifications and connections to tmf and vertex operator superalgebras, though no microscopic duality or full theory equivalence is claimed.

Abstract: Local anomaly polynomials describe local variations, but a normalized Green-Schwarz phase also depends on global background data. We explain this dependence for ten-dimensional three-sphere bundles of quaternionic lines over closed spin seven-manifolds. The backgrounds retain two specified $\mathrm{Spin}(16)$ gauge bundles and an integral differential trivialization on the base, including its boundary identifications. The trivialized class is the sum of half the first Pontryagin class of the tangent bundle and the second Chern class of the quaternionic line's underlying complex bundle. For these backgrounds, the calculation combines a generally nonzero local Green-Schwarz integral with a real eta invariant evaluated by a twelve-dimensional Atiyah-Patodi-Singer filling. Together, these terms determine an integral relative characteristic number; the additive response is one third of that number, modulo integers. When the base's third and fourth rational cohomology groups vanish, the response equals eight times the Crowley-Goette invariant, without assuming that the base is simply connected. A calibration using the actual quaternionic line bundle gives additive value one third on $\mathrm{Sp}(2)$, recovering the known third-root phase. Distinct integral String data on one fixed ten-manifold give all three phase values, showing which global background information the response can detect. The physical identifications retain their prescribed fermion data, while the separate application to integral topological modular forms and vertex operator superalgebras requires compatible integral lifts. The response calculation constructs neither a microscopic realization nor an equivalence of complete theories.

</details>


### [142] [Kasner Exponents from the Bouncing Singularity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31699)
*Hyunwoo Oh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a formula for the additional exponent $b_t$ in the singularity of thermal two-point functions of holographic CFTs, linking it to Kasner exponents near black hole singularities; it matches and extends prior numerical results and provides new predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how thermal correlators in holographic CFTs encode information about black hole singularities, particularly through the structure of geodesic bounces and reflected waves.

Method: Analyzes bouncing null geodesics and wave reflection near spacelike singularities in AdS black branes; computes the leading singularity in the large-frequency retarded correlator using Kasner parametrization of the near-singularity geometry.

Result: Derives exact formula $b_t=(d-1) \max \left( \frac{p_i-p_t}{1-p_t}, \frac{1}{2} \right)$ for planar AdS$_{d+1}$ black branes; validates against Čeplak–Valach numerics, outperforming their earlier conjecture; predicts $b_t$ for Kasner-known but uncomputed cases.

Conclusion: The exponent $b_t$ is universally determined by Kasner exponents, confirming a deep geometric origin of singularity-induced correlator singularities in holography.

Abstract: Thermal two-point functions of holographic CFTs can probe the black hole singularity. When a bouncing geodesic exists, the correlator at leading order in $1/N$ develops a singularity $G(t, \vec{x}=0) \propto (t_c-t)^{-2\Delta+b_t}$, where $\Delta$ is the scaling dimension of the operator. We show that the additional exponent $b_t$ is determined by the Kasner exponents near the singularity. From the wave reflected off the singularity, which gives an exponentially small term in the large-frequency retarded correlator, we derive the formula $b_t=(d-1) \max \left( \frac{p_i-p_t}{1-p_t}, \frac{1}{2} \right)$ for planar black branes in AdS$_{d+1}$, where $p_t$ and $p_i$ are the Kasner exponents of the time and spatial directions. We compare our formula with the numerical values of Čeplak and Valach. It reproduces the relation that they proposed for a special class of black branes. For more general black branes, where no formula had been found, our formula agrees with most of their numerical data and fits them much better than the extension of their relation. We also give predictions for $b_t$ in theories where the Kasner exponents are known, but $b_t$ has not been computed.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [143] [An Information-Theoretic Perspective on Spectral Reconstruction in Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10671)
*Nan Wang,Long-cheng Gui,Jian Liang,Jun Shi,Han-yang Xing*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces an information-theoretic framework based on KL divergence to quantify how much finite, noisy lattice correlator data constrain spectral reconstructions—accounting for both local spectral strength and global spectral correlations—and demonstrates its utility in Bayesian spectral reconstruction using mock and physical proton correlator data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spectral reconstruction from finite, noisy lattice QCD correlators is ill-posed; distinguishing data-driven features from prior-induced artifacts requires a rigorous, quantitative measure of information gain from data.

Method: Uses Kullback–Leibler divergence to measure information gain from prior to posterior distributions over spectra; incorporates both pointwise spectral strength and inter-energy correlations; applies Gaussian smearing for energy-scale-resolved assessment; implements within hierarchical Bayesian reconstruction with posterior sampling.

Result: KL-based information measures successfully identify which spectral features (e.g., peaks) are well-constrained by data versus dominated by priors; provides semiquantitative reliability metric for reconstructed structures in both synthetic (three-peak) and near-physical (proton) correlator examples.

Conclusion: Information-theoretic quantification via KL divergence offers a principled, interpretable tool to assess data support for spectral features in Bayesian reconstructions—enabling more robust, prior-aware interpretation of lattice QCD spectral functions.

Abstract: Assessing the reliability of spectral reconstruction from finite, noisy lattice correlators requires understanding the constraints supplied by the data relative to prior assumptions. We develop an information-theoretic framework using Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergences to quantify the information supplied by the data through changes from prior to posterior probability distributions. Our analysis accounts for both changes in the probability distributions of spectral strength at individual energies and constraints encoded in correlations across the spectrum. We smear these information measures over neighboring energies using Gaussian weights to obtain a smoothed assessment at a specified energy scale. We demonstrate this framework using a hierarchical Bayesian reconstruction method with posterior sampling, applied to mock correlator data generated from a three-peak spectrum and a near-physical lattice proton correlator. These calculations show that the information measures help explain the properties of reconstructed spectra. They also provide a semiquantitative criterion for assessing how strongly reconstructed spectral structures are constrained by the data and how reliable they are in light of prior effects.

</details>


### [144] [Constrained field-space estimators for background-field lattice QCD observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10773)
*Scott Moerschbacher,Frank X. Lee*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper compares fixed-order finite-difference stencils (O(B⁴)–O(B⁸)) and covariance-optimized generalized least-squares estimators for extracting weak-field response coefficients (e.g., magnetic moments) from lattice QCD correlators under background magnetic fields. Using quenched nucleon and decuplet data, it finds fixed stencils are nearly identical in precision, robust against model misspecification (e.g., exact Δ-baryon moment relations), and insensitive to temporal fit choices; in contrast, covariance-optimized fits gain only 1–2% statistical precision but introduce large bias (up to 1.8σ) and fail null-space tests due to excessive leverage on omitted higher-order response terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether covariance-optimized linear estimation improves precision and reliability over simple fixed-order finite-difference stencils when extracting weak-field response coefficients (e.g., magnetic moments) from lattice QCD correlators computed at multiple background field strengths — especially in light of potential model misspecification (truncated Taylor series) and noise in estimated temporal covariance.

Method: Treats coefficient extraction as linear estimation in field space; compares fixed O(B⁴)–O(B⁸) cancellation stencils against generalized least-squares (GLS) estimators weighted by estimated temporal covariance; applies null-space tests to evaluate model validity; validates on archival quenched nucleon double ratios (~1000 configs, six quark masses) and exact decuplet (Δ) correlators where magnetic moments obey strict SU(3) relations (e.g., μ_Δ⁺/μ_Δ⁺⁺ = 1/2, μ_Δ⁰ = 0); quantifies leverage, bias, and precision gains/losses across ensembles and fit strategies (one- vs two-state temporal fits, independent data halves).

Result: Fixed stencils achieve near-identical precision with negligible computational cost; null-space tests reject the two-term (linear + quadratic) response model in most nucleon cases; inclusion of temporal covariance noise increases null-contrast error by 1.7–2.4×; GLS yields only 1–2% precision gain (3–10% moment reduction with two-state fits) but introduces up to 1.8 marginal standard errors of bias and 10³–10⁴× more leverage on omitted modes; on exact decuplet controls, GLS violates exact moment relations by 10–25σ, while fixed stencils satisfy them within errors — confirming poor robustness of GLS under misspecification.

Conclusion: Statistical precision gains from covariance optimization are marginal and offset by significant loss of robustness to model misspecification; fixed-order stencils provide superior reliability, simplicity, and fidelity to exact physical constraints — indicating that robustness, not maximal precision under idealized assumptions, should guide estimator choice in background-field lattice calculations.

Abstract: Background-field lattice calculations extract weak-field response coefficients from correlators computed at several field strengths. We treat this extraction as linear estimation in field space and compare fixed cancellation stencils with covariance-optimized, generalized-least-squares estimators of the same coefficient, using archival nucleon double ratios on about 1000 quenched configurations at six quark masses and decuplet correlators for which exact answers follow from the lattice contractions. The fixed $O(B^4)$-$O(B^8)$ stencils are nearly coincident and cost almost nothing in precision. Weight-free null-space tests reject the two-term response model in most nucleon ensembles; whether they reject the three-term model depends on the temporal fits and on the noise of the estimated temporal covariance, which raises the null-contrast error by a factor of 1.7-2.4. With that noise included, covariance optimization gains 1-2% in precision at the five lighter masses (3-30% with two-state time fits, which also lower moments by 3-10%) and nothing on independent data halves, while its weights carry three to four orders of magnitude more leverage on omitted response modes than the fixed stencils and move the moments by up to 1.8 marginal standard errors. The decuplet controls show what such leverage does under misspecification: the first-order responses obey $\mu_{\Delta^+}/\mu_{\Delta^{++}}=1/2$ and $\mu_{\Delta^0}=0$ exactly. The lowest-order estimate misses the first by 10-25 standard errors. The fixed $O(B^4)$-$O(B^8)$ stencils satisfy it within errors in the production windows, whereas the correlated linear fit in $B^2$, with the same constraints as the $O(B^4)$ stencil, misses it by 3-17%; the null-space test rejects that fit's model. The results distinguish statistical precision from robustness to omitted response modes; they are not physical-point magnetic moments.

</details>


### [145] [Flavor decomposition of the nucleon axial and tensor charges from lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10816)
*C. Alexandrou,S. Bacchio,M. Di Carlo,A. Evangelista,C. Iona,F. Pittler,G. Spanoudes*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes nucleon axial and tensor charges using twisted-mass clover-improved fermions on four lattice spacings, confirming agreement with experiment for the isovector axial charge and providing precise values for isoscalar axial and tensor charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely determine nucleon axial and tensor charges—key quantities for testing the Standard Model and probing new physics—using controlled lattice QCD simulations with physical quark masses and multiple lattice spacings to enable robust continuum extrapolation.

Method: Lattice QCD simulations with twisted-mass clover-improved fermions on four ensembles at lattice spacings a = 0.05–0.08 fm, fixed physical volume (~5.5 fm), and near-physical u/d, s, and c quark masses; both connected and disconnected diagrams computed to extract isovector and isoscalar axial (g_A) and tensor (g_T) charges.

Result: g_A^{u−d} = 1.263(34)(26), consistent with experiment; g_A^{u+d} = 0.458(16)(1); g_T^{u−d} = 0.951(21)(5); g_T^{u+d} = 0.556(16)(5).

Conclusion: The high-precision results validate lattice methodology for nucleon matrix elements, confirm the standard model value of g_A^{u−d}, and provide benchmark values for isoscalar axial and tensor charges—crucial for dark matter direct detection and neutron electric dipole moment studies.

Abstract: We determine the nucleon axial and tensor charges using four ensembles of twisted-mass clover-improved fermions with approximately equal physical volumes of about 5.5~fm and four lattice spacings, $ a=0.05$~fm, 0.06~fm, 0.07~fm, and 0.08~fm. The mass of the degenerate up and down, strange and charm quarks are tuned to approximately their physical values. We compute both connected and disconnected contributions to determine the isovector and isoscalar charges and their flavour decomposition. For the nucleon isovector axial charge we find $g_A^{u-d}=1.263(34)(26)$, in agreement with the experimental value. For the isoscalar axial charge we find $g_A^{u+d}=0.458(16)(1)$, while for the corresponding tensor charges we find $g_T^{u-d}=0.951(21)(5)$ and $g_T^{u+d}=0.556(16)(5)$.

</details>


### [146] [Evaluating generative samplers for lattice field theory by scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11108)
*Satoshi Iso,Lingxiao Wang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a scale-resolved diagnostic—based on coarse-grained distribution overlap—to assess generative samplers in lattice field theory, showing that global effective sample size (ESS) can mislead about long-distance observable accuracy; it demonstrates empirically and theoretically that errors in short-distance modes dominate ESS, while infrared observables (e.g., susceptibility, magnetization) may remain accurate despite low global ESS, and proposes marginal Metropolis correction with exact local updates to reduce bias.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global effective sample size (ESS) is widely used but misleading for assessing accuracy of long-distance observables in lattice field theory sampling; short-distance errors inflate ESS degradation without affecting infrared physics, necessitating a scale-aware diagnostic.

Method: Introduces a scale-resolved diagnostic via coarse-grained distribution overlap across length scales; analyzes free scalar field and 2D φ⁴ theory near criticality using diffusion samplers; evaluates path-weight ESS fraction, magnetization moments, marginal Metropolis tests with exact local updates; derives bounds on relaxation length vs. proposal variance errors in free theory.

Result: In free theory, two samplers with comparable ESS exhibit 32% bias vs. zero bias in susceptibility; in 2D φ⁴, diffusion samplers show rapid decay of ESS fraction but stable low-magnetization moments; marginal Metropolis with local updates maintains O(1) acceptance and reduces—but does not eliminate—bias; uniform relative variance errors in proposals yield volume-independent relaxation bounds in free theory.

Conclusion: Global ESS is insufficient for diagnosing infrared accuracy; scale-resolved overlap provides a more faithful diagnostic; marginal corrections improve robustness but require accurate target marginals—estimating them from biased sampler weights propagates error.

Abstract: Generative samplers for lattice field theory are commonly evaluated by their effective sample size (ESS). We show that a small global ESS need not imply inaccurate long-distance observables: errors spread over many short-distance modes can dominate this diagnostic. We introduce a scale-resolved diagnostic based on the overlap between coarse-grained model and target distributions. At the lattice cutoff, this overlap determines the asymptotic ESS fraction; lower cutoffs isolate long-wavelength errors. In the exactly solvable free theory, two samplers with comparable ESS yield a $32\%$ bias and no bias in the susceptibility, respectively. In two-dimensional $\phi^4$ theory near criticality, trained diffusion samplers show the same separation of scales: their path-weight ESS fraction falls rapidly over the volumes studied, while low moments of the magnetization remain close to their target values. Varying the reference parameters substantially changes infrared observables, with little effect on the log-weight variance. Given the target magnetization distribution, a marginal Metropolis test maintains an acceptance rate of order one in our tests. Combined with exact local updates, it reduces the remaining mismatch, although residual bias remains at finite relaxation length. In the free field, uniformly bounded relative errors in the proposal mode variances give a volume-independent bound on the number of updates needed for a prescribed accuracy of the chain's stationary mode variances at fixed correlation length. The target marginal must be supplied or computed separately; estimating it from the samplers' own weights can inherit the poor global overlap.

</details>


### [147] [Renormalized symmetry probes for emergent symmetries and non-chiral lattice fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12142)
*Ting-Wai Chiu*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces renormalized, RG-invariant symmetry ratios (κ_AB) to probe symmetry restoration—especially chiral and emergent chiral-spin SU(2)_CS—in finite-temperature QCD using lattice meson correlators; it handles regularization-induced symmetry breaking via channel-wise renormalization relative to the ūd sector, ensures scheme independence for exact symmetries, absorbs cutoff dependence for emergent ones into R_AB, validates the framework across fermion discretizations, quantifies lattice artifacts, derives exact free-quark benchmarks (κ_fade = 2/3, κ_CS ≤ 1/3), and confirms hierarchical chiral symmetry restoration in N_f=2+1+1 QCD with domain-wall quarks—identifying charm-containing spin-1 mesons as optimal probes of chiral-spin symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct robust, renormalization-group-invariant probes of symmetry restoration (especially chiral and emergent chiral-spin SU(2)_CS) in high-temperature QCD, overcoming complications from chiral-symmetry-breaking regularizations (e.g., Wilson, twisted-mass, staggered fermions) and the non-exactness of emergent symmetries in the massless action.

Method: Introduces symmetry ratios κ_AB defined as ratios of renormalized thermal/screening meson correlators across channels A and B, normalized by the ūd correlator at a common reference scale; renormalization is performed channel-wise to absorb discretization-dependent Z-factors into R_AB; for exact symmetries R_AB = 1 (ensuring RG/scheme independence), while for emergent symmetries R_AB ≠ 1 encodes and cancels relative logarithmic cutoff dependence; applies to multiple fermion actions; uses tensor channel pairs (T_k, X_k) to enforce R_TkXk = 1 on isotropic lattices; quantifies lattice artifacts and derives analytical free-quark limits.

Result: κ_fade = 2/3 and κ_CS ≤ 1/3 exactly for free quarks; lattice artifacts are small for chiral-spin probes (κ_CS, κ_fade) but comparable to signal for exact-symmetry probes; in physical-point N_f=2+1+1 QCD with domain-wall fermions (a = 0.064 fm, T = 192–1539 MeV), hierarchical chiral symmetry restoration (light → strange → charm) is confirmed and renormalization-invariant; ūc and s̄c sectors exhibit widest chiral-spin windows—even at tighter thresholds—making spin-1 mesons with one charm quark optimal for probing chiral-spin symmetry.

Conclusion: The proposed renormalized symmetry ratios κ_AB provide a rigorous, scheme- and regularization-robust framework for detecting both exact and emergent symmetry restoration in lattice QCD; chiral-spin symmetry manifests most clearly in charm-containing spin-1 meson channels, enabling precise characterization of the chiral-spin regime in hot QCD.

Abstract: The degeneracy of hadron correlators in symmetry-related channels signals symmetry restoration in high-temperature QCD. RG-invariant probes of this degeneracy need care when the regularization breaks chiral symmetry, or when the symmetry is not exact in the massless action, as for the emergent chiral-spin symmetry $SU(2)_{CS}$. We renormalize the thermal and screening meson correlators of every flavor sector by the thermal correlator of the same channel in the $\bar ud$ sector at a common reference point. The symmetry ratio $\kappa_{AB}$ then depends on the renormalization only through the ratio $R_{AB}$. For exact symmetries, $R_{AB}=1$ and $\kappa_{AB}$ is RG invariant and scheme independent in mass-independent schemes. For emergent symmetries, $R_{AB}\neq1$ carries the relative logarithmic cutoff dependence of the two channels, which cancels in $\kappa_{AB}$, and the residual dependence on the reference scale is physical. The framework applies to Wilson, twisted-mass and staggered fermions, where $R_{AB}$ absorbs the channel-dependent renormalization, and the tensor pair $(T_k,X_k)$ keeps $R_{T_kX_k}=1$ on isotropic Wilson-type lattices. Lattice artifacts are a small correction to the chiral-spin probes $\kappa_{CS}$ and $\kappa_{fade}$, but as large as the signal of the exact-symmetry probes. For free quarks, we derive $\kappa_{fade}=2/3$ for every mass and separation, and $\kappa_{CS}\le1/3$. We apply the renormalized probes to $N_f=2+1+1$ QCD with optimal domain-wall quarks at the physical point, with $a=0.064$ fm and $T=192$-$1539$ MeV. The hierarchical restoration of the chiral symmetries from the light to the charm sectors is confirmed, and is invariant under renormalization. The $\bar uc$ and $\bar sc$ sectors have the widest chiral-spin windows, also at a tighter threshold. Thus spin-1 mesons with one charm quark are the most attractive channels for the chiral-spin regime.

</details>


### [148] [Optimised Distillation for Nucleon Correlation Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12474)
*J. A. Crawford,J. Finkenrath,L. Barca*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper addresses challenges in extracting the nucleon ground state energy from correlation functions in lattice QCD by developing optimized distillation profiles that enhance ground-state overlap and suppress excited-state contributions in two- and three-point functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the dual challenges of excited-state contamination at early Euclidean times and exponentially decaying signal-to-noise ratio at late times when extracting the nucleon ground-state energy from correlation functions.

Method: Modulating Laplacian eigenmode contributions to nucleon correlation functions via distillation profiles to construct optimized interpolating operators with improved ground-state overlap and excited-state suppression.

Result: Progress demonstrated in constructing optimized distillation profiles for nucleons, leading to enhanced ground-state isolation and reduced excited-state contamination in both two- and three-point correlation functions.

Conclusion: Optimized distillation profiles provide a viable and effective strategy for improving nucleon ground-state extraction in lattice QCD calculations, particularly for spectroscopy and matrix element studies requiring high precision.

Abstract: The calculation and analysis of nucleon correlation functions is essential for understanding the observed spectra and structure of these ubiquitous hadrons. Two challenges arise in their analysis: at early Euclidean times, they are dominated by excited state contributions and at later Euclidean times, the signal-to-noise ratio degrades exponentially. This leads to a common challenge in identifying a reasonable window on which to extract the ground state energy. By modulating the contribution of each Laplacian eigenmode to the correlator via distillation profiles, we build optimised operators which improve the overlap with the nucleon ground state. In these proceedings, we present our progress on constructing optimised distillation profiles for nucleons and their excited state suppression in two- and three-point correlation functions.

</details>


### [149] [Symanzik Effective Hamiltonian for Lattice Quantum Field Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12520)
*Fernanda Steffens,Carsten Urbach*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Proposes a symmetry- and dimensional-analysis-based framework to construct the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian for lattice QFTs, enabling systematic operator classification and reduction by equations of motion; demonstrated for φ⁴ and pure gauge theories, supporting future non-perturbative Hamiltonian improvement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable systematic, non-perturbative improvement of lattice quantum field theories in the Hamiltonian formulation by constructing the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian rigorously from first principles—i.e., symmetries and dimensional analysis—rather than relying on Lagrangian-based or classical approximations.

Method: Uses dimensional analysis and symmetries inherent to the Hamiltonian formulation to classify all local operators contributing at a given order in spatial lattice spacing; applies equations of motion and other identities to eliminate redundant operators; applies framework explicitly to scalar φ⁴ theory and pure Yang–Mills (gauge) theory.

Result: Recovers known classical improvement results for φ⁴ and pure gauge theories; establishes a complete, symmetry-constrained operator basis for the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian up to arbitrary finite order in lattice spacing; demonstrates feasibility of non-perturbative Hamiltonian improvement.

Conclusion: The framework provides a rigorous, general, and practical foundation for constructing improved Hamiltonians in lattice QFTs, bridging the gap between continuum symmetries and discrete lattice implementations, and paving the way for future non-perturbative improvement strategies in Hamiltonian-based simulations.

Abstract: We propose a framework to construct the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian for lattice quantum field theories directly based on dimensional analysis and symmetries in the Hamiltonian formulation. This allows to classify all operators which can contribute at a given order in the spatial lattice spacing. This list can be reduced using the equations of motion and other identities. We exemplify the framework for $\phi^4$ and pure gauge theory, obtaining results consistent with earlier publications for the case of classical improvement. It provides the means to develop a non-perturbative improvement programme in the Hamiltonian formulation of lattice quantum field theories in the future.

</details>


### [150] [Unpolarized proton PDF with physical quark masses and large momenta](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12730)
*Xiang Gao,Andrew Hanlon,Jinchen He,Xiangyu Jiang,Swagato Mukherjee,Peter Petreczky,Qi Shi,Yong Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat
