TL;DR: This paper presents a high-precision lattice QCD calculation of the proton’s isovector unpolarized parton distribution function (PDF) using the large-momentum effective theory (LaMET), with physical quark masses, Coulomb-gauge-fixed operators, and NLO+NNLL matching to the MS-bar scheme.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve a first-principles, ab initio determination of the proton's parton structure with controlled systematics—particularly reducing discretization, finite-momentum, and renormalization uncertainties—and to benchmark against phenomenological PDFs.

Method: Lattice QCD on a (2+1)-flavor ensemble (a = 0.076 fm); Coulomb-gauge-fixed quasi-PDFs extracted from nucleon matrix elements of spatially separated quark bilinears; kinematically enhanced interpolating operators and high-statistics sampling; static RI/MOM and hybrid renormalization; NLO perturbative LaMET matching + NNLL resummation to obtain light-cone PDF in MS-bar.

Result: A precise isovector u−d PDF at physical pion mass, with reduced statistical and systematic uncertainties; agreement with prior finer-lattice result (a = 0.06 fm); consistent with global-fit PDFs for 0.25 ≲ |x| ≲ 0.75; coordinate-space validation shows first-ever agreement in the imaginary part of short-distance matrix elements.

Conclusion: The calculation demonstrates robustness across lattice spacings and validates LaMET-based PDF extraction at physical conditions; marks significant progress toward precision, first-principles PDF computation from lattice QCD.

Abstract: We present a lattice quantum chromodynamics (QCD) calculation of the proton isovector ($u-d$) unpolarized parton distribution function (PDF) at physical sea and valence quark masses, using Coulomb-gauge-fixed quark bilinears within the large-momentum effective theory framework. The calculation is performed on a (2+1)-flavor ensemble with a lattice spacing of $a=0.076$ fm and proton momenta up to $2.3$ GeV. We combine kinematically enhanced nucleon interpolating operators, the Coulomb-gauge method, and high-statistics measurements to improve the signal quality at large momenta and quark separations. We use the static regularization-independent momentum subtraction and hybrid schemes to renormalize the bare matrix elements. The light-cone PDF in the $\overline{\rm MS}$ scheme is obtained from the quasi-PDF through next-to-leading-order (NLO) perturbative matching supplemented by next-to-leading-logarithmic renormalization-group resummation. With reduced uncertainties, the resulting PDF is consistent with our previous calculation on a similar lattice ensemble with $a=0.06$~fm and a valence pion mass of 300 MeV. Notably, despite the reduced uncertainties, the agreement with the phenomenological PDF persists over the longitudinal momentum fraction range $0.25\lesssim|x|\lesssim0.75$, where the lattice calculation is expected to be reliable. This agreement is further supported by a comparison in coordinate space, where our short-distance lattice matrix elements are consistent with those obtained from the phenomenological PDF through NLO matching. In particular, we observe close agreement in the imaginary part for the first time. Together, these results represent an encouraging step toward precision benchmarking of lattice QCD calculations of PDFs.

</details>


### [151] [The structure of the pion at large $N_c$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12771)
*Isaac Zhu,Fernando Romero-López,Phiala E. Shanahan*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the pion's quark momentum fraction ⟨x⟩_q in the large-N_c limit using the Twisted Eguchi-Kawai (TEK) reduced model with twisted-mass fermions; results show ⟨x⟩_q = 0.267(105) at 2 GeV, significantly smaller than N_c=3 values, implying gluon dominance in light-cone momentum in the large-N_c limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the large-N_c behavior of the pion's partonic structure—specifically, how the light-cone momentum is distributed between quarks and gluons—and to test whether gluons dominate in this limit, as suggested by theoretical expectations.

Method: Uses the Twisted Eguchi-Kawai (TEK) reduced model to simulate pure-gauge configurations at multiple lattice spacings; computes pion correlators with maximally twisted mass fermions across various quark masses; extracts the bare quark momentum fraction ⟨x⟩_q via the Feynman-Hellmann method; applies one-loop perturbative renormalization to convert to the MS-bar scheme at 2 GeV; performs global continuum, chiral, and large-N_c extrapolations.

Result: ⟨x⟩_q^{MS-bar}(2 GeV) = 0.267(105) at physical pion mass in the large-N_c limit; uncertainty dominated by statistical and systematic (especially renormalization and extrapolation) errors.

Conclusion: The quark momentum fraction in the pion is substantially suppressed in the large-N_c limit compared to N_c = 3, supporting the picture that gluons carry most of the pion’s light-cone momentum asymptotically.

Abstract: We compute the pion quark momentum fraction in the large-$N_c$ limit using the Twisted Eguchi-Kawai (TEK) reduced model. Gauge configurations for different values of the lattice spacing are generated with the pure-gauge TEK action, while the pion correlators are computed for different quark masses with maximally twisted mass fermions. The bare matrix element corresponding to the quark momentum fraction is obtained using the Feynman-Hellmann method, and we use perturbative renormalization factors to connect to the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ scheme at $2\,\mathrm{GeV}$. A global analysis at several lattice spacings, pion masses, and values of $N_c$ yields $\langle x\rangle_q^{\overline{\mathrm{MS}}}(2\,\mathrm{GeV}) = 0.267(105)$ at the physical pion mass in the large-$N_c$ limit. This value is significantly smaller than existing $N_c=3$ determinations from phenomenology and lattice QCD, indicating that, in the large-$N_c$ limit, the pion's light-cone momentum is carried predominantly by gluons.

</details>


### [152] [A regularization-independent Information Criterion for correlated data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12877)
*Davide Laudicina*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: The paper introduces RIAIC, a regularization-independent generalization of AIC for model/data subset selection in regularized fits on correlated data, validated on lattice QCD data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the regularization-dependence of standard AIC generalizations when fitting correlated data with regularization applied to the covariance matrix.

Method: Derives RIAIC as an estimator of the model-dependent contribution to the correlated Kullback–Leibler divergence; derives its Bayesian extension; validates via regularized fits on lattice QCD data.

Result: RIAIC is shown to be, on average, regularization-independent and reproduces standard correlated AIC's subset/model selection behavior with statistically compatible fluctuations.

Conclusion: RIAIC enables robust model and data subset selection in scenarios involving covariance matrix regularization—particularly relevant for lattice QCD and other correlated-data analyses.

Abstract: We generalize the Akaike Information Criterion (AIC) to allow model and data subset selection when regularized fits are performed on correlated data. While the standard generalization to regularized fits is inherently dependent on the type of regularization applied to the data covariance matrix, our definition, the RIAIC, is, on average, regularization-independent. The regularization-independence is achieved by defining the new Information Criterion as an estimator of the model-dependent contribution to the correlated Kullback--Leibler divergence. The Bayesian extension is derived as well. We test the new criterion on lattice QCD data by performing regularized fits and show that it reproduces the selection properties of the standard correlated AIC, with fluctuations which are compatible with the expected statistical uncertainties. These results enable the application of subset and model selection techniques to cases where regularizations are applied to the covariance matrix of the fitted dataset.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [153] [On the Seven-particle Clusters of Lennard-Jones and Thomson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09925)
*Li Shen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the pentagonal bipyramid (PBP) is the unique global minimizer for both the 7-particle Lennard-Jones problem in ℝ³ and the 7-charge Thomson problem on S², using a novel graph-geometric framework with weak-interaction graphs, combinatorial reduction, geometric certification, and coercivity arguments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish exact, rigorous global optimality and uniqueness for the 7-particle Lennard-Jones and Thomson problems—two canonical models of particle aggregation and electrostatics—and uncover a shared geometric organizing principle independent of interaction type.

Method: Develops a graph-geometric framework based on weak-interaction graphs (thresholded by pair geometry); applies combinatorial graph classification, subset energy bounds, force balance analysis, exact geometric certificates, uniform restoring-energy estimates (for LJ), and whole-domain coercivity plus a global localisation theorem (for Thomson).

Result: The pentagonal bipyramid is the unique global minimizer (up to rigid symmetries and relabelling) for both the 7-particle Lennard-Jones energy in ℝ³ and the 7-charge Thomson energy on S²; the Thomson result upgrades prior numerical certificates to an exact uniqueness theorem.

Conclusion: Fivefold symmetry encoded in the PBP emerges universally for seven particles across fundamentally different physical settings—demonstrating a robust, interaction-agnostic geometric organising principle rooted in graph-geometric structure.

Abstract: In this Letter, we develop a graph-geometric framework and prove that the pentagonal bipyramid (PBP) is the unique global minimiser of both the seven-particle Lennard-Jones energy in $\mathbb{R}^3$ and the seven-charge Thomson energy on $S^2$, up to rigid symmetries and particle relabelling. For the Thomson problem, this strengthens a recent deterministic global-optimality certificate to numerical tolerance into an exact global-optimality and uniqueness theorem. A weak-interaction graph, defined by thresholding pair geometry, organises configuration space without imposing symmetry or discarding any pair interaction. For Lennard-Jones, pair-energy counting, graph classification, subset bounds, and force balance reduce all possible minimisers to 29 bounded domains; exact geometric certificates exclude 28, localise the survivor near the PBP, and a uniform restoring-energy estimate proves uniqueness. The Thomson proof follows the same progression: graph reduction, geometric localisation, and rigidity, but finalises through whole-domain coercivity and a global localisation theorem. The same fivefold order therefore emerges in two very different physical settings: an unconstrained cluster with a preferred bond length and purely repelling charges confined to a sphere, showing a common geometric organising principle for seven particles that is not tied to either interaction.

</details>


### [154] [On transportation of constrictions on phase-lock areas of overdamped Josephson junction by means of isomonodromic deformation of the associated special double confluent Heun equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09931)
*S.I. Tertychniy*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes constriction locations in phase-lock areas for an overdamped Josephson junction model using isomonodromic deformations of a double confluent Heun equation, linked to a novel representation of the Third Painlevé equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To locate constrictions (parameter values where phase-lock regions shrink to points) in the parameter space of the overdamped Josephson junction model — a problem with implications for understanding synchronization and bifurcations in nonlinear oscillators.

Method: Applies isomonodromic deformation theory to the special double confluent Heun equation associated with the Josephson junction model; introduces a new representation of the Third Painlevé equation coupled with an auxiliary nonlinear second-order ODE to efficiently compute monodromy data and constriction loci.

Result: Numerical computation of constriction locations; derivation of several qualitative conjectures about the structure and distribution of phase-lock areas based on heuristic analysis of the computed data.

Conclusion: The isomonodromic approach, augmented by the proposed Painlevé-based framework, provides an effective analytic–numerical tool for studying constrictions, supporting conjectures on the fractal-like and asymptotic behavior of phase-lock tongues.

Abstract: An example of computation of locations of constrictions on the phase-lock area in the parameter space for the equation modeling dynamics of overdamped Josephson junction is discussed. Several conjectures of qualitative character are inferred from the heuristic data thus obtained. The method applying isomonodromic deformations of the special double confluent Heun equation associated with the noted model is used. Such a deformation is controlled by the data provided by solution to a Third Painlevè equation for which a new representation in conjunction with the corresponding non-linear second order differential equation fairly efficient in the treatment of the problem in question is proposed.

</details>


### [155] [Positivity Improving Quantum Markov Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10042)
*Luigi Accardi,Abdessatar Souissi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies positivity-improving Markov operators (unital completely positive maps) on matrix algebras, establishing their self-duality, equivalence to a quantum Markov–Dobrushin–Doeblin minorization condition, and a Kraus-space characterization: such an operator is positivity improving iff its Kraus space’s Hilbert–Schmidt orthogonal complement contains no rank-one operator. It derives a sharp lower bound (2d−1) on Kraus rank for d×d matrices, proves optimality via explicit Hankel-type channels, provides full classifications for qubits and qutrits, and shows that in infinite dimensions positivity improvement no longer implies contraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend classical stochastic positivity (e.g., strictly positive transition matrices) to the noncommutative setting of quantum operations, and to characterize when a quantum channel 'regularizes' all nonzero positive inputs into invertible outputs — a key property for ergodicity, mixing, and stability in quantum Markov processes.

Method: Uses operator algebra, quantum information theory, and matrix analysis: establishes equivalences via dual maps and minorization; characterizes positivity improvement via geometric properties of the Kraus space (specifically, absence of rank-one operators in its orthogonal complement); constructs extremal examples (Hankel-type channels); employs computational complexity analysis (NP-hardness proof) and explicit classification for low dimensions (d=2,3).

Result: 1) Positivity improvement ⇔ self-duality ⇔ faithful quantum Markov–Dobrushin–Doeblin minorization; 2) Equivalent to: Kraus space’s HS-orthogonal complement contains no rank-one operator; 3) Minimal Kraus rank = 2d−1, attained by explicit Hankel channels; 4) Complete effective classifications for d=2 (qubits) and d=3 (qutrits); 5) NP-hardness of general decision problem; 6) In infinite dimensions, positivity improvement does not imply norm contraction.

Conclusion: Positivity improvement is a robust, intrinsically geometric property of quantum Markov operators, fully characterized by Kraus-space structure and minorization, with sharp dimensional bounds and computable criteria in low dimensions — but becomes decoupled from contractivity beyond finite dimensions.

Abstract: A Markov operator on a matrix algebra, that is, a unital completely positive map, is called positivity improving if it maps every nonzero positive element to an invertible one. This is the noncommutative counterpart of a stochastic matrix with strictly positive entries. We show that positivity improvement is self-dual and equivalent to a faithful quantum Markov--Dobrushin--Doeblin minorization. We also compare the scalar trace minorization with the optimal common-lower-bound construction of the quantum Markov--Dobrushin coefficient. We then characterize positivity improvement through the Kraus space of the map: a Markov operator is positivity improving if and only if the Hilbert--Schmidt orthogonal complement of its Kraus space contains no operator of rank one. As a consequence the Kraus rank of a positivity improving Markov operator on the algebra of $d\times d$ matrices is at least $2d-1$, and an explicit Hankel-type channel shows that this bound is attained in every dimension. Although deciding positivity improvement is NP-hard in general, we obtain complete and effectively checkable classifications for qubits and qutrits, together with families of examples (Pauli, Weyl and Werner--Holevo channels) showing that all our estimates are optimal. Finally we show that, in infinite dimensions, positivity improvement no longer implies any contraction of the Markov operator.

</details>


### [156] [Geometric classification of Noether invariants of the Time-Dependent damped Duffing Oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10522)
*Michael Tsamparlis,and Aniekan Magnus Ukpong*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a general geometric classification of Noether point symmetries for time-dependent conformal Lagrangians modeling damped dynamical systems, yielding explicit Noether generators, gauge functions, and invariants; it recovers the Bateman invariant for the damped harmonic oscillator and discovers a new quadratic invariant for a specific parameter regime of the damped Duffing oscillator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify Noether point symmetries for time-dependent conformal Lagrangians describing dissipative systems—particularly those with time-dependent linear damping—by exploiting the interplay between temporal conformal factors and the homothetic algebra of the kinetic metric.

Method: Geometric classification via coupling the temporal parameters of the conformal factor A(t) with the homothetic algebra of the kinetic metric g_ij using compatibility and integrability conditions; derivation of a main theorem that determines Noether generators, gauge functions, and first integrals, split by cases based on D − 2ψ and gradient/non-gradient homothetic vectors.

Result: A fully general, covariant main theorem specifying Noether symmetries and invariants; application to the damped Duffing oscillator yields: (i) recovery of the classical Bateman invariant when λ = 0, and (ii) a new exact time-dependent quadratic invariant for a = 2γ²/9 in the anharmonic case, verified by direct differentiation along trajectories.

Conclusion: The theorem provides a complete and coordinate-independent framework for identifying conservation laws in time-dependent damped systems; the newly discovered invariant for the damped Duffing oscillator constitutes a rare global conservation law for a nonlinear dissipative system, enabling integration and quantization.

Abstract: We present a systematic geometric classification of the Noether point symmetries of time-dependent conformal Lagrangians of the form $L = A(t)\left(\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}\dot{x}^{i}\dot{x}^{j} - V(x)\right)$, whose Euler--Lagrange equations describe dynamical systems with time-dependent linear damping. The classification is carried out by coupling the temporal parameters of the conformal factor $A(t)$ with the homothetic algebra of the kinetic metric $g_{ij}$ through a set of compatibility and integrability conditions. The central result is a main theorem which determines the Noether generators, the associated gauge functions and the resulting invariants. The theorem splits naturally into two cases according to whether the quantity $D - 2\psi$ is non-zero or zero, with further subcases depending on whether the homothetic vectors are gradient or non-gradient. The theorem imposes no restrictions on $A(t)$, $g_{ij}$ or $V(x)$, and is therefore fully general and covariant. We apply the theorem to the time-dependent damped Duffing oscillator (DDO) in Euclidean space $E^{3}$ with $A(t) = e^{\gamma t}$ and potential $V = \frac{1}{2}a\,r^{2} + \frac{% \lambda}{4}r^{4}$. For the damped harmonic oscillator ($\lambda = 0$) the theorem reproduces the classical Bateman invariant as a special case. For the anharmonic case we obtain a new exact time-dependent quadratic invariant, valid when the linear stiffness satisfies $a = 2\gamma^{2}/9$. Each invariant is verified explicitly by direct differentiation along the equations of motion. The new invariant provides a rare example of a global conservation law for a fully nonlinear dissipative system, and can be used for the integration and quantization of the DDO.

</details>


### [157] [The random matrix hard edge --- applications, exact PDFs and large $N$ expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10990)
*Peter J. Forrester,Anas A. Rahman,Bo-Jian Shen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews hard edge phenomena in Hermitian random matrices and presents new asymptotic results for correlation and gap probability generating functions in the Jacobi ensemble under hard edge scaling, establishing 1/N expansions with a rescaled parameter N_hJ' and identifying differential operators governing finite-N corrections at β = 1, 2, 4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and extend understanding of hard edges in random matrix theory—particularly for symmetric eigenvalue spectra (e.g., ±-pairing inducing a hard edge at zero)—and to derive precise large-N asymptotics for spectral statistics in the Jacobi ensemble beyond leading order.

Method: Analytical asymptotic analysis of exact eigenvalue PDFs and correlation kernels for the Jacobi ensemble; derivation of large-N expansions for correlation functions and conditioned gap probability generating functions under hard edge scaling; identification and generalization of differential operators encoding finite-size corrections across symmetry classes β = 1, 2, 4, and conjecture for general β > 0.

Result: For all even β, correlation functions and conditioned gap probability generating functions admit asymptotic expansions in inverse even powers of N_hJ' := N − 1/2 + (a + b + 1)/β; for β = 2 (JUE), the first two corrections are expressed via second- and fourth-order differential operators acting on the limiting law; analogous second-order operators are derived for β = 1, 4, enabling a conjectured universal form for all β > 0.

Conclusion: The hard edge scaling limit in the Jacobi ensemble admits a systematic 1/N_hJ' expansion with universal differential structure governing subleading corrections; this provides a refined analytical framework connecting microscopic spectral statistics to macroscopic eigenvalue density constraints and symmetry class (β).

Abstract: A hard edge of a Hermitian random matrix occurs when the eigenvalue density is strictly zero on one side of the edge, and nonzero on the other. Another possibility is that the eigenvalues occur in $\pm$ pairs, which creates a hard edge at the origin for the positive eigenvalues. Both review material and new results are presented for random matrix ensembles exhibiting a hard edge. The review material has two focuses: one is on applications of the random matrix hard edge, while the other is on exact eigenvalue probability density functions. The new results relate to the large $N$ expansion of correlation functions and conditioned gap probability generating functions with hard edge scaling for the Jacobi ensemble. For all even $\beta$ we are able to show that these quantities admit expansions in inverse even powers of the variable $N_{\rm hJ}':= N - 1/2 + (a + b + 1)/\beta$. For the $\beta =2$ Jacobi unitary ensemble, we can relate the leading (next leading) correction to the limit law to a particular second order (fourth order) differential operator applied to the latter. The analogous second order differential operator is found for $\beta=1,4$, leading then to its conjectured form for all $\beta>0$.

</details>


### [158] [Cylinder spectral curve and 1-cut Matrix model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11015)
*Bertrand Eynard*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a method to reconstruct a formal random matrix model—including its one-body potential and two-body interaction—from the geometric data of its spectral curve, specifically for the 1-cut case with a two-sheeted cylinder structure and two simple ramification points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a constructive correspondence between the geometry of the spectral curve (e.g., ramification points, cycles, boundary operators) and the defining data (potential V(x) and interaction R(x,x')) of a formal random matrix model.

Method: Uses boundary integral operators and generalized homology cycles on a two-sheeted Riemann surface (cylinder) with two simple ramification points; constructs the potential and 2-body interaction by inverting the spectral curve geometry via analytic continuation and loop equations techniques.

Result: A constructive algorithm is provided to recover the one-body potential V(x) and two-body interaction R(x,x') uniquely (up to equivalence) from the spectral curve’s geometric data in the 1-cut, two-branchpoint case.

Conclusion: The spectral curve geometry fully encodes the random matrix model in the formal 1-cut regime; thus, the model can be reconstructed purely from the curve’s topological and analytic structure.

Abstract: For a random matrix, with eigenvalues in a one-body potential $V(x)$ and a 2-body interaction $R(x,x')$, it is known that the resolvent converges in the large $N$ limit to an analytic function in the complex plane minus cuts, called the spectral curve. In the 1-cut case, a two-sheeted neighborhood of the cut is a cylinder with two simple ramification points. We describe these spectral curve data by boundary integral operators and generalized cycles. Vice-versa, given a 2-branchpoints spectral curve in a cylinder, there exists a formal random matrix model that corresponds to it. The goal of this article is to show the method to recover a formal random matrix model, with its potential and 2-body interaction, from the geometry of the spectral curve.

</details>


### [159] [Exactly integrable field theory in $(2+1)$-dimensional space-time: the spectral equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11182)
*Sergey Sergeev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a complete system of nested Bethe Ansatz equations for the spectrum of a relativistic evolution operator on a Kagomé lattice with q-oscillator algebra and Zamolodchikov tetrahedron R-matrix, valid for arbitrary occupation-number initial data and well-defined in the thermodynamic limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine the spectrum of the one-step relativistic evolution operator in quantum field theories defined on an auxiliary Kagomé lattice with q-oscillator observables, leveraging integrability encoded in the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation.

Method: Explicit construction of a complete system of spectral equations—interpreted as a specific form of nested Bethe Ansatz equations—based on the q-oscillator R-matrix satisfying the tetrahedron equation, applied to the evolution operator on the Kagomé lattice.

Result: A fully explicit, occupation-number–agnostic system of equations characterizing the full spectrum of the evolution operator; the system remains well-defined in the thermodynamic limit.

Conclusion: The nested Bethe Ansatz framework successfully captures the spectral structure of the q-oscillator–based relativistic evolution operator on the Kagomé lattice, confirming integrability and enabling thermodynamic analysis.

Abstract: The primary object in quantum field theories is the one-step relativistic evolution operator. In this paper we consider the evolution operator defined on the auxiliary Kagomé lattice with the $q$-oscillator algebra of observables, the local ingredient of the evolution operator is the $q$-oscillator $R$-matrix of the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation. We construct explicitly the complete system of equations (which can be seen as a specific form of nested Bethe Ansatz equations) defining the spectrum of the evolution operator. This system of equations is valid for an arbitrary initial data (occupation numbers), and the thermodynamical limit is well defined for this system.

</details>


### [160] [The Lee-Yang property of the Blume-Emery-Griffiths model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11484)
*Yuri Kozitsky*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Lieb-Sokal theorem to show that a spin-1 model with quadratic and quartic interactions and a single-site field retains the Lee-Yang property under explicit low-temperature conditions on coupling strengths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Lee-Yang property beyond standard ferromagnetic pairwise interactions, specifically to spin-1 models with $S_i^2 S_j^2$ terms and on-site anisotropy, where classical criteria (e.g., Lieb-Sokal) do not directly apply.

Method: Analytic continuation and zero-free region analysis of the partition function in the complex magnetic field plane; derivation of explicit temperature- and parameter-dependent lower bounds $C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})$ on $J_{ij}$ ensuring absence of zeros on the imaginary axis.

Result: The Lee-Yang property holds for the spin-1 Hamiltonian $-\sum (J_{ij} S_iS_j + K_{ij}S_i^2S_j^2) + \sum \Delta S_i^2$ for all real $K_{ij}$ and $\Delta > 0$, provided $J_{ij} > C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})$, where $C \to +\infty$ as $\beta \to 0$ (i.e., high temperature).

Conclusion: The Lee-Yang property persists in this extended spin-1 model but only under sufficiently strong ferromagnetic coupling and low temperatures — indicating a nontrivial interplay between interaction range, anisotropy, and thermal fluctuations.

Abstract: The Lieb-Sokal theorem provides a tool for establishing the Lee-Yang property of the partition function of a spin model if the corresponding single-spin partition function has this property and the interaction term in its Hamiltonian is of the form $-\sum J_{ij} S_iS_j$ with $J_{ij}\geq 0$. According to the result of this paper, the spin $S=1$ model with the Hamiltonian $-\sum (J_{ij} S_iS_j + K_{ij}S_i^2S_j^2) + \sum \Delta S_i^2$ has the Lee-Yang property for all $K_{ij}\in \mathds{R}$ and $\Delta >0$, whenever each $J_{ij}$ exceeds an explicitly computed bound $C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})>0$, such that $C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})\to + \infty$ as $\beta \to 0$. The latter suggests that the Lee-Yang property of this model may hold only for sufficiently low temperatures.

</details>


### [161] [SU(2) Yang-Mills: Spectral Bounds, Vacuum Concentration, and Criteria for the Continuum Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11534)
*Aleksandar Mihajlovic*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops quantitative methods to analyze the continuum limit of gauge theories, focusing on preserving nonzero renormalized states and a positive spectral gap as spatial cutoffs are removed. It establishes physical-time dynamics in infinite volume via local correlations, derives entropy and fidelity bounds tied to vacuum concentration, and provides certified benchmarks for glueball and winding-channel observables — but does not prove a nontrivial 4D spatial-cutoff construction or a uniform spectral gap in the continuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously separate and quantify the requirements for a continuum mass gap—namely, preservation of nonzero renormalized states and a positive spectral estimate—while removing spatial cutoffs and taking the infinite-volume limit in lattice gauge theory.

Method: Combines gauge reduction, reflection positivity, strong-coupling estimates, local history correlation analysis, Schur decomposition, collective projection, exact blocking, and corrected effective-potential theorems; employs finite-matrix criteria, Wilson coupling analysis (e.g., at 0.354), and representation-wise 2D benchmarks.

Result: Constructs subsequential infinite-volume physical-time dynamics for fixed spatial cutoff and positive coupling; derives extensive entropy bounds, fidelity obstructions for electric cutoffs, sector-specific discarded-link bounds, and identifies minimal additional input needed for volume-uniform spectral gap; certifies a three-link conditioning functional and validates published glueball/winding-channel measurements with explicit statistical scope.

Conclusion: Rigorous continuum transfer criteria and certified benchmarks are established, but a nontrivial four-dimensional spatial-cutoff construction and a proven positive spectral bound in the full continuum limit remain unestablished; no exact continuum mass is claimed.

Abstract: A continuum mass-gap argument must preserve nonzero renormalized states and a positive spectral estimate while the spatial cutoff is removed and physical volume grows. We develop quantitative results that separate these requirements. Gauge reduction, reflection positivity and attributed strong-coupling estimates give physical-transfer bounds with nontrivial local sectors. At fixed spatial cutoff and positive coupling, we construct subsequential infinite-volume physical-time dynamics from local history correlations, with nonconstant gauge-invariant observables. For finite spatial boxes, explicit bounds apply to the entire physical vacuum complement; finite-matrix criteria include all omitted representations. Vacuum concentration gives extensive entropy bounds, quantitative fidelity obstructions for hard electric cutoffs, and necessary growth of retained representation spaces. A sharper local comparison yields complete bounds on fixed discarded-link sectors, while collective projection and Schur estimates isolate the additional input needed for a volume-uniform gap. The work also certifies a finite three-link conditioning functional through Wilson coupling 0.354, with its physical interpretation conditional on the stated block-comparison hypotheses. Exact blocking, corrected effective-potential theorems and a representation-by-representation two-dimensional benchmark clarify which approximations preserve the relevant observables. Published glueball measurements retain their original attribution; their reanalysis and the reproduced winding-channel and replica studies have explicitly statistical scope. Complete derivations and reproducible calculations support a set of certified benchmarks and continuum transfer criteria. A nontrivial four-dimensional spatial-cutoff construction and a positive spectral bound along it are not established; no exact continuum mass is asserted.

</details>


### [162] [Universal Critical Relations and Nonuniversal Scaling in Inverse-Square Ising Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11546)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes connected correlations in ferromagnetic Ising chains with long-range interactions decaying as $J_n \sim B/n^2$, showing that critical excess magnetization under a field scaling inversely with spin separation governs correlation structure; it identifies logarithmic integrability conditions for finite thermal slope, derives exact inverse-logarithmic decay and square-root cusp for finite cores, and demonstrates that a $c/\log(n+e)$ correction with $c>1$ yields continuously varying logarithmic exponents and linear cusps—while also establishing a lower bound on disordered susceptibility inconsistent with standard Kosterlitz–Thouless scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how long-range interactions ($n^2 J_n \\to B > 0$) affect critical behavior, correlation structure, and universality classes in one-dimensional ferromagnetic Ising chains—particularly whether Kosterlitz–Thouless physics applies or if novel logarithmic scaling emerges.

Method: Analytical asymptotic analysis of the critical excess magnetization and connected correlation functions under a carefully scaled magnetic field; exact tail estimation for finite-core interactions; derivation of cusp singularities via expansion near criticality; comparison of disordered susceptibility bounds against Kosterlitz–Thouless predictions.

Result: Connected correlations decay as $1/\\log n$ for finite cores, exhibiting a square-root cusp; a relative correction $c/\\log(n+e)$ with $c>1$ induces continuously varying logarithmic exponents and a linear cusp; the disordered susceptibility is bounded below in a way incompatible with the standard Kosterlitz–Thouless law.

Conclusion: The system exhibits non-KT critical behavior governed by logarithmic scaling laws, with correlation structure and singularities (cusps) tunable via subleading corrections to the interaction decay—establishing a distinct universality class for marginally long-range Ising chains.

Abstract: For ferromagnetic Ising chains with $n^2J_n\to B>0$, the critical excess magnetization in a field inversely proportional to spin separation determines the connected correlation. Their slowly varying profile has finite logarithmic integral exactly when the ordered thermal slope is finite. For any finite nonnegative core, an exact tail gives inverse-logarithmic correlations and a square-root cusp. A relative correction $c/\log(n+e)$ with $c>1$ gives continuously varying logarithmic powers and a linear cusp. A lower bound on its disordered susceptibility excludes the usual Kosterlitz--Thouless law.

</details>


### [163] [Lieb-Robinson bound for one-dimensional continuum fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11655)
*Marius Lemm,Hailun Lu,Harman Preet Singh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes the first Lieb-Robinson bound (LRB) for a realistic one-dimensional continuum fermion system, enabling locality-based methods in continuum quantum many-body physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The exponential growth of Hilbert space dimension in quantum many-body systems necessitates exploiting locality; while Lieb-Robinson bounds (LRBs) exist for lattice systems, their absence for realistic continuum systems has hindered application of the locality toolbox.

Method: Rigorous mathematical analysis to derive a Lieb-Robinson bound for 1D continuum fermions, imposing constraints on initial states via controlled local particle number and energy moments to ensure thermodynamic stability.

Result: First proven Lieb-Robinson bound for a standard 1D continuum fermionic system, applicable to thermodynamically stable initial states with bounded local moments.

Conclusion: The result removes the principal technical obstacle to extending locality-based techniques—such as quasi-adiabatic continuation, area laws, and approximate symmetries—from lattice to physically relevant continuum quantum many-body systems.

Abstract: The central difficulty in quantum many-body physics is that the Hilbert space dimension grows exponentially with the total degrees of freedom. A key feature to circumvent this problem is locality, which manifests dynamically through an approximate causal structure in the form of a Lieb-Robinson bound (LRB). LRBs have been proved for quantum spin systems and certain lattice boson systems, but so far never for realistic continuum systems. We present the first LRB that applies to a standard system of one-dimensional continuum fermions. It applies to initial states with controlled moments of local particle number and energy and is thus thermodynamically stable. Our result breaks the main technical barrier that has existed for using the vast locality toolbox on continuum systems.

</details>


### [164] [Projective symmetries and differential recursions for the large-$N$ Gross--Witten--Wadia and one-dimensional QCD matrix models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11670)
*S. Voloshyn*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a deep algebraic and projective correspondence between the fixed-charge planar Gross–Witten–Wadia (GWW) model and large-N, fixed-N_f/N one-dimensional QCD, unifying their resolvent structures via quartic (GWW) and sextic (QCD) algebraic equations for the free energy derivative; it identifies shared S₄ symmetry via signed permutations, derives differential recurrences (PDEs and divided-difference reductions), links combinatorial boundaries (Narayana triangle, decorated bipartite plane trees), and clarifies scaling limits where QCD reduces to GWW while preserving projective data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover structural universality between two distinct large-N gauge theories—the fixed-charge GWW model and 1D QCD—with shared resolvent-based integrability, algebraic geometry, and combinatorial boundary data, despite differing degrees of their defining algebraic equations (quartic vs. sextic).

Method: Analytic continuation and resolvent analysis in the planar limit; derivation of algebraic equations for ∂F/∂g from one-cut conditions; use of finite-N Toeplitz determinants to select physical QCD branch; realization of S₄ via signed permutations of homogeneous parameter triples; derivation of differential recurrences (second-order PDE, column/row recursions); combinatorial interpretation via Narayana triangle and symmetric product formulas; asymptotic analysis of the many-flavor (N_f/N fixed) scaling limit.

Result: A unified projective description of GWW and QCD using S₄ action on algebraic branches; a second-order PDE governing coefficient pyramids with dual row/column recursions; identification of the Narayana triangle as the first-resummed boundary; exact symmetric product formula for a collapsed combinatorial slice counting decorated bipartite plane trees; demonstration that many-flavor scaling collapses the QCD sextic to the GWW quartic while preserving transverse projective structure and coalescing the divided-difference triple.

Conclusion: The fixed-charge GWW model and large-N 1D QCD are not merely analogous but projectively equivalent under S₄, governed by a hierarchy of algebraic (quartic/sextic), differential (second-order PDE, third-order ODE), and combinatorial (Narayana, tree-counting) structures; their apparent disparity in algebraic degree reflects embedding dimension rather than intrinsic complexity, resolved through homogeneous coordinates and scaling limits.

Abstract: We compare the fixed-charge planar Gross--Witten--Wadia (GWW) model with source-free one-dimensional QCD at large $N$ and $N_f/N$ fixed. In both models the resolvent and one-cut condition give an algebraic equation for a derivative of the free energy: a quartic for GWW and a sextic for QCD. Finite-$N$ Toeplitz determinants independently check the selected QCD branch. Signed permutations of homogeneous parameter triples realize $S_4$; the resulting action on the algebraic branches and operator labels gives a common projective description of the two models. For QCD, the sextic yields a differential recurrence linking small-charge rows two apart. The same second-order PDE gives a finite-width column recursion that reconstructs the coefficient pyramid from its GWW boundary. A distinct boundary, obtained from the first resummed coefficient, is the Narayana triangle. A formally collapsed slice counts decorated bipartite plane trees and admits an exact symmetric product formula. The many-flavor scaling reduces the sextic to the quartic and retains transverse projective data: the QCD divided-difference triple coalesces at this boundary, whereas the limiting quartic satisfies the full standard GWW third-order system. We distinguish algebraic degree, mixed differential order, and fixed-parameter ordinary differential order throughout.

</details>


### [165] [Transformations of Hilbert Module Reference Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11683)
*Lucus Brady*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a functorial characterization of Hilbert module classical and quantum reference frames, constructs a category for equivariant couplings and Hilbert module reference frames, proves the construction is functorial, and identifies a unitary groupoid as frame transformations; examples include the indistinguishable classical two-state switch and Hilbert spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, category-theoretic foundation for classical and quantum reference frames in the context of Hilbert modules—enabling systematic treatment of symmetry, equivariance, and frame transformations across physical systems.

Method: Functorial categorical methods: defining functors between categories of equivariant couplings and Hilbert module reference frames; constructing a category whose objects are Hilbert module reference frames and morphisms are equivariant couplings; deriving frame transformation groupoids via unitarity constraints and dynamical system invariance.

Result: A well-defined category of Hilbert module reference frames; proof that assigning reference frame systems to equivariant coupling data is a functor; identification of a unitary groupoid encoding admissible frame transformations for a fixed Hilbert module dynamical system; explicit examples illustrating frame transformations in classical (two-state switch) and quantum (Hilbert space) settings.

Conclusion: Hilbert module reference frames admit a natural, functorial description compatible with equivariant structure, enabling unified treatment of classical and quantum frame dependence; the resulting unitary groupoid formalizes symmetries and transformations intrinsic to such frames.

Abstract: In this paper, we provide a functorial characterization of Hilbert module classical and quantum reference frames. Using the functorial characterization, we construct a category for equivariant couplings and Hilbert module reference frames. We prove the construction of the reference frame system from the equivariant coupling data yields a functor. We also deduce a ``unitary" groupoid as frame transformations for Hilbert module reference frames of a fixed Hilbert module dynamical system. We provide some examples of these frame transformations in the case of the indistinguishable classical two state switch and in the case of Hilbert spaces.

</details>


### [166] [Certified finite-precision readout for $p$-adic kernel aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12254)
*Su Hu,Min-Soo Kim*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops finite-precision $p$-adic certificates for norm-based readouts, aggregate exponentials, and multiclass classifiers, enabling exact decisions (e.g., maximizer identification, classification) before full numerical resolution—leveraging non-archimedean valuation theory and yielding sharp precision thresholds, rational-arithmetic algorithms, and error separation guarantees.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable mathematically exact downstream decisions (e.g., classification, maximization) in $p$-adic computation without requiring full high-precision evaluation—exploiting the discrete, hierarchical nature of $p$-adic absolute values and valuations to certify correctness at coarse finite precision.

Method: Leverages non-archimedean valuation theory ($v_p$, absolute value $|\cdot|_p$) to construct rigorous finite-precision certificates: (1) resolved-set analysis for norm-maximization; (2) centered-moment expansion with explicit remainder bound for $p$-adic exponential aggregates; (3) discriminant valuation comparison for multiclass classification; (4) application to discrete $p$-adic path integrals on affine chains. All algorithms operate in exact rational arithmetic where possible.

Result: Key results include: (i) exact total-variation identity separating unresolved $p$-adic mass from real rounding error; (ii) a rational-arithmetic centered-moment certificate for exponential aggregates with minimal bound at active phase and resolution at high order; (iii) necessary and sufficient certification condition for multiclass $p$-adic classifiers with sharp threshold $v^{(2)}+1$; (iv) explicit action centers and proof of exact output-independence at zero quadratic coupling in $p$-adic path integrals.

Conclusion: Finite-precision $p$-adic valuation information suffices to guarantee exactness of norm-based decisions—bypassing costly full-precision evaluation. The framework unifies certification across optimization, aggregation, and classification, with sharp, computable precision thresholds and practical, reproducible implementations.

Abstract: In non-archimedean numerical analysis, a verified $p$-adic approximation can determine an absolute value, and hence downstream probabilistic rankings and classification decisions, with mathematical exactness long before the underlying number is fully resolved. We turn this observation into rigorous finite-precision certificates for norm-based readouts of amplitude families. For a common error radius, a nonempty resolved set already fixes every exact maximizer, ties included, at the sharp integer absolute precision $v_*+1$, and conditioning on that set yields an exact total-variation identity separating unresolved $p$-adic mass from real rounding error. For aggregates of $p$-adic exponentials, we give a centered-moment certificate with an explicit remainder bound: on rational data it runs in exact rational arithmetic without individual exponential evaluations, attains its minimal bound at an active phase, and resolves every nonzero aggregate at high order; we also obtain an aggregate perturbation bound free of summand-count factors. For multiclass $p$-adic classifiers that decode by minimizing the norm of a class discriminant, we prove a necessary and sufficient certification condition and show that the sharp common precision threshold is $v^{(2)}+1$, governed by the second-largest discriminant valuation, so the winning discriminant need not itself be resolved. We apply these certificates to discrete $p$-adic path-integral actions on uniform affine chains, deriving explicit action centers and proving exact output-independence at zero effective quadratic coupling. Pseudocode and a reproducible reference implementation accompany the theory.

</details>


### [167] [On transition probabilities for the ASEP on the half-line](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12258)
*Alexandr Garbali,Cengiz Gazi,Jan de Gier,William Mead,Michael Wheeler*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives and verifies a new multiple contour integral formula for the transition probability in the asymmetric simple exclusion process (ASEP) on the half-line with boundary particle insertion, using coordinate Bethe ansatz and symmetric rational functions; it also computes the probability of particle number conservation over time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a second, previously unknown multiple contour integral representation for the transition probability in the boundary-driven half-line ASEP, complementing an existing one, and to compute the probability that no particles exit the system up to a given time.

Method: Coordinate Bethe ansatz is employed to verify that the proposed contour integral satisfies the discrete time-evolution equation of the ASEP; symmetric rational functions and careful contour deformations are used to prove correctness and handle initial conditions.

Result: A novel multiple contour integral formula for the transition probability is rigorously proven; summing over all final configurations yields an exact integral expression for the probability that the particle number remains unchanged up to time t.

Conclusion: Two distinct contour integral representations exist for the same transition probability in this boundary-conditioned ASEP; the new formula enables exact computation of particle-number-conservation probability, enriching the integrable structure and analytical toolkit for open ASEP systems.

Abstract: We study the asymmetric simple exclusion process on the half-line with particle insertion and no particle removal at the boundary. We point out that there exist two distinct multiple contour integral formulas for the transition probability between two finite particle configurations containing the same number of particles. We recall one of these formulas from the literature and prove a second, new formula by showing that it satisfies the evolution equation using a coordinate Bethe ansatz calculation and verify that the initial condition is implemented by the contour integrals. As an application we sum this formula over all final configurations and obtain an integral formula for the probability that the number of particles is conserved up to a given time. The proofs use symmetric rational functions and their properties together with contour deformations.

</details>


### [168] [An Algebraic Foundation of Rate-Product Style Quantity Calculus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12428)
*Dan Jonsson*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Extends quantity calculus using scalable monoid-heaps to handle non-multiplicable quantities, weak rates, and products, with applications in metrology and physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize existing quantity calculus to accommodate quantities that cannot be multiplied and to incorporate both weak rates and products—limitations not addressed in prior work [1].

Method: Introduces and employs the novel algebraic structure of a scalable (right) monoid-heap to extend the formalism of quantity calculus.

Result: A generalized quantity calculus framework capable of representing non-multiplicable quantities, weak rates, and products; validated through metrological and physical examples.

Conclusion: The scalable monoid-heap provides a robust, flexible foundation for quantity calculus beyond traditional multiplicative structures, enhancing formal rigor in metrology and physics.

Abstract: Using the new notion of a scalable (right) monoid-heap, the quantity calculus in [1] is generalized to include quantities that may not be possible to multiply as well as both (weak) rates and products of quantities. Several examples are given of applications in metrology and physics

</details>


### [169] [Real-Time Path Integral Resurgence: Which Classical Path Contributes?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12510)
*Job Feldbrugge*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes resurgence theory as a practical alternative to Picard-Lefschetz theory for identifying relevant complex classical paths in real-time Feynman path integrals, using high-order asymptotics, Borel-Padé analysis, and the South-East rule; it validates the method on a Rosen-Morse barrier (including tunneling), demonstrates exponential accuracy against the exact propagator, and sketches extension to relativistic, constraint-based path integrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Picard-Lefschetz theory identifies contributing complex paths via thimble intersection numbers, but these are intractable in infinite-dimensional path space; a computationally feasible, physically transparent method is needed.

Method: High-order computation of asymptotic series for real and complex classical paths (via Feynman diagrams and cumulant transport recursion); Borel-Padé resummation to extract divergent tail structure; adjacency inference from Stokes data; application of the South-East rule to determine path relevance; validation against exact quantum propagator for Rosen-Morse potential.

Result: Complete identification of relevant complex classical paths—including the tunneling trajectory—in front of and behind the Rosen-Morse barrier; full mapping of ordinary and higher-order Stokes phenomena as final position varies; resummed series reproduce exact propagator with exponential accuracy.

Conclusion: Resurgence provides a robust, practical, and accurate framework for selecting relevant complex saddle points in real-time quantum path integrals, bypassing the computational bottlenecks of Picard-Lefschetz theory, and extends naturally to relativistic systems with Hamiltonian constraints.

Abstract: Which complex classical paths contribute to a real-time Feynman path integral? Picard-Lefschetz theory answers this question with the intersection numbers of the thimbles, which are notoriously hard to compute in infinite dimensions. We show that resurgence provides a practical alternative. We compute the asymptotic series of the real and complex classical paths to high order, with Feynman diagrams and with a transport recursion for the cumulants, and read off the adjacency of the classical paths from the divergent tails of the series with Borel-Padé methods. The South-East rule then turns adjacency into relevance. For a particle meeting a Rosen-Morse barrier, we identify the relevant complex classical paths in front of and behind the barrier, including the complex path that carries quantum tunnelling, follow ordinary and higher-order Stokes phenomena as the final point moves, and verify the predictions with the exact propagator. The resummed series reproduce the propagator with exponential accuracy. Finally, we outline how the expansion extends to relativistic path integrals governed by a Hamiltonian constraint.

</details>


### [170] [The finite projective classification of complex spinors via momentum maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12549)
*Bartłomiej Sikorski,Adam Sawicki*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies projective orbits of complex spinors using momentum map geometry and Vinberg's theory of graded Lie algebras, providing orbit representatives via critical points of the squared momentum norm in Clifford–Fock models, recovering classical orbit counts and computing projective dimensions and (in dim 14) orbit closure order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify projective orbits of complex spinors uniformly across dimensions where finitely many orbits exist—specifically irreducible spin modules in odd dimensions 1–11 and half-spin modules in even dimensions 2–14—bypassing reliance on classical case-by-case classifications.

Method: Uses symplectic geometry: constructs spinor representatives as sums of pure basis spinors in the Clifford–Fock model, chosen to be critical points of the squared norm of the momentum map; identifies orbits via momentum values in a fixed Weyl chamber; proves completeness using Vinberg’s theory of graded Lie algebras and stabilizer actions.

Result: Recovers known orbit counts: odd dimensions → 1,1,1,2,3,5; even dimensions → 1,1,1,2,2,4,9; computes projective dimension for every orbit; in dimension 14, determines full orbit closure order via monotonicity of the minimum squared momentum norm under degeneration.

Conclusion: A unified, geometric classification of projective spinor orbits is achieved via momentum map theory and graded Lie algebras, extending beyond classical methods and enabling new structural insights—including orbit dimensions and closure relations—especially in the previously uncomputed dimension 14.

Abstract: We describe the projective orbits of complex spinors from the point of view of the momentum map. We treat the irreducible spin modules in odd dimensions 1,3,5,7,9,11 and the half-spin modules in even dimensions 2,4,6,8,10,12,14, which are the only cases with finitely many orbits. In the Clifford--Fock model, we construct representatives as sums of pure basis spinors, and the coefficients are chosen so that the representative is a critical point of the squared norm of the momentum map. The resulting momentum values in a fixed Weyl chamber distinguish the orbits. Completeness follows from Vinberg's theory of graded Lie algebras and stabilizer actions, independently of the classical spinor classifications. We recover the classical counts of 1,1,1,2,3,5 orbits in the odd cases and 1,1,1,2,2,4,9 in the even cases, and compute the projective dimension of every orbit. In dimension fourteen we also determine the closure order of the orbits, using the fact that the minimum of the squared momentum norm increases strictly under degeneration.

</details>


### [171] [One-Particle Resonances and Eigenstate Thermalization in Periodic Free-Fermion Lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12701)
*Tristan Benoist,Vojkan Jakšić,Claude-Alain Pillet,Anna Szczepanek,Cornelia Vogel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal bounds on particle-number fluctuations in eigenstates of a free-fermion model on a d-dimensional periodic cubic lattice, showing variance scales as NL^{d−1} and thermalization error as L^{d−1}/(Nη²), with optimality proven via resonance analysis and Hilbert–Schmidt estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify eigenstate thermalization in free fermionic systems by rigorously bounding particle-number fluctuations in individual energy eigenstates—addressing how well local observables concentrate around their microcanonical averages without invoking ensemble averaging.

Method: Decomposes the centered particle-number operator into zero-transfer and nonzero-transfer components w.r.t. one-particle energy differences; controls the nonzero-transfer part using a sharp resonance bound derived from Mann's theorem on linear relations among roots of unity, and the zero-transfer part via a Hilbert–Schmidt norm estimate.

Result: Proves an O(NL^{d−1}) upper bound on particle-number variance for all normalized eigenstates, and an O(L^{d−1}/(Nη²)) eigenstate-thermalization bound for relative fluctuations of size η; shows these are optimal by constructing eigenstates achieving matching lower bounds and demonstrating maximal nonzero-gap multiplicity is Θ(L^{d−1}).

Conclusion: Eigenstate particle-number fluctuations in this free-fermion model obey dimension-dependent, system-size-scaling bounds that are provably optimal; thermalization quality improves as 1/L at fixed density, confirming robustness of ETH-like behavior even in non-interacting integrable systems when restricted to local observables.

Abstract: We study eigenstate fluctuations of the particle number in the nearest-neighbour free-fermion model on the periodic cubic lattice $(\mathbb{Z}/L\mathbb{Z})^d$. For every fixed dimension, every particle number, every subvolume, and every normalized energy eigenstate, we prove a bound of order $NL^{d-1}$ for the particle-number variance. The corresponding eigenstate-thermalization estimate is of order $L^{d-1}/(N\eta^2)$ for fluctuations of relative size $\eta$. In particular, at positive particle density the bound decays as the inverse linear size. The proof decomposes the centered particle-number operator according to one-particle energy transfer and treats the zero- and nonzero-transfer components separately. The nonzero-transfer part is controlled by a sharp resonance bound, proved from Mann's theorem on relations among roots of unity, while the zero-transfer part is estimated by a Hilbert-Schmidt argument. These bounds are optimal in every dimension: the maximal nonzero-gap multiplicity has order $L^{d-1}$, and suitable many-body eigenstates realize matching variance lower bounds and show that the concentration bound cannot be improved in estimates uniform in the tolerance parameter.

</details>


### [172] [Global Asymptotics of a Toda Soliton Condensate with Random Scattering Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12748)
*Guido Mazzuca,Mateusz Piorkowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a dense N-soliton condensate for the infinite Toda lattice in the N→∞ limit, using random scattering data (eigenvalues and norming constants as independent random variables with mild moment conditions). It establishes deterministic self-averaging via a probabilistic Riemann–Hilbert framework and derives a complete macroscopic description—five distinct finite-gap regimes (elliptic and genus-2 modulated waves) with non-linear regime boundaries—via Deift–Zhou steepest descent; it also proves nonlinear dispersion relations and links macroscopic averages to the soliton gas density of states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend soliton gas theory beyond deterministic or infinitesimal norming constants, by treating both discrete eigenvalues and norming constants as independent random variables—allowing arbitrarily large norming constants under a mild moment condition—and to rigorously derive the macroscopic behavior of a dense, random Toda soliton condensate.

Method: Develops a probabilistic Riemann–Hilbert problem framework to handle randomness in scattering data, proves self-averaging with polynomial convergence rate O(N^{-δ}), and applies the Deift–Zhou nonlinear steepest descent method to the continuum limit to obtain the leading-order macroscopic solution across the full space-time plane.

Result: 1) Random Toda lattice variables converge deterministically to an effective continuum model at rate O(N^{-δ}), 0 < δ < 1/2; 2) The macroscopic solution splits into five finite-gap regimes—explicitly characterized by modulated elliptic and genus-2 waves—with error O(1/ln N); 3) Regime boundaries are genuinely non-linear curves (e.g., genus-2 region boundary is fully non-linear); 4) Nonlinear Dispersion Relations are established; 5) Macroscopic ensemble averages are shown to be determined by the soliton gas density of states.

Conclusion: The dense random Toda soliton condensate exhibits robust self-averaging and admits a precise, fully nonlinear macroscopic description governed by finite-gap solutions and nonlinear dispersion relations—fundamentally differing from classical soliton gas limits with linear characteristics—thereby providing a rigorous statistical mechanics framework for integrable soliton ensembles.

Abstract: We study a dense $N$-soliton condensate for the infinite Toda lattice in the limit $N \to \infty$. In contrast to previous mathematical studies of soliton gases where the norming constants are assumed to be deterministic or infinitesimal, we consider initial scattering data where both the discrete eigenvalues and the norming constants are independent random variables. The norming constants $\gamma_j$ are allowed to be arbitrarily large under a mild moment condition $\mathbb{E}[\gamma_j^\nu] < \infty$ ($\nu > 1$). Our main result is divided into two parts. First, we develop a probabilistic Riemann--Hilbert framework to show that the system self-averages: despite large, independent fluctuations in the initial data, the random Toda lattice variables converge deterministically to an effective continuum model at a polynomial rate $\mathcal{O}(N^{-\delta})$ for $0 < \delta < 1/2$. Second, by performing the Deift--Zhou nonlinear steepest descent analysis of the continuum problem, we obtain the complete leading-order macroscopic description of the lattice across the entire space-time plane. The solution decomposes into five distinct finite-gap regimes, characterized explicitly by modulated elliptic and genus-2 waves with an error of order $\mathcal{O}(1/\ln N)$. In contrast to the straight characteristic rays typical of standard soliton gas limits, the interfaces separating these five macroscopic regimes in the space-time plane are genuinely non-linear curves; in particular, the boundary of the modulated genus-2 region is completely non-linear. Finally, under our assumptions, we establish the Nonlinear Dispersion Relations for the Toda soliton condensate and prove that the macroscopic ensemble averages are determined by the soliton gas density of states.

</details>


### [173] [Eigenvector Occupation Statistics and Extremes in the Rosenzweig-Porter Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12858)
*Yan V. Fyodorov,Mariya Shcherbina,Tatyana Shcherbina*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes eigenvector localization in the GUE Rosenzweig-Porter model at intermediate disorder (1 < γ < 2), deriving exact finite-N projective density via Kac-Rice and Hubbard-Stratonovich methods, revealing a Lorentzian occupation profile with scale K_N ~ N^{2−γ}, exponential fluctuations, a q = 1/2 transition in inverse participation sums, Poisson limits for extremal components (Gumbel for largest, exponential for smallest), and absence of freezing for fixed-q statistics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize eigenvector delocalization–localization crossover and extremal statistics in the critical regime of the Rosenzweig-Porter model, where standard mean-field or random matrix techniques fail due to nontrivial correlations and finite-size effects.

Method: Conditional analysis using normalized one-eigenpair Kac-Rice formula; exact finite-N projective density derivation; reduction to 2×2 Hermitian Hubbard-Stratonovich integral; saddle-point evaluation; asymptotic analysis of generalized inverse participation ratios (IPR_q); extreme-value theory for occupation probabilities; quenched analysis on high-probability regularity event for diagonal disorder.

Result: Exact asymptotic occupation field is self-consistent Lorentzian with effective resonant size K_N(E) ≍ N^{2−γ}; local fluctuations are exponential; IPR_q exhibits sharp transition at q = 1/2; amplitude-counting spectrum has two branches; extremal components converge to Poisson point processes: largest follow Gumbel law, smallest obey exponential law at scale N^{−1−γ}; for all fixed q > 0, the largest component contributes vanishingly small fraction to IPR_q — no freezing.

Conclusion: In the critical γ ∈ (1,2) regime of the RP model, bulk eigenvectors exhibit multifractal-like but non-frozen statistics: they are extended yet highly inhomogeneous, with universal extremal laws and a structural transition in moment scaling at q = 1/2 — distinguishing this regime from both ergodic (γ > 2) and localized (γ < 1) phases.

Abstract: We study the occupation probabilities $p_i=|u_i|^2$ of a normalized bulk eigenvector in the GUE Rosenzweig-Porter model $A_N=B_N+\tau_NG_N$, with $\tau_N^2=N^{1-\gamma}$ and $1<\gamma<2$. Conditioning on an eigenvalue at a regular bulk energy $E$ through the normalized one-eigenpair Kac-Rice law, we derive an exact finite-$N$ projective density and reduce its asymptotic analysis to a Hubbard-Stratonovich integral over $2\times2$ Hermitian matrices, which we evaluate by the saddle-point method. The resulting occupation field is governed by a self-consistent Lorentzian profile with effective resonant size $K_N(E)\asymp N^{2-\gamma}$ and local exponential fluctuations. We prove the asymptotics of all generalized inverse participation sums and identify a transition at $q=1/2$. We also obtain a two-branch thickened amplitude-counting spectrum. At the two ends of the occupation field we prove marked Poisson point-process limits: the largest components have Gumbel fluctuations, whereas the smallest component has an exponential limit on the scale $N^{-1-\gamma}$. Finally, for every fixed $q>0$ the largest component contributes an asymptotically vanishing fraction of the inverse participation sum, showing that these fixed-$q$ statistics do not freeze. The statements are quenched, uniformly on a high-probability regularity event for the diagonal disorder, and hence also hold in the annealed setting.

</details>
