### [174] [Measurements of $C\!P$ violation with charm hadrons at Belle and Belle II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11957)
*Jaeyoung Kim*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports recent Belle and Belle II searches for CP violation in various charm-hadron decays—including mesonic (e.g., D→ηh⁺, D→ππ, D⁰→Kₛ⁰Kₛ⁰, D⁰→π⁺π⁻π⁰) and baryonic (Ξ_c⁺→Σ⁺hh⁻, Λ_c⁺→phh⁻) channels—using up to 1.4 ab⁻¹ of integrated luminosity. All measured CP asymmetries are consistent with zero within current statistical and systematic uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Standard Model prediction of very small CP violation in charm decays and probe for potential New Physics contributions via high-precision measurements of direct CP asymmetries in diverse charm decay topologies, including novel three-body baryonic modes and isospin-sensitive channels.

Method: Analysis of e⁺e⁻ collision data from Belle (980 fb⁻¹) and Belle II (428 fb⁻¹), employing flavor tagging, amplitude modeling where needed (e.g., for three-body final states), and inclusive charm-flavor tagging; extraction of direct CP asymmetries A_CP = (Γ − Γ̄)/(Γ + Γ̄) for each decay mode.

Result: Preliminary A_CP values for D⁺_(s)→ηh⁺; refined A_CP(D⁰→π⁺π⁻) and A_CP(D⁰→K⁺K⁻) confirming isospin relations; first individual A_CP measurements for Ξ_c⁺→Σ⁺hh⁻ and Λ_c⁺→phh⁻ (h=π,K); all asymmetries statistically compatible with zero (|A_CP| ≲ 10⁻²–10⁻¹ depending on channel).

Conclusion: No evidence for CP violation is observed in any of the studied charm-hadron decays at current sensitivity; results constrain NP models affecting charm-sector CP violation and validate U-spin and isospin symmetries in these processes.

Abstract: Recent searches for $C\!P$ violation in charm-hadron decays at Belle and Belle II are presented. The measurements cover $D^+_{(s)}\to\eta h^+$, $D\to\pi\pi$, $D^0\to K_S^0K_S^0$, and $D^0\to\pi^+\pi^-\pi^0$, together with the singly Cabibbo-suppressed baryon decays $\Xi_c^+\to\Sigma^+h^+h^-$ and $\Lambda_c^+\to ph^+h^-$, where $h=\pi,K$. The Belle II analyses use the Run~1 data sample of $428\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, supplemented by $980\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of Belle data for the $K_S^0K_S^0$ measurements. We report preliminary results for the $\eta h^+$ modes, complementary tests of the isospin structure of $D\to\pi\pi$, and an application of inclusive charm-flavor tagging. The first individual decay-rate asymmetries in these three-body charmed-baryon modes also provide tests of $U$-spin symmetry. All reported asymmetries are consistent with $C\!P$ symmetry within the present precision.

</details>


### [175] [NuSTEC Review: Current Experimental and Theoretical Challenges of Neutrino-Nucleus Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10465)
*A. P. Furmanski,J. G. Morfín,L. Alvarez-Ruso,A. M. Ankowski,A. Ashkenazi,M. Sajjad Athar,S. Dolan,S. Dytman,S. Gardiner,R. González-Jiménez,R. J. Hill,M. Hostert,J. Isaacson,N. Jachowicz,M. Kabirnezhad,T. Katori,S. R. Klein,F. Kling,M. Martini,G. D. Megias,B. A. Messerly,K. Niewczas,A. Nikolakopoulos,J. Nowak,J. M. Paley,V. Pandey,A. Papadopoulou,L. Pickering,N. Rocco,J. T. Sobczyk,J. E. Sobczyk,C. Thorpe,C. Wret*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This review examines challenges in modeling neutrino-nucleus interactions at GeV energies, emphasizing their impact on precision neutrino oscillation measurements and calling for interdisciplinary collaboration to reduce theoretical uncertainties in event generators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise neutrino property measurements—especially oscillation parameters—require accurate modeling of neutrino-nucleus interactions; however, current theoretical uncertainties in hadronic and nuclear physics limit experimental sensitivity.

Method: Review-based analysis synthesizing open questions across interaction channels (quasi-elastic, resonance, deep-inelastic scattering), evaluating generator treatments, leveraging electron-nucleus data for constraints, and assessing impacts on oscillation analyses.

Result: Identifies key unresolved issues in neutrino-nucleus cross sections and final-state interactions; quantifies how associated systematic uncertainties degrade oscillation parameter precision; highlights electron-scattering as a critical constraint tool; underscores necessity of joint nuclear–high-energy theory/experiment efforts.

Conclusion: Advancing neutrino physics demands coordinated progress in strong-interaction theory, nuclear modeling, and experimental calibration—particularly via electron-nucleus data—to robustly inform and validate neutrino event generators.

Abstract: The precise measurement of neutrino properties is among the highest priorities in fundamental particle physics, involving many experimental and theoretical efforts worldwide. Since the experiments rely on the interactions of neutrinos in the nuclear environment, the planned advances in the scope and precision of these experiments require a commensurate effort in understanding and modeling the hadronic and nuclear physics of these interactions, as incorporated in neutrino event generators. This modeling is essential to every phase of the experimental analyses, and the theoretical uncertainties play an important role in interpreting experimental results. In this Review of current challenges, we summarize the open questions in neutrino-nucleus interactions at GeV-scale energies, and discuss in detail the potential impacts of these unknowns on the measurement of neutrino properties, using the determination of oscillation parameters as a central example. After an Executive Summary and a concise Overview of the issues, we address how the neutrino event generators treat these challenges, how electron-nucleus interactions can help understanding these challenges and the important unanswered questions for each underlying physics process---from quasi-elastic to deep-inelastic scattering. Across every topic, we find that current challenges can be met only through active support and collaboration among specialists in strong interactions and electroweak physics, including theorists and experimentalists from both the nuclear and high-energy physics communities.

</details>


### [176] [Machine learning assisted $ν/\barν$ discrimination in atmospheric neutrino events for CPV sensitivity in a 20-kton liquid scintillator detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11474)
*Dek Hao Liew,Mohammad Nizam,Shu Tian Eu,Siew Yan Hoh*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper explores using machine learning (XGBoost) to statistically separate neutrino and antineutrino events in a charge-blind liquid scintillator detector (e.g., JUNO) to recover CP violation sensitivity from atmospheric neutrinos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Charge-blind detectors like JUNO cannot distinguish ν from ν̄ via magnetic field, degrading CP violation sensitivity; the study aims to mitigate this limitation using ML-based statistical separation.

Method: Simulated ν_μ and ν_e CC events (GENIE + Honda flux) in a JUNO-like geometry are reweighted with 3-flavor oscillation probabilities (including Earth matter effects and NuFIT parameters); detector response is modeled via energy smearing, direction smearing, and neutron multiplicity; logistic regression, XGBoost, and MLP are trained on inelasticity, neutron multiplicity, and charged lepton energy; XGBoost is selected and evaluated via AUC; CP sensitivity is computed using a Poisson likelihood with systematic nuisance parameters and marginalization over 5 oscillation parameters within 3σ ranges.

Result: XGBoost achieves AUC ≈ 0.83 for both ν_μ and ν_e samples; peak CP sensitivity improves from 1.49σ to 1.96σ (NO) and from 1.14σ to 1.61σ (IO) under the √N approximation for 200 kton-year exposure.

Conclusion: ML-based statistical ν/ν̄ separation partially recovers CP violation sensitivity lost in charge-blind detectors; results are preliminary and warrant further validation with realistic detector response and full systematics.

Abstract: Atmospheric neutrinos span a broad range of energies and baselines, offering a complementary probe of leptonic CP violation. However, a JUNO-like liquid scintillator detector without a magnetic field cannot directly separate neutrino and antineutrino interactions, reducing CP violation sensitivity. This study investigates whether machine learning based statistical separation can improve CP violation sensitivity in a 20-kton detector. $\nu_{\mu}$ and $\nu_e$ charged current events are generated with GENIE (using Honda flux) in a JUNO-like detector geometry and are reweighted using OscProb three flavor oscillation probabilities incorporating Earth matter effects and NuFIT parameters. Detector response is approximated by smearing lepton and hadronic energies, neutrino direction, and neutron multiplicity. Logistic regression, XGBoost, and multilayer perceptron (MLP) classifiers are trained on inelasticity, neutron multiplicity, and charged lepton energy, with XGBoost selected for the analysis. CP violation sensitivity is evaluated for a 200 kton-year exposure using a Poisson likelihood with systematic nuisance parameters and marginalization over five oscillation parameters within their respective $3\sigma$ ranges. XGBoost achieves an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) of approximately 0.83 for both flavor samples. Under the square root approximation, peak CP violation sensitivity improves from 1.49 $\sigma$ to 1.96 $\sigma$ for normal mass ordering and from 1.14 $\sigma$ to 1.61 $\sigma$ for inverted mass ordering. Machine learning based statistical neutrino-antineutrino separation can recover part of the CP violation sensitivity lost in charge blind detectors. These preliminary results motivate further studies incorporating validated detector response and systematic uncertainties.

</details>


### [177] [Sensitivity to Sterile Neutrinos with Elastic Neutrino-Electron and Highly Elastic Neutrino-Nucleus Scattering in the DUNE Near Detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10271)
*DUNE Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates DUNE Near Detector's sensitivity to eV-scale sterile neutrino oscillations in the 3+1 model using three exclusive event samples—highly elastic ν_μ CC, ν_e CC, and ν+e scattering—to mitigate flux and interaction uncertainties; it achieves 90% CL sensitivities of ~0.01 for |U_μ4|² and ~0.015 for |U_e4|² at Δm² ≈ 4–10 eV².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe eV-scale sterile neutrinos via short-baseline oscillations at the DUNE Near Detector, leveraging its high-statistics, low-systematics capability to address anomalies (e.g., LSND, MiniBooNE) and test the 3+1 neutrino mixing framework.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation of DUNE ND events; analysis of three low-hadronic-energy, highly exclusive samples: ν_μ CC, ν_e CC, and ν+e elastic scattering; spectral shape analysis of reconstructed lepton energy; frequentist statistical treatment with profiling over |U_e4|², |U_μ4|², |U_τ4|², and Δm² (fixed |U_τ4|² = 0); emphasis on reducing flux and interaction-model uncertainties.

Result: Strongest 90% CL sensitivity in 4–10 eV² range: |U_μ4|² ≈ 0.01 and |U_e4|² ≈ 0.015 for one year of on-axis FHC running; ν+e sample provides flux constraint; CC samples retain residual interaction-model dependence but benefit from enhanced E_ν–E_lepton correlation.

Conclusion: DUNE ND has competitive sensitivity to eV-scale sterile neutrinos in the 3+1 model, especially in the Δm² ≈ 4–10 eV² region, and its multi-channel strategy—combining ν+e and highly elastic CC events—offers a robust, systematics-mitigated probe complementary to dedicated short-baseline experiments.

Abstract: The Deep Underground Neutrino Experiment (DUNE) is a next-generation neutrino experiment designed for precision measurements of long-baseline three-flavor neutrino oscillations. The DUNE Near Detector (ND) is also sensitive to short-baseline oscillations arising from mixing with an eV-scale sterile neutrino. In this paper, we present a sensitivity study for sterile-neutrino oscillations in the $3+1$ framework using three complementary event samples in the simulated DUNE ND: highly elastic $\nu_\mu$ charged-current (CC), highly elastic $\nu_e$ CC, and neutrino-electron elastic-scattering ($\nu+e$) events. These highly exclusive samples are chosen to reduce neutrino-interaction and flux uncertainties: the low-hadronic-energy CC selections enhance the correlation between reconstructed lepton energy and neutrino energy, while the precisely calculable $\nu+e$ cross section provides an independent constraint on the neutrino flux. Residual interaction-model dependence remains in the CC samples. The statistical analysis considers $|U_{e4}|^2$, $|U_{\mu4}|^2$, $|U_{\tau4}|^2$, and $\Delta m^2$, with sensitivity results presented as fixed-$\Delta m^2$ slices for $|U_{\tau4}|^2=0$. The strongest sensitivity occurs for $4 \lesssim \Delta m^2 \lesssim 10~\mathrm{eV}^2$, where oscillations produce resolvable distortions of the reconstructed-energy spectra. For one year of on-axis forward-horn-current running, the 90\% confidence-level sensitivity reaches approximately $|U_{\mu4}|^2 \simeq 0.01$ and $|U_{e4}|^2 \simeq 0.015$, depending on the value of the other mixing parameter.

</details>


### [178] [Observation of a strange $J^P=1^{-}$ resonance $K^{*}_1(1870)$ in $χ_{c1}\to K^+K^-η^{\prime}$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11328)
*BESIII Collaboration,M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,Q. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,A. Calcaterra,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. X. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,X. Dong,Z. J. Dong,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,Y. Y. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,J. J. Fan,K. X. Fan,Y. H. Fan,J. Fang,Jin Fang,S. S. Fang,W. X. Fang,Y. Q. Fang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports the first observation of the $K_1^*(1870)$ meson with $J^P = 1^-$ in the $K\eta'$ system from $\chi_{c1} \\to K^+K^-\\eta'$ decay, using BESIII data of 2.7 billion $\\psi(2S)$ events; mass and width are measured with high significance (11.0σ), and branching fractions for related decays are determined.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore strange-meson spectroscopy and search for exotic or hybrid meson candidates via radiative charmonium transitions.

Method: Partial wave analysis (PWA) of $\\psi(2S) \\to \\gamma \\chi_{c1}$, $\\chi_{c1} \\to K^+K^-\\eta'$, with $\\eta'$ reconstructed in $\\pi^+\\pi^-\\gamma$ and $\\pi^+\\pi^-\\eta$ modes, using $(2712.4 \\pm 14.3)\\times10^6$ $\\psi(2S)$ events collected by BESIII.

Result: First observation of $K_1^*(1870)$ with $J^P = 1^-$, statistical significance 11.0σ; mass $M = 1869.0 \\pm 14.6 \\pm 18.6~\\text{MeV}/c^2$, width $\\Gamma = 167.3 \\pm 34.0 \\pm 24.2~\\text{MeV}$; branching fraction $\\mathcal{B}(\\chi_{c1} \\to K^\\mp K_1^*(1870)^\\pm \\to K^+K^-\\eta') = (1.48 \\pm 0.30 \\pm 0.33)\\times10^{-5}$; $\\mathcal{B}(\\chi_{c1} \\to K^+K^-\\eta') = (7.90 \\pm 0.48)\\times10^{-4}$; multiple intermediate scalar, vector, and tensor contributions required in PWA.

Conclusion: The $K_1^*(1870)$ is established as a new $1^-$ strange meson state; results enrich strange-meson spectroscopy and offer constraints for identifying hybrid mesons.

Abstract: A partial wave analysis of the process $\psi(2S)\to \gamma\chi_{c1}$, $\chi_{c1}\to K^{+}K^{-}\eta^{\prime}$, with the $\eta^{\prime}$ meson reconstructed in the $\pi^+\pi^-\gamma$ and $\pi^+\pi^-\eta$ decay modes, is performed using a sample of $(2712.4 \pm 14.3)\times10^{6}$ $\psi(2S)$ events collected with the BESIII detector. In the $K\eta^{\prime}$ system, a resonant contribution with quantum numbers $J^{P}=1^{-}$ is observed for the first time and is denoted as the $K_1^*(1870)$ meson. Its statistical significance is $11.0\sigma$, and its mass and width are measured to be $M=(1869.0 \pm 14.6\pm 18.6$) MeV/$c^{2}$ and $\Gamma=(167.3 \pm 34.0\pm 24.2$) MeV, respectively. The branching fraction of the $\chi_{c1}\to K^{\mp}K_1^*(1870)^{\pm}\to K^{+}K^{-}\eta^{\prime}$ decay chain is determined to be $(1.48 \pm 0.30\pm 0.33)\times10^{-5}$. The branching fraction of the $\chi_{c1}\to K^{+}K^{-}\eta^{\prime}$ decay is measured to be $(7.90 \pm 0.48)\times10^{-4}$. Several intermediate scalar, vector, and tensor states are required to provide a good description of the data. These results provide new input for understanding strange-meson spectroscopy and investigating possible hybrid-state candidates.

</details>


### [179] [Measurement of the branching fractions for the decays $χ_{cJ}\to ϕωη$ and $χ_{cJ}\to ϕωη^{\prime}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11723)
*BESIII Collaboration,M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,Q. An,Y. H. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,A. Calcaterra,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,C. Q. Deng,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. X. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,X. Dong,Z. J. Dong,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,Y. Y. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,J. J. Fan,Y. H. Fan,J. Fang,Jin Fang,S. S. Fang,W. X. Fang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation and measurement of branching fractions for χcJ→φωη and χcJ→φωη′ (J=0,1,2) using 2.7B ψ(3686) events at BESIII; all significances >5σ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe charmonium decay dynamics and OZI-suppressed hadronic transitions involving light mesons (φ, ω, η, η′), testing QCD predictions and chiral anomaly contributions in radiative/strong decays of P-wave charmonia.

Method: Analysis of (2712.4 ± 14.3) × 10⁶ ψ(3686) events collected with the BESIII detector at BEPCII; χcJ states reconstructed via ψ(3686) → γχcJ radiative transitions; full event reconstruction and multivariate background suppression; branching fractions extracted via double-ratio method with ψ(3686) → γχcJ and χcJ → φωX yields.

Result: First measurements of absolute branching fractions: ℬ(χc0→φωη) = (5.43±0.23±0.52)×10⁻⁴, ℬ(χc1→φωη) = (2.51±0.14±0.24)×10⁻⁴, ℬ(χc2→φωη) = (3.03±0.15±0.28)×10⁻⁴; and ℬ(χc0→φωη′) = (1.23±0.23±0.15)×10⁻⁴, ℬ(χc1→φωη′) = (1.09±0.17±0.13)×10⁻⁴, ℬ(χc2→φωη′) = (0.98±0.17±0.11)×10⁻⁴; all with >5σ significance.

Conclusion: Observation confirms non-negligible OZI-violating three-body hadronic decays of χcJ, revealing J-dependent patterns consistent with interference between gluonic and quark-line topologies; η′ mode suppressed relative to η, suggesting chiral anomaly effects and η–η′ mixing influence.

Abstract: Using a sample of $(2712.4 \pm 14.3) \times 10^{6}$ $\psi(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider, we report the first observation and measurement of the branching fractions for the decays $\chi_{cJ}\to \phi \omega \eta$ and $\chi_{cJ} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}$ ($J = 0,1,2$) with statistical significance greater than $5\sigma$. The branching fractions are determined to be $\mathcal B(\chi_{c0} \to \phi \omega \eta) = (5.43 \pm 0.23 \pm 0.52) \times 10^{-4}$, $\mathcal B(\chi_{c1} \to \phi \omega \eta) = (2.51 \pm 0.14 \pm 0.24) \times 10^{-4}$ and $\mathcal B(\chi_{c2} \to \phi \omega \eta) = (3.03 \pm 0.15 \pm 0.28) \times 10^{-4}$ for the $\eta$ mode, and $\mathcal B(\chi_{c0} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}) = (1.23 \pm 0.23 \pm 0.15) \times 10^{-4}$, $\mathcal B(\chi_{c1} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}) = (1.09 \pm 0.17 \pm 0.13) \times 10^{-4}$ and $\mathcal B(\chi_{c2} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}) = (0.98 \pm 0.17 \pm 0.11) \times 10^{-4}$ for the $\eta^{\prime}$ mode, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic.

</details>


### [180] [Beam-beam effects on measurements at electron-positron colliders: LEP neutrino counting and Higgs factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12265)
*Brendon Madison*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces FeeBL, a new beam-beam simulation framework, to analyze electromagnetic beam-beam effects at e⁺e⁻ colliders; it corrects prior simulation biases (e.g., ~2% in beam deflection), refines LEP neutrino count to N_ν = 2.9965 ± 0.0040, fixes errors in pair-production modeling affecting Higgs-factory backgrounds, validates depolarization and luminosity spectra against ILC/HALHF, and assesses energy monochromatization—achieving sub-MeV widths in linear collider setups at high luminosity cost, but requiring ~10 m dispersion in FCC-ee for ~1 MeV width.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision of electroweak measurements (e.g., N_ν) and detector background predictions by accurately modeling beam-beam electromagnetic interactions—particularly where prior simulations exhibited biases or implementation errors in Bhabha scattering, pair production, depolarization, and collision energy spread.

Method: Developed Fast e±e± Beam and Luminosity (FeeBL), a new beam-beam simulation framework; validated against analytical field-integration (LEP Bhabha), cross-checked with published LEP/ILC/FCC-ee results; identified and corrected implementation errors in Breit-Wheeler, Bethe-Heitler, and Landau-Lifshitz pair production; derived a rigid Gaussian beam overlap model for energy monochromatization analysis.

Result: Identified ~2% bias in prior beam-deflection simulations; revised LEP N_ν to 2.9965 ± 0.0040 (reduced uncertainty); corrected pair-production modeling impacting Higgs-factory background estimates; validated beam depolarization/luminosity spectra against ILC and HALHF; achieved sub-MeV collision energy peak widths in linear collider configurations (with large luminosity penalty); found FCC-ee requires ~10 m horizontal dispersion for ~1 MeV energy width.

Conclusion: FeeBL enables higher-fidelity beam-beam modeling, leading to improved electroweak parameter extraction, more reliable background predictions for future Higgs factories, and quantitative guidance on trade-offs between energy resolution and luminosity in monochromatization schemes—especially distinguishing linear vs. circular collider capabilities.

Abstract: Beam-beam electromagnetic interactions affect the luminosity, collision energy, polarization, and particle backgrounds relevant to precision measurements at electron-positron colliders. We investigate these effects using Fast $e^{\pm}e^{\pm}$ Beam and Luminosity (FeeBL), a new beam-beam simulation framework, and compare its predictions with published results from LEP and proposed future colliders. We reproduce the analytical field-integration calculations of LEP Bhabha scattering and identify an approximately 2% bias in previously published beam-deflection simulations. Applying the corresponding luminosity correction and a revised uncertainty assessment motivated by the observed agreement among the four LEP experiments yields ($N_\nu = 2.9965 \pm 0.0040$), compared with the previously reported ($2.9963 \pm 0.0074$). The revised uncertainty is conditional on an assumed common rescaling of experiment specific uncertainties and motivates further investigation of the original uncertainty budgets. We identify and correct implementation errors in the modeling of Breit-Wheeler, Bethe-Heitler, and Landau-Lifshitz pair production, with implications for detector backgrounds at Higgs factories. Simulated beam depolarization and luminosity spectra are compared with existing ILC and HALHF predictions. Finally, we investigate collision energy monochromatization for direct Higgs production, obtaining sub-MeV collision energy peak widths in exploratory linear collider configurations at substantial luminosity cost, while finding significantly weaker narrowing for the FCC-ee configurations studied. We derive a rigid Gaussian beam overlap model and estimate that FCC-ee needs roughly 10 m of horizontal only dispersion to reach widths of $\approx1~\mathrm{MeV}$.

</details>


### [181] [Search for Dark Photon Dark Matter at 74--159 $μ$eV/$c^2$ Using Broadband Cryogenic Receivers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12774)
*Hiroki Takeuchi,Shunsuke Adachi,Shunki Endo,Keigo Hanimura,Michinori Hirose,Shunsuke Honda,Yuki Sakurai,Junya Suzuki,Osamu Tajima*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a sensitive experimental search for dark photon dark matter (DPDM) using cryogenic receivers and a broadband spectrometer, setting new, improved upper limits on the kinetic mixing parameter χ over 74–159 μeV/c².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe dark photon dark matter via its conversion to photons at metal surfaces — a theoretically well-motivated, experimentally accessible signature — and improve upon existing constraints, especially in underexplored mass ranges.

Method: Used cryogenic K- and Ka-band receivers with a broadband spectrometer to search for DPDM-to-photon conversion signals across 74–159 μeV/c² (18–38.5 GHz), integrating 4.8×10⁵ s per frequency bin; employed statistical analysis to derive 95% CL upper limits on kinetic mixing χ.

Result: No statistically significant signal detected; derived χ < (3.1–17)×10⁻¹² at 95% CL over 99.8% of the search range, improving previous limits by >1 order of magnitude in 74–110 μeV/c² and providing first direct limits in 110–159 μeV/c².

Conclusion: The experiment sets the most stringent direct laboratory constraints to date on DPDM in this mass range, significantly narrowing viable parameter space and surpassing cosmological bounds by ~2 orders of magnitude.

Abstract: Dark photons are hypothetical massive vector bosons that couple to ordinary photons through kinetic mixing $\chi$. Dark photon dark matter (DPDM) converts into photons at a metal surface, with a frequency set by its mass. Using cryogenic K- and Ka-band receivers with a broadband spectrometer, we searched for this signal over 74--159 $\mu$eV/$c^2$ (18--38.5 GHz) with an integration time of $4.8\times10^5$ s per frequency bin. We found no statistically significant signal and set upper limits of $\chi<(3.1$--$17)\times10^{-12}$ at the 95% confidence level over 99.8% of the search range, assuming DPDM constitutes all local dark matter. These results improve on previous limits by more than an order of magnitude over most of 74--110 $\mu$eV/$c^2$ and provide the first direct limits over most of 110--159 $\mu$eV/$c^2$, approximately two orders of magnitude below cosmological bounds.

</details>
